# Dominion takeaways — what every game we read teaches

**What this is.** The plain-words list of everything the Steam review study has learned that could shape
Dominion. One entry per finished game, plus one part that looks across all of them. Rico asked for it on
2026-10-03, with every usable detail squeezed out of each game. **A game is not Done until its entry is
here** (`CLAUDE.md`, `templates/README.md`). New entries use `templates/dominion-takeaways-entry.md`
and go at the top of "The games", newest finished first.

**Dominion, for this file:** a third-person, four-player co-op sci-fi PvE extraction and arena shooter
built on runs, hosted on one player's machine (a listen server), in Unreal Engine 5. **It starts at four
players on a listen server; a larger version, possibly 200 players, may come later** (Rico, 2026-10-03,
`Rico notes.md`). Lessons about dedicated servers or large player counts are for that later step.

**How to read it.**
- **Every number comes from the game's findings pages** in `findings/`, or, where an entry marks it,
  another findings page or `GAMES-TODO.md`. Each entry names its pages. "Reviews" counts reviews;
  "times it was said" counts single points (one review can make several); "per 100" means per 100
  reviews read.
- **Reviewers' claims are theirs.** Prices, drop rates, patches, bans and events are "reviewers say"
  unless the entry says it was checked against Steam or the studio.
- **"For Dominion — our reading" is our reading, not the reviews'.** Each lesson gives the evidence, what
  Dominion could do, and how strong the evidence is: **strong** (many reviews, clear), **medium**, or
  **weak** (a handful of reviews, or one game). They are suggestions, not decisions.
- **Store facts** (camera, group size) were checked against the game's Steam store page on 2026-10-03
  where an entry says so. Where the findings pages and the store are both silent, the entry says the
  fact is not known rather than filling it in.

**How it was built (2026-10-03).** Each entry was drafted from the game's findings pages and then
checked: every number of 10 or more was matched against the findings pages by script, and every fact
not on the pages was either checked against the Steam store or taken out. Where two pages disagree on a
number, the entry says so in its Limits part. The disagreements found while building this file are
listed in `tag-tree-open-gaps.md`, round 654.

**Second pass (2026-10-03).** After every game's findings pages and `findings/cross-game.md` were rebuilt
on `templates/`, each entry was updated from the rebuilt pages: full top-25 lists, every design area,
new counts where the first pass had none, corrections (each named in the entry's Limits), and new
lessons. Every number was again matched by script against the rebuilt pages.

**The parts:**
1. **Across all games** - the patterns that hold in more than one game, and the lessons that rest on
   them. Start here.
2. **The games** - one entry each, newest finished first, every entry in the same order: what it is,
   how close to Dominion, the short version, praise, complaints, change over time, co-op and online,
   combat and feel, enemies and difficulty, progression and luck, runs and content, money, tech, the
   studio, what players asked for, what only this game shows, the lessons for Dominion, and limits.

## Across all games

This part pulls together what the study learned by putting the games side by side. The study read
English Steam reviews of 29 games: 19 large games read as samples, and 10 small roguelike games
where every English review was read (751 of 751). In all, 28,613 English reviews were summarised
(29,375 in all languages). Each review was cut into short points. A "point" is one thing a reviewer
said, good or bad. One review can hold several points.

Source pages: findings/cross-game.md (sections 1 to 25, rebuilt 2026-10-03), findings/roguelike-block.md
(sections 1 to 3), and "Rico notes.md". Section numbers below are cross-game.md sections unless named
otherwise.

How to read the numbers:
- "Per 100 reviews" means: for every 100 reviews read, how many times this was said. One review can
  say two things, so a number can go over 100.
- Some later sections count "reviews per 100" instead: how many of every 100 reviews said it at least
  once. Where the page uses that count, this file says "of every 100 reviews".
- What reviewers say about a studio, a patch or a fact is what they SAY. The study did not check it,
  unless this file says it was checked.

### The comparison table (plain words)

What each column means:
- **Thumbs up** - the share of the reviews we read that recommend the game.
- **Praise per 100** - how many good things were said for every 100 reviews read.
- **Complaints per 100** - how many bad things were said for every 100 reviews read.
- **Praise to complaint** - good things divided by bad things. Above 1 means players said more good than
  bad. Below 1 means more bad than good.
- **Points per review** - how many separate things the average review said. A high number means people
  wrote longer, more detailed reviews.

**Main table - all 19 large games, counted the same way.** Sections 20 to 25 counted their games with
one script and one rule (every good or bad point counted, after the round-470 counting fix). On
2026-10-03 the page recounted the earlier games with the same script, so sections 9 to 19 each carry a
recounted table; the section 19 table holds the first thirteen large
games. Sections 20 to 25 hold the six games added after that, and section 25's table is the latest. Where
the two tables share a game, the numbers match (Deep Rock Galactic's praise to complaint is 7.43 in
sections 9 to 19 and is rounded to 7.4 in sections 20 to 25; Space Marine 2's praise is 116.1 in
section 19 and 116.0 in section 20). Each row says which section it is taken from.

| Game | What kind of game | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 | Praise to complaint | Points per review | From |

| Crab Champions | third-person runs, solo or co-op | 97.5% | 123.2 | 18.7 | 6.6 : 1 | 1.52 | section 25 |

What the main table says in plain words:
- Complaints outnumber praise in Aliens: Fireteam Elite, Back 4 Blood, The First Descendant, Rogue Core,
  Immortal: Unchained, The Anacrusis, Redfall and Terminull Brigade. The First Descendant is the only one
  of the ten games in section 25's table where this is true; it also has the lowest thumbs up and the
  most points per review of those ten: "its reviewers argue at length" (section 25).
- The two run-based third-person games, Crab Champions and Risk of Rain 2, have the two lowest
  complaint rates: 18.7 and 18.0 per 100 (section 24). Deep Rock Galactic is next at 23.7.
- The highest complaint rates belong to games that did badly: Redfall 241.6, The Anacrusis 224.2,
  Immortal: Unchained 217.7, Rogue Core 193.2, Terminull Brigade 183.6 per 100.
- Deep Rock Galactic has by far the most praise per 100 (175.9). Terminull Brigade has the least (65.4),
  then The First Descendant (83.6) and ARC Raiders (86.8).
- The longest reviews come from games people argued about: Immortal: Unchained 4.10 points per review,
  The Anacrusis 3.97, Redfall 3.77. The shortest come from games almost everyone likes: Risk of Rain 2
  1.43, Crab Champions 1.52, ARC Raiders 1.56.
- Terminull Brigade is free, and the page warns its playtime and player-count numbers mean something
  different from the paid games (section 12).

**Where our sample and Steam's own thumbs up differ.** For Deep Rock Galactic, Helldivers 2, Back 4 Blood
and Immortal: Unchained they match. For five games they do not (top table of cross-game.md):

| Game | Thumbs up, our sample | Thumbs up, Steam's own |
|---|---|---|
| Rogue Core | 70.3% | 60.1% |
| Redfall | 48.6% | 38.5% |
| The Anacrusis | 57.4% | 46.6% |
| Terminull Brigade | 41.0% | 50.9% |
| Aliens: Fireteam Elite | 81.3% | 79.7% |

- Why our sample and Steam disagree for Redfall and Rogue Core: most of their reviews were written in
  the launch month (65% for Redfall, 75% for Rogue Core), and the study reads every month about equally,
  so the angry launch month counts for less. Weighting Redfall's months back to their real size gives
  42.9%. For Terminull Brigade and The Anacrusis, Steam's figure counts every language, not only English.
- The old table at the top of cross-game.md (rebuilt 2026-09-05) used an earlier count. Its numbers are
  close to the recount but not equal: for example Aliens: Fireteam Elite 123.7 praise and 140.2
  complaints there, 124.6 and 141.8 now; Risk of Rain 2 had an earlier figure of 15.9 complaints per 100
  and has 18.0 on the new count (section 20 note). Use the table above to compare games.

**Small roguelike games - every English review read, same count, but do not line them up exactly with
the large games.** The page keeps them in their own table "because ten games of under 200 reviews each do
not belong beside Helldivers 2 in a per-game table". Section 15, recounted 2026-10-03. VOIDCRISIS (17
reviews) and Town Of The Dead Life (1) have no column.

| Game | Reviews read | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 | Praise to complaint | Points per review |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| Alien Dawn | 74 | 63.5% | 94.6 | 90.5 | 1.04 : 1 | 2.03 |
| ArcRunner | 177 | 68.4% | 85.3 | 141.2 | 0.60 : 1 | 2.55 |
| Zombie Girl | 89 | 73.0% | 80.9 | 152.8 | 0.53 : 1 | 3.00 |
| Banzai Escape | 80 | 63.8% | 66.2 | 131.2 | 0.50 : 1 | 2.25 |

- The ten small games together: 65.1% thumbs up, 2.43 points per review, praise to complaint 0.56 : 1
  (section 15).
- Only SCP: Abhorrent and Alien Dawn have more praise than complaints. Alien Dawn is also the one small
  game whose makers kept updating it (see pattern 6); the page does not say one caused the other.
- Even the small games people like (Zombie Girl at 73.0% thumbs up) have far more complaints than praise.
  The block's recommendation is the same sentence in every game: "short, janky, cheap, liked it".

### Patterns that hold across games

**1. Playing with friends is the praise every audience agrees on.**
- What players said: "it is much better with friends." It is the top item on the list of praise found
  in every early game (section 2).
- Numbers (times said per 100 reviews): Back 4 Blood 7.5, Deep Rock Galactic 10.1, Helldivers 2 3.8.
  Later games: Aliens: Fireteam Elite 10.4, Remnant II 10.1, Gunfire Reborn 9.6 (fourth in the study),
  Earth Defense Force 5 8.3 (sections 22, 23).
- A separate claim showed up in The Anacrusis: "it is how I play with my family" - a different buyer,
  not a different friend (section 11).
- Playing in one room: Earth Defense Force 5's split screen is praised in 18 reviews (1.0 per 100, in no
  other game), and 5 more use Steam Remote Play to take it online (section 23).
- How sure: strong. Seven named games, rates near or above 5 to 10 per 100 in most of them.

**2. The short list every audience agrees on (section 2, first three games only).**
- Eight praises named in all three: play it with friends; it pulls you back; it is worth the money;
  the shooting feels good; it is hard in a good way; the difficulty steps are well built; the builds
  are deep; it works alone too.
- Three complaints named in all three: it gets repetitive; it punishes you if you play alone; the
  unlocks are a grind.
- "It gets repetitive" is the tightest match in the whole study: 1.8, 1.4 and 1.4 per 100 in a loved
  game, a hated game and a fought-over game.
- The same choice makes both sides. "Works alone" (1.4, 3.7, 0.9) sits next to "punishing alone" (1.1,
  1.3, 0.7). "Builds are deep" (5.8, 2.7, 1.0) sits next to "unlocks are a grind" (0.9, 0.7, 0.7). "The
  same design decision produces both, in every game" (section 2).
- How sure: medium. The rates are small (about 1 to 6 per 100) and it rests on three games.

**3. A shared ritual or slogan grows only where players are - and it cannot be copied over.**
- What it is: a catchphrase, salute or in-joke players write without being asked, or a name they call
  themselves.
- Numbers (times said per 100 reviews): Deep Rock Galactic 28.6, Helldivers 2 24.5, Rogue Core 7.5,
  Back 4 Blood 0.0. Back 4 Blood has none at all in 1,663 English reviews and 712 Latin American
  Spanish ones. The study had the label ready long before it read Back 4 Blood, so the zero is real.
  All player-culture talk together: Back 4 Blood 0.0, Deep Rock 35.7, Helldivers 2 29.7 per 100. A name
  players call themselves: 0.0, 6.6 and 3.3 (sections 1, 5).
- Rogue Core is from the same studio and world as Deep Rock Galactic and kept the exact catchphrase. Its
  players use it at about 26% of the strength (7.5 against 28.6). "Inheriting one does not inherit its
  hold" (section 9).
- Space Marine 2: the slogan is the second most-said thing, 21.9 per 100. Two games (it and Helldivers 2)
  now have a story catchphrase in about one review in five. Some fans write the whole review in the
  game's own voice, as a soldier of its world (section 17).
- Talk about each other: the share of all points about the player community is Deep Rock Galactic
  28.8%, Helldivers 2 28.5%, Back 4 Blood 9.4%, Rogue Core 9.2%, Redfall 2.7%. This split the two
  surviving games from the three that did not do well, across five games - the page calls it its
  "strongest single line" (section 10).
- What players praise: community is 32.9% of Deep Rock's praise and 37.3% of Helldivers 2's, but 8.3%
  of Back 4 Blood's. Back 4 Blood's praise leans on the marketing (18.5%). "The failure praises the
  marketing. The survivors praise each other" (section 1).
- Small games: the player-culture topic is 1,325 points in the nine big early games and zero in all ten
  small roguelikes, including the four that are co-op. "Culture needs a population" (roguelike-block
  section 5). Immortal: Unchained, a single-player game, has 14 community points in 435 reviews, and 13
  of them are about the studio's talk with players (section 14).
- Things players can quote last longest. Earth Defense Force 5: quotable lines are 7.6 of every 100
  reviews, and 0.6 or less in every other game with 300 or more reviews; "cheesy on purpose" is 169
  points, 9.6 per 100, in no other game. Together they are the most-praised thing about the game, ahead
  of friends (8.3) and the classes (4.0). Players quote the marching song as their whole review and sing
  along with the allied soldiers (8 reviews). Risk of Rain 2: praise for the music rises for seven years
  (3.3, 2.9, 2.2, 5.0, 6.0 per 100), the only praise line in the study that rises, because players quote
  it ("his music was electric", eleven times in 2025-26); the composer is named more often than any
  developer in the study (sections 19, 23).
- Silly actions with no game effect (emotes, the beer, the barrel, the dance) were praised 111 times in
  Deep Rock Galactic (5.2 per 100), 6 times in Helldivers 2, never in Back 4 Blood (section 5).
- Friendly fire as a story maker: 37 points praise it in Helldivers 2 (2.3 per 100), against 3 people in
  the whole study who call it a cost. Remnant II: six say friendly fire is the fun, and one says having
  none saves friendships (sections 5, 18). The page says these early counts miss some friendly-fire
  points, so they are a floor (section 6).
- Story jokes: "the satire lands" is 3.2 per 100 in Deep Rock and 3.3 in Helldivers 2, and 0.0 in Back 4
  Blood (section 5).
- How sure: strong that the pattern exists (many games, large counts). The page says plainly it cannot
  tell cause from effect: a game people stop playing has nobody to write about (sections 7, 10).

**4. Co-op connection problems: joining, hosting, dropping and scaling.**
- Earth Defense Force 5 is hosted by one player, like Dominion. Its online rules draw a list of small
  complaints (counts of reviews): solo and online progress kept separate (7), gear capped online (4), no
  joining a mission already going (4), a weapon cap per mission (2), difficulty that does not follow the
  head count evenly (2), the host leaving ends the game for everyone (1). One review praises the gear cap
  for keeping a mixed group even. "None is large, but they come from the same choice" (section 23).
- Crab Champions: online play draws almost no complaints - 1 point about a disconnect losing a run and
  2 about matchmaking, in 1,499 reviews. Three reviews call its co-op rare for its kind of game
  (section 24).
- Aliens: Fireteam Elite (players connect to each other, no dedicated servers): thumbs up by year was
  81.4%, 80.5%, 81.9%, 85.4%, 81.7%, then 75.3% in 2026. The 2026 bad reviews gather on "the game is
  dead", "cannot find games" and "keeps disconnecting", not on the design. "Nothing about the game got
  worse. What got worse is that there is nobody in it" (section 13).
- Space Marine 2: "keeps disconnecting" went 0.8 to 3.4 per 100 after an April 2025 update, then 0.0.
  Risk of Rain 2: disconnects 1.2 per 100 in 2024. The First Descendant: 15 cannot-connect complaints,
  all at launch (sections 17, 19, 25).
- ARC Raiders: matchmaking that sorts players by how they behave, not by skill, drew one praise and five
  kinds of failure: it misreads self-defence, hunters game it, it gathers the cheaters together, it gets
  worse as players leave, and it plainly does not work (section 16).
- Sharing loot in a group: in Risk of Rain 2 the complaints about sharing items between teammates are 12
  (section 19); in Rogue Core "teammates can take your things" is 5.8 per 100 (section 9).
- The Anacrusis showed a small game where the studio's chat server was the only place to find a group
  (section 11).
- How sure: weak to medium. The counts are small (1 to 15). The page itself warns that "a large share
  of a small game is still small counts" (section 23). The page gives no wider count of rejoining after
  a drop.

**5. Patches are judged the same way everywhere - and fast fixes are praised even by people who dislike
the game.**
- Section 9 table (times said per 100 reviews): "the patch made it better" Back 4 Blood 0.0, Deep Rock
  1.6, Helldivers 2 1.2, Rogue Core 3.8. "It fixed what mattered" 3.0, 0.3, 1.6, 3.7. "The studio
  listens and acts" 0.4, 1.8, 4.7, 3.1. "The studio ignores feedback" 0.8, 0.0, 0.9, 1.8.
- Rogue Core leads both patch-praise lines while getting its worst scores. "Praise for the response and
  rejection of the product are independent measurements" (section 9).
- Rogue Core's good-to-bad patch talk went 4.8 to 1 at launch, 5.8 to 1 in June, 1 to 1 in July after a
  hotfix. The thumb turned a month later.
- Redfall: the first big patch (June 2023) moved nothing. Game Update 2 (October 2023) moved thumbs up
  from 34.7% to 60.7% and it stayed. But it came five months in, when players were in single figures.
  "A recovery patch that lands after the audience leaves changes the reviews and not the business"
  (section 10).
- Patches that make it worse: Risk of Rain 2, 1.7 per 100 in 2024; Remnant II, 1.5 per 100 in 2024 for
  the DLC patches; Crab Champions, 7 points after its Island Update plus 3 who want the old version back
  (sections 18, 19, 24). The page has no separate count for weapon nerfs.
- Fixing a cheat can hurt honest players: ARC Raiders' last period adds a duplication exploit and the
  wipe that followed it, which reviewers call the studio taking away what they had earned (reviewers'
  claims; no patch notes were pulled) (section 16).
- How sure: medium. Four to seven games, rates of 1 to 5 per 100.

**6. The small-studio arc: praised for listening in year one, called abandoned by year three.**
- In the small roguelike block, "the developers listen" was said 23 times, every one in the launch
  window. "Updates stopped" was said 16 times, every one a year or more after launch.
- Five of the nine real block games made that turn (Zombie Girl, ZCREW, ArcRunner, Die After Sunset,
  VOIDCRISIS), often inside the same review, edited later. ArcRunner: 10 "they listen" and 9 "they
  left". One ArcRunner review in May 2023 says the devs "really listen", then the same review in
  January 2024 says they "seemed to have abandoned" it.
- Alien Dawn is the exception: 7 "they listen", 0 "they left", 2 "still supported", and its 2025 edits
  say so.
- Big games: "updates stopped" is 4.7 per 100 in Back 4 Blood and 0.0 in Deep Rock and Helldivers 2.
  "The studio earned my trust" is 0 in Back 4 Blood, 63 points in Deep Rock, 14 in Helldivers 2. "New
  content after launch is free" 1.5 per 100 in Deep Rock (section 5).
- Steady updates are praised most in Gunfire Reborn (3.9 per 100), Warframe (3.6) and Deep Rock (3.2)
  (section 22).
- How sure: medium. Many games, but the block counts are small (1 to 10 per game).

**7. Why the complaint line rises - five shapes, and only one is the game.**
- Helldivers 2: the game held and the studio's relationship with players collapsed.
- ARC Raiders: the design held and the lobby collapsed. Thumbs up fell 86% to 52% while cheating
  complaints rose fourteen-fold, and design complaints fell in the same time (sections 16, 17).
- Space Marine 2: everything held and price complaints took over. Thumbs up 88.5% at launch and 87.8%
  two years later, inside three points across five periods, while praise per 100 rose 107 to 128,
  complaints 55 to 71, crashes went 3.2 to 0.0 per 100, "only buy it on sale" 0.5 to 5.1, and "too
  grindy" 0.7 to 2.6. "A studio that fixes its engineering does not earn a higher thumb - it earns a
  different complaint." 46% of all reviewers wrote in the launch month.
- Remnant II: nothing changed, the reviewer did. Thumbs up 86.5%, 79.6%, 82.4%; complaints per 100 went
  67 to 91; launch reviewers argued about upscaling (1.5 per 100) and the sequel (12.7 accept it), 2024
  reviewers about the first game (7.1 "falls short") and the DLC patches, later ones about the online
  guide (7.9 to 10.0 per 100). By 2026 the hidden parts had been dug out of the game files, and three
  2025-26 reviews say the studio meant that to happen. "A complaint that rises for three years is not
  always a problem that got worse" (section 18).
- Risk of Rain 2: complaints fell for five years (22.5 to 13.0 to 9.2 per 100), then doubled to 18.3 in
  2024 when a new owner took over. Thumbs up went from 96-98% to 92.9% and 93.5%. "The publisher's name
  was the biggest single thing that ever happened to its score ... and it cost two points."
- Warframe: a flat line - a complaint built into the design that the people who stay accept. Its thumb
  is flat too, 85.4% to 92.9% by year (section 20).
- "A rising complaint line has four causes and only one of them is the game" (section 19); Warframe
  added the fifth shape (section 20).
- Also: Gunfire Reborn's complaints slid 20.9, 28.5, 36.1, 46.3 per 100 across four periods while praise
  stayed between 76.6 and 105.3, in three late groups: the English text, moving, and fewer players.
  "A game read only in its first year would miss" them (section 22).
- How sure: medium. Several games, one shape each.

**8. Owners and publishers can hurt a game the studio made well.**
- Helldivers 2: "the owner puts me off" 2.8 per 100 (section 5). Helldivers 2 also has "not sold in my
  country" at 2.4 per 100, a rate no other early game has.
- Risk of Rain 2: same complaint 1.7 per 100 in 2024; 11 complain about the new owner, 6 accept them,
  and 5 more recommend the game with a grudge. Reviewers name the publisher's other games and its chief
  executive. A user agreement added after purchase drew a review saying the buyer could no longer play
  or refund; a later review changed back when it was removed (section 19).
- Redfall: the owning company, not the studio, ended it. Its always-online check outlived the studio:
  13 of 18 "cannot connect" reviews are after the closure, 7 of them by people with zero hours. Buyers
  did the support themselves: 8 points post the fix for each other and 2 point to the one setting that
  causes the crashes (section 10).
- Remnant II: reviewers praise one company for the design and blame the publisher for save files that
  break and stay unfixed (section 18).
- Gunfire Reborn: one review about a user-agreement clause (91 found it helpful) (section 22).
- How sure: medium. Several games, small rates.

**9. Price: "fair" is praised everywhere; "wait for a sale" marks the games that struggled.**
- "The price is fair" (per 100): Back 4 Blood 1.6, Deep Rock 2.7, Helldivers 2 1.5. Warframe: 198
  points, its fourth most common tag (sections 2, 20).
- "Only buy it on sale" (per 100): Redfall 17.0, Aliens: Fireteam Elite 9.1, Back 4 Blood 8.6, Earth
  Defense Force 5 3.4, Deep Rock 0.3, Helldivers 2 0.0 (sections 5, 23). In Space Marine 2 it grew from
  0.5 to 5.1 over two years (section 17).
- Back 4 Blood's two top complaints are "wait for a sale" and the comparison it invited; its first
  complaint about how it plays is eighth (section 3).
- In the small roguelike block, one review in six (118 reviews, 15.7%) is about the price or the amount
  of game: fair price 38, too high for what it is 26, buy on sale 25, too little content 32. The
  recommendation reads the same in every small game: "it is short, it is janky, it is cheap, I liked it"
  (roguelike-block section 2).
- Immortal: Unchained: 63% of "too pricey for what it is" points come from people who still recommend
  it (section 14).
- How sure: strong for the sale line (several games, large rates in the failures).

**10. Money in free games: the fight is about time, and stacked chance reads as a sales trick.**
- Complaints about how the game makes money (of every 100 reviews): The First Descendant 14.6,
  Terminull Brigade 13.2, Warframe 2.8, Helldivers 2 1.8, every other game with 300 or more reviews 1.4
  or less (section 25).
- Warframe: praise about its money side beats complaints 134 to 103. The biggest money point is neutral:
  "paying only shortens the grind" (73). The player market makes the paid currency earnable (46).
  "I bought in to support the studio" is 0.93 per 100, the highest anywhere. "Paying affects play" is
  16 in 3,233 reviews. "The fight about money in this game is a fight about time" (section 20).
- The First Descendant: grind for a box, roll it for a part, wait for a craft, pay to skip any step.
  "One random drop leads to another" 20, "the frustration is built to sell shortcuts" 58, "the shop
  charges far too much" 75 (the last two under 0.5 per 100 in Warframe). Then the game showed drop
  odds, added bad-luck protection and catch-up rewards; complaints fell from 128 to 84 per 100 across
  four periods, and grind and price complaints fell by more than half (section 25).
- "Both this game and Warframe are free; the gap is not price but the chain players describe"
  (section 25).
- How sure: medium. Strong contrast, but only three free games and the page says the model and the game
  cannot be separated (section 12).

**11. Randomness and grind.**
- "The unlocks are a grind" is small in the early games (0.9, 0.7, 0.7 per 100). In Warframe it is the
  top complaint: 264 reviews, 8.2 per 100, flat for thirteen years (6.8 to 9.6 by period), and 223 of
  the 264 recommend the game anyway. The same reviewers say everything can be earned (133), the grind
  pays off (111) and the price is fair (198) (section 20).
- "The item you need may never drop" (per 100): Escape from Duckov 2.0, the highest; Remnant II 1.7;
  Warframe 1.2; Risk of Rain 2 0.3. In Duckov nobody can trade you the part, and its late-game grind
  complaints (14, in no other game) sit there (section 21).
- Complaints about randomness in general (of every 100 reviews): The First Descendant 7.2, Rogue Core 6.6,
  Remnant II 2.5, Escape from Duckov 2.0, Warframe 1.3 (section 25).
- Remnant II: the reroll that never gives the item (24), the roll that decides which content you ever
  see (7). Loot that feels like filler also shows up there: nine reviews call the rings filler, three
  say they are always short of the currency, two praise drops that come in planned steps (section 18).
- Slow start because the good systems are still locked (per 100): Gunfire Reborn 1.2, Warframe 1.1 -
  both put the long-term unlocks before the fun build, and both are praised for the build once it
  arrives (section 22).
- Rico's note: a Deep Rock reviewer said "I'm never playing just to reach a goal" - the missions are
  fun in themselves. Rico: "I feel this way playing Helldivers 2." Its opposite, "nothing left to chase",
  came from four Deep Rock players (Rico notes, 2026-08-31).
- How sure: medium. Many games, but rates differ a lot by game.

**12. Feeling powerful wins; losing control loses.**
- "It makes you feel superhumanly strong" (per 100): Space Marine 2 4.5, Risk of Rain 2 3.8, Crab
  Champions 2.5 (section 24). In Space Marine 2, 63 of 64 praise points come from people who recommend
  it, and 12 of 13 "never as strong as the story says" complaints come from people who do not - the
  cleanest thumb split of any pair in the study (section 17).
- What breaks the power fantasy is always control taken away: stuns, a parry that fails when needed
  (section 17). Space Marine 2's most-helpful review (632 votes, thumbs down) says one enemy faction is a
  whole difficulty step harder because of screen clutter and being stunned again and again.
- Immortal: Unchained: faults about numbers are forgiven, faults about losing the controller are not.
  Recommended share: "some options are useless" 73%, "one option rules" 72%, but "no way to fight back"
  25%, "slow weapon handling" 30%, "stuns take control away" 31% (section 14).
- Hated enemies: in Risk of Rain 2 fourteen reviews name an enemy and say "remove it"; the one that says
  why says it was killed in one hit. What players hate is how the enemy plays, not its name
  (section 19).
- Too much power has a cost: Risk of Rain 2 has 73 praise points for becoming a god, one complaint that
  inputs stop mattering (247 hours), and "the effects block your view" is its second complaint (14)
  (section 19). One review there asks for an epilepsy warning.
- Shooting that feels impactful (per 100): Back 4 Blood 5.8, Deep Rock 1.7, Helldivers 2 1.4. The
  Anacrusis's biggest complaint is weightless combat: 81 points, 3.4% of all its points (sections 2, 12).
- How sure: medium to strong. Four to six games, clear thumb splits.

**13. Movement is the feel players name - and only two games earn the praise.**
- Praise for movement (of every 100 reviews): Crab Champions 2.7, Warframe 2.6, every other game with
  300 or more reviews 0.5 or less - at most 1 review in 200 (section 24). In Crab Champions players call
  it responsive (39); in Warframe they say it rewards practice and skill (69).
- The commonest movement complaint elsewhere is "sluggish": Gunfire Reborn 15, Rogue Core 11, Immortal:
  Unchained 10 (counts of points). Moving is one of Gunfire Reborn's three late complaint groups
  (section 22).
- How sure: medium. Clear gap, small counts.

**14. Content amount: the successful game's top complaint is "not enough of it".**
- Aliens: Fireteam Elite: the two largest points sit six apart and point opposite ways: "faithful to the
  films" 193 and "too little content" 187. "From the inside it looks like 187 people saying there was not
  enough of it" (section 13).
- Space Marine 2: "too little" is the top complaint (84), and 66 of the 84 are from people who
  recommend it; median 17 hours. It was 7.6 per 100 at launch, 1.9 as missions were added, back to 5.1
  in 2026 ("thirteen missions after three years"). It is row 1 there at 5.9 per 100; in Deep Rock the
  same complaint is row 4 at 0.9 per 100 (section 17).
- Deep Rock's whole complaint list (515 points) is mostly about design and content: 57%. It is "a
  content-appetite problem, and nothing else" - no crashes, servers, money or studio conduct in its top
  25 (section 3).
- The Anacrusis: "the potential is still there" by year 35, 33, 4, 2; "the game is dead" 8, 14, 15, 10.
  68 of 74 hopeful points are from the first two years. "No other game here shows optimism collapsing
  as a separate event from sentiment falling" (section 11).
- Small block: "too little content" 32; Banzai Escape's five "too short" points all recommend it.
- How sure: strong. Many games; the complaint comes from fans.

**15. Performance: one fault can be a whole game's story.**
- Terminull Brigade: stutter is 165 points, 7.3% of everything said, the largest single item in any game.
  40 of those reviews still recommend it; 18 say their verdict flips the day it is fixed. Reviewers name
  four causes and the studio names a fifth (section 12).
- Helldivers 2: technical faults are 21.7% of complaints, more than design (19.9%), and publishing plus
  live service add another 28.4%: "half its complaints are about decisions and defects, not design"
  (section 3).
- Remnant II: four kinds of "my machine cannot run it": only works with upscaling (14), lowering settings
  does not help (7), only stable with the processor slowed (2), will not start at all (9; 8 of them
  thumbs down, median 2 hours). None of them is a crash complaint. Its technical complaints went to zero
  "because the machines caught up, not the studio" (section 18).
- The First Descendant (Unreal Engine 5): performance complaints fell from 8.8 to 4.9 of every 100
  reviews (section 25).
- Space Marine 2: crashes 3.2 to 0.0 per 100, with no rise in thumbs up (section 17).
- Redfall: buyers found one setting that causes the crashes and posted it in their reviews (section 10).
- How sure: strong. Many games, big counts in some.

**16. "One in five says nothing" - a floor that rises with approval.**
- Early games: a thumbs up with no reason given is 25.9 (Back 4 Blood), 20.6 (Deep Rock) and 22.5
  (Helldivers 2) per 100 reviews. The page first called this a constant (section 4).
- Later games broke the constant: ARC Raiders' bare thumbs up is 40% of its sample (31% with nothing
  specific at all); Space Marine 2's is 26% with 19% saying nothing at all; Risk of Rain 2 50.3 and Crab
  Champions 61.0 per 100, and 47% of Risk of Rain 2's points say nothing specific. The small block is
  10.9%, about one in nine (sections 16, 17, 19, 24; roguelike-block section 2).
- A slogan soaks up the wordless praise: "a fan with nothing to say says For the Emperor" (section 17).
- "A game that almost everyone likes says the least about why." Crab Champions' mean review is 21.1
  words; no complaint reaches 10 (section 24). Gunfire Reborn: 60% of reviews are 15 words or fewer
  (section 22).
- How sure: strong. Every game shows it; the size moves with how liked the game is.

**17. A thumbs up does not mean nothing is wrong.**
- Aliens: Fireteam Elite is 79.7% on Steam and Back 4 Blood 69.2%, ten points apart, yet their praise to
  complaint is 0.88 and 0.86. Its 1,221 thumbs-up reviews carry 1,187 complaints and 1,759 praise; its
  280 thumbs-down carry 918 complaints and 98 praise. "The thumb records whether they would buy it
  again; the text records what they would change" (section 13).
- Immortal: Unchained: a recommender writes 1.53 complaints, second to Redfall's 1.80; 45.5% of all its
  complaints sit on recommending reviews (section 14).
- Below about 0.9, the ratio stops following the thumb: Terminull (50.9% on Steam) has the worst ratio,
  Redfall (38.5%) does better (section 13).
- The ratio also hides the story. The Anacrusis (0.58) and Redfall (0.48) are close on the ratio, but
  Redfall is a launch disaster and The Anacrusis is a liked game its fans stopped hoping for: "the stories
  share nothing ... Do not let the ratio column do the reading" (section 11).
- How sure: strong for the pattern; the ratio warning rests on six games.

**18. The words move before the score.**
- Rogue Core's ratio at 300 reviews predicted its thumb at 500; its July patch talk predicted its August
  thumb. Two of the first four games showed it. "A studio watching only the percentage is reading a
  lagging indicator" (section 9).
- Crab Champions: complaints went 18.8, 19.5, 12.7, then 24.8 per 100 after its Island Update while
  thumbs up stayed at 97.8% (section 24).
- Space Marine 2 and Remnant II: the whole complaint list turned over under a flat thumb (pattern 7).
- How sure: medium. Two to three games, plus the turnovers in pattern 7.

**19. Detail follows how MANY things people disagree about, not how strongly.**
- The most negative games were the most detailed for five games (Redfall 3.77 points per review on the
  recount). Then Terminull, the most negative, said less (3.05), because its complaint is one thing:
  "unplayable, fix the stutter" is a whole review. "Detail tracks the NUMBER of disagreements, not their
  strength" (section 12).
- On the full recount the most detailed reviews are Immortal: Unchained 4.10, The Anacrusis 3.97 and
  Redfall 3.77; the least detailed are Risk of Rain 2 1.43, Crab Champions 1.52 and ARC Raiders 1.56
  (section 19, 25 tables).
- How sure: medium.

**20. Being compared with other games is a measured part of every game.**
- Back 4 Blood: arguing about which game it follows is 25.1 per 100, against Deep Rock 3.1 and
  Helldivers 2 0.9. "Accepts it as the successor" 14.5; "set a bar it could never clear" 8.4
  (sections 1, 5). "Where the other two grew a language of their own, this one kept borrowing somebody
  else's" (section 1).
- The Anacrusis names another game in 263 points (10.9%), Redfall in 145 (3.8%). A third of that is plain
  description ("Left 4 Dead in space"), not a verdict (71 points) (section 11). Talk about marketing
  and comparison is 14.4% of all The Anacrusis's points, and 13.5% of Remnant II's (sections 11, 18).
- Immortal: Unchained: 17.5 per 100 describe it by other games, its largest single item; next to it,
  the same claim both ways: "unlike anything else" 27 and "a rival does it better" 25 (section 14).
- Remnant II: "accepts the sequel" 139 against "falls short of the studio's earlier games" 53 (2.6 to
  1); both sides name the same features, and the most-helpful review (174 votes) asks to lift the cap on
  trait points. Two say the sequel changes too little. Acceptance falls 12.7 to 4.7 per 100.
  "A sequel is reviewed against its predecessor for two years and then against itself" (section 18).
  Risk of Rain 2 has five "I liked the first game more" points (section 19).
- Escape from Duckov: "beats its rivals" 6.1 per 100, the highest (The Anacrusis 5.6, Warframe 2.1);
  135 of 1,160 reviews name Tarkov, only 6 thumbs down. Its comic look "sets expectations low and then
  clears them": "looks like a joke, plays like a real game" is 39 points there, and 1.3 per 100 in Crab
  Champions (sections 21, 24).
- The First Descendant: 282 reviews (17.6%) name Warframe, half thumbs down; Destiny named in 186; "a
  named rival does it better" on 112 reviews here and 2 in the rest of the study. When it wins, reviewers
  give the shooting and respect for their time; when it loses, the grind and the shop (section 25).
- Crab Champions: 43 reviews name Risk of Rain; all 9 "beats its rivals" points are against it. Two
  reviews in 2024-10 say they came over because Risk of Rain 2 got worse for them (section 24).
- Earth Defense Force 5: no review names Helldivers before 2024; 36 do after, none thumbs down;
  "beats its rivals" rose from 0.4 to 3.2 per 100. "The same game gets a new yardstick without
  changing" (section 23).
- How sure: strong. Nearly every game.

**21. Reviewers argue with the review page in games that did badly.**
- "This game is judged unfairly" (per 100): Rogue Core 10.2, Back 4 Blood 7.1, Helldivers 2 1.3, Deep
  Rock 0.2 (section 9). Redfall: 124 "judged unfairly" against 9 "deserved it" - 14 to 1. Reputation is
  Redfall's largest subject, 356 points (9.3%), and nowhere near the top in any other game (section 10).
- "It is what a review page looks like after a public pile-on: the people arriving later spend their
  review arguing with the people who arrived first" (section 10).
- How sure: medium.

**22. Reviews change with age.**
- Reviews shrink: Warframe's mean length went 67, 54, 30, 30, 28 words across five periods; short reviews
  (15 words or fewer) 51% to 69%. A topic's fall over time only means something if it is much bigger than
  this shrink; in Warframe the falls that pass this test are "it gets repetitive" 3.3 to 0.6 and "looks
  great" 5.9 to 0.9 (section 20).
- Later reviewers have played longer and complain more: Space Marine 2 mean hours 29 to 74, complaints
  55 to 71 per 100. The First Descendant median hours 20 at launch to 50-70 later; its three most-helpful
  reviews (592, 554, 545 helpful) are thumbs down from players with 117 to 3,012 hours (sections 17, 25).
- When a loop is replaced, only long players notice: in Terminull Brigade, six reviewers who played 4,
  13, 73, 102, 147 and 402 hours (median player: five) said a season swapped the shape of play. "Nobody
  at zero hours raises it" (section 12).
- Hope runs out: The Anacrusis's hopeful reviews are almost all from its first two years (pattern 14).
- Edited reviews: Aliens 7.8% overall, falling from 12.1% (2021) to 1.9% (2026) - age, not disagreement;
  Risk of Rain 2 223 (11.8%); Warframe 581 (18.0%); Helldivers 2 18.4%; ARC Raiders 172, mostly
  negative; Space Marine 2 155, edited both ways (sections 13, 16, 17, 19, 20).
- Late reviews drift off their language: in Remnant II, reviews written in another language under the
  English tag go from 0.0 per 100 in 2023 to 2.9 in 2026 (28 across ten games) (section 18).
- How sure: strong. Many games.

**23. Things outside the game move the thumb.**
- Escape from Duckov: 96.7% up in November 2025, 74.4% in December - a mod dispute brought 13 of
  December's 34 thumbs down (reviewers' claims, not checked). "The monthly thumb needs the round notes
  beside it" (section 21).
- Terminull Brigade: a paid install campaign wrote 90 reviews, 76 at zero hours. Removing them moves the
  thumb 40.98% to 43.3%, only 2.3 points, and the ratio from 0.36 to 0.37. Two waves of it: 36.6% up,
  then 9.1% up four months later. "A studio blaming its number on the reward crowd is wrong by about nine
  tenths" (section 12).
- Crossovers: found in 25 reviews across five games; 8 up, 6 down in Terminull. "A crossover is a door,
  not a verdict."
- Redfall drew reviews from people who got it free with hardware, reviewing a game they did not buy
  (section 10).
- AI-made art: Gunfire Reborn 5 reviews, ARC Raiders 1 (section 22).
- How sure: medium.

**24. Fan service splits a review page in half.**
- Two small games (Zombie Girl, FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL): about one review in five each is this
  argument, and the two sides split the thumb almost perfectly (9-3 against; 8-0 or 11-0 for). In FULL
  METAL SCHOOLGIRL the twelve censorship reviews hold 52% of all helpful votes (section 15).
- The First Descendant: "the cast is built to titillate" is 9.3 of every 100 reviews, rising 6.6 to 16.8,
  its loudest single subject; Terminull 3.3; Earth Defense Force 5 0.8 (section 25).
- Politics: The Anacrusis drew both "put off by its politics" and "I like it for that", about the same
  cosmetic banners (section 11). In Space Marine 2, 16 say the politics of its world drew them in
  (section 17).
- How sure: medium.

**25. Small studios are graded against their size.**
- "I grade it against how small the team is": 3.2% of small-block reviews, 0.7% in the nine big early
  games (section 15).
- How sure: medium.

**26. Where a game's complaints live tells you what kind of failure it is (section 3, then later games).**
- Back 4 Blood is "a positioning failure": marketing is 10.5% of its complaints, two and a half times
  either other early game, and its first complaint about how it plays is eighth. Total complaint points:
  2,284.
- Deep Rock Galactic: design and content are 57% of its complaints; publishing 2.7%. Its whole list is
  515 points, "under a quarter of Back 4 Blood's, over nearly twice as many months".
- Helldivers 2: "the only game where the game itself is not the main target" (898 complaint points).
- ARC Raiders: the top 25 complaints are other players' behaviour 180 against design 148 (section 16).
- Immortal: Unchained (single-player): design is 53.6% of everything said (section 14).
- How sure: medium. Each game is one case, but the same sorting works on all of them.

**27. Some things only one early game had, and they mark its kind of trouble (section 5).**
- Back 4 Blood alone: "no mod support" 3.0 per 100, "the AI teammates are useless in a fight" 2.6 (Deep
  Rock 0.1, Helldivers 2 0.0).
- Deep Rock Galactic most: "every role is needed" 4.6 per 100 (Back 4 Blood 1.7, Helldivers 2 0.0).
- How sure: weak to medium. One game each, rates of 2 to 5 per 100.

**28. One review in a sample can be about the wrong game.**
- Terminull Brigade: one review with 1,470 hours describes a different game entirely. "It was caught by
  reading it, not by a check - so the rate is unknown in every game on this page" (section 12).
- How sure: unknown size.

### Where games differ

**Runs versus missions.**
- Run-based third-person games (Crab Champions, Risk of Rain 2) read alike: lowest complaints (18.7 and
  18.0 per 100), most bare thumbs up (61.0 and 50.3 per 100), and both praise getting too strong (2.5 and
  3.8 per 100) (section 24).
- Rogue Core, the run-based spin-off, has two things no other game has: a clock that decides when you
  leave (13.8 per 100) and teammates who can take your things (5.8 per 100) (section 9). Five reviews
  across three games call the timer the thrill, and three say the action that never stops is the fun
  (section 19).
- Gunfire Reborn (co-op runs with seasons) is praised for seasons you can finish later (6 reviews) and
  for steady updates (3.9 per 100, the highest) (section 22).
- Crab Champions is the run game where players set their own difficulty: detailed difficulty settings
  are 1.1 per 100, the highest (Escape from Duckov 0.5), and "hard in a good way" rose from 0.3 to 2.2 per
  100 (section 24).
- Mission games show "too little content" as their big late complaint (Aliens 187, Space Marine 2 84).
- Earth Defense Force 5 (missions) makes players pick up loot by hand: 10 call it a chore (in no other
  game), 5 use cheats and 4 use mods to collect drops, and 2 lose the drops left when the mission ends
  (section 23).
- Run games: in the small block, one game says the early floors become a chore you cannot skip (2
  sightings); not shown in a second game yet. A checkpoint you can use only once also rests on one game
  (section 15).

**Free versus paid.**
- Free games: Terminull Brigade 0.36 : 1, The First Descendant 0.68 : 1, Warframe 2.5 : 1. Money
  complaints 13.2 to 14.6 of every 100 reviews in two of them, 2.8 in Warframe.
- Paid games: the money complaint is "wait for a sale" (Redfall 17.0, Aliens 9.1, Back 4 Blood 8.6 per
  100).
- Hours, player counts and refund behaviour mean something different when the game is free (section 12).
- Within free games, what decides it is the chain players describe, not the price (section 25).

**Extraction versus not.**
- Only two extraction games were read. Each one's top request is the other's format. ARC Raiders: 55
  "a mode I expected is missing" (3.7 per 100), 42 of them ask for PvE. Escape from Duckov: 39 (3.4 per
  100), most ask for co-op (section 21).
- "No PvP is a feature": Escape from Duckov 51 (4.4 per 100), ARC Raiders 0, next is Terminull at 0.8;
  no other game reaches 1 per 100. "Won over someone who avoids the genre": Duckov 16 (1.4 per 100),
  second only to Remnant II (1.5).
- ARC Raiders (other players can shoot you): the top 25 complaints are player behaviour 180, design 148
  (about half of that is "give me a mode without other players"), technical 42, the studio's response 32,
  live service 20. Player behaviour was in no earlier game's top ten; here it is five of the top eight.
- ARC Raiders: "not knowing who to trust is the thrill" 95 (6.4 per 100) against "says friendly then
  shoots you in the back" 38; 147 reviews carry only the praise, 158 only the complaint, 11 both.
- ARC Raiders: thumbs up fell 86% to 52% while "cheaters spoil matches" rose from 0.9 to 12.4 per 100 and
  "the studio does nothing about cheaters" from 0.2 to 7.1; design complaints fell in the same time.
  "In Helldivers 2 the late reviewer argued with the publisher; here the late reviewer argues with the
  lobby" (section 16).
- One Crab Champions review asks for an extraction version (section 24).

**Small studio versus big.**
- Small games: price, amount and the studio as the ruler; the listen-then-leave arc; no player culture
  at all. Only two of eight small games with their own column have more praise than complaints (SCP:
  Abhorrent 1.24 : 1, Alien Dawn 1.04 : 1), while most large games do (section 15 and the main
  table).
- Small games give the second sighting big samples miss: "the scenery swallows your shots" was one
  point each in Helldivers 2 and Immortal: Unchained until ArcRunner gave four. "A 77-review game read
  whole tips a claim that 800,000 reviews sampled at 1,626 cannot" (section 15).
- Big games: owners and publishers enter the complaints (Helldivers 2, Risk of Rain 2, Redfall, Remnant
  II).
- Same studio, different game: Deep Rock (97.1%) and Rogue Core (60.1% on Steam) - "every variable
  except the game held constant" (section 9).

**Co-op versus single-player.**
- Immortal: Unchained (single-player): design is 53.6% of what is said, against 30.3% in the other eight;
  community 0.8% against 13.6%. How reviews are written (10.2% against 10.1%) did not change at all.
  "Take the other players away and more than half of everything said becomes how the game plays"
  (section 14).
- Escape from Duckov (single-player extraction) is praised for having no other players (4.4 per 100) and
  asked for co-op (section 21).

**Hosting and servers.**
- Peer to peer (Aliens: Fireteam Elite): late complaints are empty lobbies and disconnects (section 13).
- Hosted by one player (Earth Defense Force 5): small online-rule complaints, 1 to 7 reviews each
  (section 23).
- Always-online check (Redfall): new buyers cannot start the game once the studio is gone (section 10).
- Dedicated servers: nothing in the study (section 13).

**Licensed and sequel games.**
- Aliens: Fireteam Elite: "faithful to the films" 193; "only worth it if you already love the films"
  76, written by people recommending it. Fans ask which part of a series it was faithful to: "A series is
  not one source" (section 13).
- Space Marine 2: story is 9.2% of everything said, the highest (Redfall 8.2%, other co-op games under
  3%, ARC Raiders 0.5%). Faithful to its world 62, a world worth exploring 54 (many say it got them into
  the setting), politics drew me in 16, an iconic thing from the world is missing 10 (section 17).
- Remnant II: its top complaint, unique to it, is "the best things are hidden behind a guide" (61, 46
  of them recommending), and the same reviewers praise the secrets. Around it: invisible walls (3),
  jumping sections that do not belong in a shooter (3), lifts that pad the run (4), and one area that
  drags the rest down (7) (section 18).

**Looks and the world.**
- Earth Defense Force 5 leads the study on "looks dated" (2.5 per 100; Redfall 1.9) and on "destruction
  changes how you play" (2.5; Deep Rock 1.4) (section 23).
- The Anacrusis: a level that says nothing about what happened there is a complaint the page built a
  label for (section 11).

**Language.**
- Gunfire Reborn: "the English reads badly" is 36 reviews, 1.9 of every 100, ten times the next game
  (Escape from Duckov 0.2, Terminull 0.1, Back 4 Blood 0.1). It grows with each update (7 reviews in Early
  Access, 20 in 2025-26); late reviews say the new season text is the worst (section 22).
- Gunfire Reborn also left one platform behind: the console version kept without the seasons and DLC
  draws 6 reviews, in no other game (section 22).

**Long-running live service.**
- Warframe: "warns that it takes over your life" 74 points in 73 reviews (60 thumbs up), "interest comes
  and goes in waves" 44, "keeps playing while hating it" 9 - in no other game. Risk of Rain 2's reviewers
  use the same addiction words as praise; Warframe's write them as a warning, and still recommend it
  (section 20).
- Warframe is the longest-running game in the study, 163 months; Gunfire Reborn shows the slow late slide
  (pattern 7).

### For Dominion - our reading, across games

Dominion: third-person, four-player co-op, sci-fi, PvE extraction and arena shooter, built on runs,
hosted on one player's machine, Unreal Engine 5. Lessons marked "(page)" are "For Dominion" lines
already written in cross-game.md. Every lesson is our reading of what players said, not something the
reviews said.

1. **Make the game best with friends, and make that easy.**
   - Evidence: the top praise in every early game; 3.8 to 10.4 per 100 across seven games (Back 4
     Blood, Deep Rock, Helldivers 2, Aliens, Remnant II, Gunfire Reborn, Earth Defense Force 5).
   - Dominion could: make inviting, joining and staying together the smoothest thing in the game.
   - Strength: strong.

2. **(page) Give the squad a voice and a line players can repeat.**
   - Evidence: Earth Defense Force 5 quotable lines 7.6 of every 100 reviews (0.6 or less elsewhere),
     cheesy on purpose 9.6 per 100; slogans in Deep Rock (28.6), Helldivers 2 (24.5), Space Marine 2
     (21.9); Risk of Rain 2's quoted music the only praise that rises with age.
   - Dominion could: "a squad that talks, and a line or chant players can repeat, cost little and are
     what this game's reviews are written about" (section 23).
   - Strength: strong that it matters. But it cannot be copied in: Rogue Core kept the exact catchphrase
     and got about a quarter of its pull (7.5 against 28.6 per 100). It has to be Dominion's own.

3. **(page) Decide early whether progress and gear carry between solo and online play, and say so on the
   store page.**
   - Evidence: Earth Defense Force 5 (hosted by one player): separate progress 7, gear capped 4, no
     joining mid-mission 4, weapon cap 2, uneven scaling 2, host leaving ends the game 1. Crab Champions
     shows online play can draw almost no complaints (1 disconnect, 2 matchmaking in 1,499).
   - Dominion could: let friends join a run already going; plan what happens when the host leaves;
     make difficulty follow head count evenly; state the rules up front. "A run that survives a dropped
     player is a reachable bar" (section 24).
   - Strength: medium on the rule, weak on each item (counts 1 to 7).

4. **Plan for the day the player count falls.**
   - Evidence: Aliens: Fireteam Elite's 2026 thumbs up fell to 75.3% with bad reviews about an empty game,
     not design; the small co-op games show no community at all; ARC Raiders' matchmaking got worse as
     players left; "works alone" praised in all three early games (1.4, 3.7, 0.9 per 100).
   - Dominion could: make solo and small-group runs worth playing, so the game survives a thin night.
   - Strength: medium.

5. **Never lock the game behind an online check the studio might not be around to answer, and never add
   a new agreement after purchase.**
   - Evidence: Redfall: 13 of 18 "cannot connect" reviews after the studio closed, 7 at zero hours. "Any
     always-online gate on a single-player-capable game is a dead-man switch on the game's own shelf
     life" (section 10). Risk of Rain 2: a user agreement added after purchase drew a review saying the
     buyer could no longer play or refund (reviewers say).
   - Dominion could: keep starting, solo play and listen-server hosting working without any studio server
     in the way; never put a new agreement between a buyer and a game they already own (section 19).
   - Strength: medium-weak (two games).

6. **(page) Make movement feel quick; a third-person shooter is judged on how it moves.**
   - Evidence: movement praise 2.7 (Crab Champions) and 2.6 (Warframe) of every 100 reviews, 0.5 or less
     everywhere else; "sluggish" is the common complaint (Gunfire 15, Rogue Core 11, Immortal 10).
   - Dominion could: "a slide, a dash or a jump that feels quick is praised by name, and a slow one is
     the complaint" (section 24).
   - Strength: medium.

7. **Let players grow powerful, and never take control away from them.**
   - Evidence: "superhumanly strong" Space Marine 2 4.5, Risk of Rain 2 3.8, Crab Champions 2.5 per 100;
     Space Marine 2's thumb split 63 of 64 against 12 of 13; Immortal: Unchained keeps only 25-31% thumbs
     up on stuns and no-counterplay complaints but 72-73% on number problems. Space Marine 2's
     most-helpful review (632 votes) is about one enemy faction made harder by clutter and repeated
     stuns; Risk of Rain 2's hated enemies are hated for how they play (fourteen reviews).
   - Dominion could: tune enemies so stuns and lock-downs are rare and readable; let late runs feel
     huge, but watch that four players' effects do not hide the screen (Risk of Rain 2, 14), and think
     about a warning for flashing effects (one review asks). Patch balance with players' patience;
     stun-locks and slow weapon handling will not be forgiven (section 14).
   - Strength: medium-strong.

8. **(page) Do not stack chance on chance with money as the way out; show the odds and end bad luck.**
   - Evidence: The First Descendant 14.6 of every 100 reviews complain about money, randomness 7.2; it
     improved once it showed odds and added bad-luck protection (complaints 128 to 84 per 100). "The item
     you need may never drop": Duckov 2.0, Remnant II 1.7, Warframe 1.2 per 100. Remnant II's reroll that
     never gives the item (24) and the roll that decides what content you ever see (7).
   - Dominion could: "chance stacked on chance, with money as the exit, is what this audience reads as
     the design's purpose; showing the odds and ending bad luck are what it praises" (section 25). In an
     extraction game, give a sure path to the gear that matters. If Dominion ever sells anything after
     purchase, paying should only save time (Warframe: "paying only shortens the grind" 73, paying to win
     16 in 3,233 reviews) (section 20).
   - Strength: medium.

9. **A grind is accepted when it is fair, no step of it is sold back, and the play itself is fun.**
   - Evidence: Warframe's top complaint (8.2 per 100) comes mostly from recommenders (223 of 264), next to
     "everything can be earned" (133) and "the grind pays off" (111). Deep Rock reviewer and Rico: the
     missions are fun with nothing to earn. The First Descendant is free like Warframe; the gap is "the
     chain players describe" (section 25).
   - Dominion could: make each run worth playing even with no unlock at the end; avoid a slow start
     where the good systems are locked (Gunfire 1.2, Warframe 1.1 per 100). The page warns the money part
     "may not carry to a game sold once".
   - Strength: medium.

10. **Expect "not enough content" to be the fans' main complaint, and fight repetition early.**
    - Evidence: Aliens 187, Space Marine 2 84 (66 from fans), small block 32; "it gets repetitive" 1.8,
      1.4, 1.4 per 100 in three very different games. In Space Marine 2 it fell from 7.6 to 1.9 per 100
      as missions were added and came back at 5.1 in 2026; "it comes back when the additions stop".
    - Dominion could: plan arenas, enemies and run variety as an ongoing supply, not a launch set.
    - Strength: strong.

11. **Performance faults can become the whole review page; Unreal Engine 5 games are watched for it.**
    - Evidence: Terminull stutter 165 points (7.3%), 18 saying their verdict flips the day it is fixed;
      Helldivers 2 technical 21.7% of complaints; Remnant II's four "my machine cannot run it" types; The
      First Descendant (Unreal Engine 5) performance 8.8 to 4.9 of every 100 reviews and 15
      cannot-connect reviews, all at launch.
    - Dominion could: treat smooth frame times as a launch requirement, not a polish item; test on
      low-end machines, make settings actually help, avoid needing upscaling to play; treat launch-day
      joining and frame rate as the first test (section 25).
    - Strength: strong.

12. **Patch fast and say so; a late rescue does not bring players back; praise for fixes is not praise
    for the design.**
    - Evidence: patch praise in Rogue Core (3.8, 3.7) despite low scores; Helldivers 2 "listens and acts"
      4.7; Redfall's fix came five months late, 34.7% to 60.7%, with almost no players left.
    - Dominion could: plan a quick first-month fix cycle before launch and show players what was fixed;
      do not read thanks for quick fixes as proof the design is right (section 9).
    - Strength: medium.

13. **Read the words, not just the thumb; weigh complaints by hours played.**
    - Evidence: Rogue Core's words moved a month before its thumb; Crab Champions' complaints doubled
      while thumbs stayed at 97.8%; Aliens' 1,221 thumbs-up reviews carry 1,187 complaints; Terminull's
      loop change was seen only by players with 4 to 402 hours. The page: "when you change the loop,
      complaint volume will not tell you. Weigh it by playtime or you will not see it at all" (section 12).
    - Dominion could: track playtest and review text by hours played, especially after big updates; take
      the to-do list from the text of positive reviews too, not only negative ones (section 13).
    - Strength: medium.

14. **A small studio's early listening is praised most and trusted least; keep it going.**
    - Evidence: 23 "they listen" all at launch, 16 "updates stopped" all a year or more later, five of
      nine small games; Alien Dawn the exception.
    - Dominion could: plan a support schedule it can keep for years, and say what it is.
    - Strength: medium.

15. **Price fairly for the amount of game; "wait for a sale" is a warning sign.**
    - Evidence: "fair price" in every early game; "buy on sale" Redfall 17.0, Aliens 9.1, Back 4 Blood
      8.6 per 100; one small-game review in six is about price or amount. "A small studio's roughness is
      forgiven; too little game for the price is not" (section 15).
    - Strength: medium-strong.

16. **Expect to be compared; do not invite the comparison yourself - but a comparison can be won.**
    - Evidence: Back 4 Blood's successor framing (25.1 per 100) and its "set a bar it could never
      clear" (8.4); The First Descendant against Warframe (282 reviews); Duckov wins against Tarkov (6.1
      per 100, only 6 of 135 thumbs down); Earth Defense Force 5 got compared to Helldivers 2 after 2024.
    - Dominion could: describe itself in its own words; know which rival it will be measured against
      (other sci-fi co-op and extraction shooters) and where it wins. Being measured against a famous
      rival helps when the game is clearly a friendlier version of it (section 21). Expect the yardstick
      to change after launch when a new rival arrives (section 23).
    - Strength: strong.

17. **PvE extraction is what both extraction audiences asked for.**
    - Evidence: 42 of 55 mode requests in ARC Raiders ask for PvE; Duckov's requests ask for co-op;
      "no PvP is a feature" 4.4 per 100 in Duckov. In ARC Raiders, other players' behaviour (180) is the
      biggest block of its top 25 complaints.
    - Dominion could: say clearly on the store page that it is co-op PvE with no other players hunting
      you.
    - Strength: weak-medium (two games, and the page says it has read few extraction games).

18. **(page) Check upgrade text in English at every update.**
    - Evidence: Gunfire Reborn "reads badly" 1.9 of every 100, ten times the next game, and growing with
      each update; item descriptions "at least 3 lines long".
    - Dominion could: "any system that asks the player to read upgrade text mid-run needs its English
      checked at every update, not once" (section 22).
    - Strength: weak (mostly one game; others 0.1 to 0.2).

19. **Let players make their own stories and fun.**
    - Evidence: friendly fire praised 37 times in Helldivers 2 against 3 costs; six in Remnant II say it
      is the fun; silly actions praised 111 times in Deep Rock.
    - Dominion could: consider friendly fire and emotes as story-makers, not just risks.
    - Strength: weak-medium.

20. **Let players say what they want from a run before it starts (Rico's note).**
    - Evidence: Rico's note on a 1,790-hour reviewer who hand-picks teammates by goal; the cheap fix is
      outside the game (named Discord channels: for fun, for rewards, for clears), the costly one a lobby
      flag (Rico notes, 2026-08-31).
    - Strength: weak as evidence (one review plus Rico's call); cheap to try.

21. **Owners, user agreements and fan-service choices can sink a good game; decide them on purpose.**
    - Evidence: owner complaints in Helldivers 2 (2.8), Risk of Rain 2 (1.7, cost two points); fan service
      as The First Descendant's loudest subject (9.3 of every 100); the small fan-service fights, where
      twelve censorship reviews hold 52% of a game's helpful votes.
    - Dominion could: make these choices early and on purpose; they draw attention far beyond their count.
    - Strength: medium.

22. **Do not read a dip in the thumb as a verdict until the words say so - and do not buy empty
    installs.**
    - Evidence: Duckov's mod dispute (96.7% to 74.4%); Terminull's reward campaign (2.3 points; the second
      wave 9.1% up with 32 of 33 at zero hours). "The monthly thumb needs the round notes beside it"
      (sections 21, 22).
    - Dominion could: read the reviews behind any sudden dip before reacting; not run a promotion that
      installs the game for people with no reason to play it, and not blame a low score on one (section
      12).
    - Strength: medium.

23. **(page) If Dominion has a run clock, expect it to be the most-argued feature.**
    - Evidence: Rogue Core's clock that decides when you leave is 13.8 per 100 and in no other game;
      teammates taking each other's things 5.8. Five reviews across three games call the timer the
      thrill.
    - Dominion could: test the clock alone and with four players, and default to each player getting
      their own pickups (section 9). Earth Defense Force 5's loot picked up by hand (10 call it a chore,
      2 lose drops at mission end) points the same way.
    - Strength: medium (one game sets the count; the timer-as-thrill points come from three).

24. **The launch decides the review page.**
    - Evidence: Space Marine 2's thumb was 88.5% in the launch month and 87.8% two years later, with 46%
      of all reviewers writing in the launch month. The First Descendant: 62% of English reviews came in
      its launch month. Redfall: later reviewers spent their review arguing the game was judged unfairly
      (124 to 9).
    - Dominion could: put polish before launch, because fixes after it earn "a different complaint", not a
      higher thumb (section 17); a later defence by fans does not replace the launch verdict (section 10).
    - Strength: medium-strong.

25. **If Dominion goes into Early Access, hope lasts about two years.**
    - Evidence: The Anacrusis's "the potential is still there" ran 35, 33, 4, 2 by year; 68 of 74 hopeful
      points are from the first two years, while "the game is dead" kept coming.
    - Dominion could: deliver the promised content inside that window (section 11).
    - Strength: weak-medium (one game).

26. **Let the characters speak in the first hour.**
    - Evidence: The Anacrusis's 32 "no personality" points come from reviewers with a median of one hour;
      the nine who like the cast played a median of six; one reviewer names voice lines set to play
      rarely.
    - Dominion could: if the squad talks, make early runs play the lines that show who each character is
      (section 11).
    - Strength: weak (one game).

27. **Alone, the play is the whole review.**
    - Evidence: with other players removed, how the game plays rose to 53.6% of everything said, against
      30.3% in the other eight games (Immortal: Unchained).
    - Dominion could: make solo runs stand on the shooting and the enemies alone, with no friends to carry
      them (section 14).
    - Strength: medium-weak (one single-player game, a census).

28. **Third-person shooting and space checks.**
    - Evidence: Immortal: Unchained drew complaints about a lock-on that holds the body, not the weak
      point, and spaces built too small; the scenery swallowing your shots was seen in Helldivers 2,
      Immortal: Unchained and ArcRunner (four there); Remnant II's invisible walls (3), jumping sections
      (3) and lifts that pad the run (4).
    - Dominion could: if it has aim help, make it help with weak points; size arenas for a third-person
      camera; make sure cover and props do not eat shots that look like hits (sections 14, 15, 18).
    - Strength: weak (small counts, several games).

29. **Make secrets findable in the game, not only on a wiki.**
    - Evidence: Remnant II's top complaint, "the best things are hidden behind a guide" (61, 46 of them
      recommending), next to praise for the same secrets.
    - Dominion could: in a run game built from random parts, let players find secrets through play
      (section 18).
    - Strength: weak-medium (one game, but its largest complaint).

30. **Protect what players earned when fixing an exploit.**
    - Evidence: ARC Raiders' last period adds a duplication exploit and the wipe that followed it, which
      reviewers call taking away what they had earned (reviewers' claims).
    - Dominion could: plan how to fix an exploit without wiping honest players' progress (section 16).
    - Strength: weak (one game).

31. **Let players put it down and come back.**
    - Evidence: Warframe's "takes over your life" warnings (74, 60 thumbs up) and interest that comes in
      waves (44); Gunfire Reborn's seasons that stay playable (6) and its console version left behind (6).
    - Dominion could: give runs natural stopping points; let players return without falling behind; if it
      runs seasons, keep old ones playable and every platform in step (sections 20, 22).
    - Strength: weak-medium.

32. **Keep the late game healthy - veterans write the reviews people read.**
    - Evidence: The First Descendant's median hours rose from 20 at launch to 50-70 later; its three
      most-helpful reviews (592, 554, 545 helpful) are thumbs down from players with 117 to 3,012 hours.
    - Dominion could: watch what long-time players say, because they set what new buyers read (section 25).
    - Strength: weak-medium (one game, clear numbers).

33. **Read reviews over the years, not once at launch.**
    - Evidence: Gunfire Reborn's complaints rose 20.9, 28.5, 36.1, 46.3 per 100, in late groups a
      first-year read misses; Remnant II's complaints rose 67 to 91 because the reviewers changed, not the
      game; Warframe's reviews shrank from 67 to 28 words, so falling praise numbers are not always bad
      news.
    - Dominion could: check again in later years; compare like with like (who is playing, how long) before
      reacting to a trend; count a falling praise line only when it falls much faster than reviews shrink
      (sections 18, 20, 22).
    - Strength: medium.

34. **Ask playtesters direct questions.**
    - Evidence: a well-liked game gives few reasons: Risk of Rain 2 1.43 points per review on the recount, with 47% of
      its points saying nothing specific; Crab Champions 61.0 bare thumbs up per 100 and no complaint reaching 10.
    - Dominion could: ask testers what they liked and why, rather than wait for them to explain
      (section 19).
    - Strength: medium.

35. **Let players set the difficulty in detail, and consider playing in one room.**
    - Evidence: Crab Champions' detailed difficulty settings 1.1 per 100, the highest, with "hard in a
      good way" rising 0.3 to 2.2; Earth Defense Force 5's split screen praised in 18 reviews (1.0 per
      100) and 5 more using Remote Play (sections 23, 24).
    - Dominion could: offer fine difficulty settings; weigh whether local co-op is worth its cost.
    - Strength: weak (one game each).

### What the study cannot tell us yet

- **Only English.** Non-English reviews are not read. The page gives two old totals that do not match
  (329,785 at the top; 320,920 in section 7) and says both are out of date. Later games add 2,855
  (Terminull Brigade), 9,505 (Aliens: Fireteam Elite), 455 (Immortal: Unchained), 1,849 (the small
  block), about 164,000 (ARC Raiders), 100,000 (Space Marine 2), 30,000 (Remnant II), 113,000 (Risk of
  Rain 2), 381,000 (Warframe), 93,000 (Duckov), 60,000 (Gunfire Reborn), 4,300 (Earth Defense Force 5),
  4,000 (Crab Champions) and 61,000 (The First Descendant), none pulled. Language can change the verdict:
  Zombie Girl's English 9% is 22 points more positive than its other 91% (section 15).
- **Few extraction games, and no successful co-op PvE extraction game.** Only two were read: ARC Raiders
  (other players can shoot you) and Escape from Duckov (single-player, co-op only by mods). For co-op in
  Duckov, "the request is recorded, the experience is not" (section 21).
- **Hosting and servers.** Aliens is peer-to-peer and "nothing in this corpus has a dedicated-server
  analogue" (section 13). Listen-server counts come mostly from Earth Defense Force 5, with 1 to 7
  reviews each. Aliens cannot "separate 'the matchmaking is bad' from 'not enough people bought it'".
- **Runs and third-person feel.** Gunfire Reborn is first person; Warframe, Earth Defense Force 5 and The
  First Descendant have no runs; Crab Champions is a cartoon, not serious sci-fi.
- **Cause.** The study counts what was said, not why. It cannot say if a missing player culture caused
  a failure or came from it (section 7). A complaint's count is not its cause: The Anacrusis's "no
  personality" came from players with a median of one hour (section 11).
- **Claims not checked.** No patch notes were pulled for ARC Raiders, Space Marine 2 or others; the mod
  dispute and studio actions are what reviewers say. Whether Space Marine 2's hard enemy faction was
  fixed is unknown (one of 169 reviews from 2026-02 on mentions it).
- **Free versus paid.** Nothing separates the money model from the game (section 12); The First
  Descendant's money finding "may not carry to a game sold once" (section 25).
- **Thin months.** Aliens' launch month held 25.4% of its reviews but was read at 1.62%, and gives 66.6%
  of that game's uncertainty. Remnant II's launch week was read at 0.9% against 3.5% elsewhere. Immortal:
  Unchained has 31 reviews for 2024-2026: "a census removes sampling error; it does not manufacture
  reviews nobody wrote". Crab Champions' update period is 230 reviews (about plus or minus 7). The First
  Descendant: 62% of reviews in its launch month, so its improvement shows only in the period table.
- **Edited reviews.** Reviews edited later are filed on one date; this moves numbers in some games (Risk
  of Rain 2, ARC Raiders). In ARC Raiders it understates the cheating trend; in Space Marine 2 the
  direction is unknown. It waits on Rico.
- **Counting faults.** Two silent counting faults were found and fixed (sections 6, 13), and a third in
  round 470 (section 20). The page says each was found by accident. Friendly-fire counts in section 5 are
  still a floor. The old top table of cross-game.md is on an earlier count; the recounted tables are the
  ones to compare.
- **Wrong-game reviews.** One review described a different game; how often this happens is unknown in
  every game (section 12).
- **One-game claims.** Roguelite early-floor chores, single-use checkpoints, conduct-based matchmaking,
  translation, the run clock, hidden content and the wipe after an exploit all rest on one game. A label
  can be one game's habit: "a named rival does it better" is almost all The First Descendant (section 25).
- **What the sorting cannot tell apart.** In Immortal: Unchained 49 topics were empty, some because there
  are no other players and some because there is no live service; the sorting reads both as zero
  (section 14). Warframe was read in two sessions, before and after a stop (section 20).
- **The tag tree has not been reviewed by Rico** (section 7), and several structure questions are open
  (loot, friendly fire, publisher talk, "the first game had X", a sequel dropping a feature).
- **Whether a licence pays.** Aliens bought 193 points of goodwill and 76 "only for fans" warnings;
  nothing nets them out (section 13).

---

# The games

## ELDEN RING NIGHTREIGN

**Read:** 1,299 of 97,217 English reviews (1.3%; 1,307 were read and 8 were left out - 5 empty, plus a
made-up story, song lyrics and a crude remark), ±3.18%, written May 2025 to October 2026. The first
month was read very thinly: the game came out on 29 May, so only 73 of its 19,254 reviews were
sampled (0.38%). **87.8% thumbs up** in the sample (Steam, all languages: 82%, Very Positive).
**Pages:** `findings/elden-ring-nightreign-english.md`, `findings/elden-ring-nightreign.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` section 27.

**What it is:** A third-person fantasy action game set in the Elden Ring world, built on runs. Up to
three players drop onto one map, race a ring of deadly rain that closes in over two in-game days,
level up and gather gear, then fight a big "night boss" on day three. You can play alone, in a team of
three, and - from 31 July 2025 - in a team of two. Combat is the studio's sword-and-dodge style, with
bows and spells; each of the characters has its own skills. Made by FromSoftware, published with Bandai
Namco. Sold once, $39.99 on Steam, released 29 May 2025. One paid add-on, The Forsaken Hollows (3 December
2025, $15.00: two new characters, two new bosses, one new map change). The pages do not name the engine
or check how games are hosted (two reviewers say players connect to each other directly). (Store facts
and dates checked against Steam's store page and the studio's Steam posts.)

**How close to Dominion:** Close in shape, not in weapons. It shares the third-person camera, a small
team playing together online against the computer, runs with a clock, a boss at the end, and characters
with their own skills. It differs in melee and magic instead of guns, a fantasy world instead of
sci-fi, three players instead of four, and no extraction - a run ends at the boss, not at a way out.

### The short version (plain words)
Players judge this game as a co-op game first. "Much better with friends" was said 125 times, and
players stay a long time: half the reviews show 43 hours or more, and 275 show over 100. The fighting
is what they love - hard in a good way (61), one more run (60), great bosses (47), characters that each
play their own way (38). What they complain about is mostly the link between players: there is no
voice or text chat (36 times, the second-highest of the 21 large games we have read), two friends could
not play alone together until a patch on 31 July 2025 (26 times, then never again), dropped connections
throw away a whole run, and friends on other consoles cannot join (15, the highest of any game). Many
also feel it reuses too much of Elden Ring for its price (28, the highest of any game).

The reviews are very short (half are 8 words or fewer), so 544 thumbs up say nothing specific. Players
who leave thumbs down still played a lot: their median is 25 hours.

What the points are about: game design 34.2%, a bare thumbs up or down 25.2%, who you play with and
