talking to them 12.0%, content 7.5%, comparisons 7.3%, tech and servers 6.2%, price 2.7%, updates 2.5%,
story, art and sound together 2.5%.

### What players praised, most to least
Counts here are times it was said. 544 more thumbs-up reviews said nothing specific (41.9 per 100).
1. **Much better with friends.** Said 125 times (9.6 per 100). *"Best played with a couple of friends on
   a voice call, but I've had lots of fun playing with randoms online as well!"* (201176143).
2. **Hard in a good way.** Said 61 times (4.7 per 100, third-highest of 21 large games). Losing feels
   earned, and beating a boss after days of trying is the high point.
3. **One more run.** Said 60 times. It rose to 8.5 per 100 in the last months read.
4. **The bosses.** Said 47 times (3.6 per 100) - more than in any other game we have read (the next is
   Roboquest at 1.5). The final night boss and the harder weekly versions are named often.
5. **Each character plays its own way.** Said 38 times. *"Each class feels distinct, not just in stats
   or equipment, but in how they approach fights"* (197828923).
6. **A fresh mix of the studio's combat with runs that start over.** Said 33 times. *"a faster paced
   version of Elden Ring, you can play through game expeditions several times in an afternoon"*
   (203284135, 20 people found it helpful).
7. **More, please - as praise.** Said 31 times, rising to 4.9 per 100 in the last months read.
8. **Fine with strangers.** Said 28 times (but see the complaints: 21 say the opposite).
9. **A fair price.** Said 21 times.
10. **Weighty hits** (19), **the potential is there** (17), **a run fits a short sitting** (16 - a run is
    about 30 to 45 minutes), **it works alone** (16), **made for the studio's fans and better for it**
    (16 - bosses from the older Dark Souls games are a treat).
11. **The characters' stories** (15), told through each character's own quest with cutscenes.
12. **Better than its reputation** (14) and **patches made it better** (14).
13. **Won over people who dislike this kind of game** (12), many ways to build (11), the sprint and
    climbing (11 - *"being able to run fast and climb is a game changer honestly"*, 199242894), the
    music (10), rewarding once learned (10), best of its kind (10), up to the studio's standard (10).
14. **Smaller things:** abilities that make you briefly safe (*"Most class abilities have generous
    i-frames"*, 197828923), the closing ring as a thrill (5), route planning that makes you think (7),
    no chat keeping it civil (2: *"if this game had game chat it would be the most toxic game ever
    made"*, 236581664), it prepares newcomers for the studio's harder games (2).

### What players complained about, most to least
Counts are times it was said. 451 of the 831 complaints (54%) come from thumbs-up reviews. The
thumbs-down reviews name these most: reused Elden Ring content (14 reviews), luck deciding the run (12),
falling short of the studio's earlier games (12), and connection drops (11).
1. **No way to talk to the team.** Said 36 times (2.8 per 100), mostly from people who stayed (8 in
   thumbs-down reviews). Only map markers and pings exist. *"Communication is another huge miss. Outside
   of simple map pings, there's no text chat, no voice chat, nothing"* (212811996, 46 found it helpful).
2. **Reused Elden Ring content.** Said 28 times (2.2 per 100, the highest of any game we have read); 14
   of the thumbs-down reviews say it. One reviewer guessed "about 5%" new content (not checked).
3. **No two-player mode at launch.** Said 26 times, all in May and June 2025: *"Add duo queue"*
   (197499099). The studio added it on 31 July 2025, and the complaint stopped: *"We got the duoQ now"*
   (198583917).
4. **One hated boss.** Said 26 times. The Bell Bearing Hunter, a boss who roams the map, is named most;
   the same bosses that draw praise draw this.
5. **The connection drops.** Said 22 times (11 thumbs-down reviews). A drop can end a 30-45 minute run
   (8): *"You've had the best run of your life? Best gear?? NETWORK ERROR... STARTING OFFLINE MODE"*
   (198588917).
6. **Strangers spoil it.** Said 21 times; careless teammates 16, quitters 8, griefers 4, cheaters 3.
7. **Not random enough** (20 - one map, the same places), **luck decides the run** (19), **the weapon or
   relic you need never comes** (15).
8. **Too hard alone** (19, against 16 who say solo works). The studio made solo easier on 2 June 2025 (a
   free revive per night boss and more runes); one player later called solo too easy.
9. **Too little content** (19), **it assumes you played Elden Ring** (16 - *"Peak, but def play Elden
   Ring first"*, 200469440), **no crossplay** (15, the highest of any game).
10. **Too rushed** (14), **runs feel the same** (14), **below the studio's earlier games** (14, 12 of them
    thumbs down).
11. **Level-up choices are dull stat bumps** (12), **lose to a boss and replay the whole run** (12 -
    *"Why is it not possible to try to kill the boss after death in order to get used to it? The reward
    and kill will not be counted, but you will not have to play the entire run"*, 208671319), little to
    build (11), too expensive for what it is (11), only worth it with a group (11).
12. **Smaller things:** unfinished (9); no time to explore (8); nothing left to do after the end (8);
    the lock-on picks the wrong target (4 - *"I'll lock onto something ten miles away instead of my
    downed friend begging to be revived"*, 198146831); no ultrawide screen support (4); a 60
    frames-per-second cap (3).

### How it changed over time
Four periods: the launch summer (May to July 2025, 851 reviews), the duo mode and the harder mode
(August to November 2025, 189), the add-on (December 2025 to January 2026, 95), and no new updates
(February to October 2026, 164).
- **Complaints fell by a third after the launch summer**, from 68.6 to 44.4 per 100 reviews (not counting
  bare thumbs), because the duo request (3.1 to 0.0), no chat (3.6 to 0.5) and drops (2.1 to 1.6 to 0.0)
  faded.
- **The add-on months were the worst**: the lowest thumbs up (83.2%) and the most bare thumbs down.
- **In the last months**, luck deciding the run (0.8 to 3.0 per 100), bad strangers (1.4 to 3.0) and
  "more, please" (2.1 to 4.9) rose. Six of the seven "updates have stopped" complaints come from July to
  October 2026. The studio's last patch was on 16 January 2026 (studio post); *"No update since
  January"* (236921008) matches it.
- What players tie each turn to: the solo patch (2 June 2025), weekly harder bosses (from 26 June 2025),
  the duo mode (31 July 2025), the harder "Deep of Night" mode with ranked points (11 September 2025), the
  add-on (3 December 2025). All dates are the studio's.

### Co-op and online play
- **Friends make it.** Much better with friends 125; only worth it with a group 11.
- **Strangers split players.** Fine 28, bad 21, careless 16. Players work around it: a public
  matchmaking password from a video maker that finds skilled strangers (1), the game's Discord (1).
- **No chat.** 36 complaints; 2 like the silence.
- **Group sizes.** Solo, three, and from 31 July 2025 two. Two-player requests: 26, then none. Only 2
  asked for a bigger group.
- **Scaling after a leave.** When teammates leave or get lost on the add-on map, the rest face a boss
  scaled for three (2): *"an unfamiliar player gets lost and takes more than 10 minutes to rejoin the
  team, leaving the two teammates alone against bosses scaled for 3 players"* (222216652). A
  well-practised trio can make bosses trivial (2).
- **Drops and leaving.** Frequent drops 22; a drop loses the run 8; rejoin a level down 2; cannot
  rejoin 3; punished for leaving 5, including when trapped: *"When they leave you your not aloud to
  leave the match without a penalty and possible account bans. No matter how many times you die solo
  you cant leave the match"* (214041589); a crash is punished like quitting 2. One reviewer says there
  was no penalty at all (reviewers disagree; not checked).
- **Crossplay.** None; 15 ask for it, and one wants saves to carry over between platforms (225610557).
- **Character quests.** Only one player's quest can move forward in a group, so they are played alone
  in practice (5).
- **The ranked points mode.** Losing points to a stranger's leave or a bad connection (3): *"'The
  penalty has been waived because a player left early' Here is your -300 points"* (208660420).

### Combat, movement and feel
- Weighty hits 19. Abilities that make you briefly safe 1.
- **Movement is loved**: the sprint, climbing and no fall damage (11 praise). Against: janky climbing
  and hawk rides that force a path (2), no horse to ride (2).
- **Lock-on picks the wrong target** (4).
- Smaller: stamina runs out too fast (1); hit while getting up (1); a sword cannot block without a
  shield (1); one character's heavy-weapon moves feel bad (1 - the studio sped up those attacks on 16
  January 2026, studio post).
- **A meter that lies**: *"the ultimate gauge will look full at any point above 85% charge … only to
  stand around and die"* (214770612).
- Not being able to tell whether you are inside the deadly rain (2).

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- **Bosses are the high point** (47) and the most hated thing (one named boss 26; one too strong 3).
- Boss complaints with detail: long combos that leave no opening (2), a boss that chases a player who
  is not fighting it (2), bosses that leap and sweep all round so close fighters suffer (1), one-hit
  kills (4), too much health (6).
- **Difficulty**: hard in a good way 61; too hard 4; too easy 6 (*"I just wish it was a little more
  challenging"*, 213372815); one part far harder 4.
- **Solo**: too hard 19, works 16, only some characters can carry a solo run (3).
- **The harder mode** (Deep of Night): praised as the hardest challenge (2), called just bigger numbers
  (3) and unfair in its points (3).

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- Each character its own way 38; many ways to build 11.
- **Relics** (the lasting upgrades): a slot machine of random effects with no reroll (3), drawbacks that
  make many useless (4), a crowded inventory with a cap (5). *"Please, I beg you, Fromsoft, throw the
  relic system in the trash"* (222216652).
- **Luck**: luck decides the run 19; the needed item never comes 15; not random enough 20.
- **Level-ups**: fixed stats you cannot choose (11); choices that only change numbers (12).
- **What carries over**: lasting upgrades too small to feel (4); nothing builds up (4); a skill tree
  for each character wished for (1).
- The best rewards are fixed drops, so late builds look alike (1). Beating the final boss turns off the
  map changes, so winning makes the game duller (1). The best outfits come only after the end (2).

### Runs, content and replay value
- A run is about 30 to 45 minutes; good in short sittings 16; one more run 60.
- **The closing ring** is the most argued feature: a thrill (5) and a reason to think about routes (7),
  but too rushed (14), no time to explore or read (8), and it does not wait while you fight an event the
  map sent you (1).
- **Repetition**: one main map (20 not random enough), runs feel the same 14, too little content 19,
  runs out fast 8, nothing left to chase after the end 8.
- **A lost boss means a whole new run** (12).

### Money and price
- Fair 21; too high for what it is 11; buy on sale 7; not worth it even free 1; a cash grab 5; should
  have been an add-on for Elden Ring 3.
- **The add-on** ($15.00, checked on the store page): fair 4, not worth it 2. *"the game needs so much
  more for it to be good yet instead of adding to it they're just going to sell you more through the
  DLC"* (209882127, 43 found it helpful).

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Connection drops 22 (see co-op). Crashes 7; freezes 3; stutter 5; poorly optimised 6; saves lost 4
  (one heard on the forums, not checked).
- Every patch resets graphics settings (1): *"every patch resets my graphics settings"* (197493809).
- Anti-cheat that blocks fixes or mods (3); no ultrawide support (4); a 60 frames-per-second cap (3).
  Runs well on the Steam Deck (2).

### The studio, updates and community
- **Through 2025 the studio added a lot**, and players noticed: patches made it better 14; a steady
  stream 8. Solo help came within days (2 June 2025).
- **After January 2026 it went quiet.** Updates stopped 7, six of them from July 2026 on: *"they decide
  to only drop one 1 dlc for the game then let it become abandon ware within a year"* (230556884).
- Players trust the studio (5) or lost trust (4); the director is named in two reviews.
- **The community**: memes and jokes are common, many reviews joke about one roaming boss, and some
  reviews teach how to play (2).

### What players asked for
A way to talk 36; a two-player mode 26 (added); more content, as praise 31; crossplay 15; more
randomness 20; a boss retry without a full run 2; a minimap 3; harder bosses 6; a horse 2; a skill tree
1; modes they expected 5 (custom matches, lobby fights, raids); the add-on map as a choice 2; a relic
reroll or search 3 (a search came in the 31 July 2025 patch); a pass or weekly events with new bosses 1
(*"this looks like a perfect game to add a pass or some other system with events and other bosses"*,
236928456); keep exploring co-op Soulslike games 2 (*"Coop soulslike is such a … good formula, please
continue exploring this"*, 218330062).

### Only in this game
- **No two-player mode at launch** - 26 times, and it vanished when fixed.
- **It assumes you played the studio's earlier game** - 16.
- **The ranked mode punishes you for strangers' leaves** - 3.
- **A crash punished like quitting** - 2. **Enemies stay scaled for players who left** - 2.
- **A meter that looks full at 85%**, **a teammate's bad connection loses the boss's reward**, **the
  ring that does not wait for events**, **beating the game turns off variety**, **no horse** - 1 or 2
  each.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Ship voice chat and quick messages, or rich pings, on day one.**
   Evidence: no way to talk 36, the top complaint, second-highest of 21 games; bad strangers 21.
   Dominion could: push-to-talk voice with mute and report, quick text lines, and pings for enemies,
   items and plans.
   Strength: strong.
2. **Support one, two, three and four players from launch.**
   Evidence: 26 duo requests in two months, then none after the patch.
   Dominion could: tune and test every group size before release.
   Strength: strong.
3. **A drop must not cost the run, and a crash is not quitting.**
   Evidence: drops 22, run lost 8, crash punished like quitting 2, trapped by the leave penalty 5; runs
   of 30-45 minutes.
   Dominion could: keep a dropped player's place and gear for a while; never punish a crash or a host
   drop; let a player leave freely once the others have gone. On Dominion's player-hosted games the host
   leaving can end everyone's run, so this matters more.
   Strength: strong.
4. **Rescale the fight when someone leaves.**
   Evidence: two players left facing a boss scaled for three (2); griefed with no way out (197497249).
   Strength: medium.
5. **Let a lost boss be practised without replaying the whole run.**
   Evidence: harsh restart 12; 208671319 (9 found it helpful).
   Strength: medium.
6. **Bosses sell the game; give every attack an opening and keep the rules readable.**
   Evidence: bosses 47 (highest of any game) and hated bosses 26 (also highest); no opening 2; one-hit
   kills 4; too much health 6.
   Strength: strong.
7. **Randomness should change what you do, not only what you get.**
   Evidence: not random enough 20; luck decides 19; the needed item never comes 15; dull stat-bump
   choices 12; relics with no reroll 3.
   Dominion could: vary arenas, objectives and enemy mixes; add a reroll or a "bad luck" safety net;
   make upgrades change how a weapon plays.
   Strength: strong.
8. **A run clock will be loved and hated; let it pause for the content it sends.**
   Evidence: thrill 5, routing 7, against too rushed 14, no time to explore 8, does not wait for events 1.
   Strength: medium.
9. **Plan support after launch and say it out loud.**
   Evidence: patches praised 14 through 2025; after the last patch, updates stopped 7 and "more, please"
   rose to 4.9 per 100.
   Strength: medium.
10. **Teach the game inside the game.**
    Evidence: assumes the earlier game 16; hard at first 24; poorly explained 5; needs a guide 5.
    Strength: medium.
11. **Do not lie with the screen.**
    Evidence: a meter full at 85% (214770612); cannot tell whether you are inside the danger zone (2).
    Dominion could: make charge meters and danger edges exact and clear.
    Strength: weak (few reviews, but cheap to get right).
12. **Plan crossplay and cross-save even if Dominion starts on PC.**
    Evidence: no crossplay 15, the highest of any game.
    Strength: weak.

### Limits
- 1.3% of the English reviews were read; ±3.18% on whole-sample shares, about ±3.4 to ±10.1 per period.
- The launch month was read at 0.38% (weighted ×264); Rico ruled on 4 September 2026 that short launch
  months are read as pulled.
- Two thirds of all reviews come from the first two months, before the duo mode and the harder mode.
- Half the reviews are 8 words or fewer; most counts are under 30.
- 139 reviews (10.7%) were edited later and are counted on the day they were first written.
- Checked: the store page and the studio's Steam posts (dates, the solo patch, the duo patch, the
  harder mode, the add-on and its price, the last patch). Not checked: the leave-penalty rules, how games
  are hosted, "about 5% new content", solo boss health, save loss heard on the forums, cheaters on VPNs.
- Jabs and slurs are recorded only as jabs; family, health and home-town details are left out.
- English only; about 94,700 reviews in other languages were not read.

---

## Roboquest

**Read:** 1,786 of 17,274 English reviews (10.4%; 1,796 were read and 10 were empty), ±2.50%, written
August 2020 to October 2026. **96.4% thumbs up** in the sample (Steam, all languages: 95%,
Overwhelmingly Positive). **Pages:** `findings/roboquest-english.md`, `findings/roboquest.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` section 26.

**What it is:** A fast first-person shooter against robots, built on runs: each run goes through a set
of levels to a final boss, and you start again when you die. You play alone or with one friend online
(two players at most, from the reviews and the studio's posts; the store page does not give a number).
Cross-platform play is on. The pages do not say who hosts a game. Sci-fi, with a bright comic-book look.
Sold once, $24.99 on Steam, with no shop. Windows only on Steam. Made and published by RyseUp Studios.
In Early Access from 2020-08-20, full release 2023-11-07; the studio announced the end of updates on
2025-05-07. The pages do not name the engine. (Store facts and dates checked against Steam's store page
and the studio's Steam posts.)

**How close to Dominion:** Close in play. It shares the shooting, the sci-fi setting, play with friends
against the computer online, and runs that build power and then reset. It differs in the first-person
camera, two players instead of four, and no extraction - a run ends at a boss, not at a way out.

### The short version (plain words)
Players love how this game feels: 96% recommend it, and its players praise the music, the shooting and
the movement more than the players of any of the 20 large games we have read (music 11.3 reviews per
100, shooting 9.4, movement 9.2; the next highest are 3.8, 5.8 and 2.7). The one complaint that lasts
from 2020 to 2026 is that only two people can play together: 36 times it was said, and 34 of those 36
reviews still recommend the game. "There is too little to do" was the biggest complaint while the game
was unfinished (5.5 per 100 reviews in Early Access) and almost vanished after (0.3). Players explain the
game by naming others - Borderlands in 126 reviews, DOOM in 113, Gunfire Reborn in 93 - and say it wins:
75 times it was called the best of its kind, and 67 times it beat a game the reviewer named.

The reviews are short (half are 15 words or fewer) and kind: 615 thumbs up say nothing specific, and 73%
of all complaints come from players who still recommend it. The median review shows 12.9 hours played;
the 65 thumbs-down reviews show a median of only 5.5.

What the points are about: game design 41.3% (mostly how it feels and the builds), a bare thumbs up or
down 15.7%, comparisons and reputation 12.2%, content and Early Access 7.1%, sound 5.3% (almost all the
music), playing with friends and the community 4.3%, updates 3.9%, art 3.8%, tech 3.0%, price 2.4%,
story 0.9%.

### What players praised, most to least
Counts here are times it was said. 615 more thumbs-up reviews said nothing specific (34.4 per 100).
1. **The music.** Said 202 times (11.3 per 100). One player downloaded tracks to their phone (208087719); another says buy it *"Even if it's
   just for the soundtrack"* (213960449).
2. **The shooting hits hard.** Said 168 times (9.4 per 100). Feeling far stronger than your enemies: 11.
   Never having to reload keeps you shooting: 4. It trains your aim for other shooters: 4.
3. **Many ways to build your character in a run.** Said 144 times (8.1 per 100). Tools that combine into
   your own tactics: 11.
4. **Quick, smooth movement.** Said 132 times (7.4 per 100). Movement that rewards skill - rocket jumps,
   slides, the grappling hook, bunny hops: 33.
5. **One more run.** Said 129 times (7.2 per 100).
6. **Much better with a friend.** Said 125 times (7.0 per 100). With a family member: 12.
7. **The look.** A clean, bright comic style, said 100 times (5.6 per 100); words like *"POW"* pop up as
   guns fire. It also looks great: 24. Easy to read what is happening: 10.
8. **Unlocking things feels good.** Said 78 times (4.4 per 100).
9. **The best game of its kind.** Said 75 times (4.2 per 100), rising from 0.5 per 100 in Early Access to
   7.9 after updates ended.
10. **The action never lets up.** Said 70 times (3.9 per 100).
11. **Better than a named rival.** Said 67 times; Gunfire Reborn is the game most often beaten (32).
12. **Each class plays its own way** (51) and **each weapon feels different** (50).
13. **Hard in a way players enjoy.** Said 50 times. Difficulty levels that step up well: 18. Difficulty
    you can set in detail: 5. Losses that feel like your own fault: 8.
14. **A fair price** (48) and **worth it at any price** (11).
15. **Secrets reward exploring.** Said 47 times. Levels that are well built: 15.
16. **Made with care** (42). **Updates made it better** (38); updates came steadily (20).
17. **Overlooked, underrated** (37). It even won over players who avoid this kind of game (11), and it
    beats most big-budget games (5).
18. **Early Access was worth it** - good value while unfinished (33), and the finished game kept the
    promise (35); it never felt unfinished (17).
19. **Great bosses** (26), **good enemy variety** (15), **runs that differ** (26), **works alone** (25),
    **good in short sittings** (16).
20. **It runs well** (19), **even on weak machines** (8), **with few, small bugs** (19); it runs well on
    the Steam Deck (5).
21. **Smaller things:** fine for younger players (6 - *"family-friendly Doom with co-op"*, 156644725), a story worth following
    (8), well-made cutscenes (4), a fair, achievable set of achievements with plenty to hunt (12), a
    demo that let them try it first (13), the studio is open about its plans (11), and they will buy
    the studio's next game (11).

### What players complained about, most to least
Counts are times it was said. 533 of the 729 complaints (73%) come from thumbs-up reviews.
1. **Too little to do.** Said 48 times (2.7 per 100); 4 are thumbs down. Mostly in Early Access (5.5 per
   100), down to 0.3 after updates ended.
2. **Only two players.** Said 36 times (2.0 per 100), steady from 2020 to 2026; only 2 are thumbs down.
   *"game of the year if it had 4 player"* (134257787). Two players defend the limit:
   more would make it too easy (140071314) or the maps too small (190306353).
3. **Buy it on sale only.** Said 18 times, all thumbs up. Too expensive for what it is: 9 (5 thumbs down).
4. **Another game does it better.** Said 17 times, 7 of them thumbs down. Gunfire Reborn in 9 of the
   17, for its progression and replay value.
5. **Build choices are shallow** (16) and **some weapons are not worth using** (16); only a few guns
   carry you (8); only a few builds work (6); finds change numbers, not play (5).
6. **Runs feel the same** (15), **too few kinds of enemy** (15), **it runs out fast** (10); mostly early.
7. **An update made it worse.** Said 13 times, **8 of them thumbs down** - the most common reason given
   in a thumbs-down review: reworked abilities and classes, the final bosses, *"feature creep"*
   (162400098).
8. **You cannot stop a run and finish it later.** Said 12 times, **5 thumbs down**. *"Runs can take an
   hour … there's no saves between levels"* (149722424).
9. **The levels are the same every run** (10), too easy (10), too hard (7), a mode they expected is
   missing (10).
10. **The studio wiped their progress** (9): 5 of them at the full release, when everyone's progress
    was reset (the studio's post confirms it); 2 thumbs down. *"felt robbed"* (151158851).
11. **Sad that updates stopped** (9, 2 thumbs down).
12. **Smaller things:** the music is not to their taste (8); arena rooms too small (7); wants more
    updates (7); the robots look alike - *"They look like placeholders"* (89809773) (7);
    too much grind to unlock everything (7); the story does not land (7); the first hours are slow
    because the good tools are locked (6); a class is too weak (6); stuns and loss of control (6); one
    part far harder than the rest (6); one hated enemy or boss (5); no mod support (5); bugs that break
    play (5); crashes (4); the name does not fit the game - *"terrible title"* (202204363) (4);
    required jumping sections in a shooter (4); motion sickness (3); an empty, lifeless home base (3).

### How it changed over time
Four periods: Early Access (2020-08 to 2023-10, 767 reviews), the full-release month (2023-11, 157),
the update years (2023-12 to 2025-04, 519) and after updates ended (2025-05 to 2026-10, 343).
- **Thumbs up barely moved:** 96.0%, 97.5%, 97.1%, 95.6%.
- **Complaints fell once the game was finished:** 51.4 per 100 in Early Access, 40.1 at release, about
  30 after. Too little content 5.5 → 0.3; shallow builds 1.8 → 0.3; too few enemies 1.7 → 0.3; runs
  that feel the same 1.7 → 0.0.
- **What rose:** best of its kind 0.5 → 7.9; buy on sale only 1.2 → 1.7; updates stopped 0.0 → 2.0.
- **The release month** had the most anger about the progress reset (3.2 per 100) and the most wins over
  named rivals (7.6).
- **The ending:** the studio's post of 2025-05-07 said there would be no more updates, that the game was
  designed for 25 to 50 hours, and that online play would stay. Reviews took it calmly (below).
- **Online play broke later.** Reviews from late 2025 and early 2026 say friends could not play together
  or kept dropping; the studio posted a fix on 2026-03-25, worked out with Epic Games. One reviewer
  turned the thumb back to up once it worked (211941093).
- **Movement praise falls in the last period** (9.4 → 2.0 per 100). Part of that may be how later
  reviews were filed, not only what players said; this was not checked.

### Co-op and online play
- **Friends make it:** much better with friends, 125 times; with family, 12; one player meets a friend
  every week through it, with *"enough breathing space that you can actually talk with the other player"* (236513632).
- **Two players at most** is the top lasting complaint (36). Players ask for three, four, even *"4-6 players"* (136706547).
- **Dropping out loses the run** (4): *"you can't reinvite to your run"* (218297733); one player
  understands no reconnect is planned (234193633, the reviewer's understanding). Cannot connect at all:
  2. Frequent drops: 1. Lag: 3. Smooth online: 9.
- **The guest fights at a disadvantage** (1). **Bringing a teammate back costs your own health** (2):
  *"give my dead teammate half of my hp"* to open a door (221526073).
- **Finding people:** no server browser (3), no public matchmaking (1), cannot talk to the other player
  (3), pings that mark only some things (1).
- **Works alone** (25) - *"The most fun I had singleplayer in an fps in a long time"* (206079322) - though 3 say it punishes
  playing alone.
- **Playing well together:** partners cover each other with different classes and help in locked
  challenge rooms (3).
- Who hosts a game is not known from the pages.

### Combat, movement and feel
- **Shooting** that hits hard (168) is the heart of it; feeling far stronger than your enemies (11);
  never reloading (4). Feels like every other shooter: 4. Cannot shoot while sprinting: 1.
- **Movement** quick and smooth (132); rewards skill (33). Sluggish (3); a move that does not do what you
  asked, like a grappling hook that never pulls (4); missing modern moves (4); speed boosts capped at a
  flat percentage (1).
- **The jetpack and grappling hook are unlocks**, and one player says the levels are built for them,
  which takes 10 to 15 hours (201662266).
- **Loss of control (6):** enemy hacks that reverse your controls, stuns you break by tapping a key three
  times in a game built on movement (153663026), and a run on the hardest setting lost at the final
  boss when a "hacked" effect walked the player into it; they want a toggle to turn it off (232808117).
- **Controls** responsive and clear (13); on a controller, aim help can be tuned all the way up
  (236648445). Motion sickness: 3.
- **Reading the fight:** easy to read at a glance (10); threats unclear (3); the robots look alike (7).

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- Great bosses (26); good enemy variety (15) against too few enemy types (15, mostly early); one hated
  enemy or boss (5 - the Judge Ball boss twice); enemies that soak bullets (3); poor enemy behaviour (4).
- Hard in a good way (50); difficulty levels that step up well (18); set in detail (5). Too easy (10);
  too hard (7); one part far harder (6); difficulty badly scaled (3).
- Healing: *"all melee weapons grant extra healing on kill"*, so melee builds are never wasted runs
  (232191761);
  one player says health pickups vanish before you can reach them and heal very little (207965546);
  one dislikes being locked in arena rooms with a horde (207965546), while another likes that the
  locked rooms keep you moving (232191761).
- Losses feel like your own fault (8): *"you can recognise how your own misplays bring about your
  failed runs"* (236513632).

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- Unlocking feels good (78); many ways to build (144); each class different (51); each weapon different
  (50).
- What carries over: permanent upgrades at the base camp, classes, and weapon cards. Some say the
  permanent upgrades are too small to feel (5) and filled up quickly; one says *"The real upgrade is your skill as a
  player"* and that, unlike Hades, no huge grind is needed (222060858).
- Grind: too much to unlock everything (7); the first hours are weak because the good tools are locked
  (6); unlocks come too fast (4); nothing left to chase at the end (5).
- **Unlocks through errands outside combat** (2): powerful gadgets need graves dug at random or named
  guns shown to a character, with guides needed and runs spent not trying to win (231696370, thumbs
  down; the store page never says so).
- Luck: the levels are fixed, with random weapons and upgrades. The same levels every run (10) against
  one who likes fixed routes with random builds (226898448) and one who likes learning the maps
  (235353903). Luck decides too much (4); a run is only fun with a strong weapon (1); progress lets you
  shape what drops (2).

### Runs, content and replay value
- A run takes 30 to 60 minutes, players say. One more run (129); good in short sittings (16).
- **No way to stop a run and come back** (12, 5 thumbs down). The studio added a save and quit on
  2024-11-27, for single player only (studio post); after it, one player never found it (207408865)
  and a co-op player says a run resets if you leave (236645998).
- Too little content (48, mostly Early Access); runs feel the same (15); it runs out fast (10). Endless
  mode, added in 2024, gives more replay (3).
- Secrets reward exploring (47); quests and secrets that need a wiki or guide to understand (3).

### Money and price
- Sold once; no shop (2 say so with praise). A fair price (48); worth it at any price (11); 4 bought it to
  support the studio.
- Buy on sale only (18); too expensive for what it is (9); *"a bit pricey for the duration of the game, so get it on a sale"* (232808117).
- Came through a game subscription (6); the studio's release post lists Xbox and PC Game Pass. A demo
  let them try first (13).

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Runs well (19), even on weak machines (8); few, small bugs (19); runs well on the Steam Deck (5).
- Crashes (4); bugs that break play (5); stuck on scenery (2); demanding or hot-running (6); slows down
  the longer you play (2).
- Saves lost on two computers (233527755). One says the whole computer hard-crashes since a patch
  (224456327, the reviewer's account).

### The studio, updates and community
- Updates made it better (38) and came steadily (20); the studio is open about its plans (11) and
  listens (8). Players like the people running it (3) and will buy their next game (11).
- Changes that made it worse (13, 8 thumbs down) - the top reason in thumbs-down reviews.
- The full-release progress reset (9 complaints), confirmed by the studio's post; the studio then
  offered a way to recover progress (studio post, 2023-11-22).
- **The ending was accepted:** 9 are sad updates stopped (2 thumbs down: *"Devs are no longer updating the game, don't buy this game"*, 194438198),
  and 4 call the game finished, not abandoned - *"Its feature complete"* (229034414, 21 found it helpful).
  Across the corpus, sadness at stopped updates is 0.5 per 100 here against 4.7 in Back 4 Blood.
- A Dead Cells crossover update came in 2024-08 (studio post); 8 reviews name Dead Cells.
- No mod support (5).

### What players asked for
- More players: 36 times.
- A way to save a run and come back: 12.
- More content, as praise: 25.
- Modes: versus play (3), endless or new game plus (2, before Endless mode came), horde or survival (2),
  practice (1), online before it existed (1), playing as an enemy (1).
- A way back into a run after a drop (2); a toggle for the hack effect (1); wider pings (1); voiced
  characters (1); a codex of the world (1); run modifiers (1); a crossover DLC (1); a livelier home base,
  some with traders (3).

### Only in this game
Things said here and in no other game read so far (73 such tags), the ones that matter for design:
- The robots look alike and generic (7).
- Rewards for playing fast and aggressive (6; one names ranks for speed that raise earnings).
- Permanent upgrades too small to feel (5); finds that change numbers, not play (5).
- Never reloading keeps you shooting (4); the game's name puts people off (4).
- A teammate's revive costs your own health (2); fighting is how you heal (2).
- Fixed routes with random builds as a strength (1); learning the maps pays (1); room to talk between
  fights (1); errands outside combat to unlock gadgets (1); pings that mark too little (1); health
  pickups that vanish (1); being shut in arena rooms with a horde (1).

### For Dominion - our reading
These are suggestions, not decisions.
1. **Four players is a selling point; say it loudly.** Evidence: 36 asks for more than two players,
   from 2020 to 2026, 34 of them from players who still recommend the game; the highest rate in the
   corpus (2.0 per 100). Dominion could put "four-player co-op" on the store page and in the first
   trailer, and test scaling, revives and rewards at four, three and two. **Strong.**
2. **Feel comes first.** Evidence: music, shooting and movement praised more here than in any other
   game read (11.3, 9.4 and 9.2 per 100), in a game rated 96%. Dominion could spend early effort on
   the gun feel, a short quick move set with room to master, and the soundtrack. **Strong.**
3. **Let a group stop a run and come back to it, in co-op too.** Evidence: 12 times, 5 thumbs down; the
   single-player-only save left co-op without one. Dominion could save at each extraction or between
   arenas, for the whole group, and show where. **Strong.**
4. **Let a dropped player back into the same run.** Evidence: a drop loses the run (4, 2 thumbs down);
   online play broke for months after support ended (studio fix, 2026-03-25). Dominion could hold a
   dropped player's slot and let the host re-invite; and keep testing the online service even after
   updates stop. **Medium.**
5. **Do not take control away.** Evidence: 6 complaints, including a top-difficulty run lost to a
   "hacked" effect at the final boss. Dominion could keep stuns short, rare and readable, give a way
   out, and never reverse the controls. **Medium.**
6. **Give the movement tools the levels need from the first run.** Evidence: levels built for a
   grappling hook that takes 10-15 hours to unlock; a slow first few hours (6); gadgets behind errands
   (2). Dominion could put the grind on options, not on basic movement. **Medium.**
7. **Never wipe what players earned, or give it back.** Evidence: the full-release reset drew 5 of its 9
   complaints in one month, 2 thumbs down. Dominion could plan its save format early and, if a reset is
   ever needed, offer recovery. **Medium.**
8. **If support ends, explain it, finish the game and keep online working.** Evidence: 9 sad, 4
   "finished, not abandoned", 2 thumbs down - far calmer than other games that stopped (Back 4 Blood
   4.7 per 100); the online fault came after. **Medium.**
9. **Know the closest rival and beat it on progression and replay.** Evidence: Gunfire Reborn, the
   nearest rival, beaten 32 times and preferred 9, for progression and replay. **Medium.**
10. **Make enemies look distinct.** Evidence: the robots look alike (7, only in this game); threats
    unclear (3). Dominion's sci-fi enemies should read apart at a glance. **Weak.**
11. **Mix fixed layouts with changing runs.** Evidence: the same levels every run (10) against those who
    like learning fixed maps (2). Dominion could keep arena layouts learnable while routes, enemies and
    modifiers change. **Weak.**
12. **Reward pushing forward; let close fighting heal.** Evidence: rewards for playing fast and aggressive (6, one naming speed ranks that raise earnings);
    melee heals on a kill (2). **Weak.**
13. **A long Early Access can work if it keeps its promise.** Evidence: three years of Early Access at
    96% up; too little content fell from 5.5 to 0.3 per 100 after release; the promise kept, 35 times.
    **Medium.**

### Limits
- 10.4% of English reviews, ±2.50% on the whole sample; the periods carry about ±3.5 to ±7.8 (the
  release month is 157 reviews).
- Weights reach ×16 in several months; the release month holds 14.4% of all English reviews.
- Half the reviews are 15 words or fewer, and 16% of points say nothing specific; most complaint counts
  are under 20.
- The fall in movement praise in the last period may partly be how reviews were filed; not checked.
- 200 reviews (11.2%) were edited later and are counted on the day they were first written.
- Not checked: the publisher history some reviewers name, when the online fault began (the fix date is
  the studio's), prices quoted in reviews, and the crash one player blames on a patch.
- Rival counts come from searching review text, so a review that names a game without praising or
  faulting it is counted too.
- First person and two players: the feel lessons may not carry one to one to a third-person,
  four-player game.
- English only: about 7,200 reviews in other languages were not read. Slurs and jabs are recorded only
  as jabs; personal, family and health details were left out.

---

## The First Descendant

**Read:** 1,600 of 51,208 English reviews (3.1%; 1,604 were read and 4 were not about the game), ±2.50%,
written June 2024 to October 2026. **64.5% thumbs up** in the sample (Steam, all languages: 57%, Mixed).
**Pages:** `findings/the-first-descendant-english.md`, `findings/the-first-descendant.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` section 25.

**What it is:** A free third-person sci-fi looter shooter. You play alone or with up to four players in
online co-op. Strangers can be matched into your mission. The pages do not say who hosts a game. You play
missions and grind over and over to build characters and guns; there are no runs that end and no
extraction. It is free, with a shop, a battle pass and seasons. Built on Unreal Engine 5. Made by NEXON
Games and published by NEXON. Out on Steam on 2024-06-30. (Store facts checked against Steam.)

**How close to Dominion:** Close on the surface. It shares the third-person camera, the sci-fi setting,
four-player online co-op, power that builds up over time, and Unreal Engine 5. It is not built on runs,
has no extraction, and is free with a shop instead of sold once. Its long-term progress is a grind for
characters, not a run that resets.

### The short version (plain words)
This is the only game of the ten counted this way where complaints outnumber praise: 122.2 complaints
against 83.6 praise per 100 reviews, or 0.68 praise for each complaint. The next lowest, ARC Raiders, has
1.53 praise for each complaint; Deep Rock Galactic, the highest, has 7.4. Most of the anger is about how
the game is sold, not how it plays. Getting what you need means chance stacked on chance, and the shop
sells a way out; 294 reviews carry a complaint or remark about the shop and 125 talk about luck. Players
also judge it against Warframe all the time: 282 reviews (17.6%) name Warframe, and half of those (141)
are thumbs down. The game did get better: complaints per 100 reviews fell from 128.0 at launch to 84.3
in 2026, once it showed drop odds, added bad-luck protection and cut the grind. But 62% of all English
reviews were written in the first month, so the launch verdict still sets its Steam score.

Its reviewers argue at length. They write the most points per review of the ten games (2.37), the
reviews average 60.1 words, and only 47% are 15 words or fewer (72% in Crab Champions). Only 12.4% of
the points say nothing specific. The median review shows 31.0 hours played; 410 reviews show 100 hours
or more and 38 show 1,000 or more.

What the points are about: game design 29.8% (mostly the grind and luck), a bare thumbs up or down
14.9%, how the game is sold 12.0% (385 points about the shop), comparisons with other games 10.9%, tech
8.5%, art 7.9% (half of it the fan service), the community and studio 4.8%, content 4.2%, story 3.2%,
updates 2.8%, sound 0.8%, translation 0.2%, accessibility 0.1%.

### What players praised, most to least
Counts here are times it was said (bullets). 369 more thumbs-up reviews said nothing specific (23.1 per
100).
1. **The grind feels earned.** Said 71 times (4.4 per 100 reviews).
2. **It looks great.** Said 64 times (4.0 per 100).
3. **Everything can be earned by playing, without paying.** Said 56 times (3.5 per 100).
4. **The characters are appealing.** Said 46 times (2.9 per 100).
5. **The studio listens and acts.** Said 45 times (2.8 per 100). It peaked at 6.5 per 100 in the rest of
   2024, and was 1.1 in 2026. One review says season 1's content was moved earlier after feedback
   (173145523).
6. **It beats its rivals.** Said 44 times (2.8 per 100). Reasons: the shooting and respect for your time.
   *"The shooter experience ... feels worst in WF and in my opinion best in TFD"* (178128680). A
   500-hour Warframe player says *"Compared to Warframe, The First Descendant respects my time more"*
   (229267499). A 2,239-hour reviewer lists what it lacks from its rivals: no content islands, no
   content behind a paywall, no broken player-versus-player (203838929, 47 found it helpful). Per 100
   reviews it runs 2.4, 3.2, 2.1 and 4.3 across the four periods.
7. **The price is fair** (it is free). Said 43 times (2.7 per 100).
8. **Combat hits hard.** Said 42 times (2.6 per 100).
9. **Builds are deep and varied.** Said 34 times (2.1 per 100).
10. **An update made it better.** Said 31 times (1.9 per 100). Rose from 1.5 to 5.9 per 100 across the
    periods.
11. **The story is worth following.** Said 29 times (1.8 per 100). Rose to 4.3 per 100 in 2026.
12. **It keeps pulling you back.** Said 28 times (1.8 per 100).
13. **Each character plays its own way.** Said 27 times (1.7 per 100).
14. **Much better with friends.** Said 24 times (1.5 per 100).
15. **Movement feels quick and responsive.** Said 19 times (1.2 per 100). The grapple and double jump are
    named.
16. **The base design works and the rest can still come.** Said 18 times (1.1 per 100).
17. **Progress feels satisfying.** Said 18 times (1.1 per 100).
18. **A game you can relax to.** Said 15 times (0.9 per 100).
19. **The bosses are a highlight.** Said 15 times (0.9 per 100).
20. **Buying things to support the studio.** Said 12 times (0.8 per 100).
21. **Runs well on my machine.** Said 12 times (0.8 per 100).
22. **The shop only sells looks.** Said 12 times (0.8 per 100).
23. **Plenty of content.** Said 11 times (0.7 per 100).
24. **A fair way of making money.** Said 11 times (0.7 per 100).
25. **The drop chances feel fair.** 4 times (only in this game).
26. **The odds are shown.** 3 reviews; **bad-luck protection exists**, 2 reviews. Both come only in
    2025-2026.
27. **Free items are handed out often** (free top characters, login events, catch-up mail). Rose from 0.1
    to 2.7 per 100 across the periods.
28. **Coming back after a break, you get catch-up rewards.** Named by a returning player (229267499).
29. **Seen as better now than at launch.** *"after a streak of high-quality monthly updates, TFD is
    actually finally a very good game"* (226202933, 43 found it helpful).
30. **A stronger player can help you through.** 2 (only in this game).

Neither praise nor complaint: 149 reviews (9.3 per 100) say the characters are designed to look sexy
(fan service). Some like it, some find it embarrassing, many only joke. 124 times reviewers described the
game by naming other games; *"Like the child of Warframe and Destiny"* (170901009, thumbs up) is the
standard way to put it.

### What players complained about, most to least
Counts are times it was said (bullets) unless it says reviews. **547 of the 1,955 complaint bullets
(28.0%) come from reviews that recommend the game**; the rest come from thumbs-down reviews. The
thumbs-down reviews have a median of 15.6 hours played, the thumbs-up 39.2.
1. **A named rival does it better** (almost always Warframe). Said 113 times (7.1 per 100), on 112
   reviews. Per 100 reviews it runs 7.1, 7.4, 7.9 and 5.4 across the four periods. A Warframe veteran of
   5,000 hours finds the farming never makes him feel stronger (226240847). *"took the good parts of
   Warframe and Destiny 2 and threw it out the window"* (171640774, thumbs down).
2. **Too grindy.** Said 100 times (6.2 per 100).
3. **The shop costs far too much.** Said 75 times (4.7 per 100).
4. **Thumbs down with no reason given.** 75 times (4.7 per 100).
5. **Repetitive.** Said 67 times (4.2 per 100).
6. **The thing you need may never drop.** Said 67 times (4.2 per 100), on 66 reviews.
7. **The grind is made frustrating to sell shortcuts.** Said 59 times (3.7 per 100), on 58 reviews.
8. **Feels like a cash grab.** Said 56 times (3.5 per 100).
9. **A copy of an older game.** Said 54 times (3.4 per 100).
10. **The story is thin or forgettable.** Said 47 times (2.9 per 100). The repeated word "Ironheart" and
    the names Greg and Jeremy are mocked (names too plain: 1).
11. **Poorly optimised.** Said 41 times (2.6 per 100).
12. **The stated drop chances feel rigged.** Said 34 times (2.1 per 100).
13. **Locked behind real-world waiting time.** Said 25 times (1.6 per 100).
14. **Paying affects play** (pay to win). Said 23 times (1.4 per 100). Rose from 1.0 to 2.7 per 100.
15. **The studio teamed up with another studio the reviewer dislikes.** Said 21 times (1.3 per 100).
16. **One random drop only leads to another random drop.** Said 20 times (1.2 per 100). Built for this
    game.
17. **Crashes again and again.** Said 19 times (1.2 per 100).
18. **Long load times.** Said 18 times (1.1 per 100).
19. **Crashes at a certain moment.** Said 16 times (1.0 per 100).
20. **Bought colours are used up when you apply them.** Said 16 times (1.0 per 100). Built for this game.
21. **Needs a strong PC.** Said 16 times (1.0 per 100).
22. **One character or option is far stronger than the rest.** Said 16 times (1.0 per 100). Rose from 0.5
    to 3.2 per 100. Bunny, then Serena and Raven, are named.
23. **Cannot connect.** 15 times (0.9 per 100), every one at launch.
24. **Too little content.** 14 times (0.9 per 100).
25. **Servers are poor.** 13 times (0.8 per 100).
26. **Paying only shortens the grind** (said as a plain fact, neither good nor bad). 21 times.
27. **One strong player kills everything first** while the others follow. 10.
28. **The battle pass rewards are poor.** 7 (built here).
29. **Power creep**: new gear keeps beating old gear. 6.
30. **Skins can only be bought, not earned.** 5 (built here).
31. **Wants a token that guarantees the drop.** 5 reviews (built here).
32. **Abilities make the guns pointless.** 4 (built here).
33. **One gun type uses the scarcest ammo** without hitting hard enough. 4 (built here).
34. **A boss can only be beaten by a rule the game never hints at.** 4 (built here).
35. **Runs worse than it used to on the same PC.** 4 (built here).
36. **No guilds or clans.** 4 (built here).
37. **Locked out of my own account.** 4. Account linking that wiped progress (176109233, 236515665) and a
    support ticket closed without help (231111022). Reviewers' accounts, not checked.
38. **The studio took back a promise** (after months of "no nerfs"). 4.
39. **No fix for a purchase that went wrong** (currency bought and never received). 4. **The store would
    not take my money.** 3.
40. **Failed tries do not raise the chance.** 3 reviews (built here).
41. **Players idle and leave the work to others.** 3 (built here).
42. **The farming is slowed down on purpose.** 3 (built here).
43. **An upgrade sends the character back to level one.** 3 (built here).
44. **Everyone looks the same.** 3 (built here).
45. **A timed mini-game is too tight.** 3 (built here).
46. **Players bump into each other.** 2 (built here).
47. **Two each, all only in this game:** a matched fight starts before you load in; the game times you
    out while loading; you cannot choose a server region; loading screens split small areas; the rules
    for fitting mods are too strict; the chat filter hides ordinary words; the chat has no separate
    channels; you cannot hide other players in the hub; subtitles cannot be turned off.
48. **One each, all only in this game:** each player rolls their own drops; enemies switch off your
    abilities; carrying an objective stops you shooting; damage depends on the frame rate; movement feels
    weightless; you cannot switch the camera to the other shoulder; a revive limit restarts the whole
    fight; a revived player gets no moment of safety; a reset fills groups with low-level players; you
    join another player's mission by accident; every activity has matchmaking; you cannot choose your
    reward from the pool; locked doors send you back to old missions; detail pops in close up; the shop
    sells what the story gives free; a refund gets the account banned; an automatic ban cannot be
    lifted; the apology gift was too small; news of an outage was only on social media; players call for
    nerfs instead of buffs; players push the studio to make it easier.
49. **Smaller ones, counts not given:** a bought character comes without its upgrades; some items only
    come in bundles; a skin must be bought again for each character; prices are not set for poorer
    regions (208632208, 325 found it helpful, about African countries); regret over money spent; skins
    that break the mood of a battle; male characters weaker and less dressed up; nerfs of loved
    characters (Ines in April 2025, by the reviewers' account); one wipe ends a party; no crouch or
    slide; an unreliable grapple; shader compiling at launch; "now more about cosmetics than play" (rose
    from 0.1 to 2.7 per 100).

### How it changed over time
Four periods. Margins are about ±3 (launch), ±6, ±6 and ±7, so read for direction.

| Period | Reviews read | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Launch (June-July 2024) | 864 | 63.1% | 57.8 | 128.0 |
| Rest of 2024 | 310 | 66.1% | 69.4 | 119.7 |
| 2025 | 241 | 63.9% | 56.0 | 102.5 |
| 2026 (to October) | 185 | 69.2% | 64.9 | 84.3 |

- **Complaints fell every period.** Grind 6.7 → 2.7 per 100. Shop prices 6.0 → 1.6. Drops that never
  come 4.3 → 2.2. "A copy of an older game" 4.2 → 1.1. Poor optimisation 3.1 → 0.5. Performance
  reviews 8.8 → 4.9 per 100. Server reviews 4.3 → 0.5. Luck reviews 8.3 → 4.9. Performance, crash and
  server reviews together 18.2 → 12.4 per 100.
- **What grew:** fan service 6.6 → 16.8 reviews per 100; one character too strong 0.5 → 3.2; pay to win
  1.0 → 2.7; "now more about cosmetics than play" 0.1 → 2.7; "an update made it better" 1.5 → 5.9; free
  items 0.1 → 2.7.
- **"A named rival does it better" barely moved until 2026:** 7.1, 7.4, 7.9, then 5.4 per 100. "It beats
  its rivals" ran 2.4, 3.2, 2.1, then 4.3.
- **What players tie the turn to:** shown drop odds and bad-luck protection (praised only in 2025-2026,
  5 reviews); 2026 reviewers say a run of updates removed the container chance and cut the grind
  (232874117, 236591847).
- **Who writes later:** the median hours on a review rise from 20 at launch to 50-70 later. Late
  reviewers are veterans.
- **Warframe fades as a yardstick:** named in 172 reviews at launch, 47 in the rest of 2024, 42 in 2025,
  21 in 2026.
- **But the most-helpful reviews are angry veterans from 2025-2026:**
  - 592 helpful, thumbs down, 1,546 hours (186252899): a character nerfed right after her first buffs,
    while another stays too strong.
  - 554 helpful, thumbs down, 117 hours (231111022): a friend lost a 120-hour account when support
    closed his ticket.
  - 545 helpful, thumbs down, 3,012 hours (233515312, 2026-08): season 4's top weapons, weekly reroll
    limits and sold drop boosts are *"an open push to pay for power"*.
  - 325 helpful, thumbs up, 278 hours (208632208): premium currency too expensive in African countries.
  - 298 helpful, thumbs down, 1 hour (213357044): *"It's Warframe, but with more microtransactions and
    more micromanagement"*.
  - 289 helpful, thumbs down, 615 hours (208640843): collaboration skins at $80 and $35 (the reviewer
    says); each update makes enemies spongier.

### Co-op and online play
- **Better with friends:** said 24 times. **A stronger player can help you through:** 2 (praise).
- **Strangers cause friction.** One strong player clears everything while three follow (10). Players
  idle and leave the work to others (3). One wipe ends a party. Matchmaking can drop you into a fight that
  started before you loaded in (2). Players bump into each other's bodies (2). A reset filled groups with
  low-level players (1).
- **Joining and loading:** the game times you out while loading (2); you cannot choose a server region
  (2); you can join another player's mission by accident (1); every activity has matchmaking, which one
  reviewer did not want (1).
- **Reviving:** a revived player gets no moment of safety (1); a revive limit restarts the whole fight
  (1).
- **Loot:** each player rolls their own drops (1), so one player's luck does nothing for the group.
- **Servers:** cannot connect was said 15 times, all at launch; server reviews fell from 4.3 to 0.5 per
  100. Poor servers 13.
- **Social:** no guilds or clans (4). The chat filter hides ordinary words (2); the chat has no separate
  channels (2); you cannot hide other players in the hub (2).
- The pages say nothing about hosting type, joining a game already running (other than being matched in
  late), bots, friendly fire or crossplay.

### Combat, movement and feel
- **Combat hits hard:** 42. **Each character plays its own way:** 27. **The characters are appealing:**
  46. Characters are the draw; guns come second.
- **Abilities vs guns:** abilities that clear the map make guns pointless (4). Enemies or fights that
  switch off your abilities leave only the gun, so the character feels pointless (1). One review notes
  abilities cost both energy and a cooldown (1).
- **One gun type uses the scarcest ammo** without enough damage to be worth it (4).
- **Carrying an objective stops you shooting** (1).
- **Damage depends on the frame rate** (1), by the reviewer's account.
- **Movement is mixed.** Responsive movement 19 (grapple and double jump). Complaints: no crouch or
  slide, and an unreliable grapple; movement feels weightless (1). Players bump into each other (2).
- **Camera:** you cannot switch the camera to the other shoulder (1).
- **Balance:** one character far stronger than the rest (16, rising to 3.2 per 100); power creep (6);
  nerfs of loved characters, with the most-helpful review in the sample (592) about one.
- The pages give no count on controller support or rebinding keys.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- **Bosses are a highlight:** 15.
- **A boss rule is never hinted:** 4 (an order or target you must guess).
- **A timed mini-game is too tight:** 3.
- **A revive limit restarts the whole fight:** 1.
- **Enemies get spongier each update** (one 289-helpful review says so).
- **Enemies that switch off your abilities** draw complaints (1).
- **Players push the studio to make it easier** (1), and **call for nerfs instead of buffs** (1).
- The pages give no other counts on difficulty settings or enemy variety.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- **The grind is the game.** Too grindy 100; the grind feels earned 71; everything can be earned 56;
  progress feels satisfying 18; locked behind real-world waiting time 25; the farming is slowed down on
  purpose 3.
- **The chain of chances.** Characters and guns are crafted from parts. Parts come from containers. A
  container drops at one chance and opens to the part at another. Reviewers do the maths: *"a 25% chance
  ... then roll a separate reactor at 10%, about 2.5% each"* (176109236); *"52 runs for a material for 52
  runs of a mission, still no code"* (176097031).
- **Luck counts (reviews):** may never drop 66; chances feel rigged 34; one random drop leads to another
  20; wants a guaranteed-drop token 5; failed tries do not raise the chance 3; odds shown (praise) 3;
  bad-luck protection (praise) 2. Drop chances feel fair (praise) 4. You cannot choose your reward from
  the pool (1).
- **Randomness complaints are 7.2 reviews per 100 (116 reviews), the highest in the corpus** (DRG:
  Rogue Core 6.6, Remnant 2 2.5, Escape from Duckov 2.0, Warframe 1.3).
- **Builds are deep:** 34. **Power creep:** 6. The rules for fitting mods are too strict (2).
- **Resets and backtracking:** an upgrade sends the character back to level one (3); locked doors send
  you back to old missions (1).
- **Looks:** everyone looks the same (3).
- **Returning players** got endgame materials to catch up (229267499).

### Runs, content and replay value
- No runs. Missions are repeated to farm parts.
- **Repetitive:** 67. **Too little content:** 14. **Plenty of content:** 11. **Keeps pulling you back:**
  28. **A game you can relax to:** 15. **The potential is still there:** 18.
- **The story:** thin or forgettable 47; worth following 29 (rising to 4.3 per 100 in 2026); names too
  plain (1). Subtitles cannot be turned off (2).

### Money and price
- **This is where the reviews live.** 294 reviews (18.4%) carry a complaint or remark about the shop;
  385 points in all are about it. **234 reviews (14.6 per 100) carry a shop complaint, the highest in the
  corpus; Warframe, also free, is at 2.8.** Terminull Brigade is 13.2 and Helldivers 2 1.8; every other
  English group of 300 or more reviews is at 1.4 or less.
- **Complaints:** too expensive 75; frustration built to sell shortcuts 58 reviews; cash grab 56; pay to
  win 23 (rising 1.0 → 2.7 per 100); colours used up when applied 16; battle pass rewards poor 7; skins
  only for money 5; paying only shortens the grind 21 (said as neutral); the shop sells what the story
  gives free (1).
- **Specific grievances (counts not given):** a bought character without its upgrades; items only in
  bundles; a skin bought again for each character; prices not adjusted for a region; regret over money
  spent; collaboration skins at $80 and $35 (the reviewer says, 208640843).
- **Purchases gone wrong:** no fix for a purchase that went wrong (4), such as currency bought and never
  received; store would not take their money (3); a refund got the account banned (1).
- **Praise:** fair price 43; buys to support the studio 12; shop only sells looks 12; a fair business
  model 11; free items handed out often (rising to 2.7 per 100).
- **Where the gap with Warframe lies:** both games are free. "The grind is built to sell shortcuts" and
  "the shop costs far too much" are each under 0.5 per 100 in Warframe, against 3.7 and 4.7 here. The
  gap is the chain players describe, not the price.
- **What players will not accept:** chance stacked on chance with money as the exit, and power for sale
  (233515312, 545 helpful; the reviewer names season 4's top weapons, weekly reroll limits and sold drop
  boosts).

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- **Unreal Engine 5 costs it on PC.** Poorly optimised 41; needs a strong PC 16; long load times 18;
  crashes again and again 19; crashes at a certain moment 16; shader compiling and crashes at launch.
- **Built for this game:** loading screens split small areas (2); detail pops in close up (1); runs
  worse than it used to (4); the game times you out while loading (2); damage depends on the frame rate
  (1).
- **Runs well:** 12.
- **It got better:** performance reviews 8.8 → 4.9 per 100; poor optimisation 3.1 → 0.5.
- **Accounts:** locked out of my own account 4 (account linking that overwrote progress; a ticket
  closed); an automatic ban cannot be lifted (1); a refund got the account banned (1). Not checked.

### The studio, updates and community
- **Listens and acts:** 45, most in the first months (6.5 per 100 in the rest of 2024, 1.1 in 2026).
- **Updates made it better:** 31, rising to 5.9 per 100 in 2026. Seen as better now than at launch.
- **Nerfs:** nerfs of loved characters (Ines, April 2025, by the reviewers' account) draw complaints;
  the studio took back a "no nerfs" promise (4). The most-helpful review (592) is about a nerf. Some
  players call for nerfs instead of buffs (1).
- **Small slips:** the apology gift was too small (1); news of an outage was only on social media (1).
- **Teamed up with a studio players dislike:** 21.
- **Free items handed out often** rose to 2.7 per 100.
- **Rivals:** some came from Helldivers 2 after its nerfs and from Arc Raiders; others left for
  Warframe, Destiny or Anthem.
- **Played a test before release:** 11 reviews name a beta or test as how they came to the game.
- **Fan service** is the loudest single subject: 149 reviews, 9.3 per 100 (next game Terminull Brigade
  3.3, EDF 5 0.8), rising to 16.8 per 100 in 2026.

### What players asked for
- **A token that guarantees the drop:** 5 reviews.
- **Failed tries that raise the chance** (complaint that they do not): 3.
- **Guilds or clans:** 4.
- **Crouch and slide** (complaint that they are missing; count not given).
- **Prices set for their region** (208632208, 325 helpful).
- **Complaints that imply a request, 2 each:** a way to turn subtitles off; a way to hide other players
  in the hub; a choice of server region; separate chat channels; a chat filter that leaves ordinary words
  alone.
- **1 each:** a choice of reward from the pool; a way to switch the camera shoulder.
- The pages give no other counted requests.

### Only in this game
82 tags appear in no other game (of 515 used here). The ones that matter for design:
- One random drop leads to another: 20.
- Bought colours used up when applied: 16.
- Played a test before release: 11.
- Battle pass rewards poor: 7.
- Skins only for money: 5.
- Wants a token that guarantees the drop: 5.
- Drop chances feel fair: 4.
- Abilities make guns pointless: 4.
- One gun type uses the scarcest ammo: 4.
- A boss rule is never hinted: 4.
- Runs worse than it used to: 4.
- No guilds or clans: 4.
- The studio took back a promise: 4.
- 3 each: failed tries do not raise the chance, odds shown, players idle and leave the work, the farming
  is slowed on purpose, an upgrade sends the character back to level one, everyone looks the same, a
  timed mini-game is too tight.
- 2 each: bad-luck protection, players bump into each other, the fight starts before you load in, times
  you out while loading, cannot choose a region, loading screens split small areas, a stronger player
  can help you through, mod rules too strict, chat filter, no separate chat channels, cannot hide others
  in the hub, subtitles cannot be turned off.
- 1 each: each player rolls their own drops, enemies switch off your abilities, carrying an objective
  stops you shooting, damage tied to frame rate, abilities cost energy and a cooldown, weightless
  movement, cannot switch shoulders, a revive limit restarts the fight, no safety after a revive, a reset
  fills groups with low-level players, joining another mission by accident, matchmaking on every
  activity, cannot choose the reward, locked doors send you back, detail pops in close up, the shop sells
  what the story gives free, a refund gets the account banned, an automatic ban cannot be lifted, the
  apology gift too small, outage news only on social media, calls for nerfs instead of buffs, pushing the
  studio to make it easier. Returning players get a catch-up is also built here.
- "A named rival does it better" is almost only this game's: 112 of the 114 reviews on it in the corpus
  (the other 2 are EDF 5).

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Do not stack chance on chance, with money as the way out.**
   Evidence: shop complaints are 14.6 reviews per 100, the highest in the corpus; Warframe, also free, is
   2.8. Luck complaints are 7.2 per 100, also the highest. One random drop leads to another (20),
   frustration built to sell shortcuts (58 reviews), shop too expensive (75).
   Dominion could: keep each reward to one roll at most. Never put a random box inside a random box. If
   Dominion ever sells anything, sell nothing that skips a luck step.
   Strength: strong.
2. **Show the odds and end bad luck from day one.**
   Evidence: shown odds and bad-luck protection were praised only after the game added them (5 reviews,
   2025-2026); 5 reviews ask for a guaranteed-drop token; 3 say failed tries do not help; 66 reviews say
   the thing they need may never drop; 34 say the odds feel rigged. Luck reviews fell from 8.3 to 4.9 per
   100 once it changed.
   Dominion could: show drop chances in the run summary or codex; give a pity counter that guarantees the
   item after a set number of tries; let players trade duplicates for a token.
   Strength: medium (the praise is small; the complaints are large).
3. **Grind is fine if it feels earned and everything is earnable by play.**
   Evidence: too grindy 100 against grind feels earned 71 and everything earnable 56; real-world wait
   timers 25; grind complaints fell 6.7 → 2.7 per 100 once the grind was cut.
   Dominion could: make meta-progress between runs steady and visible; avoid real-time timers on crafting
   or unlocks.
   Strength: strong.
4. **Do not sell power. Pay to win is the rising complaint even in a free game.**
   Evidence: pay affects play 23, rising 1.0 → 2.7 per 100; the third most-helpful review (545) calls
   season 4 *"an open push to pay for power"*; "now more about cosmetics than play" 0.1 → 2.7.
   Dominion could: keep anything bought to looks only, and keep the game about play.
   Strength: strong.
5. **If Dominion sells looks, make them permanent and fair.**
   Evidence: colours used up when applied (16), skins only for money (5), battle pass rewards poor (7),
   a skin bought again for each character, items only in bundles, no prices for poorer regions (325
   helpful). Praise: shop only sells looks (12), fair business model (11), buying to support the studio
   (12), free items often (rising to 2.7 per 100).
   Dominion could: make bought colours and skins unlock for good and for every character; set regional
   prices; give some skins for play.
   Strength: medium.
6. **Unreal Engine 5 performance is judged hard at launch.**
   Evidence: poorly optimised 41, strong PC needed 16, long loads 18, crashes 19 and 16, shader compiling
   at launch; loading screens between small areas; detail popping in close; runs worse after updates (4).
   Performance reviews fell 8.8 → 4.9 per 100 once fixed.
   Dominion could: precompile shaders before the first match; set a frame budget for low PCs; watch
   pop-in and load screens; test that updates do not slow old machines.
   Strength: strong (same engine).
7. **The launch month sets the score for years.**
   Evidence: 62% of all English reviews were written in the first month. Complaints fell from 128 to 84
   per 100 later, yet Steam still shows 57%, Mixed. Cannot connect (15) was all at launch.
   Dominion could: fix the economy, performance and joining before launch, not after; run a public test
   first (11 reviews here came through a test before release).
   Strength: strong.
8. **Know your yardstick and win on one clear thing.**
   Evidence: 282 reviews (17.6%) name Warframe, half of them thumbs down; Destiny in 186. A named rival
   does it better 113; a copy of an older game 54; beats its rivals 44. When it won, it won on the
   shooting and on respecting time.
   Dominion could: expect to be compared to the co-op shooters near it, and make sure one thing is
   plainly better (feel, run length, fairness of drops).
   Strength: strong for the pattern; medium for which rival.
9. **Strangers in co-op need guard rails.**
   Evidence: one strong player kills everything first (10); players idle (3); one wipe ends a party; you
   can be matched into a fight already started (2); players bump into each other (2). Better with
   friends 24.
   Dominion could: give a late joiner a safe spawn and a moment of control before they can be hurt; make
   power gaps in a lobby smaller (scale or cap); let players walk through each other; reward taking part,
   not only kills.
   Strength: medium.
10. **Decide whether loot is shared or rolled per player, and say so.**
    Evidence: each player rolls their own drops, so one player's luck does nothing for the group (1
    review; built here).
    Dominion could: make some drops shared or tradeable inside a team, so co-op helps everyone's luck.
    Strength: weak.
11. **Keep guns worth using next to abilities.**
    Evidence: abilities make the guns pointless (4); enemies that switch off abilities make the character
    pointless (1); one gun type uses the scarcest ammo (4).
    Dominion could: let abilities set up gun damage rather than replace it; avoid bosses that turn off
    the whole kit; keep ammo types fair.
    Strength: weak to medium.
12. **Buff the weak more than you nerf the loved, and do not promise "no nerfs".**
    Evidence: one character too strong (16, rising 0.5 → 3.2 per 100); the most-helpful review (592)
    is about a nerf right after buffs; took back a promise (4); power creep (6).
    Dominion could: balance by raising weak options; never promise you will not nerf.
    Strength: medium.
13. **Telegraph boss rules.**
    Evidence: a boss rule is never hinted (4); bosses a highlight (15).
    Dominion could: show the needed order or target with clear visual or voice cues.
    Strength: weak.
14. **Movement needs the modern moves.**
    Evidence: responsive movement 19 (grapple, double jump); complaints about no crouch or slide, an
    unreliable grapple, and movement that feels weightless (1).
    Dominion could: include a slide or dodge, and make any traversal tool reliable.
    Strength: medium (it agrees with Crab Champions).
15. **Protect accounts and purchases.**
    Evidence: locked out of own account 4 (linking wiped progress; a ticket closed); the second
    most-helpful review (554) is about a lost account; purchase that went wrong 4; store would not take
    money 3; an automatic ban cannot be lifted (1); a refund got the account banned (1).
    Dominion could: never let linking a platform overwrite progress; back up saves; have a clear support
    path for lost progress and for appealing a ban.
    Strength: medium (few reviews, very high helpful votes).
16. **Choose the tone and keep looks inside it.**
    Evidence: fan service is 149 reviews (9.3 per 100) and rising to 16.8 per 100; skins break the mood;
    male characters weaker and less dressed up.
    Dominion could: decide the tone of its sci-fi look early, keep skins in that mood, and treat all
    characters with the same care.
    Strength: medium.
17. **A thin story is skipped; a light one can still win people later.**
    Evidence: thin or forgettable 47 vs worth following 29 (rising to 4.3 per 100 in 2026); a repeated
    word and names mocked.
    Dominion could: keep story short and avoid catchwords repeated too often.
    Strength: weak to medium.
18. **Listen early, keep saying so, and help returners catch up.**
    Evidence: listens and acts 45 (peak 6.5 per 100 in the first months, 1.1 in 2026); updates made it
    better rising to 5.9; returning player got catch-up rewards (229267499).
    Dominion could: post what changed and why; give returning players a catch-up.
    Strength: medium.
19. **Late reviews come from veterans, and they are read most.**
    Evidence: median hours rise from 20 to 50-70; the top three most-helpful reviews are thumbs down from
    players with 117 to 3,012 hours.
    Dominion could: watch long-time players' complaints (balance, power sales) as closely as new players'.
    Keep the late game healthy, because the longest players set what new buyers read.
    Strength: medium.
20. **Missions farmed over and over get called repetitive.**
    Evidence: repetitive 67; too little content 14.
    Dominion could: lean on runs that change, so repeating does not feel the same.
    Strength: medium.
21. **Guilds or clans are wanted in a long-running game.**
    Evidence: no guilds or clans (4).
    Dominion could: consider a light group feature if it goes live-service.
    Strength: weak.
22. **A grind is accepted when no step of it is sold back to the player.**
    Evidence: this game and Warframe are both free; "the grind is built to sell shortcuts" and "the shop
    costs far too much" are each under 0.5 per 100 in Warframe and 3.7 and 4.7 here. The cross-game page
    reads the gap as the chain of grind, roll, craft, and pay to skip, not the price.
    Dominion could: if it ever sells anything next to a grind, keep the grind itself out of the shop: no
    paid timers, no paid rerolls, no paid drop boosts.
    Strength: strong for a free game; the page warns it may not carry to a game sold once.
23. **Protect players at the edges of a fight: loading, joining and reviving.**
    Evidence: a matched fight starts before you load in (2); the game times you out while loading (2); a
    revived player gets no moment of safety (1); a revive limit restarts the whole fight (1); one wipe
    ends a party.
    Dominion could: hold a joiner safe until they have loaded and can move; give a short safe moment after
    a revive; let a failed revive cost the player, not restart the whole fight for the team.
    Strength: weak (a handful of reviews), but it fits lesson 9.
24. **Never take back progress a player has earned.**
    Evidence: an upgrade sends the character back to level one (3); the farming is slowed down on purpose
    (3); locked doors send you back to old missions (1); you cannot choose your reward from the pool (1).
    Dominion could: make upgrades add to a character, never reset it; avoid hidden caps on how fast
    players can earn; where a run gives a reward pool, let players pick from it.
    Strength: weak.
25. **Third-person basics: a shoulder swap, and shooting while carrying.**
    Evidence: you cannot switch the camera to the other shoulder (1); carrying an objective stops you
    shooting (1). Both are in a third-person shooter like Dominion.
    Dominion could: let players swap shoulders; if an extraction objective must be carried, decide on
    purpose what the carrier can still do, and show it.
    Strength: weak (one review each).
26. **Give players control over the social space.**
    Evidence: the chat filter hides ordinary words (2); the chat has no separate channels (2); you cannot
    hide other players in the hub (2); subtitles cannot be turned off (2); you cannot choose a server
    region (2).
    Dominion could: offer team and general chat, a filter that can be loosened, an option to hide other
    players in any shared hub, a subtitle switch, and a region choice if matchmaking is used.
    Strength: weak.
27. **Keep damage independent of frame rate, and talk to players inside the game.**
    Evidence: damage depends on the frame rate (1, by the reviewer's account); news of an outage was only
    on social media (1); the apology gift was too small (1).
    Dominion could: test that damage over time and fire rate give the same result at any frame rate; post
    outage and patch news inside the game; make any apology gift worth the trouble.
    Strength: weak.
28. **Leave room in timed tasks.**
    Evidence: a timed mini-game is too tight (3).
    Dominion could: give timed hacks or objectives enough slack for a player under fire or on a slow
    connection.
    Strength: weak.
29. **Let stronger players lift weaker ones, but keep groups mixed on purpose.**
    Evidence: a stronger player can help you through (2, praise) against one strong player kills
    everything first (10); a reset filled groups with low-level players (1).
    Dominion could: reward a veteran for helping a newer player finish, and match players so one is not
    left with only beginners.
    Strength: weak.

### Limits
- The sample is 3.1% of English reviews, ±2.50% on whole-sample shares; the periods carry about ±3 to
  ±7.
- The launch month holds 31,555 of 51,208 English reviews (62%), and 864 of the 1,600 kept reviews are
  from the launch period. From 2024-10 on most months are read at 20 reviews (2026-10 at 6); only
  2024-07 (862), 2024-08 (139) and 2024-09 (65) are read at more.
- 199 reviews (12.4%) were edited later and are counted on the date they were made; many edits judge
  later seasons.
- Drop rates, lawsuits or fines, account losses, bans, prices and what each season added are the
  reviewers' words, not checked. Season and nerf dates are as reviewers give them; no Steam news was
  read.
- Only English was read; about 61,000 reviews in other languages were not. 16 English-tagged reviews are
  in other languages.
