- **Inventory caps:** what you can own is capped unless you pay, 21. The neutral "what not to spend on"
  advice (22) gives the same tip 8 times: *"Spend your free plat on slots"*.

### Runs, content and replay value
- **There are no runs** with a start and an end; this game is an open loop of missions.
- **It keeps pulling you back** - 221. **Interest comes and goes in waves** - 44. **Keeps playing while
  hating it** - 9. **Takes over your life** - 74.
- **Plenty of content** - 142 (peak 6.0 per 100 in 2019-21). **Too much of everything** - 11.
- **Repetitive** - 56, falling for real after 2019 as **more to do than fighting** (23) grew.
- **A mode is not worth playing** - 33; one mode has no players - 4.
- **Each update is a content island** - 5.
- **Nothing left to chase** - 27.

### Money and price
Dominion is not free-to-play, so most of this is context. It is here because it is most of what the
reviews say. Of the 564 remarks on price and the business, 348 are about how it makes money.

| Side | What players said | Times |
|---|---|---|
| **Fair** | fair price 198, everything earnable 133, a fair business model 37, looks only 35, buy to support the studio 30, premium currency earnable 22, the player market works 46 | **501** |
| **Neutral** | paying only shortens the grind 73, advice on what not to spend on 22, unclear 16 | **111** |
| **Unfair** | capped unless you pay 21, paying affects play 16, you end up paying 15, the shop charges far too much 12, feels like a cash grab 10, others | **103** |

- **Within the shop talk, praise outnumbers complaint 134 to 103.**
- **The player market makes the premium currency earnable**: one reviewer says the studio's platinum
  prices are very high but player prices are rock bottom.
- **The complaints are about slots and prices, not power**: paying affects play is 16 in 3,233 reviews.
  The four shop complaints (capped, pay affects play, you end up paying, too expensive) come to 64 - less
  than a quarter of the grind complaint. Paying to win is about a twentieth of the grind line.
- **Buying to support the studio** - 30 (0.93 per 100), the highest in the corpus.
- **The fight about money in this game is a fight about time.**

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Engineering is 3.9% of everything said (253 times). **Crashes: 4 times in 3,233 reviews.**
- Good: runs on modest hardware 29; well optimised 17; bugs rare and minor 7.
- Bad: buggy 33; will not start at all 13; needs a strong PC 12; locked out of my own account 11; bugs
  that break play 8; each update brings new bugs 8; known bugs never fixed 5; banned with no reason given
  4; huge install size 3.
- Network: lag and out-of-sync play 4; high latency 3; cannot connect 3; frequent disconnects 2; router
  blocks play 2.
- Support is heavy-handed - 9.
- 6 reviewers say the hours Steam shows are not their real time played.
- The engine is not named.

### The studio, updates and community
- **Updates keep coming** - 117 (3.6 per 100). **A patch made it better** 51; **the developers listen**
  43. Against: **a patch made it worse** 27; **a patch weakened what players liked** 14; each update
  brings new bugs 8; each update is a content island 5.
- **The community is a strength**: welcoming 122, the ninth most common thing said; the clan hall worth
  building 8.
- **Support and moderation:** heavy-handed support 9; the chat filter bans for harmless words 6; banned
  with no reason given 4.
- **Live-service problems seen only here** (now with counts): returning players get no catch-up 5, the
  early game was left behind 2, the interface keeps changing 2, the lore was rewritten 1. The story got
  worse over time 8.
- **Story is a real draw** in this game: worth following 119, against thin 22.

### What players asked for
The pages give no list of requests; these are the wishes behind the complaints, with their counts.
- **Less grind, or a faster way through it** - 264.
- **Better teaching in the game** - 267 remarks on learning it (outside research 43, poorly explained
  42, slow start 36, overwhelming 72); tips that players give each other instead.
- **No or shorter crafting timers** - 49.
- **More variety** - repetitive 56; modes worth playing 33.
- **Something to chase at the end** - 27; more than unlocks 5.
- **More slots without paying** - 21.
- **Story quests playable in co-op** - 3.
- **Gentler moderation and support** - chat filter 6, heavy-handed support 9.
- **Patches that do not weaken favourites** - 14.
- **Cross-save** - 8; **another platform** - 2.
- **A catch-up for returning players** - 5.
- **Missions that keep going when the host leaves** - 4.

### Only in this game
- **When the page was first written (2026-09-25), 156 of the 548 kinds of remark used here appeared in
  no other game**; 119 new kinds were built while reading it (2.4 per batch, against Risk of Rain 2's
  0.3), and 89 of the 156 were seen only once. The most used at that time: it takes over your life (74);
  the player market works (46); interest comes and goes in waves (44); a fair business model (37); more
  to do than fighting (23); advice on what not to spend on (22); what you can own is capped unless you pay
  (21); you end up paying sooner or later (15); you grind for gear you will never use (12); too much of
  everything (11); locked out of my own account (11); keeps playing while hating it (9); support is
  heavy-handed (9).
- **Corrected in the rebuild (2026-10-03): a fresh script run lists 91 kinds used here and in no other
  game**; games read later have since used the rest - including "it takes over your life", "interest
  comes and goes in waves" and the money remarks above. The top of the fresh list: the player market
  works 46; keeps playing while hating it 9; heavy-handed support 9; the story got worse over time 8; the
  clan hall is worth building 8; the chat filter bans for harmless words 6; each update is a content
  island 5; returning players get no catch-up 5; the others are 4 or fewer.
- **The pull read as a harm**: other games' players use addiction words as praise (Risk of Rain 2's sat
  on the praise side); Warframe's write them as a warning and still recommend it.
- **A thirteen-year live service** brings things no other game has had time to do: lore rewritten, the
  early game left behind, returning players with no catch-up, an interface that keeps changing.
- **The top complaint that does not move the thumb**: the cross-game page calls Warframe "the flat
  line" - a complaint built into the design and accepted by the people who stay - one of five shapes of
  complaint line in the corpus (the others: Helldivers 2, the relationship; Space Marine 2, the price;
  Remnant II, the reviewer; Risk of Rain 2, the owner).
- **The second highest share of edited reviews**: 581 (18.0%), behind Helldivers 2 (18.4%).
- **Two reviews were not reviews** and were left out: a nonsense poem and a pancake recipe.

### For Dominion - our reading
The findings page's own "For Dominion" line, with Rico's stop reason in mind: a grind is tolerated when
everything can be earned and the reward is visible; a tutorial that lags the systems is a complaint for
years; a co-op game praised for its company also has to work alone; movement that rewards practice is
named by name. The run, extraction and arena questions Dominion needs answered are not in this game.

1. **A long grind is accepted when everything can be earned and the pay-off is visible.** Evidence: too
   grindy 264, but 223 of them thumbs up (84%); everything can be earned 133; the grind is worth it 111;
   the thumb flat at 87.9% to 91.4% for thirteen years. Dominion could: make all between-run unlocks
   earnable in play, and show clear progress bars toward each one. Strength: **strong**.
2. **Respect the player's time, even for fans.** Evidence: a thumbs-down at 6,720 hours with 456 helpful votes
   names *"a general disrespect to the player's time"*; *"a damn second job"* (1,917 hours); the 41
   thumbs-down grind reviews have a median of 194 hours. Dominion could: cap how long any single unlock
   takes, and avoid farming the same mission over and over for one part. Strength: **strong**.
3. **Teach the game inside the game; a tutorial that lags the systems is a complaint for years.**
   Evidence: 267 remarks on learning it; overwhelming 72, outside research 43, poorly explained 42, slow
   start 36; peak in 2016-18; players do the teaching instead. Dominion could: explain each system when it
   first appears, in plain words, and test with new players at every big update. Strength: **strong**.
4. **Avoid real-world timers.** Evidence: 49 complaints (12 hours to 3 days) against 3 who like them.
   Dominion could: tie unlocks to play, not to clock time. Strength: **medium**.
5. **Random drops need a limit on bad luck.** Evidence: the part you need may never drop 39; boosters do
   not change the odds; gear you will never use 12. Dominion could: add bad-luck protection or a way to
   craft or buy the missing part. Strength: **medium**.
6. **Give the end game something to chase besides more unlocks.** Evidence: nothing left to chase 27; the
   end game is only more unlocks 5; the end game demands the best-known build 2. Dominion could: plan
   harder run types, high-stakes extraction or rotating challenges for players who have unlocked most
   things, with more than one build that can clear them. Strength: **medium**.
7. **Movement that rewards practice gets praised by name.** Evidence: 69 times, 17 naming parkour or
   bullet jumping; movement responsive 14 against unreliable 4; 2 say the levels do not suit the
   movement. This is a third-person game, like Dominion. Dominion could: give its movement a skill
   ceiling (chained slides, jumps, dodges), and build arenas that reward it. Strength: **medium to
   strong**.
8. **Combat weight and feeling strong are core praise.** Evidence: combat hits hard 113; feeling
   overpowered 34; each frame plays its own way 53. Dominion could: give each class a clearly different
   way to play and strong hit feedback. Strength: **medium**.
9. **A co-op game praised for its company still has to work alone.** Evidence: much better with friends
   114; works solo 42 against too hard alone 11 and needs a group 9; story quests solo-only drew 3
   complaints. Dominion could: let every mode be played solo or in a group, and never lock story to one.
   Strength: **medium**.
10. **A welcoming community can be designed for.** Evidence: welcoming community 122 (ninth most common);
    teaching done by other players; against: new players need carrying 12, a stronger player kills
    everything first 3, veterans will not help 3. Dominion could: reward veterans for helping newcomers
    (bonus loot for carrying a new player), and stop very strong players from emptying a newcomer's run.
    Strength: **medium**.
11. **If anything is sold, sell time or looks, never power.** Evidence: paying only shortens the grind
    73; looks only 35; paying affects play just 16; buying to support the studio 30, the highest in the
    corpus; money praise outnumbers money complaints 134 to 103. Dominion is not free-to-play, but any
    cosmetic store or DLC should follow this. Strength: **medium** (different price model).
12. **Do not cap what players can own behind payment.** Evidence: capped unless you pay 21; the most
    repeated spending tip is *"Spend your free plat on slots"* (8 times). Dominion could: give a generous
    stash and loadout space from the start. Strength: **weak to medium**.
13. **The pull is praised, but it can be read as harm.** Evidence: keeps pulling you back 221; takes over
    your life 74 (60 of 73 still thumbs up); keeps playing while hating it 9; interest comes in waves 44.
    Dominion could: make runs with natural stopping points, and welcome back players who take a break.
    Strength: **medium**.
14. **Bring returning players back up to speed.** Evidence: returning players get no catch-up 5; the
    early game was left behind 2; the interface keeps changing 2 (counts from the rebuild's script run;
    the old entry had none). Dominion could: plan a short "what changed" catch-up for players who return
    after a season or a big patch. Strength: **weak**.
15. **Variety beyond fighting cuts "repetitive".** Evidence: repetitive fell from 3.3 to 0.4-1.0 per 100
    after 2019, while "more to do than fighting" (23) grew. Dominion could: mix objectives inside runs
    (carry, defend, hack, escort) beside the fights. Strength: **medium**.
16. **Every mode must be worth playing - and a small player base should not be split across too many.**
    Evidence: a mode is not worth playing 33 (peak 2.2 per 100 in 2016-18); one mode has no players 4,
    even in a game whose population is called healthy (5). Dominion could: ship fewer modes, each one
    tested for fun, and keep public matchmaking in as few queues as possible. Strength: **medium**.
17. **Too many systems works against the game.** Evidence: too much of everything 11; overwhelming at
    first 72. Dominion could: keep the number of currencies and systems small. Strength: **weak**.
18. **Steady updates help; patches that weaken favourites hurt.** Evidence: updates keep coming 117;
    developers listen 43; a patch made it better 51 against made it worse 27 and weakened favourites 14;
    each update brings new bugs 8; known bugs never fixed 5. Dominion could: when a weapon or build must
    be weakened, explain why and offer something back, and re-test old features with every patch.
    Strength: **medium**.
19. **A good-looking game ages.** Evidence: "it looks great" fell from 5.9 to 0.9 per 100, six-fold, far
    more than the fall in review length. Dominion could: plan visual refreshes for a long life, and lean
    on art direction more than raw detail. Strength: **weak**.
20. **Story can be a draw even in a looter, but it must hold up over time.** Evidence: worth following
    119 against thin 22; the story got worse over time 8; the lore was rewritten 1. Dominion could: tell
    its story between runs, not by forcing solo story missions, and plan the long arc before changing
    what is already told. Strength: **weak to medium** (a looter with a long-told story; Dominion's story
    needs are not tested here).
21. **Solid tech is noticed by its absence of complaints.** Evidence: crashes 4 in 3,233; runs on modest
    hardware 29 and well optimised 17, against needs a strong PC 12; but will not start 13 and locked out
    of account 11. Dominion could: keep minimum specs modest and make sign-in and account recovery simple.
    Strength: **weak to medium**.
22. **Moderation that is too strict costs goodwill.** Evidence: the chat filter bans for harmless words 6;
    heavy-handed support 9; banned with no reason given 4. Dominion could: if it has chat, filter rather
    than ban, say why when anyone is banned, and give a human appeal. Strength: **weak**.
23. **Trading between players can make rare things reachable.** Evidence: the player market works 46,
    works badly 6, scammers 1. Dominion could: consider letting teammates trade or hand over drops inside
    a run. Strength: **weak** (a free-to-play market is not Dominion's model).
24. **On a listen server, the host leaving must not end everyone's run.** Evidence: the host leaving ends
    everyone's run 4; peer to peer rather than dedicated servers, so quality depends on whoever hosts, 3;
    the reviewer's router blocks hosting or joining 2; cannot connect 3; frequent disconnects 2. These
    are small counts, but this is the one game in this group hosted the way Dominion is (by the
    reviewers' account). Dominion could: move hosting to another player when the host drops (host
    migration), or save the run so the group can resume it; route connections through a relay so a home
    router cannot block play; and show each player's connection to the host. Strength: **weak** by count,
    high in relevance to Dominion's listen server. (Gunfire Reborn's entry has the same lesson from the
    other side: a dropped player who cannot get back in.)
25. **Harder settings should change how enemies play, not just the numbers.** Evidence: harder settings
    only change the numbers 3; one-hit kills 4; too easy 8; poor enemy behaviour 3; bosses are a chore 2.
    Dominion could: add new enemy behaviours, mixes or arena rules at higher difficulties, rather than
    only more health and damage. Strength: **weak**.
26. **A shared home for a group is a draw.** Evidence: the clan hall worth building 8 (only in this game).
    Dominion could: consider a shared base or hub the four-player group builds up together between
    runs. Strength: **weak**.
27. **Updates should connect to the core loop, not sit apart.** Evidence: each update is a content island,
    emptied and then left, 5 (only in this game). Dominion could: make new content feed into the main runs
    and extraction loop, so old areas stay worth visiting. Strength: **weak**.
28. **Good default controls matter, even when rebinding exists.** Evidence: controls responsive and clear
    14; but the default layout is awkward 5, controls take hours to get used to 3, rebinding broke the
    controls 1. Dominion could: test the default layout with new players and keep rebinding robust.
    Strength: **weak**.
29. **If Dominion ships on more than one platform, carry progress across.** Evidence: cross-save works 9
    against no cross-save 8; another platform asked for 2. Dominion could: plan cross-save from the start
    if it goes to more platforms. Strength: **weak**.
30. **When tracking Dominion's reviews over years, allow for shorter reviews.** Evidence: the mean review
    fell from 67 to 28 words and the share of 15-word reviews rose from 51% to 69%, so most praise rates
    fell with them; only falls far bigger than that (repetitive, looks great) are real. The cross-game
    page names this as a reading rule. Dominion could: compare a complaint with others in the same period,
    not with itself years earlier. Strength: **medium** (a method lesson, well supported here).

### Limits
- A 1.07% sample; whole-sample shares carry ±2.16%; each period about ±3.7 to ±4.5; single months are
  direction only (weights up to ×591 in 2018-11, where 20 of 11,813 reviews were read).
- The launch is barely covered: 5 reviews from 2013-03, out of 460.
- Reviews shrank from 67 words to 28 on average, so most falls in per-100 rates measure that, not opinion.
  Only falls much bigger than that (repetitive, looks great) are read as real.
- 581 reviews (18.0%) were edited later and are counted on the day they were written; how to treat edits
  is open with Rico.
- Read in two sessions, before and after Rico's stop (batches 1-14, 700 reviews from 2013-03 to 2016-02;
  then batches 15-64). "Best of its kind" vanishing after 2018 may be a change in filing, not in players.
- No patch notes were pulled; update names and dates, prices, timer lengths, booster rules, bans and the
  peer-to-peer hosting come from reviews.
- Hours are those shown on the review, which may be total hours at the time of the pull; 6 reviewers say
  the hours shown are wrong.
- 1,382 remarks (21.2%) said nothing specific.
- About 381,000 reviews in other languages were not read; 41 English-tagged reviews carry the
  other-language mark.
- **Corrected in the rebuild:** the old entry said the pages say nothing about hosting; the rebuilt page
  counts the host leaving ending everyone's run (4) and peer-to-peer play (3). The old entry gave no
  counts for the live-service problems seen only here; the rebuilt page gives them (5, 2, 2, 1). The
  count of kinds of remark found only in this game was 156 when first written and is 91 on the fresh run
  (2026-10-03); "takes over your life" and the money remarks are no longer only in this game.
- Many of the rows added in this pass (hosting, controls, enemies, tech) come from a count of remarks by
  kind across the summary files, not from the ranked tables, and most are small (2 to 17).
- The game has no runs, no extraction and no arenas, so it cannot answer the questions Dominion most
  needs answered. It is free-to-play, unlike Dominion.

---

## Risk of Rain 2

**Read:** 1,885 of 239,309 English reviews (0.79%), ±2.64%, written 2019-03 to 2026-09, 91 months
(the sampling grid's 239,309 matches Steam's own English count of 239,276 to within 33; 9 of the 1,885
were not reviews of the game, so 1,876 are kept on today's script run). **95.6% thumbs up** in the
sample, 1,802 of 1,885 (1,793 of 1,876 on today's script run, also 95.6%; Steam, all languages: 93.8%,
*Very Positive*, 352,630 reviews).
**Pages:** `findings/risk-of-rain-2-english.md`, `findings/risk-of-rain-2.md`, `findings/cross-game.md`
section 19 (with side-by-side notes in sections 24 and 25).

**What it is:** A third-person co-op roguelite shooter from Hopoo Games, published by Gearbox. Early
access from 2019-03 (opened 2019-03-28), 1.0 in 2020-08. The 3D sequel to *Risk of Rain* (2013).
Each run starts weak; you pick up items (some from gambling shrines) until your character is far too
strong, while a clock makes the enemies harder the longer you take; then you die and start again.
Survivors (characters) are unlocked over time. Co-op is hosted on a player's machine (the pages show
this through the disconnect and host-leaving complaints). Paid game with three paid expansions the
reviews name: *Survivors of the Void* (2022-03), *Seekers of the Storm* (2024-08) and *Alloyed
Collective* (2025-11) - dates from reviews and review volume, not patch notes. Gearbox bought the
series from Hopoo in 2022 (the reviews date it). Mods are common. The pages do not give the largest
group size or the engine.

**How close to Dominion:** Close in shape: third-person, co-op, sci-fi, runs that build power and
end, a player hosts the game. It differs in having no extraction (the run ends when you die or
win). It is the best example in the corpus of a run loop players cannot put down.

### The short version (plain words)
The most-recommended game in the queue (95.6% up), and its fans mostly do not say why - 47% of what
they write says nothing specific, and four reviews in ten told the studio nothing at all. When they do
say why, it is four things: it pulls you back like an addiction (172 times), it is better with friends
(88), each run turns you into a god (73), and the music (71). Complaints are rare - none reaches 1 per
100 reviews - and fell for five years. Then in 2024, after the new owner's expansion launched broken
(reviewers say), complaints doubled and the thumb fell from about 98% to about 93%, the biggest drop
the game ever had, with nothing about the design changed. On the newer count it has the lowest
complaint rate (18.0 per 100) of the ten games in the latest cross-game table.

### What players praised, most to least
Counts are times it was said in 1,885 reviews; "per 100" means times per 100 reviews. Where the page
gives it, the rate is shown across five periods: early access (2019-03 to 2020-07), 1.0 (2020-08 to
2021-12), 2022-23, 2024, and 2025-26.
1. **It keeps pulling you back** - 172 times, 9.1 per 100, the highest in the corpus (11.5, 10.6, 7.8,
   10.4, 6.5). Addiction words: *crack* (six times), *drugs*, *doom scrolling*, *risk of addiction*,
   *one more loop*, *how do I have 40 hours on this game*, and two jokes about leaving family for the
   game. Thumb up every time but one. (Warframe's reviewers use the same kind of words as a warning;
   here they are praise.)
2. **Much better with friends** - 88 times, 4.7 per 100 (7.7, 6.1, 3.1, 4.1, 2.9).
3. **The run makes you a god** - 73 times, 3.9 per 100; 35 reviews use the word *god*. *"start slow,
   gain power, become god, die anyway, do it all again"*; *"everything dies without you inputting any
   movement, attacks or abilities - that's Risk of Rain at its peak."* Peaks at 6.9 per 100 after 1.0,
   1.7 by 2025-26. Only Space Marine 2 (4.5) has more of this praise per 100 on the cross-game count
   (this game 3.8 there; Crab Champions 2.5).
4. **The music fits** - 71 times, 3.8 per 100. The only praise in the corpus that rises for seven
   years (3.3, 2.9, 2.2, 5.0, 6.0 per 100). The composer, Chris Christodoulou, is named more than any
   developer in the corpus; *"and his music was electric"* is quoted eleven times in 2025-26.
5. **A true sequel to the first game** - 44 times (8.5 per 100 in early access, 2.7 after 1.0, 0.4 or
   less since 2022, 0.0 by 2025-26).
6. **Hard in a good way** - 44 times, 2.3 per 100 (3.2 after 1.0, 0.8 in 2024).
7. **Unlocking things feels satisfying** - 39 times, 2.1 per 100 (3.5 after 1.0).
8. **Each survivor plays its own way** - 37 times, 2.0 per 100. Another 60 reviews just name a
   favourite survivor (*Acrid*, *Loader*, *Huntress*, *Railgunner*, *MUL-T*) and say nothing else.
9. **It works alone** - 34 times, 1.8 per 100 (3.0 in early access, 1.0 in 2025-26).
10. **Mods extend the game** - 31 times, 1.6 per 100 (2.7 in 2022-23).
11. **The price is fair** - 30 times, 1.6 per 100 (against 2 who say it is too high; 2.7 in early
    access, 0.5 in 2025-26).
12. **Builds are deep and varied** - 29 times, 1.5 per 100.
13. **The randomness keeps it fresh** - 26 times, 1.4 per 100. Another 24 name a gamble and say
    nothing (*Shrine of Chance* five times, *let's go gambling*, *just one more lunar roll*).
14. **The DLC is fair** - 26 times, 1.4 per 100 (0.0 in early access, 2.5 in 2024, 2.2 in 2025-26).
    *Alloyed Collective* got three "worth it" and no thumbs-down.
15. **Good value while unfinished** - 18 times; 4.9 per 100 in early access, zero after 1.0.
16. **It looks great** - 16 times. **A patch fixed what mattered** - 16 times (2.2 per 100 in early
    access). **Clear difficulty steps** - 15 times (1.4 in 2022-23).
17. **Deep once learned** - 13 times. **The world is worth exploring** - 12 times (the lore is never
    argued with).
18. **The controls are tight and clear** - 11 times, 2.5 per 100 in early access, then zero.
19. **Updates arrive regularly** - 11 times.
20. **The art direction looks well done** - 10 times. **Plenty to do** - 9 times.
21. **Random rule changes to a run are welcome** - 9 times, 0.5 per 100.
22. **The new owner is accepted / has earned trust** - 6 times. *"Gearbox had me scared with SOTS,
    but they fixed it and made the practically bugless Alloyed Collective; they've earned my trust."*
    A patch made it better - 4 times.
23. **The timer is the thrill** - 4 times here (and 3 in Rogue Core). **The action never stops** - 3
    times.
24. One each: a weaker add-on is still welcome; a player defends the starter survivor others call weak.

The four top praises are 404 of the 1,953 good remarks. Also: 948 thumbs-up reviews say nothing but
*good*, *peak*, *yes* or a weather joke (946 on today's script run); 34 copy the same meme text. The
bare thumbs-up grows from 24% of remarks in early access to 42% in 2025-26. Counted per 100 reviews,
the bare thumbs up is 50.3 here and 61.0 in Crab Champions - the two highest in the corpus. 30 raise
the difficulty and say nothing (*Risk of Pain*); 44 name an enemy and say nothing.

### What players complained about, most to least
299 complaint remarks in 1,885 reviews, **173 of them (57.9%) from people who recommend the game.**
The five numbers in brackets are per 100 in early access, 1.0, 2022-23, 2024 and 2025-26.
1. **Thumbs down with nothing said** - 22 times (*no*, *bad*, *go away*; 0.5, 0.5, 1.0, 2.5, 1.7);
   doubles in 2024.
2. **The effects block your view** - 14 times (12 on today's script run; 0.8, 0.3, 0.6, 2.1, 0.5).
   Written as a joke 12 times in 14 (*vision is overrated anyway*; *I don't know what killed me, would
   recommend*; *what is happening on my screen? 10/10*), and thumbs up on 12 of 14. Twice a real
   complaint: *chaos for the sake of chaos* (thumbs down at 4 hours); one says the screen becomes too
   much to bear after a point.
3. **Teammates take the items** - 12 times, from people who stayed (11 of 12 thumbs up; 1.1, 0.5, 0.4,
   0.4, 0.7). *"I really wish my teammates would stop taking all the items"*; *"NO ITEM SHARING IN
   MULTIPLAYER... many mods fix this."* As old as the game, the only complaint early access made more
   than later years, and the one design complaint the owner did not touch.
4. **One kind of enemy is hated; remove it** - 12 on today's script run, which ranks it third (a mode
   built after the page was written). 14 reviews name a hated enemy (blind pests, wisps, Elder
   Lemurians, Brass Contraptions); only one says why (*one-shot by Brass Contraptions*).
5. **The new owner puts me off** - 11 times (0.0, 0.0, 0.6, 1.7, 1.0). *"For as long as Gearbox has the
   IP I could never recommend this."* Five more hold a grudge under a thumbs-up; today's script run
   adds 4 who say they dislike the owner and 1 who says the new owner cares less than the makers.
6. **Frequent disconnects** - 10 times (0.5, 0.0, 0.2, 1.2, 1.0), mostly since 2024.
7. **A patch made it worse** - 10 times (11 on today's script run; 0.0, 0.8, 0.4, 1.7, 0.2). *"I don't
   even have the DLC and they've introduced bugs from it."*
8. **Repetitive** - 9 times.
9. **Unstable frame rate** - 8 times (1.1 per 100 after 1.0).
10. **One-hit kills** - 7 times (8 on today's script run; rising to 1.0 per 100 in 2025-26).
11. **Hard to find the way onward (the teleporter)** - 6 times.
12. **Buy it on sale only** - 6 (0.8 in 2024). **The item you need may never drop** - 6 (three of the
    six are about unlocks gated on a chance spawn, not items; 0.3 per 100, against 2.0 in Escape from
    Duckov). **Too easy** - 6 (all thumbs-down or *2 ez*; rising to 1.0 per 100 in 2025-26). **The DLC
    is not worth it** - 6, all 2024-26 (1.2 per 100 in 2024).
13. **Luck decides the outcome** - 5. **Grindy** - 5.
14. **Cannot save and come back later** - 4 (0.8 per 100 in early access). **Buggy** - 4. **The music
    is annoying** - 4. **A crash loses the run** - 4. **Nothing left to chase** - 4.
15. **Poorly explained; no tutorial** - 4 (all in 2025-26, 1.0 per 100). One reviewer holds three
    late complaints at once: *"Too easy, until those moments where it isn't and you die in a second...
    also the tutorial doesn't work, or maybe even exist."* Today's script run adds 4 who say you have
    to look things up outside the game.
16. **Too little content** - 4 (on today's script run).
17. **Two each:** the levels are too small; the first stages of every run are dull; the strongest
    items are locked behind hard challenges (*"so the player who needs them most can't get them"*);
    mods fixed what the patch broke; stuck with the worst starter survivor (*"Commando is extremely
    boring and the game forces you to play him for hours"*); a disconnect loses the run.
18. **One each:** the host leaving ends everyone's run; high latency; the run becomes an idle game -
    *"is someone really playing the game when there is no point to user inputs? Yet I can't stop
    playing it"* (223339415, 247 hours, thumbs down); the weaker teammate resents the power gap; the
    loot does not grow with the group; no LAN play; a skill stops working; an attack you cannot see
    coming; the boss arena can roll badly; an objective spawns inside the scenery; an update broke the
    mods; worried the content will run dry.
19. **The 2025 terms change** (see "The studio"): three reviews, two of them among the most-helped
    thumbs-down.

Also: 6 reviews have a thumb that says the opposite of the words. The pages had counted this as a
complaint; a correction (round 470) struck it out as neutral.

**All complaints together:** on the old count 299 remarks, 15.9 per 100, praise to complaint 6.5 to
1. On the newer count used across games: 338 complaint remarks, 18.0 per 100, against 1,967 praise
remarks, 104.9 per 100 - 5.8 to 1. That is the lowest complaint rate of the ten games in the latest
cross-game table (Crab Champions 18.7, Deep Rock Galactic 23.7; every other game 33.1 or more, up to
The First Descendant at 122.2). Its praise per 100 is eighth of the ten, because so few reviewers say
what they like.

**What is not here:** price (too high 2, against 30 fair), performance beyond a frame-rate line,
crashes as a pattern, cheating, matchmaking, the story, the art.

**Who leaves, and when:** the 83 thumbs-down reviewers show a median of 27 hours; the thumbs-up
reviewers 38. Over all kept reviews the median is 38 hours; 474 show 100 hours or more and 5 show
1,000 or more. Of the 83 thumbs-down, 18 name the owner, the expansion or the terms; the rest say
*no*, *bad*, *2 ez*, *the game is dead*.

### How it changed over time
| Period | Reviews read | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 | Mean hours |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Early access (2019-03 to 2020-07) | 364 | 96.2% | 119.8 | 22.5 | 63 |
| 1.0 (2020-08 to 2021-12) | 376 | 98.4% | 115.7 | 13.0 | 78 |
| 2022 to 2023 | 489 | 96.1% | 96.7 | 9.2 | 92 |
| 2024 (Seekers of the Storm) | 241 | 92.9% | 90.0 | 18.3 | 100 |
| 2025-01 to 2026-09 | 415 | 93.5% | 94.5 | 19.0 | 77 |

- With the wordless reviews left out (today's script run, kept reviews only), praise and complaints per
  100 are: early access 79.6 and 24.0; 1.0 64.1 and 14.5; 2022-23 44.1 and 10.7; 2024 47.5 and 19.2;
  2025-26 39.7 and 18.6. Each period carries about ±5 to ±6.
- **No launch spike and no cliff.** 1,000 to 9,000 reviews a month for seven and a half years. The
  early-access year (2019-03 to 2020-02) holds 44,658 reviews (18.7% of all) and the 1.0 year (2020-08
  to 2021-07) 44,698 (another 18.7%). The biggest months are events: 2020-11 (9,159, anniversary update
  and autumn sale), 2020-08 (8,867, 1.0), 2020-04 (7,132, the Artifacts update and the first lockdown
  month), 2019-11 (6,992), and the expansions 2022-03 (6,863), 2024-08 (4,906), 2025-11 (6,003).
  Between events the floor is 1,200 to 3,000 a month and has not fallen in seven years (2026-08 holds
  1,043).
- **Complaints fell for five years** as early-access bugs and missing content were fixed, **then
  doubled in 2024**: the owner (1.7 per 100), disconnects (1.2), patch made it worse (1.7), DLC not
  worth it (1.2) - all zero before 2022. 2024-08 and 2024-09 are the two lowest months in 91 (76.2%
  and 80.0% up). Steam's 93.8% is a seven-year average that hides this two-point step.
- **The repair did not bring the complaint line down.** In 2025-26 fixes outnumber "made it worse" 2
  to 1 on tiny numbers, the thumb is 93-96% by month, but complaints stay at 19 per 100. The page's
  reading: those put off did not come back to say so.
- **The cross-game page sets this beside three other games:** a complaint line rose with the
  relationship in Helldivers 2, the price in Space Marine 2, the reviewer in Remnant II, and the owner
  here - *"a rising complaint line has four causes and only one of them is the game."*
- **Early access was the best-explained period.** Good value while unfinished 4.9 per 100, waiting for
  promised content 4.1, fixed what mattered 2.2, tight controls 2.5 - all near zero after 1.0.
- **What rose:** the music (3.3 to 6.0), DLC fair (0 to 2.2), the owner (0 to 1.0 via 1.7),
  disconnects (0 to 1.0), too easy / one-hit kills / no tutorial (each 0 to 1.0), other languages under
  the English tag (0.3 to 1.4), the bare thumbs-up (24% to 42% of remarks).
- **What fell:** sequel praise (8.5 to 0), better with friends (7.7 to 2.9), pulls you back (11.5 to
  6.5), the god run (6.9 to 1.7), hard in a good way (3.2 to 1.4), works alone (3.0 to 1.0), price
  fair (2.7 to 0.5), explained by naming other games (2.5 to 0). The page reads this as the later
  reviewer describing less, not the game changing. Mean hours played rose from 63 in early access to
  100 in 2024.
- Two 2024-10 Crab Champions reviews say they came over because this game got worse for them; 43 Crab
  Champions reviews name Risk of Rain.

### Co-op and online play
- Better with friends 88; works alone 34.
- **Items go to whoever picks them up, and teammates take things** (the page calls it per-player loot
  in a shared arena) - 12 complaints over seven years; mods fix it. One review also says the loot does
  not grow with the group.
- **Player hosting shows its cost late:** frequent disconnects 10 (mostly since 2024), a disconnect
  loses the run 2, the host leaving ends everyone's run 1, high latency 1, a crash loses the run 4.
- **Cannot save and come back** - 4.
- One review says the weaker teammate resents the power gap; one asks for LAN play (one sighting each).
- Friendly fire: one line in 1,885. Crossplay: one Linux crossplay bug.
- Matchmaking, cheating and strangers: nothing said on the pages.

### Combat, movement and feel
- The god run 73: weak at the start, unstoppable by the end. Combat feel as a subject: 79 remarks.
- Tight, clear controls 11 (early access, then zero). Nothing else on speed, sprint, jump, controllers
  or rebinding.
- **Screen clutter:** the effects block your view 14 (peak 2.1 per 100 in the expansion year); one
  review asks for an epilepsy warning (152614012: *"there should definitely be an epilepsy warning
  somewhere in the game"*).
- When power outgrows the game, play stops needing input (1 review, 247 hours).
- One review each: an attack you cannot see coming; a skill stops working.
- Stuns and loss of control: nothing said on the pages.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- Hard in a good way 44; too easy 6; clear difficulty steps 15; 30 raise difficulty and say nothing
  (*Risk of Pain*, *let me beat a Monsoon run on Merc*). Difficulty as a subject: 111 remarks.
- One-hit kills 7. 44 reviews name an enemy and say nothing; 14 name a hated enemy and only one says
  why. Enemies as a subject: 72 remarks.
- **The clock as difficulty:** the late reviewer says both *too easy* and *you die in a second* - the
  timer's climb read as a spike by someone who has not learned it. 4 say the clock is the thrill.
- Random rule changes to a run are welcome: 9.
- The first stages of each run are dull - 2 (plus 1 in another game). One: the boss arena can roll
  badly.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- Satisfying unlocks 39; deep builds 29; deep once learned 13. Grindy 5; nothing left to chase 4.
  Progression as a subject: 141 remarks.
- The strongest options are locked behind hard challenges - 2.
- Stuck with the worst starter survivor - 2 (and one review defends him).
- Randomness: 62 remarks. Keeps it fresh 26; a gamble named 24; never drops 6; luck decides 5. **Three
  of the six "never drops" lines are about unlocks**: an achievement gated on a key that spawns by
  chance, and a survivor whose unlock needs luck on three things plus the final boss, in one chance.
  The page: the run is a slot machine the reviewers know is a slot machine.
- Item complaints are tiny: some items are trash (1), stacking falls off (1).

### Runs, content and replay value
- Pulls you back 172 - the strongest in the corpus. Replayability as a subject: 176 remarks.
- Repetitive 9. Levels too small 2. The early stages of each run are dull 2. Too little content 4
  (script run). One worries the content will run dry.
- Hard to find the way onward 6.
- Cannot save mid-run 4; a crash or disconnect loses the run.
- Mods extend the game 31.

### Money and price
- Price fair 30 against too high 2; buy on sale only 6.
- DLC fair 26 (much of it the first expansion) against DLC not worth it 6 (all about the 2024
  expansion, which both sides say shipped broken).
- One review each: the DLC price as a caveat; the DLC does not fit the theme; buying copies for
  friends; a weaker add-on is still welcome.
- Good value while unfinished 18 (early access).

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Tech is 3.1% of all remarks, the lowest of any large game.
- Unstable frame rate 8; buggy 4; a crash loses the run 4; disconnects 10.
- Platform lines only from 2025: Steam Deck broken (1), Mac through a compatibility layer (1), a
  Linux crossplay bug (1). One 16 GB RAM line; one Mac request; one objective that spawns inside the
  scenery.

### The studio, updates and community
- **The owner change.** Gearbox is named in 35 reviews. First line on its own date: 2023-01-07, *"The
  devs sold out to Gearbox, the game has no future."* (130388821). Owner puts me off 11; owner accepted
  6; grudge under a thumbs-up 5 (*"top 3 favourite games of all time despite gearbox existing"*).
  Reviewers carry blame from the publisher's other games (*Aliens: Colonial Marines*, *Borderlands*)
  and jab its chief executive. 15 of the 35 Gearbox lines are 2024-26 edits sitting on 2019-23 dates.
- **The 2024 expansion:** *"unplayable and basically ruined"* (2024-08-31); *"You need a roadmap for bug
  fixes because you did a quick cash grab on a DLC that was nowhere near ready"* (189635801, 6 helpful).
  Then fixes: *"was good, then bad, now it's good again"*; *"the game is now in good hands, and I'm
  willing to wait and see"* (181186472). The two sides agree on the facts (it shipped broken, the fixes
  came); they argue about whether the repair counts.
- **The 2025 terms change (EULA), in three steps, as reviewers tell it:** *"Nice EULA change, tracking
  user information such as passwords, addresses, phone numbers, and IP addresses"* (193373656,
  2025-04-22, 10 helpful); *"Added EULA that I have no intent of accepting after I bought the game. Now
  I cannot play the game nor refund it"* (196109434, 2025-05-31, 9 helpful); then *"Changing my review
  as they seem to have removed/not acted on their new EULA"* (196660257, edited 2026-07). The wall went
  up, and came down. The terms themselves were not checked.
- The most-helpful review in the sample is a joke (324 votes, thumbs up, 2026-02).
- Updates regular 11; fixed what mattered 16; made it better 4; made it worse 10.
- Mods extend the game 31; mods fixed what the patch broke 2; one update broke the mods.
- The music and composer are the community's quotable thing; a copied meme in 34 reviews.
- Story: 23 remarks in 1,885 (0.9% of everything, the lowest in the corpus), 12 of them "worth
  exploring", none against.

### What players asked for
- Item sharing in multiplayer (in the 12 "teammates take the items" complaints).
- A tutorial and explanations (4, all 2025-26; plus 4 who had to look things up outside the game).
- Remove hated enemies (14 name one).
- An epilepsy warning (1).
- Save and come back mid-run (4).
- Bigger levels with more to find (2).
- A different starter survivor (2).
- A Mac version (1). LAN play (1).
- Five prefer the first, 2D game or name what it had (*"the first game had Chef"*).

### Only in this game
- **The music rising for seven years** - the only praise line in the corpus that does.
- **The new owner accepted** (built here) and **a terms wall added after purchase** (its third sighting
  here).
- **The strongest options locked behind challenges**, **stuck with the worst starter**, **the early
  stages of every run are dull**, **the levels are too small**, **the host leaving ends everyone's
  run**, **a crash loses the run**, **mods fixed what the patch broke** - built during this game's read
  (8 of the 240 kinds of remark used here appear in no other game; 16 of 286 on today's script run).
- **Today's script run also finds, here only:** dislikes the owner (4), the new owner cares less than
  the makers, a weaker add-on still welcome, worried it will run dry, defends a character others call
  weak, the boss arena can roll badly, the loot does not grow with the group, an objective spawns in
  the scenery, a skill stops working, no LAN play, an update broke the mods, weaker teammates resent
  your power (one each).
- **The timer as the thrill** and **the action never stops** - praise twins built here.
- **Addiction words as praise**, at the highest rate in the corpus.
- **The most said with the least:** 1.42 remarks per review, the lowest in the queue; the "said
  nothing specific" share (39.5% of everything) is the highest in the corpus.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Make the run loop short and "one more go".**
   Evidence: pulls you back 172 (9.1 per 100, the corpus high); *one more loop*.
   Dominion could: keep runs short enough to start another, put the restart one button away, and end
   each run with a hook (a new unlock, a near-miss).
   Strength: **strong**.
2. **Let players go from weak to god inside one run - but keep them playing.**
   Evidence: the god run 73; 35 use the word *god*; one 247-hour reviewer says it turns into an idle
   game.
   Dominion could: let power snowball within a run, but keep the end of a run (extraction, the arena
   finale) asking for skill - rising threats, a timer, a final fight that power alone does not win.
   Strength: **strong**.
3. **Cap screen clutter and add an effects option and a seizure warning.**
   Evidence: effects block your view 14 (the second complaint; 2.1 per 100 in the expansion year); two
   real complaints; one asks for an epilepsy warning.
   Dominion could: let players turn down teammates' effects, keep enemy attacks readable above the
   clutter, ship a photosensitivity warning.
   Strength: **medium**.
4. **Decide loot sharing on purpose; do not let a fast teammate take everything.**
   Evidence: teammates take the items 12, steady for seven years, fixed by mods; one says the loot
   does not grow with the group.
   Dominion could: give each player their own drops, or show who claimed what, or split extraction
   loot fairly, and scale drops with the number of players.
   Strength: **medium** (small count, but steady, and it matters more with extraction loot).
5. **On a player-hosted game, protect the run from drops.**
   Evidence: disconnects 10 (mostly since 2024), a disconnect loses the run 2, the host leaving ends
   everyone's run 1, a crash loses the run 4, cannot save and come back 4.
   Dominion could: let a dropped player rejoin a running game; consider host migration or at least save
   each player's run state when the host leaves; allow suspend-and-resume.
   Strength: **medium** (small counts, but exactly Dominion's listen-server risk).
6. **Music is worth real money.**
   Evidence: the music 71, the only praise in the corpus that keeps rising; the composer named more
   than any developer.
   Dominion could: hire a strong composer, build tracks that rise with the run, make them quotable.
   Strength: **strong**.
7. **Never change the deal after people bought it.**
   Evidence: the terms wall (*"now I cannot play the game nor refund it"*, 9 helpful) and the data
   complaint (10 helpful) were two of the most-helped thumbs-down; the owner's name cost two points.
   Dominion could: keep terms, data use and accounts the same as on day one; do not add a sign-in or
   consent screen later.
   Strength: **medium**.
8. **Ship expansions finished; a broken one costs more than it earns.**
   Evidence: 2024 complaints doubled (9.2 to 18.3 per 100); patch made it worse 1.7 per 100; DLC not
   worth it 6 against DLC fair 26; the line never came back down. Two reviews of a rival runs game say
   they moved over because this one got worse.
   Dominion could: test DLC and big updates on the base game too (one reviewer got bugs without the
   DLC); delay rather than ship broken.
   Strength: **medium**.
9. **Teach new players, and put a good character first.**
   Evidence: no tutorial 4, too easy 6, one-hit kills 7 - all rising together in 2025-26; 4 more had to
   look things up outside the game; stuck with the worst starter 2; the early stages dull 2; the way
   onward hard to find 6.
   Dominion could: add a short tutorial run, make the first class fun, mark the exit or objective
   clearly, and make the first minutes of a run interesting.
   Strength: **medium**.
10. **Support mods.**
    Evidence: mods extend the game 31; mods fixed what a patch broke 2; the item-sharing fix is a mod;
    one update broke the mods.
    Dominion could: plan for mod support or at least private-match options, and do not break mods
    with every patch.
    Strength: **medium**.
11. **Let randomness feel like a fun gamble, but never gate unlocks on luck.**
    Evidence: randomness keeps it fresh 26, gambles named 24; but never drops 6 (three of them unlocks
    gated on chance), luck decides 5, the strongest items locked behind hard challenges 2.
    Dominion could: put gambling shrines in runs for items, but give unlocks a sure path.
    Strength: **medium**.
12. **A clock that raises the danger can be the thrill.**
    Evidence: the timer is the thrill 4 (plus 3 in Rogue Core, where the clock is also the top
    argument).
    Dominion could: test a rising-threat clock in runs, with clear warnings as it climbs.
    Strength: **weak**.
13. **Early access works when it is honest and fast.**
    Evidence: good value while unfinished 4.9 per 100 in early access; fixed what mattered 2.2; tight
    controls 2.5; complaints fell 22.5 to 9.2 per 100 over five years.
    Dominion could: if it goes into early access, sell it as unfinished, fix the top issues fast, and
    say what is coming.
    Strength: **medium**.
14. **Characters are identity; make each one play differently.**
    Evidence: each survivor plays its own way 37; 60 just name a favourite.
    Dominion could: give each class a distinct feel and a fan-favourite personality.
    Strength: **medium**.
15. **If Dominion is ever sold or partnered, the partner's name becomes part of the review.**
    Evidence: the owner change was the biggest event in this game's score.
    Strength: **weak** for design; noted for business.
16. **Watch the text, not just the score - and ask testers direct questions.**
    Evidence: 47% of remarks say nothing; 1.42 remarks per review; the 2024 rise shows first in
    specific complaints. The cross-game page adds: a well-liked build gives few reasons.
    Dominion could: ask playtesters set questions (what did you like, what annoyed you, what would make
    you stop) rather than waiting for them to explain.
    Strength: **medium**.
17. **Offer run modifiers players can switch on.**
    Evidence: random rule changes to a run are welcome 9; the randomness keeps it fresh 26; a gamble
    named 24.
    Dominion could: let the squad pick or roll rule changes for a run (low gravity, double enemies,
    no healing) as an optional layer on top of the difficulty steps.
    Strength: **weak** (small count).
18. **Keep every player useful when one gets far stronger.**
    Evidence: one review says the weaker teammate resents the power gap; one says the run becomes an
    idle game (247 hours); teammates take the items 12.
    Dominion could: share some of the power across the squad (team buffs, shared upgrades), and give
    each class a job that matters at any power level.
    Strength: **weak** (one sighting each, but four-player power gaps are a Dominion risk).
19. **Find out which enemy players hate, and why.**
    Evidence: 14 reviews name a hated enemy and say *remove it*; only one says why (*one-shot*); one-hit
    kills rise to 1.0 per 100 in 2025-26.
    Dominion could: in playtests, ask which enemy is the most annoying and what it does; fix the
    reason (a one-shot, an unseen attack), not the enemy's existence.
    Strength: **weak**.

### Limits
- The thinnest sample in the queue: 0.79% at ±2.64%; single months are 20 to 39 reviews and only show
  direction. Read the five periods (±5 to ±6 each).
- 1.42 remarks per review and 47% saying nothing specific: counts are built from the half who spoke.
  A mode seen six times is just six times.
- 223 reviews (11.8%, the highest share in the queue) were edited later and counted on the day first
  written. This moves the ownership story: 15 of the 35 Gearbox lines are 2024-26 edits sitting on
  2019-23 dates, so the owner complaint shows in 2022-23 (0.6 per 100) a year before its first line on
  its own date.
- The launches are barely covered: 5 reviews from the four-day early-access month, 37 from the 1.0
  month.
- No patch notes were pulled; expansion dates and the EULA story are from reviews and volume.
- English only; about 113,000 reviews in other languages were not pulled; 16 "English" reviews were in
  other languages (Portuguese, Spanish, Russian, French, Polish, Turkish or Hungarian).
- 9 files are not reviews of the game (empty, the review-box placeholder, invisible characters, a
  pasted weather forecast, a campaign slogan, picture art made of Braille characters).
- The pages give no largest group size and no engine.
- The complaint rate is 15.9 per 100 on the game's own pages and in cross-game section 19, but 18.0 in
  the later cross-game recount (after a counting fix); praise to complaint is 6.5 : 1 on its pages and
  5.8 : 1 in the recount. The recount is the one to compare across games.
- **What changed from the earlier entry:** the pages now give hours per thumb, per-period rates for
  each list, a script recount, five more praise rows (art direction, plenty, random rule changes) and
  new script-run complaints (a hated enemy type 12, too little content 4, look things up outside the
  game 4, dislike the owner 4). "The thumb says the opposite of the words" (6) was struck out as
  neutral. Frequent disconnects are not quite "all since 2024": the page shows 0.5 per 100 in early
  access and 0.2 in 2022-23 before 1.2 in 2024. The script run moves a few counts (effects 12, made it
  worse 11, one-hit kills 8; "buggy" drops out of its top 25).
- Jabs at a named executive and at other players are not repeated; the family jokes are described,
  not quoted.

---

## Remnant II

**Read:** 1,382 of 38,120 English reviews (3.63%), ±3.27%, written 2023-07 to 2026-09, 39 months
(the sampling grid's 38,120 matches Steam's own English count of 38,117 to within 3 reviews). **83.6%
thumbs up** in the sample, 1,156 of 1,382 (Steam, all languages: 82.3%, *Very Positive*, 68,559
reviews).
**Pages:** `findings/remnant-2-english.md`, `findings/remnant-2.md`, `findings/cross-game.md` section 18.

**What it is:** A third-person co-op "souls-like" shooter from Gunfire Games, published by Gearbox
(the reviews later name Arc Games). Released 2023-07-25 at full price. It is the sequel to *Remnant:
From the Ashes* (2019). Worlds are put together by the computer from set pieces, so two players may
see different areas, bosses and secrets. You pick two classes ("archetypes") at once. Three paid DLCs
are named by reviewers (*The Awakened King*, *The Forgotten Kingdom*, *The Dark Horizon*), plus a
boss rush mode, a prism system, loadout slots and a region filter that reviewers say came later (not
checked; no patch notes were pulled). Co-op is hosted by one player, who keeps the campaign; others
join as guests (the pages show this through the guest complaints). The pages do not give the largest
group size or the engine.

**How close to Dominion:** Very close on the outside: third-person, co-op, sci-fi/fantasy shooter,
and one player hosts - so its online complaints are the ones Dominion is most likely to meet. It
differs in being a long campaign with checkpoints rather than runs, having no extraction, and being
hard in the "souls" way.

### The short version (plain words)
Players accept it as a worthy sequel (139 times) and say it is much better with friends (139 times).
The number one complaint is that the best things - whole classes, dungeons, items - are hidden so well
that you need a wiki to find them (61 times, 46 of them from people who still recommend it). That
complaint rose every year, from 2.6 per 100 reviews at launch to 10.0 in 2026, while the game itself
did not change: the people writing reviews changed. Players love the builds (98) and the worlds (92),
and on PC many could not run it well without upscaling. The thumb fell from 86.5% at launch to 79.6%
in 2025 and 82.4% in 2026, while complaints per 100 reviews rose from 67 to 91. Game design is 36.6%
of everything said, the highest share of any large game read: it is reviewed as a set of systems.

### What players praised, most to least
Counts are times it was said in 1,382 reviews; "per 100" means times per 100 reviews. Where the page
gives it, the rate is shown across five periods: launch quarter (2023-07 to 09), late 2023, 2024, 2025,
2026.
1. **A worthy sequel; better than the first game** - 139 times, 10.1 per 100. *"An improvement on the
   first in every way."* Falls from 12.7 per 100 at launch to 12.1, 10.1, 6.7 and 4.7 in 2026.
2. **Much better with friends** - 139 times, 10.1 per 100. Rises from 7.3 to 10.5, 12.3, 10.8 and 12.4;
   by 2026 one review in eight says it. Only Aliens: Fireteam Elite says it more often (10.4); Deep
   Rock Galactic ties at 10.1.
3. **Builds are deep and varied** - 98 times, 7.1 per 100 (peak 9.3 in 2024). *"pair an Engineer
   turret with Hunter crit chains and suddenly you're John Wick in an interdimensional maze"*
   (201175078).
4. **The world is worth exploring** - 92 times, 6.7 per 100 (8.6 at launch, 8.3 in 2025).
5. **It keeps pulling you back** - 75 times, 5.4 per 100 (7.7 at launch, 2.9 in 2026).
6. **Hard in a good way** - 71 times, 5.1 per 100 (falls from 6.5 at launch to 2.5 in 2025, 3.5 in 2026).
7. **The shooting has punch** - 63 times, 4.6 per 100 (7.1 in late 2023, 1.8 in 2026). One pistol
   sounds *"like slamming a hammer on a solid wood door"* (234976329). Nobody says the guns feel weak.
8. **Memorable special enemies and bosses** - 44 times, 3.2 per 100 (peak 4.6 in 2025).
9. **It looks great** - 35 times, 2.5 per 100 (4.5 at launch).
10. **The computer-built worlds keep it varied** - 33 times, 2.4 per 100 (0.9 at launch, peaks at 4.6
    in 2025).
11. **It works alone** - 25 times, 1.8 per 100 (3.4 in 2024, 0.4 in 2025). One 2025 reviewer beat every
    difficulty solo (206126223, 18 votes).
12. **Clear difficulty steps** - 24 times, 1.7 per 100 (2.9 in 2025).
13. **The story is worth following** - 23 times, 1.7 per 100.
14. **It won over someone who avoids this kind of game** - 21 times, 1.5 per 100 - the highest rate in
    the corpus (Escape from Duckov is second at 1.4). It falls from 2.8 at launch to 0.0 in 2026.
15. **Places that set a mood** - 20 times, 1.4 per 100.
16. **The price is fair** - 19 times, 1.4 per 100 (2.1 in late 2023, 0.0 in 2026).
17. **The atmosphere draws you in** - 17 times. **Plenty of content** - 17 times.
18. **The music fits** - 15 times.
19. **The levels are well built** - 13 times. **The DLC is fair** - 13 times (0.0 at launch, 2.1 in late
    2023).
20. **Each class plays its own way** - 12 times. **Best of its kind** - 12 times.
21. **The dog** - the world reacting to you is 11 times and almost all of it is the Handler class's
    dog: *"you get a dog which you can pet"* (230565254); *"never got attached to any of the
    characters except the dog"* (218327994). The only character named with affection.
22. **The studio earned my trust** - 11 times (today's script run puts it just outside its top 25).
23. **Secrets as a feature**, from the people who found them: *"finding one by chance, or finally
    solving something after hours of curiosity, the payoff is incredible"* (202701424); *"the community
    maps secrets together"* (144280701). No separate count.
24. **Firing range and loadout slots** named as thoughtful extras (214769098), after players asked for
    a way to save loadouts (4 times). No count for the thanks.
25. Smaller praise with no count on the pages: you do not need to have played the first game; your
    choices change the story; one review calls the final boss their favourite ever (228019753); two
    praise the item drops being placed by hand rather than random.

The systems - builds, pulling you back, hard in a good way, the shooting, the special enemies and the
varied worlds - add up to 384 praise remarks, and the world they run in is another 92.

Also: 396 thumbs-up reviews say nothing specific (395 on today's script run); 135 reviews explain the
game by naming another one (*Dark Souls* in 57, *Souls* in 64, Elden Ring 22, Destiny 21, Diablo 7,
Borderlands 7, Gears 4) - *"Dark Souls with guns"*, said as praise and as complaint. Those who reject
the label say: *"this game is more of a third-person shooter with RPG touches"* (215419968). 32
remarks raise performance without saying good or bad.

### What players complained about, most to least
Counts are out of 1,382 reviews; the five numbers in brackets are per 100 in the launch quarter, late
2023, 2024, 2025 and 2026, where the page gives them.
1. **The best things are hidden behind a guide** - 61 times, 4.4 per 100 (2.6, 0.8, 4.1, 7.9, 10.0 -
   after a dip in late 2023 it rises in every period; the page calls it the only top-ten complaint
   that rises every year). **46 of the
   61 are thumbs up**, median 49 hours. *"You will play this one with the wiki open on another
   monitor"* (197321698); *"an archetype hidden behind a cloud of death that has a completely
   inconspicuous location"* (189605023); *"Easter egg simulator"* (213370670); the most-helpful
   negative review (112 votes): *"'wiki-checklist' styled secrets that hide a lot of content"*
   (204418952).
2. **It falls short of the first game** - 53 times, 3.8 per 100 (2.6, 2.1, **7.1**, 4.6, 3.5), **30 of
   them thumbs down**, median 28 hours (those who accept the sequel: 47 hours). Named losses: armour
   set bonuses (7 reviews), the trait cap that let you max everything (9), Survival mode (6 mentions),
   the desert biome, the old worlds. The most-helpful review in the sample is a short line: *"uncap
   trait points"* (174 votes, thumbs up, launch week). Twenty-plus reviews name a feature the first
   game had and this one dropped. Only 2 say the sequel changes too little.
3. **Thumbs down with nothing said** - 47 times (46 on today's script run; 1.1, 2.5, 2.2, 6.2, 8.8 per
   100).
4. **The story is thin or forgettable** - 47 times, 3.4 per 100 (4.7 in 2026).
5. **The item you need may never drop** - 24 times, 1.7 per 100 (3.0 in 2024). *"I had a buddy grind
   for two weeks for a ring"* (202191613); *"a 3 hour long pure rng based quest to get a useless gun"*
   (216597954). Escape from Duckov is higher at 2.0; Warframe 1.2.
6. **Buy it on sale only** - 20 times, 1.4 per 100, steady at 1.7 to 1.9 per 100 every year after
   launch.
7. **Needs a strong PC** - 17 times, 1.2 per 100.
8. **Too little content** - 16. **Price too high** - 16. **Repetitive** - 16 (2.9 per 100 in 2025).
9. **Grindy** - 15 (0.0 in late 2023 and in 2026, 2.1 in 2025). **Buggy** - 15. **Build choices
   shallow** - 15 (1.9 in 2024, 0.0 by 2026).
10. **The ending lets it down** - 14 times.
11. **Only runs right with upscaling on** - 14 times (13 on today's script run; 1.5 per 100 at launch,
    1.7 in late 2023, 0.0 by 2026).
12. **Hard and worse alone** - 14 times. *"solo is genuine torture"* (216597954).
13. **A patch made it worse** - 13 times (1.1, 1.3, 1.5, 0.4, 0.0 - the DLC patches: *"these new
    updates ruined most of the game's performance"*, 183452969).
14. **Menus and map hard to use** - 13 times (12 on today's script run; 1.8 per 100 in 2026; the
    minimap has no compass directions).
15. **Some options are useless** - 12. In this game it is mostly rings: nine reviews call them filler,
    *"2/3 of all loot in the game, most of them useless"*.
16. **Lost saves** - 12 times (1.3 per 100 at launch, 0.0 in 2026); two got them back. One review
    (187410273, 31 votes) says the save bug went unfixed for a year and names a launcher workaround
    players found themselves.
17. **Bosses are a chore** - 12 times (0.2 per 100 at launch, 2.5 in 2025, 1.8 in 2026; much of it one
    boss).
18. **Characters flat or annoying** - 12 (11 on today's script run; 0.4 to 2.4 per 100).
19. **One-hit kills** - 11 (1.1, 0.4, 0.7, 0.8, 0.6). *"insta-kills are the worst and laziest kind of
    game design"* (144291151).
20. **Cannot connect** - 11 (1.1, 1.7, 0.0, 0.4, 0.6). **Missing basic comforts in the menus** - 11.
21. **No way to talk to other players** (no voice, no text, only a ping) - 11. The only community
    complaint in double figures; today's script run ranks it 25th.
22. **Upgrading makes the game harder** - 9 times. Enemies scale with your gear: *"upgrading your weapon
    often makes your enemies deal more damage and take less"* (209854285); *"you're better off not
    upgrading them"* (223833959).
23. **The game will not start at all** - 9 reviews, 8 thumbs down, median 2 hours played.
24. **One area drags the rest down** - 7 (the Labyrinth in 3, N'Erud in 4).
25. **Lowering the settings does not help** - 7.
26. **The roll decides which content you ever see** - 7. *"I'm not even guaranteed to see the other
    bosses or areas"* (202739424); asks for a *"true-playthrough"* option.
27. **The final boss cannot be read** - 7. *"a visual tragedy to read"*; *"turn the in-game music off
    and listen for the sound cues"*. (No one here calls the final fight a pushover.)
28. **A new weapon starts the grind again** - 6.
29. **Slow doors and lifts pad the run** - 4 (*"20 elevators in 2 hours"*).
30. **No way to save a loadout** - 4 (asked in 2023 and 2024; later added, reviewers say).
31. **Three-each complaints:** some rewards need a second player (*"many unlocks are available only
    through multiplayer mechanics"*, 234695004); invisible walls; jumping sections that do not belong
    in a shooter; the boss hides behind its adds; attacks land beyond their visible reach; healing too
    slow for the pace; levelling up changes nothing; a guest can decide your campaign; a dead player
    watches until the next checkpoint; the guest fights at a disadvantage; currency always short.
32. **Two-each complaints:** the game only stable with the processor slowed down; attacks timed to bait
    your dodge; stamina runs out too fast; melee has no parry or block; only the host keeps progress;
    a third-party tool to beat the reroll; the sequel changes too little; stuck in aim-down-sights; a
    guest can play the DLC but not keep its loot; no cloud save; equipped items vanish.
33. **One:** *"I do wish that there was a way in-game to see what you are missing"* (182187651); no way
    out of a joined game; you miss what a teammate hears; the interact key picks the wrong thing; music
    that cuts in and out; got only part of the add-on.
34. **Named, with no count on the pages:** stuns that take control away; spaces built too big; the
    game talks too much; drab colours; the generated levels add nothing; a reward that never arrives;
    no ping shown before joining.

Mostly from people who stayed: the hidden-content complaint (46 of 61 thumbs up). Mostly from people
who left: "it falls short of the first game" (30 of 53 thumbs down) and "it will not start"
(8 of 9). The pages give no split for the rest.

**Who could have prevented the top 25** (remarks): game design 188 (85 of them the hidden content and
the item that never drops), narrative 73, engineering 69, the first game's shadow 53, nothing said 47,
the price 36, how much game there is 32, the studio's patches 13. All complaints together: 1,071
remarks, 77.5 per 100, against 1,681 praise remarks, 121.6 per 100 - 1.57 to 1 on the newer count
(1.60 to 1 on the game's own page). That is more complaint than Deep Rock Galactic (23.7), Risk of
Rain 2 (18.0), Warframe (50.9), Helldivers 2 (55.5), ARC Raiders (56.7) and Space Marine 2 (61.9), and
less than Back 4 Blood (137.2). Randomness complaints of all kinds are 2.5 per 100 (The First
Descendant 7.2).

**Who leaves, and when:** the 226 thumbs-down reviewers show a median of 16 hours; the thumbs-up
reviewers 38. Over all reviews the median is 34 hours; 204 show 100 hours or more and 2 show 1,000 or
more.

### How it changed over time
| Period | Reviews read | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 | Mean hours |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Launch quarter (2023-07 to 09) | 465 | 86.5% | 126.2 | 67.3 | 47 |
| Late 2023 | 239 | 82.0% | 118.8 | 60.3 | 60 |
| 2024 | 268 | 84.7% | 126.1 | 81.3 | 67 |
| 2025 | 240 | 79.6% | 122.1 | 91.2 | 61 |
| 2026 (to 09) | 170 | 82.4% | 104.1 | 91.2 | 59 |

- With the wordless reviews left out (today's script run), praise and complaints per 100 are: launch
  96.6 and 67.7; late 2023 90.0 and 58.6; 2024 96.6 and 80.2; 2025 94.6 and 88.3; **2026 80.0 and 84.1**
  - in 2026 the specific complaints outnumber the specific praise, yet four reviewers in five still
  recommend it. In 2026 the bare thumbs up took over from specific praise.
- **Half of all English reviews (19,047 of 38,120) came in the first ten weeks.** The launch week alone
  holds 7,906; the biggest month is 2023-08 (9,677). The second biggest month after launch is 2023-11
  (4,397), which reviewers tie to the first DLC, and it is the last month over 1,400. From 2024-02 no
  month reaches 800; from 2025-02 none reaches 350. From 2025-01 to 2026-08 the game holds 191 to 339
  English reviews a month with no slope - a game sold on sale keeps a steady trickle. Steam's 82.3% is
  the launch quarter's 86.5% pulled down by three years of a smaller, crosser audience.
- **Three different reviewers, one after another, and the game stood still.** The launch reviewer
  liked the first game and had a PC that struggled (upscaling 1.5 per 100, waiting for promised
  content 2.2). The 2024 reviewer owned the DLCs and missed the old game (falls short 7.1 per 100,
  patch made it worse 1.5, shallow builds 1.9). The 2025-26 reviewer never played the first game
  (sequel praise 4.7), plays with friends (12.4), and cannot find the hidden classes (7.9 then 10.0;
  bosses a chore 2.5). The cross-game page names this as one of four shapes of a rising complaint line:
  the relationship (Helldivers 2), the price (Space Marine 2), the reviewer (this game) and the owner
  (Risk of Rain 2).
- **The tech complaints went to zero** because, the page says, players' machines caught up - not
  because the studio fixed them.
- **By 2025-26 the hidden classes had been dug out of the game files by players.** Three reviews from
  then say the studio meant it: *"the developers fully intended on players data-mining solutions"*
  (209854285); *"some classes are gated behind data that had to be datamined as an intentional step"*
  (223872294); *"one character was hidden in the files and the community had to dig to unlock it"*
  (209316088). The complaint is not that secrets exist; it is that a new player is handed the answer
  key and told to go look.
- **A sequel is judged against the first game for two years, then against itself.** The people who
  miss the first game wrote in 2023 and 2024; the people who love this game's systems kept writing.
- Bad single months: 2024-10 (59.3% up, 27 reviews), 2025-09 (50.0%); good ones: 2024-01 (97.2%),
  2025-12 and 2026-05 (100.0%). Each rests on 20 to 36 reviews - noise.
- Reviews written in other languages under the English tag rose from 0.0 to 2.9 per 100 (12 in all:
  Russian, Finnish, Portuguese, Arabic, Spanish).

### Co-op and online play
- **Friends:** much better with friends 139, rising every year to 12.4 per 100. Playing with friends
  is 151 of the 223 community remarks.
- **Alone:** works alone 25, hard alone 14; some rewards need a second player 3.
- **Hosting and the guest are the weak point.** A guest can decide your campaign (3): *"if the other
  person does not talk to the NPC you have to Alt+F4... some random can control your progress in a
  game you paid for"* (222191203). Only the host keeps the progress (2). The guest fights at a
  disadvantage - worse timing on dodges than the host (3). A dead player watches until the next
  checkpoint, sometimes a whole boss (3). A guest can play the DLC but not keep its loot (2). No ping
  shown before joining (no count). One review each: no way out of a joined game; you miss what a
  teammate hears; map exploration not shared in co-op; a launcher that decides which friends you can
  see.
- **Connection:** cannot connect 11.
- **Talking:** no voice, no text, only a ping - 11. The only community complaint in double figures.
- **Cheating:** one review says a cheater could corrupt a co-op partner's save (one sighting).
- **Friendly fire:** 6 say it is the fun; one says having none *"saves friendships"* (201175078).
- No player-behaviour argument and no slogan in this game's reviews.
- Joining a game in progress, scaling to player count, bots and matchmaking with strangers: nothing
  said on the pages.

### Combat, movement and feel
- The shooting has punch (63); no one says the guns feel weak. Combat feel as a subject: 93 remarks.
- **The complaints are all about control taken away:** one-hit kills 11; stuns; the boss hides behind
  its adds 3; attacks land beyond their visible reach 3; attacks timed to bait your dodge 2; stamina
  runs out too fast 2; healing too slow for the pace 3; melee has no parry or block 2; stuck in
  aim-down-sights 2; the interact key picks the wrong thing 1.
- The final fight cannot be read (7): too much on screen, music drowns the sound cues.
- Jumping sections that do not belong in a shooter (3).
- Speed, sprint, controllers and rebinding: nothing said on the pages.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- Memorable special enemies 44. Bosses a chore 12, rising in 2025 (much of it one boss). Enemies as a
  subject: 141 remarks; difficulty: 147.
- Hard in a good way 71; clear difficulty steps 24; won over someone who avoids the genre 21.
- Upgrading makes the game harder (9) - the enemies scale with your gear.
- One review each: difficulty cannot be changed mid-campaign; the top difficulty is locked until the
  story is done; four reviews say the first boss is the hardest part.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- Deep builds 98 (two classes at once, mods, rings) against shallow builds 15. Progression as a
  subject: 226 remarks.
- The trait cap (9 reviews; *"uncap trait points"*, 174 votes). Armour set bonuses missed (7; one
  reviewer prefers the rings).
- Grindy 15; a new weapon starts the grind again 6; levelling up changes nothing 3; upgrading makes it
  harder 9. The page sums it up: the builds are loved, the small stat upgrades are invisible or harmful.
- **Loot:** rings as filler (9); currency always short (3); two praise the drops being placed by hand.
  The page calls this the strongest case in the corpus for a missing "loot" subject.
- Randomness (44 remarks in all): the thing you need may never drop 24; the roll decides which content
  you see 7; a third-party "world analyzer" tool to beat the reroll 2.
- No way to save a loadout 4, then loadout slots praised.

### Runs, content and replay value
- Not a run game: a campaign with checkpoints, rerolled to see other content.
- Keeps pulling you back 75; varied worlds 33; plenty 17. Repetitive 16; too little 16; the
  generation adds nothing (no count).
- **The hidden-content cluster** - hidden behind a guide 61, never drops 24, roll decides content 7,
  needs a second player 3, reroll tool 2 - is about seven complaints per 100 reviews, the biggest
  cluster.
- Level pacing: one area drags the rest down 7; slow doors and lifts 4; invisible walls 3; menus and map
  hard to use 13; spaces built too big; audio logs that make you stand still (one review). Level design
  as a subject: 107 remarks, 61 of them the guide complaint.

### Money and price
- Price fair 19 (falls to 0.0 in 2026); too high 16; buy on sale only 20, steady every year. The page
  reads it as a game that was already a sale purchase in 2023.
- DLC fair 13 (2.1 per 100 in late 2023). One review each: a new player is started inside the DLC; got
  only part of the add-on; a pre-order remark.
- Price and sales are 36 of the top-25 complaint remarks.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- **The PC that cannot run it, said four ways:** needs a strong PC 17; only runs right with upscaling
  14; lowering settings does not help 7; only stable with the processor slowed down 2. None is a crash
  complaint.
- **Will not start at all:** 9 reviews, 8 thumbs down, median 2 hours; fixes ranged from slowing the
  processor to Windows 7 compatibility mode.
- Lost saves 12; no cloud save 2; equipped items vanish 2; a reward never arrives (no count). Buggy 15.
  Cannot connect 11.
- One review each: performance gets worse as the save file grows; sound in one ear only; music that
  cuts in and out.
- Tech is 7.8% of all remarks; it falls to near zero by 2026.

### The studio, updates and community
- Studio earned my trust 11. A patch made it worse 13 (peaks with the 2024 DLC patches).
- Waiting for promised content, including DLSS and roadmap items, 2.2 per 100 at launch, gone by 2025.
- Two companies are named: Gunfire Games for the design, Arc Games for the unfixed save corruption
  (*"Arc Games is breaking the law"*, 187410273, 31 votes - the reviewer's claim). Seven communication
  complaints do not say which.
- No slogan, no culture, no player-conduct fight.
- **The reputation talk is the sequel and the comparison:** the sequel is 145 remarks (139 accept it),
  and 135 explain the game by naming another one. In Space Marine 2 the comparison was Gears of War; in
  ARC Raiders, Tarkov; here it is a whole genre's name.

### What players asked for
- Uncap the traits (9; 174 votes on the top review).
- Bring back armour set bonuses (7), Survival mode (6), the old worlds and biomes.
- An in-game way to see what you are missing (1) and a "true-playthrough" that guarantees content (in
  the 7 reviews saying the roll decides what you see).
- Voice or text chat (in the 11 "no way to talk" complaints).
- A way to save loadouts (4; reviewers say it was added).
- DLSS and roadmap content (2.2 per 100 at launch).
- A ping shown before joining; cloud saves (2); a way to leave a joined game (1); shared map exploration
  in co-op (1); a minimap with compass directions (1); a way to take off a backpack (1); difficulty
  that can change mid-campaign and the top difficulty open before the story ends (1 each).

### Only in this game
- **Content hidden on purpose as the top complaint** - the mode was built for this game, and after a dip
  in late 2023 the line rises in every period. 38 of the 363 kinds of remark used here appear in no other
  game (37 on today's script run).
- **Upgrading makes the game harder** (9) - no other game has it.
- **The trait cap** - one of nine modes this game built that no other uses.
- **The guest-control complaints** (a guest can decide your campaign; a dead player spectates until
  the checkpoint) were first built here.
- **The four-way "my PC cannot run it"** set (upscaling, settings do not help, slowed processor, will
  not start).
- **The final fight cannot be read**, sitting beside "the final fight is a pushover" as the two ways a
  final boss fails.
- **A sequel judged against the first game for two years, then against itself.**
- **The most genre-avoiders won over** (1.5 per 100, the corpus high) and the highest share of talk
  about game design (36.6%; Space Marine 2 26.2%).
- **Others named only here, a few each:** the roll decides which content you see (7), no way to save a
  loadout (4), slow doors and lifts (4), healing too slow for the pace (3), the boss hides behind its
  adds (3).

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Hide secrets, but never hide the core: let players see in the game what is left to find.**
   Evidence: hidden behind a guide 61 (4.4 per 100, rising to 10.0 in 2026), 46 of them from fans; the
   one ask for an in-game list (182187651); three 2025-26 reviews say the studio meant players to dig
   the classes out of the files.
   Dominion could: keep secrets for cosmetics and lore; put every class, perk and major item on an
   in-game codex that shows "not yet found" and hints where; never gate a class on a hidden puzzle.
   Strength: **strong**.
