- **The shared threat is what makes strangers cooperate** (8).
- **Runs that become stories:** 26 - "players, ARC machines and chaos everywhere".
- **No counterplay:** 9, falling to 0.2 per 100 in 2026.
- **Hard in a good way:** 7. **One option beats the rest:** 8.
- **Difficulty change:** a reviewer says the 2026-03-31 patch made it too hard for new solo players
  (222215525).
- The pages name no bosses.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- **Loot is the core, but the study has no heading for it yet** (gap 331, Rico's call); loot rooms,
  blueprint drop rates and the duplication economy are filed elsewhere. "This game is where the evidence
  lives."
- **Nothing left to chase:** 18, rising (1.0 to 1.6 per 100 by year; 0.8 to 3.5 by period on the
  re-run).
- **Unlocking feels good:** 14, gone in 2026. **Grindy:** 10.
- **The optional reset is not worth what it costs:** 7 (re-run).
- **Your rank drops if you stop playing:** seen once.
- **The outfits make you look silly:** seen once.
- **Gear risk, both ways:** too afraid to use the good gear, 8; dying costs nothing, 11 (free loadouts mean
  "nobody tries to progress"). "Eleven people say death is too cheap and eight say gear is too precious
  to use - both in a game with one punishment model." Failing costs too much: 8. Dying costs little and
  you are back fast: 7. Stakes worth the risk: 32.
- **Wipes:** the studio wiped what I had earned (built here; the currency wipe around April 2026, as
  reviewers tell it). What a cheater took is never given back (2 on the re-run; one says it was given
  back).
- **The rules contradict the story:** the game rewards the opposite of what its fiction says you are
  there to do (3 on the re-run).

### Runs, content and replay value
- **Keeps pulling you back:** 65 (4.8 per 100 in 2025, 3.8 in 2026).
- **Runs become stories you retell:** 26. **Stakes worth the risk:** 32.
- **Nothing left to chase:** 18. **Plenty to do:** 8. **A good choice of modes:** 14.
- **Updates too slow:** 12, rising.
- **The missing PvE mode:** 55, 42 of them asking for PvE. The cross-game page: the two extraction games
  read so far each ask for the other's format - ARC Raiders players want PvE, Escape from Duckov (PvE
  alone) players want co-op (39 missing-mode requests there, mostly for co-op). "No PvP is a feature" is
  said 51 times in Escape from Duckov (4.4 per 100) and 0 times here; no other game reaches 1 per 100.
  One review here says the PvP is what keeps the game alive (seen once).
- **Easy to start:** 25 - first extraction shooter for many.

### Money and price
- Paid, $40. **The price is fair:** 12 (0.8 per 100 in 2025, 0.9 in 2026).
- Publishing is only 1.7% of everything said. The pages name no shop complaints.
- Cheat accounts at "fifty cents to four dollars" (221530335, the reviewer's claim) - cheap accounts make
  bans cheap to dodge.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Engineering is 7.9% of everything said, and "this game's engineering is not what people argue about".
  Crashes are row 14 here; they were row 5 in Helldivers 2.
- **Runs well:** 18 (1.7 per 100 in 2025, 0.5 in 2026).
- **Crashes:** 11. **Lag and desync:** 10, falling to 0.2. **Buggy:** 8.
- **Exploits:** 8, rising in 2026 - duplication and an unprotected API (reviewers' account).
- **Stuck on scenery; banned with no reason; a frame rate that locks until you switch windows** (no
  counts or seen once).
- No patch notes were pulled. The engine is not named on the pages.

### The studio, updates and community
- **Cheating is the studio's test, and players say it failed.** "Does nothing about the cheaters" 22,
  rising to 7.1 per 100; "what a cheater took is never given back" (2); "support replied with a form
  letter"; "banned with no reason given". "They refuse to put an anticheat and only ban a small fraction"
  (234982159) - the reviewer's claim.
- **The wipe:** reviewers say a currency wipe after the duplication exploit also took honest players'
  currency (223877518, 230568877).
- **Weakened what players liked:** 8. **Updates too slow:** 12.
- **Live-ops** is only 3.2% of everything said (Helldivers 2: 7.7%) - patches are not the argument here.
- **Reputation:** best of its kind 37; the praise is undeserved 10; won over someone who avoids the genre
  15; streamers play a different game from yours (seen once).
- **Generated voices:** four voices; "the voices are generated and it shows" (built here). Seen once
  each: the studio should have paid voice actors; the studio will not say where it used generated
  content. An ethics complaint about generated voices is left open.
- **Embark is both studio and publisher**, so there is no studio-versus-publisher split here.
- **The other players are the community:** player conduct is 78% of the community division. Two reviews
  take a side in a dispute between players (re-run).
- **Wordless thumbs up:** 591 reviews, 40% of the sample - the highest in the study (Helldivers 2 22.5
  per 100, where the slogan took that place).

### What players asked for
- **A PvE-only mode** - 42 of the 55 "expected mode missing", rising to 8.0 per 100.
- **Real anti-cheat and bans** - "does nothing about the cheaters" 22.
- **A price on attacking other players** - a karma flag, a bounty, a penalty - 8.
- **A way to see a hostile player before they strike** - 2.
- **Players sorted by how they play** - 4 (not knowing the matchmaker already does it).
- **Something left to chase** - 18.
- **Faster updates** - 12.
- **A private base / a shared place between runs** - 3 sightings, and the "no shared place" label.
- **Lost items given back after cheating** - 2 say they never are.
- **A reset that is worth what it costs** - 7.

### Only in this game
When the game was read, 37 of the 279 labels used here appeared in no other game, and 33 of those 37
are about the other players or the matchmaker; the 2026-10-03 re-run counts 296 labels and still 37
used here only. 38 labels were built in this run - the most of any game, in thirty batches. Before this
game the study had no way to say a stranger could shoot you. The ones that matter for Dominion, with
re-run counts:
- Not knowing who to trust is the thrill (95); strangers team up (41); betrayal (38); exit campers (27);
  the players turned hostile after launch (22); third-player swoops (13); solo friendly, squads shoot on
  sight (12).
- The behaviour-sorting matchmaker: works (23), five ways it fails (7, 6, 5, 5, 2), wanted by people who
  did not know it existed (4), and made it bland (2).
- The studio does nothing about the cheaters (22).
- Too afraid to use the good gear (8); a price on attacking other players (8); a shared threat turns
  strangers into allies (8).
- The optional reset is not worth what it costs (7); the gear gap decides the fight (5).
- Smaller teams matched against full squads (3); the rules contradict the story (3).
- Wants hostile players flagged before they strike (2); the camera lets you peek round corners (2); what
  a cheater took is never given back (2); you cannot tell above from below by sound (2).
- Seen once each: players ambush the objective; one region gets content first; your rank drops if you
  stop; the outfits look silly; the PvP is what keeps it alive; streamers play a different game; should
  have paid voice actors; will not say where it used generated content; what a cheater took was given
  back.
- Built here but no longer on the re-run's "only here" list, so other games have used them since:
  looting, reviving or healing locks you in an animation; players spawn on top of each other; dropped
  into a match underway; managing the inventory is a chore; no shared place between runs; the studio
  wiped what I had earned; banned with no reason; stuck on scenery; the voices are generated and it
  shows; the runs turn into stories you retell; support replied with a form letter.

### For Dominion - our reading

1. **Lesson:** Players who come for extraction tension often do not want other players shooting them.
   **Evidence:** the top complaint is the missing mode (55), 42 asking for PvE, tripling to 8.0 per 100 by
   the last period; five of the top eight complaints are about other players; Escape from Duckov players
   praise having no PvP 51 times (4.4 per 100) and ask for co-op. One review here says the PvP is what
   keeps the game alive (seen once). **Dominion could:** treat being PvE-only as a selling point and say
   it plainly on the store page; get the risk and tension from the world and the clock, not from other
   squads. **Strength:** strong.

2. **Lesson:** Cheating kills a game slowly, and "the studio does nothing" follows it.
   **Evidence:** cheaters 0.9 to 12.4 per 100 in eleven months, the thumb 86% to 52%; "does nothing"
   0.2 to 7.1; exploits 0.1 to 1.2; the wipe that hit honest players. **Dominion could:** in PvE on a
   listen server, cheating mostly hurts the host's own squad - but any shared progress, trading or
   leaderboards need server-side checks; never trust the client for item grants; if a fix wipes items,
   protect players who did nothing wrong. **Strength:** strong (for anything shared between players).

3. **Lesson:** Gear risk must sit in the middle: too cheap and nothing matters, too dear and nobody uses it.
   **Evidence:** dying costs nothing 11; too afraid to use the good gear 8; stakes worth the risk 32 (the
   most-helpful review: "a successful extraction is a real adrenaline rush"); failing costs too much 8;
   quick recovery praised 7. Stakes worth the risk fell from 3.0 to 0.9 per 100 as reviewers piled up
   hours. **Dominion could:** let players lose some of what they carry, but keep a safety net (an insured
   slot, a cheap standard kit that still feels good, rewards for using good gear); test how often players
   actually bring their best gear. **Strength:** medium-strong.

4. **Lesson:** Runs that turn into stories are the praise to aim for.
   **Evidence:** runs become stories you retell 26; keeps pulling you back 65; "like scenes from a sci-fi
   movie" (220132764). **Dominion could:** build runs around surprises (a big machine arrives, a squad
   member goes down at the exit) and make it easy to clip and share. **Strength:** medium.

5. **Lesson:** Smart, readable robot enemies win praise, and a shared threat bonds players.
   **Evidence:** good AI 25; "robots that move like nothing he has seen"; a shared threat turns strangers
   into allies 8. No counterplay complaints 9. **Dominion could:** invest in enemy movement and behaviour;
   give every big attack a way to dodge, block or answer it. **Strength:** medium.

6. **Lesson:** Do not lock players in long animations when danger is near.
   **Evidence:** "interacting freezes you and that is when you die" built from this game (no count).
   **Dominion could:** let players cancel looting, reviving and healing, or make them short; give a squad
   mate a way to cover. **Strength:** weak (no count), but cheap to act on.

7. **Lesson:** Joining a match in progress and spawning beside others both annoy.
   **Evidence:** dropped into a match already underway 13; players spawn on top of each other (no count);
   smaller teams matched against full squads (3 on the 2026-10-03 re-run). **Dominion could:** if late
   joining exists, tell the joiner how far along the run is and give them a fair start; spread spawns;
   scale enemies to squad size. **Strength:** medium-weak.

8. **Lesson:** Launch praise fades as reviewers get hundreds of hours; plan goals for the long tail.
   **Evidence:** mean hours went 58 to 293; sound, unlocks and "won me over" fell to 0.0 in 2026; nothing
   left to chase 18 and rising (0.8 to 3.5 per 100 by period on the re-run); updates too slow 12 and
   rising (0.5 to 4.4). **Dominion could:** plan end-game goals and a steady update pace before launch,
   aimed at the 200-hour player. **Strength:** medium.

9. **Lesson:** Easy to start brings in new players.
   **Evidence:** easy to start 25, rising to 2.4 per 100; won over someone who avoids the genre 15; many
   say it was their first extraction shooter. **Dominion could:** keep the first runs simple and safe,
   teach extraction step by step. **Strength:** medium.

10. **Lesson:** A system that sorts players needs to be clear and hard to game.
    **Evidence:** the behaviour matchmaker: works 23; misreads self-defence 7; puts cheaters with fighters
    6; peaceful players still meet killers 5; stopped working as the player count fell 5; gamed by killers
    2; made it bland 2; 4 did not know it existed. **Dominion could:** for public PvE matchmaking, keep any
    sorting (skill, play style, voice chat) simple, shown to the player, and safe at low player counts.
    **Strength:** medium.

11. **Lesson:** Sell the game's community feel early; strangers teaming up is a launch effect.
    **Evidence:** strangers team up 4.1 per 100 at launch, 0.9 by mid-2026; welcoming community 17 (1.5,
    0.7 by year); much better with friends 25. **Dominion could:** reward strangers for helping (shared
    rewards for revives, emotes, pings). **Strength:** medium-weak.

12. **Lesson:** Inventory and between-run spaces matter.
    **Evidence:** inventory is a chore; no shared place between runs; a private base asked for (3).
    **Dominion could:** keep inventory quick with sorting and bulk actions; consider a hub where the squad
    meets between runs. **Strength:** weak.

13. **Lesson:** Sound must tell up from down in 3D spaces.
    **Evidence:** cannot tell above from below (built here; 2 on the 2026-10-03 re-run); punchy sound
    praised 17 at launch. **Dominion could:** test sound positioning on multi-level maps. **Strength:**
    weak.

14. **Lesson:** Generated voices get noticed.
    **Evidence:** "the voices are generated and it shows" (four voices, built here); "should have paid
    voice actors" and "will not say where it used generated content", seen once each; an ethics complaint
    left open. **Dominion could:** use recorded actors for key voices, or expect the comment; if any
    content is generated, say so plainly. **Strength:** weak.

15. **Lesson:** Support and bans must explain themselves.
    **Evidence:** support replied with a form letter; banned with no reason given; what a cheater took is
    never given back (2 on the re-run; one review says it was given back). **Dominion could:** give real
    replies and a ban reason; restore items lost to bugs or cheats. **Strength:** weak.

16. **Lesson:** The fiction and the rules should agree.
    **Evidence:** "the rules contradict the story" (3 on the 2026-10-03 re-run). **Dominion could:** make
    what earns rewards match what the story says the squad is there to do. **Strength:** weak.

17. **Lesson:** A strong launch sets the score for years.
    **Evidence:** 62.8% of English reviews in the first three months; Steam's Very Positive "is the launch,
    and it will stay the launch for years" even as the late thumb fell to about 50%. **Dominion could:**
    put the most polish into the first three months. **Strength:** strong.

18. **Lesson:** The players who turn against a game are its most invested ones.
    **Evidence:** the middle thumbs-down reviewer has played 92 hours, the middle thumbs-up reviewer 41;
    429 reviews show 100 hours or more; by the last period the mean reviewer has 282 hours and the thumb is
    52.2%. Helldivers 2 shows the same (73 against 40). **Dominion could:** treat complaints from
    long-time players as the core audience speaking; check every late change against what it does to a
    200-hour player. **Strength:** medium-strong (two games, whole-sample medians).

19. **Lesson:** An optional reset must pay back what it takes.
    **Evidence:** "the optional reset is not worth what it costs" 7 (2026-10-03 re-run, used in no other
    game); nothing left to chase 18 and rising. **Dominion could:** if Dominion offers a reset or prestige,
    make the reward clearly worth the loss, show it before the player commits, and test how many players
    take it. **Strength:** weak-medium.

20. **Lesson:** Do not punish players for taking a break.
    **Evidence:** "your rank drops if you stop playing", seen once and used in no other game. **Dominion
    could:** avoid ranks or rewards that decay with time away. **Strength:** weak (one review).

21. **Lesson:** Plan what happens to matchmaking when fewer people play.
    **Evidence:** "the sorting used to work and no longer does" 5, with five reviewers tying it to the
    falling player count; "the player count is falling" rose 0.0 to 1.8 per 100 by period. The cross-game
    page reads this as confirming "plan for the day the player count falls". **Dominion could:** make sure
    friends-only and solo-with-bots (or solo-scaled) play stay good when public queues are thin; widen
    matching rules automatically when queues are slow. **Strength:** medium-weak.

### Limits
- A 0.59% sample, ±2.69% on whole-sample shares, wider on any month. The last five months are 113 reviews
  together; one review moves 2026-09 by ten points. They are reported only because all five point the
  same way. No margin was worked out for each period.
- The launch month is two days and 19 reviews; November 2025 is the launch month in practice. The sample
  plan was 1,541 reviews; 1,473 came back. Steam's English count on the day the game was added was
  251,586, against 251,588 summed by month.
- 172 reviews (11.7%) are counted on the day first written, not last edited; mostly negative edits landing
  on 2025-11, so the cheating rise is understated.
- No patch notes were pulled: the 2026-03-31 patch, the duplication exploit, the account prices, the
  anti-cheat claim and the currency wipe are what reviewers say.
- Whether the matchmaker broke or the player count fell below what it needs cannot be told apart.
- English only; about 164,000 reviews in other languages were not pulled (Oceania lobbies, a China-first
  release). Reviews written in another language under the English label were not counted.
- 31% of what was said was too vague to file; 591 reviews are a bare thumbs up.
- All six matchmaker labels (seven with "made it bland") rest on this one game.
- **Two counts (new since the old entry).** The page's figures are from 2026-09-11. A re-run on
  2026-10-03 differs by a few: 2,298 things said instead of 2,296; 699 too vague to file (30.4%, 590 of
  them a bare thumbs up) instead of 712 (31%, 591); 296 labels instead of 279 (still 37 used here only);
  griefers 41 instead of 42; updates too slow 11 instead of 12; the praise is undeserved 9 instead of 10.
  This entry gives the page's count first and the re-run beside it. The re-run's per-period figures leave
  out lines about the review itself, so its praise rates are much lower.
- Several things the old entry listed with "no count" now have counts from the re-run (the reset 7, the
  gear gap 5, smaller teams 3, the rules against the story 3, above or below by sound 2, corner peeking 2,
  what a cheater took 2).
- **Wording change:** the old entry said the voices "are made by AI"; the rebuilt pages call them
  generated voices, and this entry follows the pages.
- Section 16 gives praise to complaint as 1.55 : 1; the later recomputed table gives 1.53 : 1 (86.8 praise
  and 56.7 complaints per 100) - below Space Marine 2 (1.87 : 1) and Helldivers 2 (1.95 : 1), above Back 4
  Blood (0.86 : 1). The "won over someone who avoids the genre" count is 15 on the game pages and 16 on
  the cross-game page.
- Engine, hosting and squad size are not stated on the pages (the duo and trio quote and the label for
  "full three-player squads" imply three).

---

## Immortal: Unchained

**Read:** 435 of 435 English reviews (100%, every review - so there is no margin of error), written
September 2018 to August 2026 (all 77 months). **64.6% thumbs up** (281 up, 154 down; Steam's own
English count is the same) (Steam, all languages: Mixed, 890 reviews).
**Size of the read, in numbers:** 1,785 things said by the script count on 2026-10-03 (4.10 per
review); 1,781 (4.09) on 2026-09-10. Only 73 of them (4.1%) say nothing specific (2026-09-10: 79, 4%).
579 are praise and 948 complaints (947 by the script), with 254 neither. 254 different labels by the
script (245 on 2026-09-10), 5 of them used in no other game. Hours on the review: median 10;
thumbs-down reviewers played a median of 4 hours, thumbs-up reviewers 16; 4 reviews show 100 hours or
more; 47 show zero hours, 33 of them thumbs down.
**Pages:** `findings/immortal-unchained-english.md`, `findings/immortal-unchained.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` section 14.

**What it is:** A single-player "soulslike" third-person shooter from 2018: hard, lock-on combat in
the style of Dark Souls, but with guns. Players level and customise weapons. The world is
built from large, linked areas ("biomes") full of paths and secrets. Paid, finished, and no longer
updated: no seasons, no roadmap, no shop. No co-op, no online play. Released whole (no early access).
Engine not named on the pages. It is in the study as a test: does a way of sorting reviews built from
co-op shooters still work on a single-player game? (It did: 240 of the 245 labels it needed already
existed.)

**How close to Dominion:** Half close. It shares the third-person camera, shooting as the main
action, enemies with weak points, builds and weapon levelling, and a hard,
skill-based tone. It does not share co-op, hosting, runs, extraction or arenas: it is one player,
one long campaign. Its lessons for Dominion are about how a third-person shooter feels in the hands,
how aim help and weak points fight each other, what players forgive, and what they do not. It is a
middling game, not a disaster, but many of its lessons are "what went wrong".

### The short version (plain words)
People who like this game still list its faults: a thumbs-up reviewer writes 1.53 complaints on
average, and 45.5% of all complaints (431 of 948) come from people who recommend it. They forgive
faults about numbers - bad balance, one weapon that beats the rest, the price. They do not forgive
the game taking control away from them: stuns, slow weapon handling, attacks you cannot answer. The
level design is the most-praised thing (46 times), and hard-but-fair difficulty is next (39). The
lock-on, borrowed from sword games, grabs the enemy's body instead of its weak point, and that
clashes with shooting. Nobody can describe the game without naming another game (76 times). Overall
reviewers write 217.7 complaints and 133.1 praises per 100 reviews (the cross-game table: 217.9 and
133.1, 0.61 praise per complaint): the third-highest complaint rate of the nine games in that table,
after Redfall and The Anacrusis, and the second-highest praise rate, after Deep Rock Galactic.

### What players praised, most to least
Counts are the 2026-10-03 script. Per 100 reviews in 2018-19, 2020-23 and 2024-26 where it matters
(the last period is only 31 reviews).
1. **The levels are well built** - 46 times. Praised by both camps. Even a review that calls the game
   *"an exercise in frustration"* says the areas are *"multi-levelled, sprawling areas full of
   criss-crossing paths and secrets to discover"* (233349028). Another game's page sums it up: this
   game was praised for its levels.
2. **Hard in a way that feels good** - 39 times. Almost all early: 13.6 per 100 in 2018-19, 1.4 in
   2020-23.
3. **Many builds to try** - 31 times.
4. **Nothing else plays like it** - 27 times. *"If you want a soulslike with guns, this is it. There is
   no other option."* (218154663)
5. **The mood pulls you in** - 25 times.
6. **It gets good once you learn it** - 24 times. Most often the lock-on, or how to level a weapon.
   *"This game became enjoyable after I started using lock-on. I hated the game until I learned this
   trick."* (78807510)
7. **The story is worth following** - 20 times.
8. **The price is fair** - 20 times.
9. **The art style is well directed** - 19 times.
10. **I want a sequel** - 18 times.
11. **The world is worth exploring** - 16 times.
12. **It keeps pulling you back** - 16 times.
13. **More than one way into a place** - 16 times.
14. **Better than its bad name** - 16 times (1.5, 5.8, 12.9 per 100: it rose as the game aged).
15. **Shooting hits hard** - 13 times (against 14 that say it feels weightless).
16. **When you lose, it is your fault** - 13 times (against 24 that say losing feels random).
17. **The controls respond well** - 13 times.
18. **Build choices change how you play** - 13 times.
19. **It beats its rivals** - 13 times.
20. **It runs smoothly** - 10 times.
21. **The music fits** - 10 times.
22. **Some enemies stand out** - 9 times.
23. **The studio listens and acts** - 9 times.
24. **The voices are well acted** - 8 times.
25. Praise overall: 579 positive things said, against 948 negative and 254 neutral. 22 thumbs-up
    reviews say nothing about why.

### What players complained about, most to least
"Recommenders" means the share written by people who gave a thumbs up (the people who stayed); the
2026-09-10 page gives it where it counted it. Per 100 reviews in 2018-19, 2020-23 and 2024-26 where
it matters.
1. **Costs too much for what it is** - 38 times. **63% from recommenders.** It faded: 12.1, 4.3, 0.0
   per 100.
2. **Enemies appear with no chance to react** - 30 times. 37% from recommenders: mostly people who
   left. It rose: 4.5, 7.9, 22.6 per 100.
3. **Some choices are never worth taking** - 26 times. **73% from recommenders.**
4. **One choice beats all the others** - 25 times. **72% from recommenders.**
5. **Another named game does it better** - 25 times. *"Poor mans remnant... don't bother"*
   (171161481). (Remnant is the game reviewers name.) It rose: 2.6, 7.9, 22.6 per 100.
6. **It needs more work** - 24 times.
7. **Losing feels random rather than your fault** - 24 times.
8. **Weapon handling is slow** (swapping, reloading, readying) - 23 times. **30% from recommenders:**
   mostly people who left.
9. **The story is thin and easy to forget** - 20 times.
10. **The animation is stiff** - 19 times.
11. **The frame rate jumps around** - 18 times.
12. **Every enemy is fought the same way** - 18 times: *"every enemy has a head and a back"*.
13. **Buggy** - 18 times. 56% from recommenders.
14. **The rules favour the enemy** - 17 times.
15. **The jump between difficulty levels is wrong** - 17 times. 59% from recommenders.
16. **Too few resources to go round** - 16 times.
17. **Attacks you cannot dodge, block or answer** - 16 times. **25% from recommenders:** mostly people
    who left.
18. **Only a few builds work** - 15 times.
19. **Shots feel weightless** - 14 times.
20. **Shots go where they want** (spread beats aim) - 14 times.
21. **Traps and hazards punish unfairly** - 14 times.
22. **The enemies are dumb** - 13 times.
23. **Hits stun you and take control away** - 13 times. **31% from recommenders:** mostly people who
    left.
24. **It copies an older game** - 13 times.
25. **Bosses are a chore** - 12 times.
26. **Moving feels slow and heavy** - 10 times (from the cross-game page, which lists it beside
    Gunfire Reborn at 15 and DRG: Rogue Core at 11 as the commonest movement complaint).
27. **One setting causes the slowdown** - 6 times (players found the single graphics option behind
    the poor frame rate).
28. **The lock-on holds the body, not the weak point** - 4 times.
29. **The camera gets in the way** - 1 time.
30. **A shot that should pass is stopped by the scenery** - 1 time.
31. **The game makes you remember things with no text to help** - 1 review (an accessibility point,
    filed with a later game's matching review).
32. **You cannot skip what the game plays at you** (scenes you must sit through) - found while
    reading this game; four sightings across two games, this game's share not given.

Also: 20 thumbs-down reviews give no reason.

**The dividing line is control.** Faults about numbers (balance, a top weapon, price) are mostly
written by people who still recommend the game. Faults about the game taking the controller away
(stuns, slow handling, no counterplay, unfair spawns) are mostly written by people who do not.

### How it changed over time
Thumbs up by the year the review was written:

| Year | Up | Down | Reviews | % up |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2018 | 118 | 45 | 163 | 72.4% |
| 2019 | 69 | 33 | 102 | 67.6% |
| 2020 | 16 | 19 | 35 | 45.7% |
| 2021 | 16 | 11 | 27 | 59.3% |
| 2022 | 25 | 11 | 36 | 69.4% |
| 2023 | 25 | 16 | 41 | 61.0% |
| 2024 | 4 | 10 | 14 | 28.6% |
| 2025 | 5 | 4 | 9 | 55.6% |
| 2026 | 3 | 5 | 8 | 37.5% |

By period (2026-10-03 script):

| Period | Reviews | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2018-19 | 265 | 70.6% | 145.7 | 200.0 |
| 2020-23 | 139 | 59.0% | 101.4 | 203.6 |
| 2024-26 | 31 | 38.7% | 96.8 | 364.5 |

- **61% of every English review came in the first sixteen months** (163 in 2018, 102 in 2019). The
  review rate never came back.
- **What fell:** "hard in a way that feels good" 13.6 to 1.4 per 100 between the first two periods;
  the price complaint 12.1 to 4.3 to 0.0. **What rose:** unfair spawns 4.5 to 7.9 to 22.6; "another
  game does it better" 2.6 to 7.9 to 22.6; "better than its bad name" 1.5 to 5.8 to 12.9.
- **Do not read a trend into 2024-2026**: only 31 reviews across three years; one review moves its
  figures by about three points per 100. 2024 is four up and ten down; one more review either way
  moves it eight points.
- **The score is flat because the game is finished.** Talk about updates is 0.5% of everything said,
  against 4.1% in the co-op games. No patches, seasons or roadmap to argue about. Only who wanders in
  changes.
- 58 reviews (13.3%) were edited after posting.

### Co-op and online play
None: it is a single-player game. The pages show what that does: talk about other players and the
community is 0.8% of everything said (14 things said, 13 of them about the studio), against 13.6% in
the eight co-op games. 12 whole topics that co-op games fill (co-op design, bots, solo play, roles,
friends, player behaviour, population, matchmaking, servers, netcode, crossplay, social features) were
never mentioned. The pages do not record players asking for co-op.

### Combat, movement and feel
- **Guns and souls-style lock-on clash.** Lock-on comes from sword games, where the enemy is one
  target. Put it in a shooter with weak points and the aim help fights the player.
  - Lock-on holds the body, not the weak point: 4 times. *"when you lock on, your reticule becomes
    suddenly glued to the enemy's torso, making targeting the weak spots nigh on impossible"*
    (233349028). *"Lock on does not autotarget weak points"* (45153643).
  - Shots go where they want: 14 times.
  - Every enemy fought the same way (head and back): 18 times.
  - 39 word hits for "lock-on" and "aim assist" across the whole study; 22 are this game.
- **Loss of control is what makes people quit.** Slow weapon handling 23 (30% recommenders); stuns
  take control away 13 (31%); no counterplay 16 (25%).
- **Hit feel:** weightless 14 against hits hard 13; the payoff of a hit lands flat 5 against lands well
  7; weapons behave as you would expect 1. Stiff animation 19.
- **Moving feels slow and heavy**: 10 times. Missing modern moves 6; choppy 2; responsive 2.
- **Controls:** unresponsive 9 against responsive and clear 13; **you have to switch input device
  (pad or mouse) to play well 6**; an expected button missing 2; cannot rebind 1; rebind anything 1;
  aim sensitivity cannot be tuned 1.
- **Camera:** hides the action 4; disorienting 2; gets in the way 1. **The scenery swallows a shot**:
  1 time (a third-person camera fault later seen in other games).
- **Space:** rooms too small for the game's own moves (no room to dodge or use the camera) 5, first
  named in this game; too big 5. (The first version of this entry had no count.)
- **Learning it pays off**: 24 say the game became good once they learned something, most often the
  lock-on (78807510).

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- **Hard in a good way**: 39 times. **Gets good once learned**: 24 times.
- **Unfair spawns** (enemies appear with no chance to react): 30 times, mostly from people who left.
- **Attacks with no answer**: 16 times, 25% from recommenders.
- **Fairness:** losing feels random 24 against losing feels earned 13; the rules favour the enemy 17;
  traps and hazards punish unfairly 14; enemies always know where you are 6; enemies ignore physical
  logic 10.
- **Enemies:** every enemy fought the same way 18; dumb enemies 13; overwhelming numbers 11; bullet
  sponges 9.
- **Bosses are a chore**: 12 times. (The first version of this entry said bosses were not counted.)
- **Difficulty:** the jump between difficulty levels is wrong 17 (59% from recommenders); one part far
  harder than the rest 13; too hard 6; too easy 4; the player is too fragile 4.
- **Used a cheat to get through it**: 2 times said, by a search of this game's summaries. (The first
  version of this entry said one review, one of two such reviews in the study; the page does not say
  whether the 2 come from one review or two.)

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- **Many builds to try**: 31 times; build choices change how you play 13.
- **Some choices are useless**: 26 (73% recommenders). **One choice dominates**: 25 (72%
  recommenders). **Only a few builds work**: 15. Players forgive these, but they are among the
  most-said complaints.
- **Resources too scarce**: 16. Choices cannot be undone 7. Grindy 7; locked behind farming 3.
- **Cannot change how you look**: 12.
- **Luck decides the outcome**: 7.
- **Levelling a weapon** is one of the things players say they had to learn before the game clicked.
- **The game does not show what is left to earn**: 2 times, a complaint first named for this game.
  (The first version of this entry had no count.)

### Runs, content and replay value
- **Well-built levels**: 46, the top praise; sprawling, layered, full of secrets. More than one way
  into a place 16.
- **Layout faults:** layouts repeat 10; badly laid out 9; confusing 7. One review says getting lost is
  part of it (seen in no other game).
- **The spaces can be too tight** for dodging and camera: 5 (first named here).
- **Length:** plenty of content 6 against too little 2; runs out fast 9 against keeps pulling you back
  16. (The first version of this entry said there were no counts on length or repeat play.)
- **Getting around:** fast travel comes late and costs something 4 (seen in no other game); a harsh
  restart after death 9; one mistake locks content away 1 (seen in no other game).
- **Players asked for a checklist of secrets** (the Remnant II section of the cross-game page says
  this game "had the checklist ask"; no count).
- Generated levels: nothing said.

### Money and price
- **Costs too much for what it is**: 38 times, the fourth most-said thing, and **63% of it comes from
  people who still recommend it.** A fair price fault is forgiven. Against: the price is fair 20.
- Only buy it on sale 3. Paid add-ons (DLC) not worth it 7; content after launch is free 1.
- A finished game bought years later, often on sale: the pages note it has no shop, no currency and no
  refund or pre-order talk at all.
- One reviewer says they got the game free from the studio (one of three such reviews across three
  games in the study). One says they played it for a Steam platform reward. (The first version of this
  entry called this trading-card talk, seen only here; on 2026-10-03 that bullet is filed as "played it
  for the platform reward", 1.)

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- **Buggy**: 18 times, 56% from recommenders. Bugs break play 10.
- **Frame rate jumps**: 18. Runs smoothly 10.
- **One graphics setting causes the slowdown**: 6 times. Players found the setting and turned it down.
  (On 2026-09-10 this was used in no other game; by 2026-10-03 it is no longer on this game's
  only-here list.)
- **Falling through the floor**: 3 times here. It was first named while reading this game (nine
  sightings across six games in the study).
- Crashes 3; progress not saved 3.
- One review says it cannot tell the game's faults from its own machine's (seen in no other game).
- Tech talk is 5.4% of everything said, against 7.3% for the eight co-op games.

### The studio, updates and community
- The game is finished and abandoned. Talk about updates is 0.5% of everything said.
- **Talk about the studio**: 13 of the 14 community mentions. The studio listens and acts 9; open
  about plans 1; working on the wrong thing first 1; talks but never about the problem 1; **replied
  to my review on the store page** 1; unclear 1. The studio lost my trust: 4.
- **Grading against the studio's small size**: 34 times, one review in thirteen, the fifth most-said
  thing. The reviewer tells you which ruler they used. (An earlier version of the page called it third;
  its own table ranks it fifth.)
- **Reviewers state their own record in the genre first**: 8 times.
- **"Average, not good or bad"**: 26 reviews say this instead of a verdict.
- **Comparisons everywhere**: describing the game by naming other games is the top thing said, 76
  times (17.5 per 100 reviews, the biggest by a factor of 1.6). Its two neighbours are the same claim
  with opposite signs: "nothing else plays like it" 27, "Remnant did it better" 25. It beats its rivals
  13; it copies an older game 13.
- **Fans want more:** I want a sequel 18; better than its bad name 16.
- Where the talk goes (2026-09-10, against the eight co-op games): how the game plays 53.6% (co-op
  games 30.3%); reputation and marketing 12.5% (10.0%); the review itself 10.2% (10.1%); art 5.2%
  (4.0%); how it was made and shipped 2.0% (7.4%); other people 0.8% (13.6%); updates 0.5% (4.1%).
  The 2026-10-03 script gives how the game plays 53.4% and the review itself 10.4%; tech 5.4%;
  price 4.1%; story 3.9%; sound 1.7%.
- The share of "the review itself" barely moved across the genre change (10.2% against 10.1%): it
  measures how people write reviews, not what game they are about.

### What players asked for
- **A checklist of what is left to find or earn** (the cross-game page notes the ask; the complaint
  that the game does not show it is said 2 times).
- **Lock-on that targets weak points** (4 + quotes).
- **A sequel**: 18.
- **A mode they expected is missing**: 9.
- **A way to change how you look**: 12 complain they cannot.
No other counted requests on the pages.

### Only in this game
- **Lock-on that grabs the body, not the weak point** - 4 times, only here on both counts.
- **The clearest split between forgiven and unforgiven faults**: numbers forgiven, loss of control
  not.
- **The most game-design talk** of any game read before it (53.6%), because there are no other players to
  talk about.
- **The only game where "nothing else plays like it" and "another game did it better" are the same
  claim with opposite signs** (27 and 25).
- **Things said here and in no other game.** On 2026-09-10: reviewers posting their genre record 8;
  one setting causes the slowdown 6; the lock-on 4; trading-card talk 1; the camera gets in the way 1.
  On 2026-10-03 the script finds a different five: fast travel comes late and costs 4; the lock-on 4;
  cannot tell the game from their own machine 1; one mistake locks content away 1; getting lost is part
  of it 1. Only the lock-on is on both lists.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Never take the controller away for long.**
   Evidence: stuns 13 (31% recommenders), slow weapon handling 23 (30%), no counterplay 16 (25%),
   unfair spawns 30 (37%). These are the complaints that make people leave; balance and price faults
   are forgiven.
   Dominion could: keep stuns and knockdowns short and rare, always with a tell and a way out (dodge,
   break-free, teammate save). Every enemy attack should have an answer by skill. Make reload, swap
   and ready times quick, and let actions cancel into a dodge.
   Strength: **strong** (clear split across several complaints).
2. **Aim help must respect weak points.**
   Evidence: lock-on holds the body 4 (only here); shots go where they want 14; every enemy fought the
   same way 18; 22 of 39 lock-on and aim-assist mentions in the study are this game.
   Dominion could: if it adds aim help or lock-on for pad players, let it snap to weak points or
   release near them; keep spread small enough that aim matters; give enemies weak points that need
   different answers, not just "head and back".
   Strength: **medium**.
3. **Spawns must give the player a chance.**
   Evidence: unfair spawns 30 times, mostly from people who left, and rising (4.5 to 22.6 per 100);
   enemies always know where you are 6.
   Dominion could: spawn enemies out of sight with an audio or visual tell, never right behind a
   player with no warning; test spawn points in every arena.
   Strength: **medium to strong**.
4. **Third-person movement must feel quick.**
   Evidence: moving feels slow and heavy 10 times; missing modern moves 6; the cross-game page says a
   third-person shooter is judged on how it moves, and a slow dash or slide is the complaint.
   Dominion could: tune sprint, slide and dodge for speed and quick response before release.
   Strength: **medium**.
5. **Hard is good when it is fair, and when learning pays off.**
   Evidence: satisfyingly hard 39; gets good once learned 24; but badly scaled difficulty 17 and one
   part far harder than the rest 13.
   Dominion could: offer hard modes that reward skill, with even steps between levels, and teach the
   key trick (dodge, weak points) early so the "click" comes in the first session.
   Strength: **medium**.
6. **Teach the main trick early.**
   Evidence: *"I hated the game until I learned this trick"* (78807510); 24 say it only got good once
   learned.
   Dominion could: make the first run show the core moves clearly, so players do not quit before the
   game clicks.
   Strength: **medium**.
7. **Balance faults are forgiven, but they are said a lot.**
   Evidence: some choices useless 26 (73% recommenders); one choice dominates 25 (72%); only a few
   builds work 15; many builds praised 31.
   Dominion could: test that every gun and ability has a use; nerf or buff in patches; do not panic,
   but do not ignore it.
   Strength: **medium**.
8. **Level design is a strong praise point, even for critics.**
   Evidence: well-built levels 46, the top praise, from both camps; more than one way in 16; but
   spaces too small 5 (first named here).
   Dominion could: make arenas layered with routes and secrets, and leave room to dodge and use the
   camera.
   Strength: **medium**.
9. **Show players what is left to earn.**
   Evidence: the "does not show what is left to earn" complaint was first named here, 2 times; a
   checklist was asked for (no count).
   Dominion could: give a codex or unlock list showing locked items and how to get them.
   Strength: **weak**.
10. **One graphics option should never wreck performance.**
    Evidence: one setting causes the slowdown, 6 times; players had to find it.
    Dominion could: test each high setting for cost; flag the expensive ones in the menu; give
    sensible defaults.
    Strength: **medium**.
11. **A third-person camera must not block the view or the shot.**
    Evidence: the camera hides the action 4; disorienting 2; gets in the way 1; scenery swallows a
    shot 1 (later seen in more third-person games).
    Dominion could: test camera collision and shots near cover, rails and fences.
    Strength: **weak to medium** here (stronger across the study).
12. **Expect to be compared; be clear what you are.**
    Evidence: describing by other games 76 (17.5 per 100); "nothing like it" 27 vs "Remnant did it
    better" 25; "another game does it better" rose from 2.6 to 22.6 per 100 over the game's life.
    Dominion could: know the games it will be named next to, and be better at one clear thing.
    Strength: **medium**.
13. **Price faults are forgiven if the game is liked, but they still cost.**
    Evidence: too pricey 38 times, 63% from recommenders.
    Dominion could: price to content; know that even fans will say it.
    Strength: **medium**.
14. **Answering reviews and showing you listen is noticed even in a solo game.**
    Evidence: listens and acts 9; replied to my review 1; the studio lost my trust 4.
    Dominion could: reply to store reviews and patch what players name.
    Strength: **weak**.
15. **Co-op and live updates fill a lot of what players talk about.**
    Evidence: with no other players, game-design talk rose to 53.6% (30.3% in co-op games), and talk
    about players fell to 0.8% (13.6%).
    Dominion could: expect its co-op, matchmaking and update talk to be a big share of its reviews,
    and plan for it. Solo runs will be judged on the shooting and enemies alone, with no friends to
    carry them (the cross-game page's reading).
    Strength: **medium** (one game, clear numbers).
16. **A loss must feel like the player's fault.**
    Evidence: losing feels random 24 against losing feels earned 13; the rules favour the enemy 17;
    traps and hazards punish unfairly 14; enemies ignore physical logic 10.
    Dominion could: make every death readable (what hit you, from where, and what you could have
    done), keep enemies under the same rules as players, and mark hazards clearly.
    Strength: **medium**.
17. **Pad and mouse must both play well, with no switching.**
    Evidence: you have to switch input device to play well 6; controls unresponsive 9 against
    responsive 13; an expected button missing 2; cannot rebind 1.
    Dominion could: test every action on pad and on mouse and keyboard on its own; ship full rebinding.
    Strength: **weak to medium**.
18. **Bosses must not be long chores or bullet sponges.**
    Evidence: bosses are a chore 12; bullet sponges 9.
    Dominion could: give bosses phases and weak points that change the fight, rather than more health.
    Strength: **medium**.
19. **Let players fix a bad build, and do not starve them.**
    Evidence: too few resources 16; choices cannot be undone 7; only a few builds work 15; grindy 7.
    Dominion could: allow a cheap reset of perks or upgrades between runs, and tune drops so trying a
    new build does not cost hours.
    Strength: **weak to medium**.
20. **Players want to change how they look.**
    Evidence: cannot change how you look 12.
    Dominion could: give each squad member some look options from the start.
    Strength: **weak to medium**.
21. **Keep the trip back short.**
    Evidence: fast travel comes late and costs something 4; a harsh restart after death 9; one mistake
    locks content away 1.
    Dominion could: after a wipe or a death, get players back into the action quickly; never let one
    mistake shut content for good.
    Strength: **weak**.

### Limits
- English only: 455 non-English reviews (51% of the total) are unread.
- Census: no sampling error, but small numbers after 2023 (31 reviews in 2024-2026).
- 58 reviews (13.3%) were edited after posting and are filed under the date first written.
- One review labelled English is in Portuguese (177878863); it was read on its content.
- The game is finished; these numbers change only if someone writes a new review.
- Single-player: co-op and live-service lessons cannot come from this game.
- Loot luck beyond "luck decides the outcome" (7) and grind beyond "grindy" (7) are not detailed on the
  pages.
- Claims about a free copy and platform rewards are what reviewers say.
- The recommender shares, the year table, the 49-subject table and the lock-on word count are from
  the 2026-09-10 page and were not re-run.
- **What changed from the first version of this entry:** the rebuilt pages now give counts where the
  entry said "no count" or "not counted" (bosses are a chore 12, spaces too small 5, does not show what
  is left to earn 2, content length and repeat play); the cheat count is now 2 times, not one review;
  the "only in this game" list changed between 2026-09-10 and 2026-10-03, and the trading-card bullet
  is now filed as a platform reward. Counts moved slightly between the two dates: things said 1,781
  then 1,785; nothing specific 79 then 73; complaints 948 then 947; how the game plays 53.6% then
  53.4%.

---

## The small-game block (ten small games read whole)

**Read:** 751 of 751 English reviews across ten games (100%). Every English review was read, so there
is no margin of error on any game. Checked against Steam's own English totals. **65.1% thumbs up**
across the block. Read on 2026-09-10, the same day Rico cleared it.
**Pages:** `findings/roguelike-block.md`, `findings/cross-game.md` section 15, and two pages per game
for the eight with more than forty reviews: the full read (`findings/<slug>-english.md`) and, since
2026-10-03, a ranked page (`findings/<slug>.md`). VOIDCRISIS and Town Of The Dead Life have no page of
their own.

### What the block was, and why it was read
The block is every small third-person game sold as a roguelike that had 100 or more Steam reviews and
had failed or been forgotten. They were measured on 2026-09-02; Rico put them off that day (*"We could
touch upon these at a later time"*), then cleared all ten as one block on 2026-09-10 (*"start the
roguelike block"*). Most of them have very few English reviewers: Zombie Girl has 979 reviews and 89 in
English; Town Of The Dead Life has 166 and one.

**The question the block was chosen to answer:** can the study say *why* a game with a hundred reviews
failed, when the honest answer is usually "nobody showed up" and not "the combat was bad"?

Not all ten are third-person roguelikes in the end: ZCREW is top-down, Banzai Escape is an on-rails
phone port, Alien Dawn is VR open-world survival, SCP: Abhorrent is a free thirty-minute horror game.
Four are co-op: ZCREW, ArcRunner, Alien Dawn and VOIDCRISIS.

| Game | Reviews read | Thumbs up | Things said per review | Praise for each complaint |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Zombie Girl | 89 | 73.0% | 2.97 | 0.53 |
| ZCREW | 77 | 49.4% | 2.78 | 0.42 |
| ArcRunner | 177 | 68.4% | 2.55 | 0.60 |
| FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL | 116 | 69.8% | 2.65 | 0.45 |
| Banzai Escape | 80 | 63.8% | 2.25 | 0.50 |
| SCP: Abhorrent | 69 | 63.8% | 1.41 | 1.18 (mostly ironic praise) |
| Alien Dawn | 74 | 63.5% | 2.03 | 1.03 (mostly praise for the two developers) |
| Die After Sunset | 51 | 58.8% | 2.33 | 0.38 |
| VOIDCRISIS | 17 | 64.7% | 2.18 | 0.29 |
| Town Of The Dead Life | 1 | (one thumb up) | 4 things said | none against |
| **Whole block** | **751** | **65.1%** | **2.43** (1,823 things said) | **0.56** |

These are the figures from the day the block was read. **The 2026-10-03 recount** (cross-game section
15) counts every praise and every complaint per 100 reviews, for the eight games with a page of their
own; a few comments moved between counts, so it differs a little from the table above:

| Game | Things said per review | Praise per 100 reviews | Complaints per 100 reviews | Praise for each complaint | Median hours shown on a review |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Zombie Girl | 3.00 | 80.9 | 152.8 | 0.53 | 2 |
| ZCREW | 2.79 | 72.7 | 171.4 | 0.42 | 2 |
| ArcRunner | 2.55 | 85.3 | 141.2 | 0.60 | 4 |
| FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL | 2.65 | 69.8 | 156.0 | 0.45 | 5 |
| Banzai Escape | 2.25 | 66.2 | 131.2 | 0.50 | 3 |
| SCP: Abhorrent | 1.41 | 66.7 | 53.6 | 1.24 | 0 |
| Alien Dawn | 2.03 | 94.6 | 90.5 | 1.04 | 1 |
| Die After Sunset | 2.33 | 58.8 | 154.9 | 0.38 | 1 |

On the recount, two games, not one, have more praise than complaint: SCP: Abhorrent (ironic) and
Alien Dawn (real, about the people). The hours column is from each game's own page; most block
reviewers had played a few hours at most.

### The short version (plain words)
In small games, one review in six is about the price or how much game there is, and one in nine is a
thumbs up that says nothing. Players grade small games against the size of the team. Five of the nine
real games were praised for "the developers listen" in year one and called abandoned within two years;
only Alien Dawn kept its players' trust. Two of the ten have review pools that are mostly a fight about
fan service, not play. And in the four co-op games nobody talked about other players at all: there was
nobody there to talk about. Reviewers judged them as small, cheap, rough games and liked them anyway.
The small games also gave the second and third sightings of things that big games had only shown once.

### Block-wide findings

**1. Price and amount are what small games are judged on.**
| What players said | Reviews | Share of 751 |
|---|---|---|
| Price or amount, in total | 118 | 15.7% |
| - the price is fair | 38 | |
| - there is too little game | 32 | |
| - the price is too high for what it is | 26 | |
| - buy it only on sale | 25 | |
| A thumbs up that says nothing | 82 | 10.9% |
| Described by naming other games | 53 | 7.1% |
| Graded against the size of the studio | 24 | 3.2% |
| The developers listen and act | 23 | 3.1% |

The recommenders write the same sentence in every game: *it is short, it is janky, it is cheap, I liked
it.* Examples: 7 FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL reviewers name the bundle price they paid; all 5 of Banzai
Escape's "too short" comments are thumbs up; all 14 of Zombie Girl's "price is fair" comments are
thumbs up; all 11 of ZCREW's "price is fair" comments are thumbs up. FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL's price talk
is 11.1% of everything said about it, the highest of any game in the study.

**2. The studio is the ruler.** 24 reviews say outright they are judging a one- or two-person team, and
another 12 are written to the studio rather than to a buyer. In the nine big games of the study,
judging by studio size came up in 0.7% of reviews; in this block it is 3.2%. One Zombie Girl reviewer
says the thumbs up is encouragement to the maker, not a verdict.

**3. The year-one-to-year-three arc.** "The developers listen and act" was said 23 times, every one in
the game's launch window (2018 to 2023). "Updates stopped" was said 16 times, every one a year or more
after launch (2021 to 2026). "Still supported" was said 3 times (Alien Dawn 2, Zombie Girl 1).

| Game | "The developers listen" | "The developers left" | "Still supported" |
|---|---|---|---|
| ArcRunner | 10 | 9 | 0 |
| Zombie Girl | 3 | 2 | 1 |
| ZCREW | 1 | 2 | 0 |
| Die After Sunset | 1 | 1 | 0 |
| VOIDCRISIS | 0 | 2 | 0 |
| Alien Dawn | 7 | 0 | 2 |

Five of the nine real games turned from "the developers listen" to "the developers left" inside two
years, and the same review that praised the developers is often the one later edited to say they left:
*"the devs really listen to the community, insane the speed they're putting patches out"* became
*"absolute GEM that the devs seemed to have abandoned"* (ArcRunner, 137512385, May 2023 then January
2024). One ArcRunner reviewer votes thumbs down while calling the game fun, as a protest against the
studio leaving (189831000). The thumb follows the arc: ArcRunner fell from 74% thumbs up in 2023 to 45%
in 2025, Die After Sunset from 71% in early access to 40% in its 1.0 year and 17% after, ZCREW from 56%
at the early-access launch to 44% from 2023 on. Alien Dawn is the exception, and its reviews say so in
2025. Banzai Escape is the exception the other way: never praised for updates, never called abandoned,
and the same ammo complaint every year from 2016 to 2026.

**4. Two review pools that are fights about fan service.**
| | Zombie Girl | FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL |
|---|---|---|
| The argument | the store's adult-content tags lied | the black shadow under the skirt is censorship |
| Reviews making the claim | 12 (9 thumbs down) | 12 (9 thumbs down) |
| Reviews rejecting it | 8 (all 8 thumbs up) | 11 (all 11 thumbs up) |
| Share of the game's helpful votes on the claim | not given | 52% |

19 of Zombie Girl's 89 reviews and 22 of FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL's 116 are this argument, and the two
sides split the thumb almost perfectly. Take them out and the rest are 77.1% and 72.3% thumbs up, with
little to fight about. The study records both sides and does not judge either. In FULL METAL
SCHOOLGIRL the seven most-helpful English reviews are all the censorship claim, and five of them are
under ten words: a buyer sorting by "most helpful" sees nothing about how it plays.

**5. No players, no community talk.** Subjects the nine big games talk about a lot never came up in
the block at all. 24 subjects the big games use were never used in the block; the top five:
| What players talk about | Times said in the nine big games | In the block |
|---|---|---|
| The player community's habits, in-jokes and rituals | 1,325 | 0 |
| How other players behave | 315 | 0 |
| How they found the game | 235 | 0 |
| Friends lists, chat and other social features | 181 | 0 |
| The moment a reward drops | 82 | 0 |

The four co-op games are included in those zeros. A game with 80 players has no shared ritual, no
in-joke, no bad behaviour to complain about. The block's answer to its question: **the study can tell a
game failed for lack of players, because the places where players would be are empty.** What the
co-op games' reviewers do say is that nobody is there: ZCREW 4 and Alien Dawn 5 say nobody plays, so
there are no groups; ArcRunner 2 call it a dead game. The only community topic that survives a game
with no players is how the developers talk to people (Rico ruled on 2026-09-10 that it stays filed
under community).

**6. Small games supply the second sighting.** Eight new kinds of comment were added to the study's
list during the block; six were claims already scattered in bigger games one at a time, which a small
game read whole finally confirmed (for example, shots stopped by scenery in third person: one each in
Helldivers 2 and Immortal: Unchained, then four in ArcRunner; a reviewer disclosing a free copy from the
studio had never been captured at all until ZCREW made it three sightings). The study's own line: *"A
77-review game read whole tips a claim that 800,000 reviews sampled at 1,626 cannot."* Four kinds of
comment are used only by block games. The small games did not find new kinds of complaint; they
confirmed ones the big samples could not. Across the block, 327 different kinds of comment were used.

**7. Where the money goes wrong is inside the game, not only on the store page.** Banzai Escape's
pay-for-ammo rule (17, 11 thumbs down, ten years), FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL's used-up floor keys (9),
VOIDCRISIS's paid AI teammates (3) and ArcRunner's upgrades wiped on finishing (12, 7 from people who
still recommend it) are four games punishing players for using what they earned or needed.

**8. Getting out is the moment that must always work.** ZCREW's evacuation helicopter that will not
come is among its 11 game-stopping bugs (10 thumbs down), and 3 more ZCREW reviewers resent the timer
that fails the mission on the way to it. In Alien Dawn, a bug stopped one player boarding the
helicopter. Two of the block's games end a session by getting out, and both drew this complaint.

**9. Open questions the block left (each seen in one game only, not yet confirmed):**
- The thumbs up given as encouragement to the studio, not as a verdict (Zombie Girl, 1 review). It now
  has its own name in the study, used by this game alone.
- Permanent upgrades make early floors trivial and nothing lets you skip them (ArcRunner, 2 times). The
  next floor-climbing roguelite decides whether it holds.
- The floor-skipping key that is used up when you use it (FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL, 9 times): it also
  rests on one game.
- The game changes your aim sensitivity by itself near traps (FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL, 5 times on the
  first count, 4 on the recount, patched).
- AI teammates cost in-game currency every run (VOIDCRISIS, 3 times).
- A new study topic was created for players who need text instead of memory (from one ArcRunner
  review); the first comment filed under it is that the game relies on remembering icons with no text.
  It now has two sightings in two games; the positive side has one.
- How the 1,849 non-English reviews would change any of this: Zombie Girl's English 9% is 22 points
  more positive than its other 91%.

### VOIDCRISIS (no page of its own)
17 English reviews read of 141 in all languages; 64.7% thumbs up in English (Steam, all languages:
53.9%, Mixed). 37 things said, 2.18 per review, 0.29 praise for each complaint: the most negative text
in the block. A four-player co-op mech game in early access from August 2022; reviewers argue whether
it is tower defence or a mech battle game without human opponents (*"Gundam mixed with League of
Legends"*, 120923709). From the block page and the open-questions file:
- **Abandoned fast.** 2 reviews: *"not even three months in early access and they abandon ship"*
  (125209428, who says it was due to poor sales; not checked).
- **AI teammates cost currency every run.** 3 times it was said, all this game. *"you have to buy VA's
  (Mech suit) for the CPU's with in game currency each time to join you on solo play"* (120026161).
  One player's counter-advice: *"hire an AI's (CPU's) to fill the slots, don't turn the game into solo
  slog"* (149271537).
From the review notes (no counts on the pages for these): solo was *"a forty-minute slog"* while one
human with two AI teammates took eight minutes (120026122); the AI teammates run in and die (120026161,
120056760), though a 2023 reviewer found them *"surprisingly competent"* (149271537); four players who
split roles well make a strong team (120058013, 120279267); the tutorial is poor (120026122, 120279267,
120376388); network errors in multiplayer meant one player never finished the fifth mission
(120221788); failing a mission restarts you from the first and makes you rebuy the mechs (125209428);
bosses have cheap instant deaths (125209428); one map, lightly randomised (149271537); by 2023 it was
*"almost impossible to find anyone to play with"* (149271537). One reviewer refunded it as too little for
the price (120026161); another says it should have been priced at $15 to $20 (120047206).

### Town Of The Dead Life (no page of its own)
1 English review of 166 in all languages (Steam, all languages: 68.1%, Mostly Positive). The one
reviewer (95049171, thumbs up) says it mixes strategy, crafting, survival, city building, tower
defence and roguelike well, the resource management is deep, it has an English translation and it is
cheap for what you get. He also links his own curator page. Nothing to learn for Dominion from one
review; its English audience barely exists.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **A small game is forgiven for being short or rough, never for not working.**
   Evidence: ZCREW's game-stopping bugs were said 11 times, 10 thumbs down; Zombie Girl's "too short"
   14 times, 12 thumbs up; Banzai Escape's "too short" 5, all thumbs up.
   Dominion could: put "every mission and every extraction can always finish" ahead of new content.
   Strength: medium to strong (clear in two games, matches the rest of the block).
2. **Plan the after-launch years, not just the launch month.**
   Evidence: "the developers listen" 23 times, all in launch windows; "updates stopped" 16 times, all a
   year or more later; five of nine games turned within two years; Alien Dawn kept updating and kept
   its praise (7 listen, 0 left, 2 still supported). Cross-game section 15 names this lesson "A small
   studio's early listening is praised most and trusted least; keep it going".
   Dominion could: set an update rhythm the team can keep for years, say what it is, and when it must
   slow, say so openly.
   Strength: strong for this block (five games, same shape); medium as a general rule.
3. **Price for the hours and the polish.**
   Evidence: 118 of 751 reviews (15.7%) are about price or amount; 26 too high, 25 sale only, 38 fair,
   32 too little. FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL at $49.99 drew 22 "too high", 14 of them from fans. Grading
   against the studio's size is 3.2% here against 0.7% in the nine big games: a small studio's
   roughness is forgiven; too little game for the price is not.
   Dominion could: test how many hours a team gets out of the game before setting the price; expect
   sales to be how many players arrive.
   Strength: strong (the biggest single theme in the block).
4. **A co-op game needs a way to play when nobody is online.**
   Evidence: the four co-op games produced zero comments about other players (player culture is 1,325
   comments in the nine big games); ZCREW 4 and Alien Dawn 5 say nobody plays; VOIDCRISIS: *"almost
   impossible to find anyone to play with"*, solo *"a forty-minute slog"*; its AI teammates cost
   currency every run (3).
   Dominion could: make solo and two-player runs fair (scale enemies to team size), offer free AI
   teammates if it has them, and make inviting friends to a listen-server game very easy, since
   strangers may not be there.
   Strength: medium (the zeros are clear; the VOIDCRISIS details are a few reviews).
5. **Never charge players for basics or bots.**
   Evidence: Banzai Escape's pay-for-ammo rule, 17 times, 11 thumbs down, for ten years; VOIDCRISIS's
   paid AI teammates, 3; FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL's used-up floor keys, 9.
   Dominion could: keep ammo, AI help and checkpoints free inside a run; put costs on choices, not on
   being able to play.
   Strength: medium.
6. **The store page must match the game exactly.**
   Evidence: two of ten games have review pools that are mostly a fight over fan service (19 and 22
   reviews); in FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL 12 reviews carry 52% of all helpful votes. Cross-game section 15
   adds this to "Decide fan-service choices on purpose": such choices draw attention out of all
   proportion to their count.
   Dominion could: check its own and player-added tags before and after launch, decide fan-service
   choices on purpose, and say plainly what the game is.
   Strength: medium.
7. **Expect to be described by the genre leader's name.**
   Evidence: 53 of 751 reviews (7.1%) describe a game by naming another; in ArcRunner it is tied for
   the most common thing said, and the most-helpful review is three words naming Risk of Rain 2; 5 of
   ZCREW's 10 comparisons name Helldivers.
   Dominion could: decide which game players will compare it to, and make the difference obvious in
   the first run and on the store page.
   Strength: medium.
8. **Be visible as people.**
   Evidence: Alien Dawn, 21 of 74 reviews about the two developers, 20 warm; 24 block reviews grade
   against studio size and 12 more write to the studio; replies on reviews changed one verdict (SCP:
   Abhorrent) and became a three-year scorecard in another (ZCREW).
   Dominion could: answer reviews where a fix is coming, keep every public promise, and be present in
   its community.
   Strength: medium.
9. **Third-person shots must not be eaten by scenery.**
   Evidence: 6 times across three games, 4 in ArcRunner.
   Dominion could: check the shot from the camera and from the gun, and show when the gun cannot reach
   the aim point.
   Strength: medium.
10. **Movement and dodge feel are where roguelite shooters lose players.**
    Evidence: ArcRunner "too slow" 17 times (largest complaint), Die After Sunset 7; VOIDCRISIS "too
    slow and boring for a mecha shooter" (125209428); Die After Sunset's dodge unreliable 3; Zombie
    Girl no proper dodge 2; FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL's perfect dodge only works standing still 2.
    Dominion could: playtest sprint, dodge and speed early and often.
    Strength: medium.
11. **Full release must not be worse than early access.**
    Evidence: Die After Sunset fell from 71% to 40% to 17% thumbs up across early access, 1.0 year and
    after; ZCREW's bug reports are all after 1.0.
    Dominion could: lock features well before 1.0 and test the first run end to end.
    Strength: medium.
12. **Long runs need a save, and earned skips should not be used up.**
    Evidence: FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL, runs too long 8, no save 4, used-up keys 9; ArcRunner, no way to
    skip outgrown floors 2 and upgrades wiped on finishing 12; ArcRunner players praise saving and
    resuming a run (3).
    Dominion could: keep runs to a session, let the host save between stages, keep earned progress.
    Strength: weak to medium.
13. **Readability basics: brightness, landmarks, words on icons.**
    Evidence: SCP: Abhorrent too dark 6 (a brightness slider flipped one thumb); empty spaces with
    nothing to remember in Alien Dawn (4), ArcRunner and Zombie Girl; one ArcRunner reviewer could not
    remember icons; ArcRunner enemies blend into the background 6 and poor lighting makes levels hard
    to read 6.
    Dominion could: ship brightness calibration, give each map strong landmarks, keep enemies apart
    from the scenery's colours, add text to icons.
    Strength: weak to medium.
14. **Extraction is the moment that must never fail or punish.**
    Evidence: ZCREW's helicopter that will not come (among 11 game-stopping bugs, 10 thumbs down) and
    its timer that fails the mission on the way (3); Alien Dawn's helicopter that one player could not
    board.
    Dominion could: give extraction a backup trigger and a host command to force it, make the timer a
    pressure rather than an instant fail, and test it with every player count and under host lag.
    Strength: weak by count, high stakes for an extraction game.
15. **Every weapon and upgrade in the pool has to change something.**
    Evidence: ArcRunner many guns and skills useless 12, upgrades dull 6; FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL in-run
    upgrades only small number changes 8; Die After Sunset builds barely change anything 4 and "not
    really a roguelite" 2; ZCREW upgrades mostly bigger numbers 2.
    Dominion could: make each upgrade change a behaviour, and cut or lift the options nobody picks.
    Strength: medium (four games, same complaint).
16. **Enemies must play fair: warn before hitting, respect cover and walls.**
    Evidence: Banzai Escape unfair deaths 8 (hit behind cover, no warning); Die After Sunset hit from
    off screen 2 and cannot tell when being hit 2; Zombie Girl enemies shoot through walls 4; ZCREW
    enemies appear behind you in cleared areas 2.
    Dominion could: give every attack a tell, show where hits come from, test that enemy shots respect
    cover, and spawn enemies out of sight.
    Strength: medium (four games).

### Limits
- Every English review was read, so there is no margin of error, but every year-by-year figure rests
  on small numbers; each page states its counts.
- 1,849 reviews in other languages across the ten were not read. Zombie Girl's English reviewers are
  only 9% of its audience and 22 points more positive than the rest.
- Three games were first counted from their full-release date and missed early-access reviews (ZCREW
  35, Die After Sunset 40, VOIDCRISIS 11). This was fixed before reading.
- Two games (Banzai Escape, SCP: Abhorrent) have gained one review each since the pull.
- 9 reviews marked English across the block were in other languages; they were read from content.
- The VOIDCRISIS notes beyond the block page come from the per-review notes, not a findings page; they
  carry no counts.
- The Die After Sunset page says its studio later made ArcRunner; the ArcRunner page names a different
  studio. Not settled here.
- Claims about patches, prices paid and why a studio stopped are what reviewers say, not checked.
- **What changed from the earlier entry:** each of the eight larger games now has a ranked page as well
  as its full read. Cross-game section 15 was rebuilt on 2026-10-03 with a recount of praise and
  complaint per 100 reviews (the second table above); on it, Alien Dawn as well as SCP: Abhorrent has
  more praise than complaint, Zombie Girl's things said per review is 3.00 rather than 2.97, and the
  recount differs a little from the figures written at the time, which are left as they were. The
  open question about encouragement now has a name in the study. The aim-sensitivity count is 4 on
  the recount, against 5 first. `findings/roguelike-block.md` itself was not rebuilt and is still dated
  2026-09-10.
- The study is now 19 games and 12,300 English reviews by cross-game section 15's
  count; its total of unread reviews is out of date and should be recomputed before it is quoted.
- The direction check across the study found 0 disagreements in more than 32,000 comments when the
  block was read.

---

## ArcRunner

**Read:** all 177 of 177 English reviews (100%). Every English review was read, so there is no margin
of error. Written April 2023 to July 2026 (30 months, every month read). **68.4% thumbs up** in the
English reviews, 121 up and 56 down (Steam, all languages: 69.1% of 236 reviews, Mostly Positive).
**Pages:** `findings/arcrunner-english.md`, `findings/arcrunner.md`, `findings/roguelike-block.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` section 15.

**What it is:** a co-op roguelite shooter (runs that end, with some upgrades that stay between runs),
third person, up to three players. Made by Trickjump Games. Released 2023-04-27 at $20, with a free
demo. Sci-fi, cyberpunk look. The engine is not named on the page. How it is hosted is not on the
page as a fact; one reviewer complains it is peer to peer, not dedicated servers. It is the largest
game in the small-game block and the only one with a real launch: 137 of its 177 English reviews came
in 2023. The busiest months were May 2023 (64 reviews) and April 2023 (44): 108 of the 177 in the
first two months.

**How close to Dominion:** very close in shape. Third person, co-op, sci-fi, built on runs, a shooter.
It is three players, not four. It has no extraction. Hosting is not recorded beyond one reviewer.

### The short version (plain words)
Players liked it at launch and liked how fast the studio patched it, then watched the studio go quiet.
The thumbs-up share fell from 74% in 2023 to 56% in 2024, 45% in 2025 and 0% in 2026 (2 reviews). Ten
reviews praised the studio for listening, all in 2023; nine called the game abandoned, from 2024 on.
Almost everyone describes it by naming Risk of Rain 2. The biggest design complaints are that you move
slowly (17 times it was said) and that finishing the game on normal wipes all your permanent upgrades
(12 times it was said, 7 of them by people who still recommend it). The other big complaints are about
sameness: many guns and skills are useless (12), the levels repeat (12), key tools are locked at the
start (10) and there is too little in the game (9). What players like most is the neon look (14) and
playing with friends (12). Across all reviews there are 85.3 praise points and 141.2 complaint points
per 100 reviews.

### What players praised, most to least
Counts are from the 2026-10-03 re-run of the page's tables.
1. **Thumbs up with no reason given.** 23 of the 121 thumbs-up reviews (19%) say nothing at all.
2. **The look.** 14 times a reviewer praised the striking neon, Tron-like look.
3. **It is much better with friends.** 12 times it was said. One more says it works with strangers
   too, and one plays it with family.
4. **The studio listened and patched fast at launch.** 10 reviews, all in 2023. *"the devs really
   listen to the community, insane the speed they're putting patches out"* (137512385, May 2023).
5. **You feel yourself getting stronger from run to run.** 7 times it was said.
6. **Upgrades and weapon mods change how you play.** 7 times it was said.
7. **The shooting feels good.** 6 times it was said.
8. **The music fits.** 6 times it was said.
9. **Each zone brings new enemies.** 4 times it was said.
10. **It is hard in a good way.** 4 times it was said.
11. **The demo let them try it first.** 4 times it was said.
12. **The difficulty climbs at a fair rate.** 3 times it was said.
13. **The price is fair.** 3 times it was said.
14. **You can save and quit a run and pick it up later.** 3 times it was said. One reviewer says this
    works if you host publicly.
15. **The controls are clear and responsive.** 2 times it was said (one after rebinding for a keyboard
    that is not laid out the usual way).
16. **It keeps pulling you back.** 2 times it was said.
17. **The mood draws you in.** 2 times it was said.
18. **A slow, calm pace.** The re-run counts 2 reviews on "the slow pace is relaxing"; the earlier
    count was three recommenders who say the slow speed is the reason to buy (see Limits). *"risk of
    rain was to fast for me, if ur older with slower reflexes give this a shot"* (137464145).
19. **It is better than the games it is compared with.** 2 times it was said. *"This reminds me of
    Mass Effect multiplayer"* (137415655, 77 people found it helpful). *"arcrunner is more like its
    parents, doom and returnal"* (139378615).
20. **Each character plays differently.** 2 times it was said.
21. **The levels are well built.** 2 times it was said.
22. **Updates keep coming.** 2 times it was said.
23. **Enemies are simple and readable, "like a game of chess".** 1 reviewer.
24. **The systems are simple, and that is all it needs.** 1 reviewer.
25. **No paid extras in the game at all**, and **bought it to support the studio**: 1 reviewer each.

### What players complained about, most to least
Counts are from the 2026-10-03 re-run.
1. **You move too slowly.** 17 times it was said, the largest complaint, 7 from thumbs-up reviews and
   10 from thumbs-down. *"For a game with 'runner' in the title, you move remarkably slow, even after
   the patch to speed you up."* (138076943). *"Arc-Casual-Jogger"* (154220887). So the studio did
   patch speed, and it was still called slow.
2. **Many guns, grenades, skills and ultimates are too weak to bother with.** 12 times it was said.
3. **The levels look and play the same; the map pool is small.** 12 times it was said.
4. **Finishing the game wipes your permanent upgrades.** 12 times it was said, 7 from thumbs-up
   reviews, so both people who stayed and people who left name it. Finishing the campaign on normal
   resets every permanent upgrade. *"Had lots of fun.... Until the first clear on normal and all your
   meta progression is wiped"* (138323944, thumbs up). *"None of the problems I mentioned until now
   are even remotely as bad as the fact that finishing the game resets all of your metaprogression."*
   (138076943, thumbs down). One reviewer says the studio patched it into a different reset instead
   of removing it (137459084).
5. **It is worse than Risk of Rain 2 (or another game).** 11 times it was said, 10 from thumbs-down
   reviews. *"like playing Risk of Rain 2 and Gunfire Reborn at the same time, but without the fun"*
   (137666125).
6. **Key tools are locked at the start.** 10 times it was said: the second weapon and the ultimate
   ability are locked behind the currency you earn across runs, so the first hours are weak.
7. **Too little in it: too few characters, weapons and levels.** 9 times it was said.
8. **The studio left the game.** 9 reviews from 2024 on. *"absolute GEM that the devs seemed to have
   abandoned"* (137512385, the same review that praised the studio, edited in January 2024).
9. **The guns feel dull and have no punch.** 7 times it was said (against 6 who say the shooting feels
   good).
10. **A few guns or characters are so strong the rest do not matter.** 7 times it was said.
11. **Enemies blend into the background.** 6 times it was said.
12. **The upgrades are dull and do not change how you play.** 6 times it was said (against 7 who say
    they do).
13. **Poor lighting and harsh colours make levels and menus hard to read.** 6 times it was said.
14. **Luck decides whether you find a gun that fits your upgrades.** 5 times it was said.
15. **It feels unfinished.** 5 times it was said.
