16. **They asked for their money back.** 5 reviews.
17. **Exploding objects everywhere in the levels hurt you and your team.** 4 times it was said.
18. **The music is forgettable or grating.** 4 times it was said (against 6 who say it fits).
19. **It gets boring quickly.** 4 times it was said.
20. **The difficulty climbs too abruptly.** 4 times it was said.
21. **Your shots hit the scenery.** 4 times it was said in this game: shots stopped by fences, rails,
    lamp posts, ankle-high ledges, and once by something behind the camera.
22. **Shots go where they want:** slow bullets and odd aim make shots miss. 3 times it was said.
23. **A bug stops play**, such as challenge kills not counting. 3 times it was said.
24. **The same few guns drop over and over.** 3 times it was said.
25. **It is buggy.** 3 times it was said.

Smaller ones from the page: bosses are a chore (3), enemies behave poorly (3), the early floors become
a boring chore you cannot skip (2, see Progression), you have to remember what each gun and skill does
from its picture because there is no text (1 reviewer).

### How it changed over time
| | 2023 | 2024 | 2025 | 2026 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Reviews | 137 | 27 | 11 | 2 |
| Thumbs up | 74% (73.7%) | 56% (55.6%) | 45% (45.5%) | 0% |
| Praise per 100 reviews | 79.6 | 51.9 | 36.4 | 50.0 |
| Complaints per 100 reviews | 136.5 | 188.9 | 81.8 | 50.0 |
| "The studio listens" | 10 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| "Updates stopped" | 3 | 3 | 3 | 0 |

The three "updates stopped" reviews listed under 2023 were all edited in 2024 or later, so they
really belong to 2024 or later. The turn players name is the studio going quiet. One reviewer says
the game is fun and they would give it a thumbs up, but *"as of right now I believe that this is
abandonware"* (189831000), so the thumb down is a protest against the studio, not a verdict on play.
2025 and 2026 together are only 13 reviews, so those percentages are shaky.

- **What fell:** praise per 100 fell from 79.6 in 2023 to 51.9 in 2024 and 36.4 in 2025. "Too slow"
  fell from 11.7 per 100 reviews in 2023 to 3.7 in 2024 and 0 after, which fits the speed patch
  reviewers describe. "The studio listens" was 7.3 per 100 in 2023 and 0 after.
- **What rose:** complaints per 100 rose to 188.9 in 2024. Inside them, "the levels repeat" rose from
  4.4 to 22.2 per 100, "key tools locked at the start" from 4.4 to 14.8, "shots hit the scenery" from
  0 to 14.8, and "the studio stopped updating" from 2.2 to 11.1 in 2024 and 27.3 in 2025. So once the
  speed was fixed, the people who came later complained about sameness, locked tools and the scenery.

### Co-op and online play
- 12 say it is much better with friends; 1 says it is fine with strangers; 1 plays it with family; 1
  says playing alone is punishing.
- Online: 1 complains it is peer to peer, not dedicated servers; 1 could not connect; 1 says online
  play is smooth. 2 call it a dead game.
- One reviewer lists co-op bugs that force a save and reload: loot boxes players can no longer open,
  and an armoury that shows the host no weapons.
- One reviewer says save and quit works if you host publicly.
- Nothing is said about how it scales to the number of players, bots, friendly fire, loot sharing or
  joining a game already running.
- No review talks about a player community, its habits or its in-jokes. The block page reads this as a
  sign there were too few players to form one.

### Combat, movement and feel
- Movement is slow: 17 times it was said, even after a speed patch (see complaints). Movement
  unreliable 1, responsive 1, missing modern moves 1.
- Guns: dull, with no punch 7, against the shooting feels good 6. Shots go where they want 3. Weapon
  handling sluggish 2. Feels like every other shooter 1.
- Balance: 12 say many guns and skills are useless and 7 that a few dominate.
- Third-person camera problem: shots blocked by fences, rails, lamp posts, ankle-high ledges and once
  by something behind the camera. 4 times in ArcRunner; 6 times across three games in the whole
  study (one each in Helldivers 2 and Immortal: Unchained). The study had no name for this until
  ArcRunner supplied the count.
- Seeing things: enemies blend into the background 6; poor lighting and harsh colours make levels and
  menus hard to read 6. One reviewer, playing at 4K on ultra, asks for outlines or a distinct palette
  so enemies stand out.
- Controls: clear and responsive 2; unresponsive 1; actions fire by themselves 1; abilities awkward to
  trigger 1; you have to switch input device to play well 1. Camera: the narrow view is a handicap 1,
  works well 1.
- The Die After Sunset page says ArcRunner's reviewers called its upgrades ("augments") bland. The
  ArcRunner page now counts it: the upgrades are dull and do not change how you play, 6 times.
- Nothing is said about stuns or loss of control.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- Enemies vary from zone to zone 4; poor enemy behaviour 3; forgettable special enemies 2, memorable 1;
  unfair spawns 1; enemies simple and readable "like a game of chess" 1.
- Bosses are a chore 3. One reviewer says only the Eden mid-boss and the final boss were a challenge.
- Difficulty: hard in a good way 4, badly scaled (climbs too abruptly) 4, climbs fairly 3, the player
  is too fragile 2, the lower settings are not worth playing 1, harder only changes the numbers 1.
- Exploding objects all over the levels hurt you and your team: 4 times it was said.
- Permanent upgrades make the first world too easy later on: *"By the time you reach world 3, the world
  1 that was once challenging and fun becomes just a chore as you are over level for those enemies"*
  (138105024). One reviewer says they used a cheat to get through; before this, only one other game
  in the study (Immortal: Unchained) had a reviewer say that.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- Finishing on normal wipes permanent upgrades: 12 times it was said, 7 from people who still
  recommend it. It is the one design choice in the game named on both sides of the thumb.
- The second weapon and the ultimate are locked behind the between-run currency: 10 times it was said.
- Progression feels satisfying 7. Upgrades change how you play 7, against dull upgrades 6.
- Luck: luck decides whether you find a gun that fits your upgrades 5; the same few guns drop over and
  over (not random enough) 3; randomness keeps it fresh 1. One reviewer asks for a shop inside the run
  so the right gun can be found.
- Gated behind farming 2; every unlock is a sideways swap, not a step up 2; grindy 1; nothing left to
  chase 1; everything can be earned 1.
- Using a weapon enough lets you start runs with it, and permanent upgrades keep growing, so the early
  areas become *"trivial time-wasters. Roguelites with metaprogression need floor skip options"*
  (138076943). 2 times it was said, both in this game; the study expects it to come up again in
  other roguelites.

### Runs, content and replay value
- Levels repeat: 12. Too little content (characters, weapons, levels): 9. Runs out fast: 4. Keeps
  pulling you back: 2. Nothing happens between fights: 2. The early stages of every run are dull 1.
  Levels too short 1.
- You can stop a run and resume it later: 3.
- The forced replay of early floors you have outgrown (2) and the wipe on finishing (12).
- The Alien Dawn page says ArcRunner's reviewers said its arenas have nothing in them to remember; no
  count is given there. The ArcRunner page counts "nothing happens between fights" at 2.
- No run length is recorded on the page.

### Money and price
- $20 with a demo. The demo let them try it first: 4.
- Fair price 3; too high for what it is 2; buy it on sale only 3; refunded 5.
- No paid extras at all 1; bought it to support the studio 1.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Bugs: 7 in all (buggy 3, a bug breaks play 3, one saying challenge kills do not track; rare and
  minor 1).
- Crashes and stability: 5 (crashes again and again 1, crashes on a specific event 1, makes the whole
  computer unstable 1, the glitches are half the fun 1, lost progress could be got back 1).
- Performance: 3 (well optimised 1, unsteady frame rate 1, not said what 1). Runs well on my machine 1.
- Co-op bugs that force a save and reload (one reviewer, see Co-op).
- Shots hitting the scenery (4).

### The studio, updates and community
- 10 reviews praise fast patches and listening, all in the first five weeks of 2023.
- 9 reviews call it abandoned, from 2024 on. Often the same reviewer, editing the same review.
- The studio went silent after a bad launch 2; a steady stream of updates 2; open about what it is
  doing 1; waiting on promised content 1; the studio lost my trust 2.
- One reviewer gives a thumbs down only to protest the studio leaving (189831000).
- Speed was patched once; still called slow. The upgrade wipe was patched into a different wipe, the
  reviewer says (137459084).
- 3 reviews are written to the studio, not to people thinking of buying. 7 reviews are adverts for the
  reviewer's own curator page.
- The Die After Sunset page calls ArcRunner the next game from the Die After Sunset studio (Playstark),
  and reads the same "listening, then gone" turn in both games a year apart. The ArcRunner page names a
  different studio. See Limits.

### What players asked for
- A way to skip early floors once you have outgrown them (138076943).
- Faster movement (17 times it was said that it is slow).
- Keep permanent upgrades after finishing the game (12 times the wipe was named).
- Outlines or a distinct colour scheme so enemies stand out (one reviewer, at 4K on ultra).
- A shop inside the run, so the right gun can be found (one reviewer).
- Text that says what each gun and skill does, not just a picture (one reviewer).
- Bug and balance fixes before more characters (one reviewer).
- Continued updates (9 reviews say they stopped).

### Only in this game
- Nothing players said about ArcRunner is said about no other game: the 2026-10-03 re-run finds 0
  kinds of comment used only here. What stands out is the share and the mix, below.
- ArcRunner supplied most of the sightings (4 of 6) for shots stopped by scenery in third person.
- A new topic in the study, "memory and attention" for players who need on-screen text instead of
  remembering icons, was created because of one ArcRunner review (Rico cleared it on 2026-09-10).
- The early-floors-become-a-chore problem (2 times) has only been seen here.
- A group of reviewers who push back on the Risk of Rain comparison. *"maybe I'd simply enjoy one
  thing without constantly being upset that it isn't something else"* (137916317).
- The most-helpful review of the game is three words of comparison: *"Risk of Cyberpunk Rain 2"*
  (137389141, 118 helpful). 18 of the reviews that name another game name Risk of Rain, and naming
  other games is tied for the most common thing said (23 times). The page reads it this way: the
  reviews that recommend it use the comparison as a description; the negative ones use it as a
  verdict.
- Hours shown on reviews are low: median 4 hours; median 1 hour on thumbs-down reviews and 4 on
  thumbs-up. Only 2 reviews show 100 hours or more.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Make sure shots are not eaten by scenery in third person.**
   Evidence: 4 times in ArcRunner, 6 times across three third-person games; in ArcRunner it rose from
   0 in 2023 to 14.8 per 100 reviews in 2024.
   Dominion could: test the shot from the camera against the shot from the gun, so low ledges,
   rails and things behind the camera do not block a shot the crosshair says is clear; show a
   "blocked" marker on the crosshair when the gun cannot reach the aim point.
   Strength: medium (small counts, but in three games and a known third-person fault).
2. **Do not wipe permanent upgrades when players finish the game.**
   Evidence: 12 times it was said, 7 by players who still recommend it; one reviewer calls it worse
   than every other problem combined (138076943).
   Dominion could: keep earned upgrades after a "win"; if a reset is wanted, make it a choice the
   player opts into, with a clear reward.
   Strength: medium.
3. **Let players skip content they have outgrown.**
   Evidence: 2 times it was said, one game: early floors become a chore once upgrades stack up.
   Dominion could: let a team start later in a run, or scale early areas up, once they have proven
   they can clear them.
   Strength: weak.
4. **Do not lock core kit behind the between-run currency.**
   Evidence: 10 times it was said that the second weapon and the ultimate were locked at first; it
   rose from 4.4 to 14.8 per 100 reviews in 2024.
   Dominion could: give every player their core tools from the first run; put side options, not core
   tools, behind unlocks.
   Strength: medium.
5. **Movement speed decides the feel of a shooter built on runs.**
   Evidence: 17 times it was said that it is too slow, the largest complaint, even after a speed
   patch; recommenders liked the slow pace (3 on the first count, 2 on the re-run).
   Dominion could: tune sprint and dodge early with playtests; if a slower pace is part of the
   identity, say so on the store page.
   Strength: medium.
6. **Fast patches at launch win praise, but going quiet later costs more than the praise earned.**
   Evidence: 10 reviews praise listening (all 2023), 9 call it abandoned (2024 on); thumbs up fell
   from 74% to 45% by 2025. One thumbs down is a protest about the studio, not the game.
   Dominion could: plan updates at a pace that can last for years, not a burst at launch; tell
   players when updates will slow down rather than going silent.
   Strength: medium in this game; strong when read with the rest of the block (five games show it).
7. **Expect to be judged against the genre leader.**
   Evidence: naming other games is tied for the most common thing said (23 times); 11 say it is worse
   than another game, 10 of them thumbs down; the most-helpful review is "Risk of Cyberpunk Rain 2".
   Dominion could: decide early which game players will name, and make the difference clear in the
   first minutes and on the store page.
   Strength: medium.
8. **Put words next to icons.**
   Evidence: one reviewer could not remember what each gun and skill did from the picture.
   Dominion could: add short text to every gun, skill and upgrade icon, on hover or hold.
   Strength: weak.
9. **Every gun and skill in the pool has to be worth taking.**
   Evidence: 12 say many guns, grenades, skills and ultimates are too weak to bother with; 7 say a few
   are so strong the rest do not matter; 6 say the upgrades are dull, against 7 who say they change
   how you play.
   Dominion could: track which weapons and upgrades teams pick and win with in playtests, and lift or
   cut the ones nobody takes before adding new ones.
   Strength: medium.
10. **A small set of levels wears out fast once people play for longer.**
    Evidence: 12 say the levels look and play the same, 9 say there is too little content; "the
    levels repeat" rose from 4.4 per 100 reviews in 2023 to 22.2 in 2024.
    Dominion could: give arenas changing layouts, events or objectives, so the same map does not play
    the same way twice, and plan new maps into the update schedule.
    Strength: medium.
11. **Enemies must stand out from a stylish world.**
    Evidence: 6 say enemies blend into the background and 6 that poor lighting and harsh colours make
    levels and menus hard to read, while 14 praise the neon look; one reviewer asks for outlines or a
    distinct palette.
    Dominion could: keep enemy colours and outlines apart from the scenery's colours, and test enemy
    visibility in the darkest and busiest areas.
    Strength: medium.
12. **Hazards in the level should not punish the team more than the enemies do.**
    Evidence: 4 say exploding objects everywhere in the levels hurt them and their team.
    Dominion could: keep exploding objects few and easy to see, and decide on purpose whether they
    hurt teammates.
    Strength: weak.
13. **Co-op bugs that hit only the host or only the guests break the shared run.**
    Evidence: one reviewer lists loot boxes that cannot be opened and an armoury that shows the host
    no weapons, fixed only by save and reload; 1 complains it is peer to peer; 1 could not connect.
    Dominion could: test the shop, the armoury and every pickup from both the host's seat and a
    guest's seat on a listen server, before each update.
    Strength: weak.
14. **Let players stop a run and finish it later.**
    Evidence: 3 praise saving and quitting a run and picking it up later.
    Dominion could: offer save and quit mid-run, at least for a host and their friends.
    Strength: weak.
15. **A demo earns trust for a small game.**
    Evidence: 4 say the demo let them try it first.
    Dominion could: ship a demo or free weekend that shows one full run with friends.
    Strength: weak.
16. **A co-op game with few players has no community to lean on, so playing alone has to hold up.**
    Evidence: no ArcRunner review talks about a player community; across the block, player culture is
    zero in all four co-op games (cross-game section 15); 2 call ArcRunner a dead game; 1 says playing
    alone is punishing.
    Dominion could: make a solo or two-player run fully playable and fun, and do not rely on strangers
    being online.
    Strength: medium (block-wide reading).

### Limits
- Every English review was read: no margin of error. But 2025 and 2026 are only 13 reviews together,
  so their percentages are shaky.
- 17 reviews (9.6%) were edited after posting and are counted on the date they were first written;
  three of them belong to a later year than they are filed under.
- 59 reviews in other languages (25% of the audience) were not read.
- **What changed from the earlier entry:** the game now has its own ranked page,
  `findings/arcrunner.md` (the earlier entry said there was none). The rebuilt page fills in co-op,
  enemies, bosses, price and crashes, which the earlier entry had to leave thin. Counts are from the
  2026-10-03 re-run: 151 praise and 250 complaint points (the 2026-09-10 count was 150 and 252), 153
  kinds of comment (152 before). The calm-pace praise is 2 on the re-run against 3 recommenders on the
  first count; the page did not trace the difference.
- Patches, the upgrade-wipe change, the end of updates and the price are what reviewers say; the page
  did not check them. Steam's own review counts are the only checked figures.
- Reviews written in another language under the English tag were not counted.
- The studio name does not match across pages: this page says Trickjump Games; the Die After Sunset
  page says ArcRunner is the next game from Die After Sunset's studio (Playstark). Not settled here.

---

## Zombie Girl (末世少女 Zombie Girl)

**Read:** all 89 of 89 English reviews (100%). Every English review was read, so there is no margin
of error. Written October 2023 to August 2026 (27 months, every month read). **73.0% thumbs up** in
the English reviews, 65 up and 24 down (Steam, all languages: 51.0% of 979 reviews, Mixed).
**Pages:** `findings/zombie-girl-english.md`, `findings/zombie-girl.md`, `findings/roguelike-block.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` section 15.

**What it is:** a single-player third-person zombie shooter made by one person in Unreal Engine 5.
Released October 2023 at about $7. Floors are generated by the game. Short: about two hours by most
accounts. The store page carries the tags *Sexual Content*, *Nudity* and *NSFW*. All voice audio is
Chinese or Japanese. English speakers are only 9% of its reviewers. The launch month, October 2023,
holds 28 of the 89 English reviews.

**How close to Dominion:** shares the third-person camera, the shooting and Unreal Engine 5. It is
single-player, has no co-op, no runs in Dominion's sense, and is not sci-fi.

### The short version (plain words)
Most of this game's review pool is a fight about fan service, not about play. 19 of the 89 reviews
(one in five) argue about whether the adult store tags lied, and the two sides split the thumb almost
perfectly. Take those out and the other 70 reviews are 77.1% thumbs up. The rest of the case is "short
but cheap": 14 say the price is fair (all thumbs up) and 14 say there is too little game (12 of them
still recommend it). The text is far more negative than the thumb: 152.8 complaints and 80.9 pieces
of praise per 100 reviews, and people who recommend it write most of the complaints, like a wish list
to the maker. The studio was praised for quick updates in 2023 and called gone by 2026.

### What players praised, most to least
1. **The price is fair.** 14 times it was said, all 14 thumbs up. *"its not bad enough to not
   recommend it at the price its being sold at"* (148299530).
2. **"The buyers did not read the tags."** 8 times it was said, all 8 thumbs up: the game never
   promised adult content. *"Game literally does not say that is has sex or nudity in it, if you read
   the description."* (148267026). Three reviewers say the tags were added by Steam users, not the
   studio (*"the 'user tags'"*, 168049101). One argues it cannot be adult content because it is sold
   in Germany (163896162). The page did not check this.
3. **Graded kindly because one person made it.** 8 reviews (9%) say so outright, 7 of them thumbs up.
4. **Thumbs up with no reason given.** 7 times.
5. **The shooting feels good.** 6 times it was said.
6. **Quick updates and a studio that listens, in 2023.** 3 reviews say updates came steadily and 3 say
   the developer listens: *"already got multiple QoL updates... a minimap, new weapons, a melee
   attack"*. One 2024 review says a patch made it better; one more says it is still getting updates.
7. **It is hard in a good way**, especially on the hardest setting. 3 times it was said.
8. **It looks good.** 3 times it was said.
9. **Each visit to an area has a new layout.** 3 times it was said.
10. **It is not like other games of its kind.** 2 times it was said.
11. **It is easy to pick up.** 2 times it was said.
12. **It still has potential.** 2 times it was said.
13. **The defence mode is the best part.** 2 times it was said.
14. One reviewer each: a fan-made add-on makes it better; it won over someone who avoids shooters;
    bought it to back a maker's freedom to make what he wants; it has been reviewed unfairly; the keys
    can be changed; zombies shot in the legs crawl; the maps are nice to move through; it runs on Steam
    Deck; simple, and well made in that simplicity; armour, health and ammo are well balanced; the
    diaries are funny; the plot is simple to follow; you can climb almost anything.

### What players complained about, most to least
1. **Too little game.** 14 times it was said, 12 from thumbs-up reviews (people who stayed). Most say
   about two hours; one reviewer says they finished the story in thirteen minutes (154333287).
2. **The store tags promise adult content the game does not have.** 12 times it was said, 9 from
   thumbs-down reviews (people who left), 3 from thumbs up. *"It promises nudity, sexual content, NSFW
   and fails to deliver."* (148135531, 147 helpful, the most-helpful review of the game).
3. **Not clear where to go.** 7 times it was said, 4 from thumbs-up reviews. Players get lost and ask
   for a map.
4. **Only worth buying on sale.** 5 times it was said.
5. **The story is thin, mostly scattered notes.** 5 times it was said.
6. **Enemies take too many bullets.** 4 times it was said, 2 from thumbs-up reviews.
7. **It is buggy.** 4 times it was said.
8. **Bugs stop play:** stuck on floor clutter, key items not appearing. 4 times it was said.
9. **Enemies spot you across the map and shoot through walls.** 4 times it was said.
10. **Enemy sounds play close by when nothing is near.** 3 times it was said.
11. **Aiming stops when a wall or an enemy is close.** 3 times it was said.
12. **You cannot change how the character looks.** 3 times it was said.
13. **It feels unfinished.** 3 times it was said.
14. **Movement is stiff:** no reloading while running, a slow roll. 2 times it was said.
15. **Enemies swing from foolish to deadly accurate.** 2 times it was said.
16. **Enemies and places look generic.** 2 times it was said.
17. **The view is blocked against walls and in stairwells.** 2 times it was said.
18. **It looks blurry or low-resolution in places.** 2 times it was said.
19. **You can fall out of the level.** 2 times it was said.
20. **No English voices.** All in-game audio is Chinese or Japanese. 2 times it was said.
21. **There is a lot of grinding.** 2 times it was said.
22. **Some keys cannot be changed** (side mouse buttons, movement keys). 2 times it was said.
23. **Thumbs down with no reason given.** 2 times.
24. **There is no proper dodge.** 2 times it was said.
25. **The developer left.** 2 reviews in 2026: *"the developer has run away"* (221730277).

Smaller ones: spider sections, flagged as a problem for people afraid of spiders (1 reviewer); the
Alien Dawn page says Zombie Girl's reviewers said its generated floors have nothing memorable in them,
and the Zombie Girl page counts "nothing memorable" in the levels once.

Overall the text is far more negative than the thumb: 136 complaints against 72 pieces of praise
(0.53 praise for each complaint), and 86 of the 136 complaints (63.2%) were written by people who
recommend the game. The page reads the recommenders as writing a wish list to the developer. Every top
complaint except the tag argument comes mostly from thumbs-up reviews. The 0.53 is the fourth most
negative text in the study, below every game except Terminull Brigade, Redfall and The Anacrusis, all
of which are under 58% on the thumb. Only Deep Rock Galactic has a higher share of complaints from
recommenders (68.5%), and its recommenders write 0.17 complaints each against 1.32 here.

### How it changed over time
| Year | Reviews | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 | What the update talk says |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2023 | 36 | 78% (77.8%) | 100.0 | 200.0 | steady updates (3), the developer listens (3) |
| 2024 | 36 | 64% (63.9%) | 47.2 | 119.4 | a patch made it better (1); waiting on a promised skill tree (2) |
| 2025 | 8 | 100% | 75.0 | 75.0 | *"hasn't received any updates in 2025 atm, I hope it hasn't been abandoned"* |
| 2026 | 9 | 67% (66.7%) | 66.7 | 144.4 | updates stopped (2): *"the developer has run away"* |

2025 and 2026 are 17 reviews together, so their percentages are shaky. The turn players name is the
promised skill tree not arriving and updates stopping.

- **What fell:** both praise and complaints halved from 2023 to 2024; the launch reviews say more of
  everything. "Too little game" fell from 27.8 per 100 reviews to 5.6; "fair price" from 25.0 to 5.6.
- **What rose:** "enemies always know where you are" went from 0 in 2023 to 8.3 per 100 in 2024;
  "updates stopped" appears only in 2026, at 22.2 per 100.

### Co-op and online play
Single-player. No review asks for co-op.

### Combat, movement and feel
- The shooting feels good: 6. Weapon handling sluggish 1; shots go where they want 1; feels like every
  other shooter 1. The 2023 updates added a melee attack and new weapons, players say.
- Aiming stops when a wall or enemy is close, or needs the aim button held: 3.
- Movement stiff (no reload while running, slow roll): 2; no proper dodge: 2; responsive 1 (you can
  climb almost anything).
- Keys: cannot rebind (side mouse buttons, movement keys) 2; rebind anything 1; clear controls 1; aim
  sensitivity cannot be tuned 1; must switch input device 1.
- Camera: blocked against walls and in stairwells 2; moves more than asked 1; works well 1.
- Sound: enemy sounds play close by when nothing is near, 3.
- Loss of control: only one reviewer, who says a bug takes control of the character away entirely.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- Bullet-sponge enemies: 4 (2 from thumbs up).
- Enemies always know where you are and shoot through walls: 4 (none in 2023; 8.3 per 100 in 2024).
- Enemy behaviour swings from foolish to deadly accurate 2; good enemy behaviour 1 (zombies shot in the
  legs crawl); too little variety 1; unfair spawns 1; too few on screen 1; ignores physical logic 1; a
  boss is a chore 1.
- Difficulty: hard in a good way 3 (one on the hardest setting); too easy 1; player too fragile 1; one
  part far harder than the rest 1; harder only changes the numbers 1; the challenge outgrows the
  player 1.
- Spider sections (1 reviewer).

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- Cannot change how the character looks: 3, plus 3 more about outfits as rewards (asking for more, or
  finding the harder ones too hard to earn).
- Grindy 2. Nothing left to chase 1; nothing builds up 1.
- New layouts each visit keep it fresh 3; luck decides 1.
- Easy to grasp 2; armour, health and ammo well balanced 1.
- A skill tree was announced and players waited for it (2 times in 2024).

### Runs, content and replay value
- About two hours of game; too little content was said 14 times, but 12 of those 14 still recommend it.
- Not clear where to go was said 7 times; a minimap was added in an early update, a reviewer says.
- Story thin, mostly scattered notes: 5.
- The defence mode is the best part 2; a mode falls flat 1.
- Levels: well built 1, too big 1, repetitive 1, nothing memorable 1, confusing 1.
- Good in short sittings 1; cannot save in the middle of a level 1.

### Money and price
About $7. Every one of the 14 "fair price" comments is on a thumbs-up review. *"I beat the game in 2
hours, so i wouldnt necessarily recommend it, but its not bad enough to not recommend it at the price"*
(148299530). Buy it on sale: 5. Not worth it at any price: 1. Low price is what makes a short game
acceptable here.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Bugs: 10 (buggy 4; breaks play 4, such as being stuck on floor clutter and key items not appearing;
  falling out of the level 2).
- Performance: 5 (well optimised 1, runs on modest hardware 1, unsteady frame rate 1, one setting
  causes the slowdown 1, demanding 1). Makes the whole computer unstable 1. Runs on Steam Deck 1.
- Enemy sounds placed wrongly: 3.
- Looks blurry or low-resolution in places: 2.

### The studio, updates and community
- One person made it. 8 reviews grade it against that.
- 5 reviews are written to the developer, not to a buyer. One says the thumbs up is encouragement,
  not a verdict: *"give a good review first... a little encouragement to the production team"*
  (148210236). This kind of review was new to the study; on 2026-10-03 it has its own name and is
  used by this game and no other.
- Steady updates and listening in 2023 (3 and 3), a patch made it better (1), a promised skill tree in
  2024 (2), still supported (1), quiet in 2025, *"the developer has run away"* in 2026 (2).
- The maker talks, but never about the problem: 1.
- A fan-made add-on extends the game: 1.
- 4 reviewers say their thumb will flip when one thing is fixed, one of them if more adult content is
  added. 5 call it average rather than good or bad.

### What players asked for
- More content (14 say too little).
- A map and clear objectives (7 say it is unclear where to go).
- Fewer bullet-sponge enemies (4).
- A dodge button (2).
- Key rebinding (2).
- Character customisation and more outfits (3).
- The promised skill tree (2).
- English voice audio (2).
- Clear store labels that match what is in the game (12 and 8 on the two sides).

### Only in this game
- The fight over whether the adult store tags lied, which is most of the argument in the review pool.
  (FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL has the same shape of fight, about a different thing.) Beside it, 11 times a
  reviewer said the main character is designed to titillate, without calling it good or bad.
- No English voice audio (2 times), used by no other game in the study.
- A thumbs up given as encouragement to the maker, not as a verdict (1 time), used by no other game.
- English reviewers are 22 points more positive than the other 91% of its audience (73.0% against
  51.0% for all languages). The page cannot say why.
- Hours shown on reviews are tiny: median 2 hours; median 0 on thumbs-down reviews and 2 on thumbs-up;
  none show 100 hours or more.
- Design talk is a third of everything said (33.3%), against Immortal: Unchained's 53.6%. A two-hour
  game gets two hours of design comments; the tags, the price and how much you get fill the rest.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **The store page must match what is in the game.**
   Evidence: 12 times players said the store tags promised something the game does not have, 9 of
   them thumbs down; the most-helpful review (147 helpful) is this claim. 8 thumbs-up reviews defended
   it. 19 of 89 reviews (one in five) are this argument.
   Dominion could: check the store tags (including the ones players add) before launch and after; if
   a user tag is wrong, say plainly on the store page what the game is and is not.
   Strength: medium (one game, but clear and one-sided on the thumb).
2. **A short game is forgiven when the price matches.**
   Evidence: 14 say too little content, 12 of them still recommend; 14 say the price is fair, all
   thumbs up.
   Dominion could: set the launch price against the hours a team will really get out of it.
   Strength: medium.
3. **Promising a feature starts a clock.**
   Evidence: 2 reviews in 2024 waiting on a promised skill tree; by 2026, 2 say the developer left.
   Dominion could: only announce features with a date it can keep; if plans change, say so.
   Strength: weak.
4. **Show players where to go.**
   Evidence: 7 times it was said, 4 from people who still recommend it.
   Dominion could: always show the next goal and the extraction point on screen and on the map.
   Strength: weak to medium.
5. **Enemies that soak bullets read as lazy difficulty.**
   Evidence: 4 times it was said.
   Dominion could: make harder enemies harder by what they do, not only by more health; show hits
   landing.
   Strength: weak.
6. **Voice audio in the players' language matters.**
   Evidence: 2 times it was said that there are no English voices.
   Dominion could: plan which languages get voices, and make sure key callouts also show as text.
   Strength: weak.
7. **Enemies must not see or shoot through walls.**
   Evidence: 4 say enemies spot you across the map and shoot through walls; it was 0 in 2023 and 8.3
   per 100 reviews in 2024; 2 more say enemy aim swings from foolish to deadly accurate.
   Dominion could: give enemies sight lines and hearing ranges players can learn, and test that
   enemy shots respect cover.
   Strength: weak.
8. **The third-person camera and aim must not fight the player near walls.**
   Evidence: 3 say aiming stops when a wall or enemy is close; 2 say the view is blocked against walls
   and in stairwells; 1 says the camera moves more than asked.
   Dominion could: keep aim working at close range and pull the camera in cleanly in tight spaces;
   test stairwells and doorways.
   Strength: weak.
9. **Sounds must come from where the danger is.**
   Evidence: 3 say enemy sounds play close by when nothing is near.
   Dominion could: check that enemy sounds come from the enemy's real place and distance, so players
   can trust them in a fight.
   Strength: weak.
10. **Let players move and act freely: dodge, reload on the run, change keys.**
    Evidence: no proper dodge 2; stiff movement with no reload while running 2; some keys cannot be
    changed 2; aim sensitivity cannot be tuned 1.
    Dominion could: let every action be rebound, reload while sprinting, and give a dodge from day one.
    Strength: weak.
11. **Let players change how their character looks.**
    Evidence: 3 say you cannot change how the character looks; 3 more ask for more outfits as rewards
    or find the hard ones too hard to earn.
    Dominion could: give looks to earn in play, with some easy enough for every player to get.
    Strength: weak.
12. **A small studio's early listening is praised most and trusted least; keep it going.**
    Evidence: steady updates and listening 3 and 3 in 2023; updates stopped 2 in 2026; across the
    block, "the studio listens" is 23 bullets, all in a launch window, and "updates stopped" 16, all a
    year or more after, in five games (cross-game section 15).
    Dominion could: plan a support schedule it can keep for years, and say what it is.
    Strength: medium (block-wide reading).

### Limits
- Every English review was read: no margin of error. But English is only 9% of this game's audience;
  the other 890 reviews (91%) were not read and are far less positive. The page cannot say why.
- 2025 and 2026 are 17 reviews together.
- 11 reviews (12.4%) were edited after posting and are counted on the date first written.
- Whether the adult tags were the studio's or players' cannot be settled from reviews; the page
  records both sides and does not judge.
- 3 reviews marked English were in Chinese or Thai; they were read from their content.
- **What changed from the earlier entry:** the game now has its own ranked page,
  `findings/zombie-girl.md` (the earlier entry said there was none). The rebuilt pages add combat,
  enemy, tech and progression counts the earlier entry did not have. The "encouragement, not a
  verdict" review now has a name and is used only here. The spider warning is no longer counted as
  used by this game alone; the page did not trace why. The re-run counts 2, not 3, reviews written to
  the studio under that name (5 in all read that way). Bullets: 264 on 2026-09-10, 267 on the
  2026-10-03 re-run.
- The price, the update history, the skill tree announcement and the Germany claim are what reviewers
  say, not checked.
- Adult content is recorded as what reviewers argue about, in neutral words; crude remarks are left
  out.

---

## ZCREW

**Read:** all 77 of 77 English reviews (100%). Every English review was read, so there is no margin of
error. Written December 2020 to July 2026 (31 months, every month read). **49.4% thumbs up** in the
English reviews, 38 up and 39 down (Steam, all languages: 52.6% of 363 reviews, Mixed).
**Pages:** `findings/zcrew-english.md`, `findings/zcrew.md`, `findings/roguelike-block.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` section 15.

**What it is:** a top-down twin-stick co-op shooter against zombies, made by Snail Games. In early
access from December 2020; full release (1.0) in September 2022. Missions with objectives and a timed
run to an evacuation helicopter at the end. Four classes, each with its own skill tree. Paid; one
reviewer recommends it "at $5" (the page does not give the full price). The engine, the hosting model
and the player count are not on the page. 29 of the English reviews were written in early access in
2020 and 2021 (the 2022 reviews before September are not split out). The busiest month is the 1.0
release, September 2022, with 15 reviews.

**How close to Dominion:** shares co-op, PvE, missions that end in getting out by helicopter (close to
extraction), and classes. It is top-down, not third person, and zombies, not sci-fi.

### The short version (plain words)
This game's thumbs-down reviews are about whether it works, not how it plays. "A bug stops me from
playing" was said 11 times, and 10 of those were thumbs down. Mission goals that get stuck, an
evacuation helicopter that will not come, enemies that stop spawning. The only real case for buying it
is that it is cheap: 11 say the price is fair, all thumbs up. After that come confusing menus (7) and
a game pad that only half works (4). The studio was praised for updates in early access in 2021; after
full release the reviews are bug reports, and some call it abandoned. Its text is the second most
negative in the study: 171.4 complaints and 72.7 pieces of praise per 100 reviews.

### What players praised, most to least
1. **The price is fair.** 11 times it was said, all thumbs up; tied for the most common thing said
   about the game. *"At $20 or more, I'd be giving it a negative review. But at $5? Yes, I can
   absolutely recommend it."* (122276388). *"its cheaper than the £7 they charge at the cinema for
   popcorn"* (197864285).
2. **Steady updates in early access (2021).** 4 times it was said. *"an example to All Early Access
   games"* (87208986).
3. **Thumbs up with no reason given.** 4 times.
4. **Good value even while unfinished.** 3 times it was said.
5. **Each of the four classes plays its own way.** 3 times it was said.
6. **Early access grew into what it promised.** 3 times it was said, all 2021.
7. **It runs well on Steam Deck or with a Steam controller.** 3 times it was said.
8. **It is fun in co-op.** 2 times it was said.
9. **It can be finished alone.** 2 times it was said.
10. **Updates fixed what mattered:** the blur gone, hit feedback and gore added. 2 times it was said.
11. **It is judged too harshly for a cheap game.** 2 times it was said.
12. One reviewer each: it won over someone who never plays this kind of game; it runs smoothly on a
    modest graphics card; the enemy types are well made; the empty, ruined world sets the mood; the
    studio listens; it is fun and somewhat hard; it has a lot of potential; the weapon sounds stand
    out; levelling and gear let you shape your class; good if you love crafting parts; better than
    expected for five dollars; there are usually enough players to find a group; played level after
    level for hours.
13. **"Nothing does what it does."** One reviewer (122526677).

### What players complained about, most to least
1. **A bug stops the game from being played.** 11 times it was said: 10 from thumbs-down reviews
   (people who left), 1 from thumbs up. A mission goal that sticks at 8 of 10, an evacuation helicopter
   that does not respond, enemies that stop spawning, triggers that never fire. *"after the tutorial
   mission, you cannot progress any further due to: crashes, game not spawning any enemies, game not
   giving you any missions... there is no excuse for this after 4 years"* (158283309, 2024).
2. **The menus and screens are confusing.** 7 times it was said.
3. **It feels unfinished.** 6 times it was said, all 6 thumbs down. *"probably not totally finished
   despite it not saying early access on steam"* (122526677).
4. **It is buggy.** 4 times it was said.
5. **Nobody is playing, so co-op is hard to find.** 4 times it was said. One could not judge the co-op
   because nobody was online.
6. **A game pad only half works; you have to play with a mouse.** 4 times it was said (menus do not
   work, or the stick gives no movement).
7. **It is worse than another game, mostly Helldivers.** 4 times it was said; 10 of the 77 reviews
   name another game and 5 of those name Helldivers. *"Zcrew is trying to be some kind of Helldivers
   mixed with zombies. The problem is it never even comes close"* (108187929). *"It's a lot like a
   favorite game of mine called helldivers, but with all the soul/charm sucked out"* (122276388). One
   calls it a reskin of Kill Squad (118117616).
8. **Ammo runs out too fast.** 4 times it was said (1 says it is too plentiful).
9. **A timer forces a run to the helicopter and fails the mission if you miss it.** 3 times it was
   said; reviewers want it optional, or a button to call the helicopter.
10. **It crashes again and again.** 3 times it was said, all thumbs down.
11. **The frame rate jumps around.** 3 times it was said, 2 thumbs down.
12. **Even basic zombies knock you down and keep you down.** 2 times it was said; it makes the melee
    classes miserable.
13. **Enemies appear behind you in cleared areas** or right on top of objectives. 2 times it was said.
14. **Upgrades are mostly bigger numbers.** 2 times it was said.
15. **Combat is clunky and the guns have no punch.** 2 times it was said (one says there was no gore at
    first).
16. **Only worth it on sale.** 2 times it was said; one says *"$4.99 on sale is all it is worth"*.
17. **The studio left it.** 2 reviews call it abandoned on the re-run (the page earlier counted 3 about
    abandonment in 2023 to 2026; see Limits). *"another forsaken child of the game industry"*
    (230608709).
18. **It is boring; nothing interesting happens between fights.** 2 times it was said.
19. **Trailers and videos oversell it.** 2 times it was said.
20. **Wait for more updates before buying (early access).** 2 times it was said.
21. **Interacting with things is a chore and sometimes fails.** 2 times it was said.
22. **The class you pick decides your character's gender.** 2 times here; 4 times across ZCREW and one
    other game (DRG: Rogue Core).
23. **Enemy damage and player power are out of balance** (ranged enemies all shoot at you at once).
    2 times it was said.
24. **No setting changes the locked frame rate.** 1 reviewer.
25. **The colours and menus look dull.** 1 reviewer.

Also: achievements do not unlock when you do the thing (once here); promised controller support never
came (one review, see Studio); barren at the early-access launch: *"an interesting idea and a good
foundation, BUT... so barren"*.

Overall: 132 complaints against 56 pieces of praise (0.42 praise for each complaint), the second most
negative text of any game in the study, after Terminull Brigade. Bugs and tech are 15.0% of everything
said about ZCREW (14.9% on the re-run), against 7.3% for eight larger co-op games and 5.4% for
Immortal: Unchained: the highest share of any game read.

### How it changed over time
| Period | Reviews | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 | What they say |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Dec 2020, early access opens | 9 | 56% (55.6%) | 155.6 | 322.2 | a good start, but barren |
| 2021 | 20 | 45% | 75.0 | 160.0 | steady updates (4), it grew into its promise (3) |
| 2022 (full release in September) | 30 | 53% (53.3%) | 50.0 | 160.0 | not really finished |
| 2023 to 2026 | 18 | 44% (44.4%) | 44.4 | 127.8 | a bug stops play (7; 6 on the re-run), abandoned (3; 2 on the re-run) |

All the praise for update pace is from 2021. All the bug reports are from after full release. "It
grew into its promise" (3) and "it never did" (1) both appear: the same game judged a year apart.
These periods are 9, 20, 30 and 18 reviews, so the percentages are shaky.

- **What fell:** praise per 100 fell at every step, 155.6 to 44.4. Menu complaints fell from 22.2 per
  100 reviews at the early-access launch to 0 after 2022; the helicopter timer from 22.2 to 0 after
  2021; steady updates from 15.0 in 2021 to 0 after.
- **What rose:** bugs that stop play rose from 0 at launch to 15.0 per 100 in 2021, 6.7 in 2022 and
  33.3 in 2023 to 2026.

### Co-op and online play
- 4 say nobody is playing (one could not judge the co-op because nobody was online; one says solo works
  fine); 1 says there are usually enough players to find a group.
- Fun in co-op 2; can be finished alone 2.
- No loot sharing makes co-op worse: 1. Friends have connection problems: 1. Co-op needs a better way
  to find players: 1.
- Nothing is said about hosting, joining a game already running, scaling to player count or friendly
  fire.
- No review talks about a player community, its habits or its in-jokes. The block page counts zero
  such comments in all four of the block's co-op games, and reads it as a sign there were too few
  players to form one.

### Combat, movement and feel
- Combat is clunky and the guns have no punch: 2. Weapon handling sluggish 1. Weapon sounds are a
  standout 1. Updates added hit feedback and gore, players say (2).
- Stuns: even basic zombies knock you down or keep you stunned, which makes melee classes miserable: 2.
- Game pad only half works (menus do not, or the stick gives no movement): 4. Steam Deck and a Steam
  controller work well: 3. Controller support was promised by the studio and never arrived, says one
  reviewer.
- Interacting is a chore and sometimes fails: 2. Missing expected key bindings 1; movement unreliable
  1, sluggish 1.
- The Helldivers comparison: the "soul" and "charm" are missing.
- An image so blurry it hurt one reviewer's eyes (since fixed, says another): 1.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- Enemies appear behind you in cleared areas or right on top of objectives: 2.
- Enemies that stop spawning, so a mission cannot finish (part of the 11 game-stopping bug reports).
- Enemy types well made 1.
- Difficulty: badly scaled (ranged enemies focus fire, player power out of balance) 2; hard in a good
  way 1; one part far harder 1; harder only changes the numbers 1.
- Ammo runs out too fast: 4; too plentiful: 1.
- Nothing is said about bosses.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- Each class has its own skill tree and plays its own way: 3.
- Upgrades mostly bigger numbers 2; deep (crafting parts) 1; satisfying 1; shallow 1; grindy 1; slow
  start 1; gated behind a different mode 1; overwhelming at first 1. Not random enough 1.
- The class decides the character's gender: 2 here, 4 across ZCREW and DRG: Rogue Core.
- Achievements that do not track what you did (once here; 3 times across three games).
- One reviewer asks for skills that can be swapped between classes.

### Runs, content and replay value
- "Barren" at the start of early access.
- Mission goals that stick (8 of 10) and an evacuation that will not come stop a mission from ending at
  all.
- The end-of-mission timer: 3 say a clock forces the run to the helicopter, fails the mission if
  missed, and should be optional or a call button.
- Cannot save and come back 1; cannot pause 1.
- Nothing happens between fights 2; runs out fast 1; keeps pulling you back 1; too little 1; levels too
  short 1; repetitive 1. A mode falls flat 1; an expected mode is missing 1; good selection of modes 1.

### Money and price
- 11 say the price is fair, all thumbs up; the argument is "not good, but cheap". Recommenders write
  1.16 complaints each (Zombie Girl's wrote 1.32).
- Buy on sale only 2; refunded 1. Good value while unfinished 3; not worth it yet in early access 2.
- Trailer and videos oversell it: 2.
- One reviewer says they got it for a platform reward (Steam trading cards); one says the studio gave
  them a free copy.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Game-stopping bugs: 11 times, 10 thumbs down. Stuck goals, a helicopter that will not respond,
  enemies that stop spawning, triggers that never fire. General bugs 4; rare and minor 1.
- Feels unfinished: 6, all thumbs down.
- Crashes again and again: 3, all thumbs down. Freezes 1; makes the whole computer unstable 1; the
  glitches are half the fun 1. Stopped working on my setup 1.
- Frame rate jumps: 3, 2 thumbs down. Changing a setting does nothing (the frame rate is locked) 1.
  Runs well on modest hardware 1.
Small games are forgiven for being small. This one is not forgiven for not working.

### The studio, updates and community
- 2021: steady updates (4), early access grew into its promise (3), *"an example to All Early Access
  games"* (87208986). Fixed what mattered 2; listens 1.
- Never grew into its promise 1; updates too slow 1; waiting on promised content 1; updates stopped 2;
  known bugs never fixed 1; ignores feedback 1; the studio lost my trust 1.
- **The studio answered a review on the store page.** In November 2021 it promised *"full, intuitive
  controller support"*. The reviewer (96754160) then edited the review in September 2022, January
  2024 and May 2024 to say it never came. A reply on the store page became a promise the review kept
  score against. The studio replied to reviews 2 times in all. One more reviewer says controller
  support still had not come a year on and the studio no longer answered in its chat server.

### What players asked for
- Fix the bugs that stop missions (11).
- Full controller support, which the studio promised (1 review, edited three times; 4 say the game pad
  half works).
- An optional helicopter timer or a button to call the helicopter (3).
- More ammo (4).
- Cleared areas that stay clear (1).
- Character looks not tied to the class (2 here).
- Achievements that track correctly (1).
- Skills that can be swapped between classes (1).
- A better way to find co-op players (1).
- More in it at the start of early access ("barren").

### Only in this game
Nothing is used by ZCREW alone (the 2026-10-03 re-run agrees: 0). But ZCREW tipped four claims that
had been scattered in the study one at a time: the class decides your gender; a reviewer says they got
a free copy from the studio; achievements do not track what you did; a reviewer played it for a Steam
platform reward. The study's reading: *"A 77-review game read whole tips a claim that 800,000 reviews
sampled at 1,626 cannot."* And it is the clearest case in the study of a studio's reply on the store
page turning into a broken promise. Hours shown on reviews are low: median 2 hours; median 0 on
thumbs-down reviews and 4 on thumbs-up; 1 review shows 100 hours or more.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **A bug that stops a mission is the one thing a small game is not forgiven for.**
   Evidence: 11 times it was said, 10 thumbs down; stuck goals, an evacuation helicopter that will not
   respond, enemies that stop spawning. Compare Zombie Girl, where 12 of 14 "too short" comments still
   recommend.
   Dominion could: give every objective and every extraction a fail-safe (a timer or a host command
   that forces the next step), log stuck states, and test extraction under host lag and client
   disconnects on a listen server.
   Strength: medium for this game alone; the contrast with forgiven "too short" makes it stronger.
2. **The extraction moment must always work.**
   Evidence: the evacuation helicopter that does not respond is named among the 11 game-stopping bugs.
   Dominion could: make extraction its most tested system, with a backup path if the main trigger
   fails.
   Strength: weak by count, high stakes for an extraction game.
3. **Full release raises the bar; bugs after 1.0 are judged harder.**
   Evidence: praise for updates is all 2021; game-stopping bugs are 7 of the 18 reviews after 2023
   (6 on the re-run, 33.3 per 100 reviews); *"no excuse for this after 4 years"* (158283309).
   Dominion could: do not call it 1.0 until missions finish reliably.
   Strength: medium.
4. **Anything the studio promises in a reply becomes a scorecard.**
   Evidence: one review edited three times over two and a half years, counting the missing controller
   support.
   Dominion could: only promise in public what is scheduled; follow up on the same thread when it ships
   or is dropped.
   Strength: weak (one review), clear.
5. **Do not tie a character's look or gender to the class.**
   Evidence: said in ZCREW and one other game, 4 times across the two.
   Dominion could: let any player pick any look for any role.
   Strength: weak to medium.
6. **Expect comparison to the big co-op name, and to lose on "soul".**
   Evidence: 10 of 77 reviews name another game, 5 name Helldivers; 4 say it is worse.
   Dominion could: give the game its own personality, voice lines and moments so it is not "the same
   but with the charm sucked out".
   Strength: weak to medium.
7. **Make achievements track reliably.**
   Evidence: once here, 3 times across three games.
   Strength: weak.
8. **A timer that fails the mission on the way out is resented.**
   Evidence: 3 say the clock forcing a run to the helicopter, and failing the mission if missed, should
   be optional or replaced by a call button; it was 22.2 per 100 reviews at the early-access launch.
   Dominion could: make the extraction timer a pressure (more enemies, less loot) rather than an
   instant fail, or let the team call extraction when ready.
   Strength: weak, but it sits on the moment Dominion is built around.
9. **Controller support must be whole, or not claimed.**
   Evidence: 4 say a game pad only half works (menus, the stick); 3 praise it on Steam Deck or a Steam
   controller; the promised controller support is the scorecard review.
   Dominion could: test every menu and every action with a game pad and on Steam Deck before saying it
   is supported.
   Strength: weak to medium.
10. **Menus must be easy to find your way around.**
    Evidence: 7 say the menus and screens are confusing, the second largest complaint; 22.2 per 100 at
    the early-access launch.
    Dominion could: playtest the menus (loadout, upgrades, lobby) with new players, and fix the first
    screens first.
    Strength: weak to medium.
11. **Do not let basic enemies take control away.**
    Evidence: 2 say even basic zombies knock you down and keep you down, which makes melee classes
    miserable.
    Dominion could: keep knockdowns and stuns for special enemies with a warning, and give a way out of
    them.
    Strength: weak.
12. **Cleared ground should stay cleared, and spawns should not land behind you.**
    Evidence: 2 say enemies appear behind them in cleared areas or right on top of objectives.
    Dominion could: spawn enemies out of sight and away from the team's path, and not on top of an
    objective a player is working.
    Strength: weak.
13. **Ammo should run low as a choice, not run out as a wall.**
    Evidence: 4 say ammo runs out too fast; 1 says it is too plentiful.
    Dominion could: tune ammo per class and per weapon, and always leave a fallback weapon.
    Strength: weak.
14. **A co-op game with few players has no community to lean on, so playing alone has to hold up.**
    Evidence: 4 say nobody is playing; 2 say it can be finished alone; across the block, player culture
    is zero in all four co-op games (cross-game section 15).
    Dominion could: make a solo or two-player run fully playable, and add a simple way to find players.
    Strength: medium (block-wide reading).

### Limits
- Every English review was read: no margin of error. The periods are 9, 20, 30 and 18 reviews.
- 13 reviews (16.9%) were edited after posting and are counted on the date first written.
- 286 reviews in other languages (79% of the audience) were not read.
- The first count of this game missed its 35 early-access reviews; it was fixed before reading.
- **What changed from the earlier entry:** the game now has its own ranked page, `findings/zcrew.md`
  (the earlier entry said there was none). The rebuilt pages fill in co-op, enemies, combat, menus,
  ammo, the helicopter timer and stuns, which the earlier entry had to leave thin. The 2026-10-03
  re-run puts game-stopping bugs after 2023 at 6, not 7, and abandonment at 2 (updates stopped 1 and
  bugs never fixed 1), not 3; the page did not trace why. Bullets: 214 on 2026-09-10, 215 on the
  re-run; bugs and tech 15.0% then, 14.9% now.
- The price, the 1.0 date, the studio's reply, the patch history and the end of updates are what
  reviewers say, not checked.
- 2 reviews marked English were in Chinese and Portuguese; they were read from their content.

---

## FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL

**Read:** all 116 of 116 English reviews (100%). Every English review was read, so there is no margin
of error. Written October 2025 to August 2026 (11 months, every month read). **69.8% thumbs up** in
the English reviews, 81 up and 35 down (Steam, all languages: 69.8% of 235 reviews, Mostly Positive).
**Pages:** `findings/full-metal-schoolgirl-english.md`, `findings/full-metal-schoolgirl.md`,
`findings/roguelike-block.md`, `findings/cross-game.md` section 15.

**What it is:** a single-player third-person roguelite shooter from YUKE'S, published by D3Publisher.
Released 2025-10-23 at $49.99. You climb a hundred-floor tower as one of two schoolgirl cyborgs, framed
as a livestream. Runs of two to seven hours, no save mid-run, and keys that skip floors but are used up
when you use them. A black shadow is drawn under every skirt. The engine is not on the page. The
youngest game in the study: eleven months of reviews, and the launch month holds 46 of the 116 (40%).

**How close to Dominion:** shares the third-person camera, the shooting and runs that end. It is
single-player, not co-op, and has no extraction.

### The short version (plain words)
Most of the loudest reviews are a fight about fan service, not about play. 12 reviews say the game
censors itself (the shadow under the skirt), 9 of them thumbs down, and those 12 carry 52% of every
helpful vote on the game. The seven most-helpful English reviews are all this claim. On the other side,
11 reviews say buyers were wrong to expect more, all 11 thumbs up. Under the fight, the real complaint
is the price (22 times it was said, 14 from people who still recommend it), and the real design
complaint is the floor-skipping key that is used up, in runs of two to seven hours with no save. What
players like is the jokes, the shooting and that it runs, every one of those comments a thumbs up.
There are 69.8 pieces of praise and 156.0 complaints per 100 reviews, and it got calmer after launch.

### What players praised, most to least
1. **Thumbs up with no reason given.** 15 of 81 thumbs-up reviews (19%) say nothing.
2. **Buyers were wrong to expect more** (the other side of the fan-service fight). 11 times it was
   said, all 11 thumbs up. *"a game that is identical across all platforms and regions"* (207594888).
   *"It's not a H-game. It was never advertised as one"* (208277404). Four more say the negative
   reviews are not about the game.
3. **Cheap-looking in a good way.** 11 reviews describe it by naming budget games (EDF, Onechanbara,
   Simple 2000, Lollipop Chainsaw, Hi-Fi Rush). *"It's slop but it's good slop."* (218057177).
4. **Funny, memorable writing and characters.** 9 times it was said, all thumbs up.
5. **Shooting that hits hard.** 6 times it was said, all thumbs up.
6. **A game you can relax to.** 6 times it was said, all thumbs up.
7. **It runs well.** 6 times it was said, all thumbs up.
8. **Endless mode and new game plus keep pulling you back.** 4 times it was said.
9. **The random tower keeps runs fresh.** 3 times it was said.
10. **Harder settings make enemies faster, not tankier;** walls take one or two tries. 3 times it was
    said.
11. **The art style and the livestream framing are stylish.** 3 times it was said.
12. **A fan-made add-on removes the shadow on PC.** 2 times it was said.
13. **Enemies fall apart limb by limb.** 2 times it was said.
14. **The studio fixed the aim-speed bug.** 2 times it was said.
15. **The story is fun.** 2 times it was said.
16. **The livestream viewer challenges add a risk-for-reward twist.** 2 times it was said.
17. **The permanent upgrades are actually useful.** 2 times it was said.
18. **It runs on Steam Deck despite the label.** 2 times it was said.
19. **Fun enough to earn every achievement.** 2 times it was said.
20. One reviewer each: it rewards learning its quirks; some odd guns play differently; the add-ons are
    only extras you can skip; easy to pick up; enough guns and melee weapons for a hundred floors; the
    soundtrack is very nice; the screen is clear and weak points are marked; it does not deserve the
    hate.

### What players complained about, most to least
1. **The price is too high for what it is.** 22 times it was said, the most common thing said about
   the game; 14 from thumbs-up reviews (people who stayed). *"an easy recommendation... at like
   $20-30. 50 is complete nuts lol. Do not pay that."* (207594888).
2. **The game visibly censors itself.** 12 times it was said, 9 from thumbs-down reviews (people who
   left). Many are one word long: *"Censored"* (207472674, 36 helpful). *"The game is censored. Because
   of that, I cannot recommend the game."* (207503610, 97 helpful).
3. **Buy it only on sale.** 10 times it was said, 6 from thumbs up.
4. **The floor-skipping key is used up when you use it.** 9 times it was said, 5 thumbs up and 4
   down. A second death costs more than the first. *"smarter players will never use their higher
   level keys, because you need to build up your arsenal so you don't get smoked"* (217406229). *"If
   you only have one 85 key and die, your starting at the lobby."* (208277404).
5. **It repeats itself, especially high in the tower.** 9 times it was said. The recommenders' usual
   line is "overpriced, repetitive, and I liked it".
6. **In-run upgrades are only small number changes.** 8 times it was said.
7. **Runs take too long.** 8 times it was said; runs of two to seven hours (one says one to two hours
   per twenty floors).
8. **The camera fights you,** worst in platforming, and pans after encounters. 6 times it was said.
9. **Traps everywhere break the flow** (area attacks, traps that appear from nothing). 5 times it was
   said.
10. **Random item picks leave you stuck with bad luck** (when starting from an elevator). 5 times it
    was said.
11. **Another game does this better.** 4 times it was said.
12. **The game changes your aim speed by itself near traps** (doubles it or resets it). 4 times on the
    re-run; 5 on the first count; two reviewers say it was patched. (*"When you're near traps it
    doubles your sensitivity"*, 207491180.)
13. **Most guns or moves are useless next to one or two.** 4 times it was said.
14. **Thumbs down with no reason given.** 4 times.
15. **Losing sends you back to repeat the whole tower.** 3 times it was said.
16. **You cannot save and come back.** 3 times on the re-run (4 on the first count). *"desperately
    needs a quicksave between floors"*.
17. **Super moves leave you open the moment they end.** 2 times it was said.
18. **The jokes fall flat; the villain is thin.** 2 times it was said.
19. **Music cannot go below ten per cent volume.** 2 times it was said.
20. **Permanent upgrades never make runs faster.** 2 times it was said.
21. **Sensitivity options are too narrow.** 2 times it was said.
22. **The perfect dodge only works standing still.** 2 times it was said.
23. **The studio has done this better before.** 2 times it was said.
24. **A crash lost a run hours in** (at floor fifty and at floor ninety). 2 times it was said.
25. **Camera pans and cutscenes cannot be skipped.** 2 times it was said.

Also: bullet-sponge bosses and minibosses 2; dying costs you half your materials 1; content held back
for a second purchase 1 (one reviewer posts a sketch mocking it); only 1080p and 4K offered, no 1440p 1.

Overall: 181 complaints against 81 pieces of praise (0.45 praise for each complaint; the block as a
whole is 0.56). 47.5% of the complaints are on thumbs-up reviews; a recommending reviewer writes 1.06
complaints.

### How it changed over time
| Period | Reviews | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Launch quarter, Oct to Dec 2025 | 64 | 67.2% | 51.6 | 175.0 |
| Early 2026, Jan to Apr | 44 | 72.7% | 72.7 | 136.4 |
| Mid 2026, May to Aug | 8 | 75.0% | 12.5 | 62.5 |

It got calmer after launch. Mid 2026 is only 8 reviews, so its numbers are shaky.
- **What fell:** the censorship claim fell from 15.6 per 100 reviews in the launch quarter to 2.3 in
  early 2026; dull upgrades from 10.9 to 2.3; the camera from 9.4 to 0; traps from 7.8 to 0; aim speed
  jumping near traps from 6.2 to 0, after the fix. Funny writing fell from 14.1 to 0.
- **What rose:** praise per 100 rose from 51.6 to 72.7; "it runs smoothly" from 1.6 to 11.4; luck
  deciding the run from 0 to 11.4.
- The one change players name is the patch to the aim-speed bug.

### Co-op and online play
Single-player. No review asks for co-op.

### Combat, movement and feel
- The shooting hits hard: 6, all thumbs up. Enemies fall apart limb by limb: 2.
- Balance: most guns or moves useless next to one or two 4; one option dominates 1; weightless 1;
  sluggish weapon handling 1.
- Super moves leave you open the instant they end: 2.
- Aim speed changes by itself near traps and pits (doubles or resets): 4 on the re-run (5 on the first
  count); two say patched. Sensitivity options too narrow: 2.
- The perfect dodge only works standing still: 2. Cannot rebind 1; unresponsive 1; sluggish 1.
- Camera fights you in platforming and pans after encounters: 6.
- Relaxed pace: 6, all thumbs up.
- The livestream viewer challenges add a risk-for-reward twist: 2.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- Traps everywhere, area attacks, traps that appear from nothing: 5.
- Harder settings make enemies faster, not tankier: 3.
- Bullet-sponge bosses and minibosses 2; the final fight is a pushover 1; one part far harder 1; enemy
  variety lacking 1; poor enemy behaviour 1; forgettable special enemies 1; threats unclear 1.
- The danger players name most is losing a long run: the used-up key means the second death costs
  more than the first, so players hoard keys to build up first.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- Keys skip floors and are spent when used: 9 (5 up, 4 down). Players hold back their best keys to
  build up gear first (217406229).
- In-run upgrades are only number changes 8; levelling up changes nothing 2; permanent upgrades are
  useful 2; slow start 2; grindy 1; respects your time 1; deep 1.
- Luck decides the run 5 (random item picks when starting from an elevator); randomness keeps it fresh
  3. One reviewer asks for a way to banish junk items.
- Achievements are a fair set to finish: 2.
- Dying costs you half your materials: 1.

### Runs, content and replay value
- A hundred floors. Runs of two to seven hours (8 say too long). No save between floors (3 on the
  re-run, 4 on the first count); progress lost to a crash 2; locked in once started 1; losing sends you
  back to the bottom 3.
- The page says every review that reached the top says some version of this: a hundred floors, no
  mid-run save, and a checkpoint you spend.
- Repetitive: 9. Endless mode and new game plus keep pulling you back: 4. Forced camera pans and
  cutscenes you cannot skip: 2. Runs out fast 1; padding 1.

### Money and price
- $49.99 at launch. Too high: 22 times (14 from people who still recommend it). Sale only: 10 (6 up).
- 7 reviewers say they paid bundle prices: $2.77, $10, $25 for nine games, and judge it worth that.
  *"I paid $2.77, that was a 9 games bundle for $25"* (215897327). *"the value of the bundle was
  probably almost $100 since these games are so overpriced"* (219672669).
- Paid add-ons: one says they are only extras you can skip; one says content is held back for a second
  purchase.
- Talk about price is 11.1% of everything said about this game, the highest of any game in the study;
  the co-op average is 7.1%.
- The 22 price reviews carry 271 helpful votes, 19% of the helpful votes.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- It runs well: 6, all thumbs up; runs on Steam Deck despite the unsupported label 2.
- Crashes 2 (again and again 1, on a specific event 1); a crash lost a run hours in 2.
- Unsteady frame rate 1; stutter 1; demanding 1.
- Aim speed changes near traps: 4 to 5, reportedly patched.
- Music cannot go below ten per cent volume: 2. Only 1080p and 4K offered, no 1440p: 1.

### The studio, updates and community
- The studio fixed the aim-speed bug: 2. YUKE'S falls short of its earlier games: 2. Nothing else on
  update pace or studio talk.
- The community story is the fan-service fight: 12 against and 11 defending, plus 4 who say the bad
  reviews are not about the game. 2 say a fan-made add-on removes the shadow. 4 more say the main
  characters are built to titillate, without calling it good or bad.

### What players asked for
- A lower price, or wait for a sale (22 and 10).
- A save between floors (3 to 4: *"desperately needs a quicksave between floors"*).
- Keys that are not used up (9).
- Shorter runs (8).
- No shadow under the skirt (12).
- Aim speed that stays put (4 to 5).
- Music that can be turned off (2).
- A dodge in any direction (2).
- A way to banish junk items (1).

### Only in this game
- The claim that the game visibly censors itself (12) is used by this game alone, and it decided the
  public face of the game: 750 helpful votes, 52% of all helpful votes; the seven most-helpful reviews
  are all this claim, and five of them are under ten words. A buyer sorting by "most helpful" sees
  nothing else. The 22 price reviews carry 19%; everything else, 82 reviews, carries 409 votes (29%).
- The used-up floor key (9) is used by this game alone.
- The aim sensitivity that changes near traps (4 to 5) has been seen only here.
- Hours shown on reviews: median 5 hours; median 3 on thumbs-down reviews and 5 on thumbs-up; none
  show 100 hours or more.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **A few short, angry reviews can become the whole store page.**
   Evidence: 12 reviews carry 52% of all helpful votes; the seven most-helpful are one claim.
   Dominion could: find out before launch what one-line complaint could top the store page, and
   either fix it or address it plainly on the page.
   Strength: medium (one game, but the numbers are stark).
2. **Do not make the reward for progress something you lose by using it.**
   Evidence: 9 say the floor key is used up, so smart players hoard instead of using it.
   Dominion could: make checkpoints, skips or saved progress in a run permanent once earned, or
   rebuyable at a fair cost, so using them never feels like a trap.
   Strength: weak to medium.
3. **Long runs need a way to stop and come back.**
   Evidence: 8 say runs are too long (two to seven hours), 3 to 4 ask for a save between floors, 2 lost
   a run hours in to a crash.
   Dominion could: keep runs short, or allow the host to save the team's run between stages and
   resume with the same group; for a listen server, decide what happens if the host has to leave.
   Strength: medium.
4. **The game must never change the player's input settings on its own.**
   Evidence: 4 to 5 times it was said that sensitivity doubled near traps; patched; it fell from 6.2
   per 100 reviews to 0 after the fix.
   Dominion could: never scale mouse or stick input in special zones; test aim with every camera mode.
   Strength: weak (one game, one bug), but a cheap rule to keep.
5. **Price shapes the whole conversation.**
   Evidence: 22 too high (14 from recommenders), 10 sale only, 7 paid bundle prices and found it fair;
   price talk is 11.1% of everything said, the highest in the study.
   Dominion could: price for the hours and polish players will get; a full price on a budget-feel
   game is judged harshly even by fans.
   Strength: medium.
6. **Punchy shooting and a sense of humour win fans even in a cheap-looking game.**
   Evidence: hard-hitting shots 6, funny writing 9, all thumbs up.
   Dominion could: spend on hit feel and personality before spending on extra content.
   Strength: weak to medium.
7. **In-run upgrades should change how you play, not just nudge numbers.**
   Evidence: 8 say in-run upgrades are only small number changes; 2 say levelling up changes nothing;
   4 say most guns are useless next to one or two.
   Dominion could: make each upgrade change a behaviour (a new effect, a new way to use a weapon), and
   check that more than one build can win.
   Strength: weak to medium.
8. **Luck should not decide a run before it starts.**
   Evidence: 5 say random item picks leave them stuck with bad luck, rising from 0 to 11.4 per 100
   reviews in early 2026; one asks for a way to banish junk items; 3 say randomness keeps it fresh.
   Dominion could: offer rerolls or a way to remove unwanted items, so a bad draw can be fixed by
   choices.
   Strength: weak.
9. **Keep the camera out of the player's way, and let them skip what the game plays at them.**
   Evidence: 6 say the camera fights them, worst in platforming and with pans after fights; 2 say camera
   pans and cutscenes cannot be skipped.
   Dominion could: never take the camera away mid-fight, and let every pan and cutscene be skipped.
   Strength: weak to medium.
10. **Traps should be readable and fair.**
    Evidence: 5 say traps everywhere break the flow, including traps that appear from nothing.
    Dominion could: give every trap a clear tell, and keep traps out of the main fighting space unless
    the team can see them.
    Strength: weak.
11. **Let every sound be turned fully down, and a dodge work while moving.**
    Evidence: 2 say music cannot go below ten per cent; 2 say the perfect dodge only works standing
    still.
    Dominion could: let every volume go to zero, and make the dodge work from any movement.
    Strength: weak.
12. **Harder should mean smarter or faster, not only tougher.**
    Evidence: 3 praise harder settings for making enemies faster, not tankier; 2 complain of
    bullet-sponge bosses and minibosses.
    Dominion could: raise speed, numbers or behaviour on higher difficulty rather than health alone.
    Strength: weak.
13. **Fan-service choices draw attention far beyond their count.**
    Evidence: 12 censorship reviews carry 52% of every helpful vote; across the block, two games' review
    pools are mostly arguments about fan service (cross-game section 15).
    Dominion could: decide fan-service choices on purpose before launch, and say plainly on the store
    page what the game shows.
    Strength: medium.

### Limits
- Every English review was read: no margin of error. Only eleven months of reviews, and mid 2026 is
  8 reviews, so no long trend is drawn.
- 10 reviews (8.6%) were edited after posting and are counted on the date first written.
- 119 reviews in other languages (51% of the audience) were not read.
- Whether the shadow is censorship cannot be settled from reviews; the page records both sides.
- 15 of 81 thumbs-up reviews (19%) say nothing.
- **What changed from the earlier entry:** the game now has its own ranked page,
  `findings/full-metal-schoolgirl.md` (the earlier entry said there was none). The earlier entry said
  the page gave no trend; the rebuilt page has a three-period table. On the 2026-10-03 re-run,
  "cannot save and come back" is 3, not 4 (plus 2 runs lost to a crash and 3 harsh restarts), and the
  aim-speed comments sit at 4 where the first count found 5. The rebuilt pages also fill in enemies,
  traps, the camera, crashes and the full lists.
- The sensitivity patch, the bundle prices and the claim that the game is identical across platforms
  are what reviewers say; the page did not check them.
- 1 review marked English was in Portuguese; it was read from its content.
- Adult content is recorded in neutral words; crude remarks are left out.

---

## Banzai Escape

**Read:** all 80 of 80 English reviews there were when the reviews were pulled (100%). Every English
review was read, so there is no margin of error; one more review has arrived since. Written March 2016
to July 2026 (48 months with reviews, across ten years, every one read). **63.8% thumbs up** in the
English reviews, 51 up and 29 down (Steam, all languages: 68.5% of 168 reviews, Mostly Positive).
**Pages:** `findings/banzai-escape-english.md`, `findings/banzai-escape.md`, `findings/roguelike-block.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` section 15.

**What it is:** a single-player on-rails cover shooter (like the arcade game Time Crisis, played with a
keyboard and mouse) from 2016, made by one person and ported from Android phones. About $6. Missions
you replay to earn money; money buys guns and ammunition. About two hours, eight levels, two bosses and
twelve guns, says one reviewer. Not a roguelike and not a third-person shooter. The oldest game in the
block: 26 of its 80 reviews are from 2016 and no later year has more than eight. The engine is not on
the page (two reviewers call it a Unity shooter).

**How close to Dominion:** not close. It shares shooting, cover and boss fights. It is single-player,
on rails, made for phones first, and has no runs, co-op or extraction. Its lessons are about economy,
fairness and controls.

### The short version (plain words)
