One rule is the whole complaint: you have to buy your ammunition. It was said 17 times, twice as often
as anything else, 11 of them thumbs down, and it shows up in every year from 2016 to 2021 and again in
2024 and 2026. The other half of the complaints come from it being a phone game moved to PC:
controls that do not respond (9), deaths that feel unfair (8), no weapon switch (5). The people who
like it say it is small, cheap, funny and hard, and five of them want a sequel. All 5 who say it is too
short still recommend it. Overall there are 66.2 pieces of praise and 131.2 complaints per 100
reviews.

### What players praised, most to least
1. **Thumbs up with no reason given.** 9 times.
2. **Funny, memorable writing and characters.** 5 times it was said, 4 thumbs up.
3. **Hard in a good way.** 5 times it was said, 4 thumbs up (the helicopter fight is named).
4. **Want a sequel.** 5 times it was said, all thumbs up; more, for the game's size, than any other
   game in the study. *"I'll be waiting for Episode 2!"* (35074164).
5. **A likeable cast of characters.** 4 times it was said, all thumbs up.
6. **The price is fair.** 3 times it was said.
7. **Good voice acting**, some say: *"pretty good for a low cost game. Well done."* (25319033). 3 times
   it was said. (See complaints: others say the opposite about the same voices.)
8. **It beats bigger games it is compared with.** 3 times it was said.
9. **The achievements are fair to finish.** 2 times it was said.
10. **The difficulty climbs fairly.** 2 times it was said.
11. **Deaths feel earned if you watch closely.** 2 times it was said.
12. **It runs on Linux, or with a light gun.** 2 times it was said.
13. **Made with passion.** 3 reviewers in 2016 and 2017 name the developer and say so; one grades it as
    a first game. (The re-run counts 1 on "made with care".)
14. One reviewer each: the options cover the basics (the reviewer calls them bare); outfits and guns
    come free, no paid extras; each gun has its own trade-offs; the shooting controls are tight; the
    places look like anime cities; the maker fixed the grammar with fan help; the soundtrack is great.

Only 20 kinds of praise were used in this game in all.

### What players complained about, most to least
1. **You must buy your ammunition, so you replay old levels to afford it.** 17 times it was said, 11
   from thumbs-down reviews (people who left). Run out of ammo mid-mission and you fail.
   *"Playing the same mission over, and over, and over again to earn money to buy ammo to use a good
   gun in order to complete the mission is not a good gameplay mechanic."* (25319033). *"This is the
   first shooter I've seen do this, and I can see why."* (55038865). *"grinding the same level again,
   and again... to eventually have enough money to buy enough ammo to fight a boss for 5min
   straight"* (226313875, 2026). This one rule is 9% of everything said about the game and 16% of all
   complaints.
2. **Controls do not respond.** 9 times it was said. The laser room is steered by dragging the mouse:
   *"the character moves everywhere except where you want"*.
3. **Deaths feel unfair.** 8 times it was said: hit while behind cover, no warning markers, melee
   attacks that kill instantly.
4. **Bad voice acting.** 8 times it was said; voice acting overall comes up 8 times on the first
   count, 5 of them thumbs down, and 11 times on the re-run (8 bad, 3 good): *"6th grade school
   theater level of bad"* (77141318). Some praise the same voices.
5. **Thumbs down with no reason given.** 6 times.
6. **A control is missing.** 5 times it was said: there is no weapon switch, so you may have to fight a
   boss with the pistol.
7. **Too short.** 5 times it was said, all 5 thumbs up: a complaint from people who still recommend it.
8. **It is a phone game.** 4 times it was said. *"a mobile port... the HUD and all the point and click
   stuff instead of a proper keyboard binding"*.
9. **Some enemies and doors take forever to shoot down.** 4 times it was said.
10. **It explains itself badly**, like the pistol being useless on bosses. 4 times it was said.
11. **Only buy it on sale.** 3 times it was said.
12. **The story is thin** or makes no sense until the end. 3 times it was said.
13. **It is buggy:** clipping, shots through walls, a gun that stops firing in the final stage. 3 times
    it was said.
14. **One gun makes the game far too easy.** 2 times it was said.
15. **Dying sends you back to the start of the stage**, with no checkpoint. 2 times it was said.
16. **It looked like a third-person shooter, not a rail shooter.** 2 times it was said.
17. **It looks dated or rough.** 2 times it was said.
18. One reviewer each: no recoil and a random spread; melee enemies kill you instantly with no
    counter; play Blue Estate or Typing of the Dead instead; no Steam Workshop; some guns sound like BB
    guns; black screens instead of cutscenes; enemies reload out of cover; the English text needs a
    native speaker; you can be hit behind cover though the maker says you cannot; hits do not register
    on some enemies; the aiming mark does not show where shots go; the cover switches side to side by
    itself in one mission; a bit pricey; not worth it except at $0.99; only a few outfits; the options
    are bare (native resolution only, one volume slider).

Overall: 105 complaints against 53 pieces of praise (0.50 praise for each complaint; the block as a
whole is 0.56). A recommending reviewer writes 0.86 complaints, the lowest in the block. Fans write
short; critics write about ammo.

### How it changed over time
| Period | Reviews | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2016 | 26 | 57.7% | 65.4 | 173.1 |
| 2017 to 2019 | 22 | 81.8% | 77.3 | 95.5 |
| 2020 to 2022 | 16 | 50.0% | 25.0 | 125.0 |
| 2023 to 2026 | 16 | 62.5% | 37.5 | 81.2 |

No trend is claimed: 26 reviews in 2016 and under nine in every later year. The ammo complaint is the
same in every year from 2016 to 2021 and again in 2024 and 2026: 22.7 to 25.0 per 100 reviews in every
period to 2022 and 12.5 after. Unresponsive controls fell from 19.2 per 100 in 2016 to 0 in 2020 to
2022; the phone-port complaint from 11.5 in 2016 to 0 after 2019. Unfair deaths were highest in 2023 to
2026, at 18.8. The developer told a 2018 reviewer it would be fixed in the sequel (47975155, the
reviewer says); this game was never changed.

### Co-op and online play
Single-player. No review asks for co-op.

### Combat, movement and feel
- Controls that do not respond (9), mostly the mouse-drag laser room.
- No weapon switch (5), so the gun you have is the gun you fight the boss with.
- Interface and controls built for touch, not for a keyboard (4).
- The cover switches side to side by itself in one mission (1).
- Guns: one gun makes the game far too easy 2; each gun has its own trade-offs 1; tight shooting
  controls 1; some guns sound like BB guns 1; no recoil, a random spread 1; sluggish weapon handling 1;
  hits do not register on some enemies 1; the aiming mark does not show where shots go 1.
- Works with a light gun, and on Linux with workarounds (2).

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- Unfair deaths (8): hits through cover, no warning markers before attacks, instant-kill melee. One
  reviewer says the developer insists cover hits do not happen (1).
- Enemies and doors that soak bullets (4). Melee units kill instantly with no counter (1). Enemies
  reload out of cover (1).
- Hard in a good way (5, 4 thumbs up; the helicopter fight is named); difficulty climbs fairly 2;
  deaths feel earned if you study 2. So players liked the challenge but not the unfair parts.
- Bosses that take five minutes of ammo you had to grind for (226313875).

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- Money buys guns and ammo; running out of ammo fails the mission; the fix is to replay old missions
  for money. 17 times it was said, 11 thumbs down. The single largest complaint, by a factor of two.
- Only a few outfits (1). The achievements are fair to finish (2).
- No randomness is recorded in the game.

### Runs, content and replay value
- Too short (5), all from people who recommend it; levels too short 1.
- Dying restarts the stage with no checkpoint (2).
- The replaying it does have is forced replaying for money, which players hate (17).
- Explains itself badly (4). Story thin (3); no cutscenes, black screens between levels (1).

### Money and price
- About $6. Fair 3; buy on sale only 3; a bit pricey 1; not worth it except at $0.99 1.
- Outfits and guns come free, no paid extras (1).
- The real money problem is inside the game (the ammo economy), not the store price.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Bugs: 3 (clipping, shots through walls, a final-stage bug that stops a gun firing).
- The options are bare: native resolution only, one volume slider (1).
- Looks dated or rough (2).
- The rest of the tech problems are the port: four complaints (unresponsive controls, unfair losses,
  missing controls, built for phones) the page says share one cause, the move from phone to PC.

### The studio, updates and community
- One developer, named by 3 early reviewers who say it was made with passion; one reviewer knows him
  from the Garry's Mod community (1).
- The developer corrected the dialogue's grammar with fan help (1). English subtitles still need a
  native speaker, says one.
- Never praised for updates, never called abandoned. The developer told a 2018 reviewer the ammo issue
  would be fixed in a sequel (47975155); this game kept it for ten years.
- 4 reviewers grade it as a one-person game; 3 reviews are adverts for the reviewer's own curator page.

### What players asked for
- A sequel (5).
- No paying for ammo, or no forced grinding to afford it (17).
- A weapon switch key (5).
- Proper keyboard and mouse controls instead of phone controls (4, plus 9 on unresponsive controls).
- Warning markers before attacks; cover that works (8).
- More game (5).
- Checkpoints inside a stage (2).
- Mod support through Steam Workshop (1).

### Only in this game
Nothing is used by this game alone (the 2026-10-03 re-run agrees: 0). What stands out: one economy
rule complained about for ten years without change, and four separate complaints that the page says
all come from one cause (the phone port). The page notes the study files them as four feelings on
purpose (Rico's ruling of 2026-09-10); adding them up to one cause is the developer's job. It also
drew the fewest comments per review of any game read (2.25) and the fewest kinds of comment (66), and
needed nothing new to describe it. Hours shown on reviews: median 3 hours; median 1 on thumbs-down
reviews and 3 on thumbs-up; none show 100 hours or more.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Never make players pay for the basics they need to finish content.**
   Evidence: 17 times it was said, 11 thumbs down, every year for ten years; 16% of all complaints.
   Dominion could: keep ammo found or refilled in the run; if ammo is a resource, never let running
   out mean grinding old content to come back; if extraction rewards buy gear, keep a free basic kit.
   Strength: medium (one small game, but the largest and longest-lasting complaint in it).
2. **Cover must protect and attacks must warn.**
   Evidence: 8 times it was said that deaths felt unfair: hit behind cover, no warning markers,
   instant-kill melee; one says the maker insists cover hits do not happen; unfair deaths were highest
   in 2023 to 2026, at 18.8 per 100 reviews.
   Dominion could: make cover rules exact and readable; give every big enemy attack a wind-up and a
   visual or sound warning; avoid instant kills without a tell.
   Strength: medium.
3. **Players expect to switch weapons whenever they want.**
   Evidence: 5 times it was said; a boss fought with only a pistol.
   Dominion could: always allow switching and rebinding.
   Strength: weak to medium.
4. **Build controls for the platform players are on.**
   Evidence: 4 say it is a phone port, 9 say controls do not respond, all from one cause.
   Dominion could: design for keyboard and mouse and for controller from the start, with full
   rebinding.
   Strength: medium.
5. **A small, funny, hard game earns a loyal few.**
   Evidence: 5 want a sequel; funny writing 5, likeable cast 4, satisfyingly hard 5.
   Dominion could: give characters a voice and humour; it buys goodwill that bugs and roughness do not
   erase.
   Strength: weak.
6. **When a fix is promised for "the sequel", players remember.**
   Evidence: the 2018 reply (47975155); the complaint continued to 2026.
   Strength: weak.
7. **Do not let a weak weapon be the only answer to a boss, and tell players which weapons fit which
   enemy.**
   Evidence: 4 say it explains itself badly, like the pistol being useless on bosses; 4 say some
   enemies and doors take forever to shoot down; 2 say one gun makes the game far too easy.
   Dominion could: show plainly which weapons hurt which enemies, and keep a fallback weapon that can
   still finish a fight.
   Strength: weak.
8. **The store page must show what kind of game it is.**
   Evidence: 2 bought it thinking it was a third-person shooter and got a rail shooter.
   Dominion could: show real play from the player's camera in the first seconds of the trailer and
   screenshots.
   Strength: weak.
9. **Voice acting splits players; budget for it or keep it small.**
   Evidence: 8 say the voice acting is bad and 3 that it is good, about the same voices.
   Dominion could: cast and direct the main voices with care, and keep lines short where the budget is
   thin.
   Strength: weak.
10. **Checkpoints inside a long stage stop one death from wiping a stage.**
    Evidence: 2 say dying sends you back to the start of the stage.
    Dominion could: decide on purpose what one death costs a team in a run, and keep it from wiping
    long stretches without warning.
    Strength: weak.

### Limits
- Every English review was read at the time of the pull; one more has arrived since.
- 26 reviews in 2016 and under nine in every other year: no trend is claimed.
- 6 reviews (7.5%) were edited after posting.
- 88 reviews in other languages (52% of the audience) were not read.
- 19% of the reviews say nothing (9 thumbs up, 6 thumbs down).
- **What changed from the earlier entry:** the game now has its own ranked page,
  `findings/banzai-escape.md` (the earlier entry said there was none). The earlier entry said the page
  recorded no price comments and no tech findings; the rebuilt pages count price (fair 3, sale only 3,
  pricey 1, worth it only at $0.99 1) and bugs (3), bare options (1) and rough looks (2). The rebuilt
  pages add a four-period table; still no trend is claimed. Voice acting is 11 on the re-run (8 bad, 3
  good).
- The price, the developer's 2018 reply and the grammar fix are what reviewers say; the page did not
  check them.
- 2 reviews marked English were in Spanish and Indonesian; they were read from their content.

---

## SCP: Abhorrent

**Read:** all 69 of 69 English reviews there were when the reviews were pulled (100%). Every English
review was read, so there is no margin of error; one more review has arrived since. Written March 2022
to July 2026 (25 months with reviews, every one read). **63.8% thumbs up** in the English reviews, 44
up and 25 down (Steam, all languages: 57.7% of 111 reviews, Mixed).
**Pages:** `findings/scp-abhorrent-english.md`, `findings/scp-abhorrent.md`, `findings/roguelike-block.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` section 15.

**What it is:** a free, thirty-minute horror game set in the SCP world, from 2022, made by students. A
pitch-black corridor built by the game, a pistol that only kills with headshots, a flamethrower, and an
opening cutscene people cannot stop talking about. The page does not say how many players it takes,
and nothing on it mentions co-op beyond one reviewer who would like it. The camera, engine and hosting
are not on the page. 42 of its 69 reviews are from 2022; the launch month, March 2022, holds 28.

**How close to Dominion:** not close. It shares shooting and a corridor the game builds for you. It is
free horror, very short, with no runs, co-op or extraction.

### The short version (plain words)
The praise is for how bad it is. The most common real praise is funny writing (11 times it was said),
but 4 of those 11 are on thumbs-down reviews: people are laughing at the game. *"greatest opening
cutscene of all time. completely unplayable tho"* (158938519). The only sincere praise is the music (7,
all thumbs up). The one honest complaint is that it is too dark to see (6 times). The studio fixed that
with a brightness slider after a reviewer complained, and the reviewer changed his thumb to up. Players
also find it too hard (4) and unclear about what to do (3). 19 of the 69 reviews (28%) say nothing at
all.

### What players praised, most to least
1. **Thumbs up with no reason given.** 12 times.
2. **The writing is funny or memorable** (mostly the opening scene, mostly as a joke). 11 times it was
   said, 4 of them on thumbs-down reviews. *"I was excited because the intro was so bad it's good,
   then I was disappointed because the game was so bad it's bad"* (131402396). *"It's free, so get it
   to get omegalulz out of how bad it is."* (112645927, thumbs up).
3. **The music fits.** 7 times it was said, all thumbs up: *"Music slaps"*. The only praise the page
   reads as sincere.
4. **The dark corridors are tense and spooky.** 4 times it was said.
5. **It is free, so there is little to lose.** 4 times it was said.
6. **It is tough until you learn to save ammo and pick the right gun** (the flamethrower for the
   jumping enemies). 3 times it was said.
7. One reviewer each: it is worth replaying; a trick gives endless ammo (the reviewer asks for a fix);
   the ending is good; marking places with spray paint is fun; better than the mixed reviews made them
   expect; points for using real voice actors.
8. **The developers fixed a problem after a review** (see Studio).

Only 11 kinds of praise were used in this game.

### What players complained about, most to least
1. **Thumbs down with no reason given.** 7 times.
2. **Too dark to see.** 6 times it was said, 4 thumbs down. *"Visibility was too limited for me to
   continue playing."* (117699810).
3. **Too hard; nothing seems to die.** 4 times it was said (one says the spider kills anything near it;
   one asks if anyone has beaten it).
4. **Nothing tells you what to do or where to go** (find the ladder). 3 times it was said.
5. **Ammo and health run out fast.** 2 times it was said.
6. **Over in under an hour** (fifty minutes; under thirty minutes). 2 times it was said.
7. **Aiming is rough and only headshots kill.** 2 times it was said.
8. **It looks dated and ugly.** 2 times it was said.
9. One reviewer each: pickups are hard to see; dead enemies' voices follow you for the rest of the
   game; too few kinds of enemy; you never know if the exit is near or far; dying does not restart or
   return to the menu; the opening voice acting is very bad; headshot-only kills are a lazy way to make
   it hard; enemies see through walls; the sound gets annoying; a boss whose small target is hard to
   hit at range.
10. **Unplayable / bad** in general, wrapped in jokes: *"completely unplayable tho"* (158938519); *"so
    bad it's bad"* (131402396).

Only 17 kinds of complaint were used in this game.

Overall: 39 complaints against 46 pieces of praise on the first count (1.18 praise for each
complaint); the 2026-10-03 re-run counts 37 complaints and 46 praise, 53.6 and 66.7 per 100 reviews,
which the cross-game table gives as 1.24 : 1. The page warns that this number is a joke told straight:
a quarter of the bullets are ironic praise. It does not mean the game is better than Helldivers 2
(1.95); it means the study has met sarcasm at volume.

### How it changed over time
| Period | Reviews | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2022 | 42 | 73.8% | 50.0 | 42.9 |
| 2023 to 2026 | 27 | 48.1% | 48.1 | 44.4 |

No trend is claimed from these two periods: 42 of 69 reviews are from 2022 and no later year has more
than ten. The thumb fell from 73.8% to 48.1% while praise and complaints per 100 stayed nearly level.
Inside them, music praise fell from 14.3 per 100 reviews to 3.7 and the mood from 9.5 to 0; "nothing
tells you what to do" rose from 0 to 11.1 and the "it's free" line from 2.4 to 11.1. The one change
named is the brightness slider.

### Co-op and online play
Nothing is said about online play, and the player count is not on the page. One reviewer asks for
co-op, in a comment asking for more enemy variety and unique mini-bosses (1).

### Combat, movement and feel
- A pistol that only kills with a headshot, and a flamethrower.
- Enemies only die to headshots and the aiming is rough: 2; headshot-only kills are a lazy way to make
  it hard: 1.
- Tough until you learn to save ammo and match weapons to enemies: 3.
- Spray-painting marks is fun: 1.
- Nothing is said about movement or controls beyond the aiming.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- Too hard: 4. Too few kinds of enemy: 1. Enemies see through walls: 1. A boss whose small target is
  hard to hit at range: 1.
- Ammo and health scarce: 2. A trick gives endless ammo: 1.
- One reviewer jokes that the true ending can only be reached by NOT typing the god-mode cheat:
  *"the true ending which you can 100% only get by NOT pressing tilda and typing in God"* (116897590).
  That is the third time in the study a reviewer mentions using a cheat to get through.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
Nothing beyond the ammo. It is a thirty-minute game. The corridor the game builds means you never know
whether the exit is near: 1.

### Runs, content and replay value
- Thirty minutes, in a corridor the game builds. Over in under an hour: 2. Worth replaying: 1.
- Nothing tells you what to do: 3. Dying does not restart or return to the menu: 1.
- The ending is good: 1.

### Money and price
Free. Fair, because it is free: 4. The free price is part of the joke recommendation: *"It's free, so
get it..."* (112645927). One gives points for using real voice actors.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Too dark to see (6). One reviewer's monitor showed nothing; a brightness slider fixed it (112105387).
- Pickups hard to see: 1. Dead enemies' voices follow you: 1. Looks dated: 2.
- One reviewer's 374-hour count is a joke about the loading screen.

### The studio, updates and community
**The studio answered a review and fixed the problem.** 112105387 did not recommend the game because
his monitor showed nothing. *"As you can see from the comments to my review, the developers quickly
released a patch fixing this issue using a brightness slider."* He changed his thumb to up. The page
counts this as the fourth time in the block a reviewer says the studio replied to their review, and the
second time the reply changed the verdict. 4 grade it as a student or small-team project. Nothing is
said about update pace.

### What players asked for
- A way to see in the dark (6): a brightness setting, which came.
- A tutorial or a clear objective (3).
- More visible pickups and more health kits (1).
- A fix for the endless-ammo trick (1).
- Unique mini-bosses and co-op (1).
- A remaster (1).

### Only in this game
Nothing is used by this game alone (the 2026-10-03 re-run agrees: 0). What stands out: the only game in
the study whose praise is mostly ironic, and the fewest things said per review of any game read (1.41),
with 37 different kinds of comment, the fewest of any game. It is also the only game where the bare
verdicts and "small studio" grading are the largest share of everything said (32.0%). The page calls
it a floor: below about 1.5 comments per review, the reviews read more like a poll than text. Hours
shown on reviews: median 0 for both thumbs.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Darkness must never stop players from seeing.**
   Evidence: 6 times it was said that it is too dark, 4 thumbs down; a brightness slider turned one
   thumbs down into a thumbs up; 1 more says pickups are hard to see.
   Dominion could: ship with a brightness and gamma setting and a calibration screen; test dark areas
   on cheap monitors; keep enemies and pickups readable in the dark (outlines, glow, sound).
   Strength: medium (small count, but a cheap fix that flipped a verdict).
2. **A fast, visible fix after a public complaint can change the review.**
   Evidence: 112105387 flipped to thumbs up after the studio replied and patched.
   Dominion could: read reviews in the launch weeks, reply where a fix is coming, and point to the
   patch when it lands.
   Strength: weak (one review), but repeated across the block (see the block entry).
3. **Laughs are not the same as liking.**
   Evidence: 4 of 11 "funny writing" comments are on thumbs-down reviews; the high praise number is
   ironic.
   Dominion could: when reading its own playtest or review praise, check whether people laugh with the
   game or at it.
   Strength: weak.
4. **Music can carry a weak game.**
   Evidence: 7 times it was said that the music fits, all thumbs up, the only sincere praise.
   Strength: weak.
5. **Tell players what to do and where the exit is.**
   Evidence: 3 say nothing tells them what to do, rising from 0 in 2022 to 11.1 per 100 reviews after;
   1 never knew if the exit was near or far in the generated corridor.
   Dominion could: always show the current goal and the way to extraction, even in generated levels.
   Strength: weak.
6. **"Only headshots kill" reads as lazy difficulty.**
   Evidence: 4 say it is too hard and nothing seems to die; 2 say only headshots kill and aiming is
   rough; 1 calls headshot-only kills a lazy way to make it hard.
   Dominion could: let body shots always do real damage, and make weak points a bonus, not the only way.
   Strength: weak.
7. **After a death, get players back in fast.**
   Evidence: 1 says dying does not restart or return to the menu.
   Dominion could: after a wipe, offer restart, return to the hub and quit right away.
   Strength: weak.

### Limits
- Every English review was read at the time of the pull; one more has arrived since.
- 42 of 69 reviews are from 2022; no trend is claimed.
- 4 reviews (5.8%) were edited after posting.
- 42 reviews in other languages were not read.
- 19 of 69 reviews (28%) say nothing: 12 thumbs up, 7 thumbs down.
- **What changed from the earlier entry:** the game now has its own ranked page,
  `findings/scp-abhorrent.md` (the earlier entry said there was none). The rebuilt pages count
  difficulty, unclear goals, length, enemies and the period split, which the earlier entry did not
  have. The re-run counts 37 complaints, not 39. The game pages still call it the only block game
  with more praise than complaint; on the cross-game recount, Alien Dawn is also just above one
  (1.04 : 1), so that claim holds only on the first count.
- The patch is what the reviewer says; the page did not check it.
- The page cannot record why players laugh; the study has no way to mark praise as ironic.
- 1 review marked English was in Spanish; it was read from its content.

---

## Alien Dawn

**Read:** all 74 of 74 English reviews (100%). Every English review was read, so there is no margin of
error. Written October 2021 to August 2026 (21 months with reviews, every one read). **63.5% thumbs
up** in the English reviews, 47 up and 27 down (Steam, all languages: 64.0% of 100 reviews, Mixed).
**Pages:** `findings/alien-dawn-english.md`, `findings/alien-dawn.md`, `findings/roguelike-block.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` section 15.

**What it is:** a co-op open-world survival shooter against aliens, playable in VR and on a normal
screen, and VR players and normal-screen players share the same server. Made by two people. In early
access since October 2021 at about $15, still in early access (all 74 reviews were written in early
access). A session: crash-land, loot farmhouses, avoid the alien mothership, reach the helicopter to
get out. One reviewer describes a change of engine; the page does not name the engines. One reviewer
lists ten-player multiplayer; the hosting model is not on the page. The busiest months are October
2021 (the early-access launch) and August 2022, 14 reviews each.

**How close to Dominion:** fairly close in shape. Co-op, sci-fi (aliens), PvE, a session that ends by
reaching a helicopter to leave (close to extraction). It differs in being open world, survival, VR-first
and not built on runs. The camera is first person in VR; the page does not say what the flat-screen
camera is.

### The short version (plain words)
The two developers are the most-praised thing about this game. 21 of the 74 reviews say something
about them, and 20 of those 21 are warm. 6 say they bought it to fund the studio. It is the one game in
the block that players did not call abandoned: a 2025 edit says the new update was *"proof the devs
have been doing their jobs"*. VR is the fault line: playing VR and normal-screen players together is
loved (5 times, all thumbs up), but VR that will not launch or misreads your hands is the main reason
people leave (8 times, 7 thumbs down). Finding other players is hard: 5 say nobody is playing. Praise
and complaint are almost even (94.6 and 90.5 per 100 reviews), and the praise is for the people, not
the game.

### What players praised, most to least
1. **Thumbs up with no reason given.** 7 times.
2. **The developers listen and act.** 7 times it was said, all 7 thumbs up. *"invite those who have
   negative experiences to the discord to chat about where the problems lie"* (101695010).
3. **Bought it to support the studio.** 6 times it was said, 4 thumbs up. *"Buy to support
   development, but not to play a game yet as it is still very WIP."* (104979629, a thumbs down
   *"only because Steam has no sideways thumbs"*).
4. **Much better with friends.** 6 times it was said.
5. **VR and normal-screen players can play together.** 5 times it was said, all thumbs up. *"The fact
   that you can play with friends that don't have VR is amazing."* (101852717).
6. **The price is fair.** 4 times it was said (one says a thumbs down at $24.99, fun at the sale price).
7. **Graded kindly because the studio is small.** 4 times it was said, 3 thumbs up.
8. **Night, caves and music make it spooky.** 3 times it was said.
9. **It has real potential.** 3 times it was said.
10. **Nothing else is like it**, including its automatic film camera. 3 times it was said.
11. **Players know the developers by name.** 3 times it was said, all thumbs up. *"I had a lot of fun
    running with the developers (Christian and Jes) the day after writing this review"* (101584057).
12. **Reaching the way out alone is hard in a good way.** 2 times it was said.
13. **The guns feel fairly real.** 2 times it was said (against 2 who say they feel like toys).
14. **Flare guns, voice chat and messing about with friends add fun.** 2 times it was said.
15. **Updates keep coming.** 2 times it was said.
16. **You never crash-land in the same spot**; random loot and hidden areas. 2 times it was said.
17. **Every escape is an hour of fun**, and it never gets old. 2 times it was said.
18. **Patches fixed performance** (a CPU problem, and after the engine change). 2 times it was said.
19. **Still supported.** 2 reviews, 2022 (*"Devs are NOT abandoning this game"*) and a 2025 edit.
20. One reviewer each: the VR options cover everything (free motion, smooth turning); co-op and
    driving are really good; players can join mid-match, and a walkie-talkie helps you find each
    other; hoped to share it with someone close, too clunky for now; a thumbs up for what it will
    become; easy to pick up; a funny moment with food; the VR controls are solid; a steady 90 frames a
    second; tried the demo first.

### What players complained about, most to least
1. **VR controls do not respond well.** 10 times it was said, 5 thumbs up and 5 down: grabbing,
   holsters, the circle menus for the inventory.
2. **It does not work on my VR setup.** 8 times it was said, 7 from thumbs-down reviews (people who
   left): will not launch in VR, hands misaligned on Valve Index controllers, launches in the wrong VR
   system (Windows Mixed Reality), player height stuck. *"Wouldn't buy in its current state if you
   intend on playing VR."* (103293267).
3. **Nobody plays, so there are no groups to join.** 5 times it was said (one never managed to join
   one; one says hosting is the best way to find players).
4. **The world has nothing to remember or find your way by.** 4 times it was said. *"there are no
   unique landmarks that are important in open world games for pathfinding... Everything is designed
   on a flat axis plane"* (104979629).
5. **They asked for their money back.** 3 times it was said.
6. **The guns feel floaty, like toys**, and enemies like paper bags. 2 times it was said.
7. **Motion sickness in VR.** 2 times it was said, both thumbs down.
8. **Not worth paying for yet.** 2 times it was said.
9. **It looks built from bought store parts.** 2 times it was said.
10. **A bug stopped play**, such as not being able to board the helicopter on the first map. 2 times it
    was said.
11. **It looks dated.** 2 times it was said.
12. **Only buy it on sale.** 2 times it was said.
13. **Thumbs down with no reason given.** 2 times.
14. **All guns point thirty degrees low in VR, and shots spray.** 2 times it was said.
15. One reviewer each: the frame rate drops in built-up areas; it is less fun alone; a friend
    connected, spoke and vanished from the list; died to an alien they could not see; desktop mode
    shows it was made for VR (the backpack is worse with a mouse); enemy animations are clunky; ten
    players make it easier than solo; the trailer promises a different game; you can eat a downed
    teammate's food and leave them; bought for friends who did not care for it; it feels thrown
    together; the positive rating is not deserved; buggy; the grass setting makes it run badly; every
    update hangs one player's computer for hours; little VR customisation and forced toggle grab; one
    male and one female character, no way to change looks, and found clothes are lost with the match;
    paid $1.39 in a sale and still felt ripped off.
16. **Too early to play** ("still very WIP"), from people who bought to support it.
17. One support request went unanswered (the one cold comment out of 21 about the developers).

Overall: 68 complaints against 70 pieces of praise on the first count (1.03 praise for each
complaint); the 2026-10-03 re-run counts 67 complaints (1.04 : 1 in the cross-game table). The page
says this is real, not ironic, and mostly praise for the developers. It warns that it does not make
this a better game than larger ones (a reader using the number would rank it above Back 4 Blood, at
0.86); it is a smaller game with a closer studio.

### How it changed over time
| Period | Reviews | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2021 (early-access launch) | 25 | 80.0% | 124.0 | 108.0 |
| 2022 | 38 | 57.9% | 76.3 | 73.7 |
| 2023 to 2026 | 11 | 45.5% | 27.3 | 81.8 |

63 of the 74 reviews are from 2021 and 2022. The game after the engine change one reviewer describes
is covered by only eleven reviews, so no trend is claimed. What the numbers show: praise per 100 fell
from 124.0 to 76.3 to 27.3. Crossplay praise fell from 16.0 per 100 reviews to 2.6 to 0; "listens and
acts" from 12.0 to 10.5 to 0. Broken VR setups rose from 8.0 to 10.5 to 18.2. "Still supported" is
2.6 in 2022 and 9.1 after; never "abandoned". One edited review moves praise from 2023 to 2025.

### Co-op and online play
- VR players and normal-screen players on one server: 5 times praised, all thumbs up. The page notes
  this is the reason to buy and, when VR breaks, the reason it does not work.
- Much better with friends 6; co-op and driving reward teamwork 1.
- Joining: players can join mid-match, and a walkie-talkie helps you find each other (1); another
  names flare guns to find teammates and local voice chat.
- Scaling: one reviewer lists ten-player multiplayer; one says ten players make it easier than solo.
- You can feed downed teammates, or eat their food and leave them (1). Players messing about with each
  other, running each other over, is fun (1).
- Problems: nobody plays, so no groups (5); a friend connected, spoke and vanished from the list (1);
  bought for friends who did not care for it (1); less fun alone (1).
- Players played alongside the developers (101584057).
- Nothing is said about hosting beyond one reviewer's tip that hosting is the best way to find
  players, or about lag.
- No review talks about a player community, its habits or its in-jokes; the block page counts zero
  such comments in all four of the block's co-op games.

### Combat, movement and feel
- VR handling of guns and gear (grabbing, holsters, inventory circles) does not respond well: 10.
- VR setup failures: 8. Motion sickness: 2.
- The guns feel floaty and toy-like, enemies like paper bags: 2; the guns feel fairly real: 2.
- All guns point thirty degrees low in VR, and shots spray: 2. Melee is called useless twice inside
  these comments.
- Little VR customisation and forced toggle grab: 1. The options cover free motion and smooth turning:
  1. VR controls solid: 1.
- Desktop mode is worse with a mouse: 1.
- Nothing is said about stuns.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- Reaching the way out alone is hard in a good way: 2.
- Died to an alien that could not be seen: 1. Enemy animations clunky: 1. Enemies attack faster than
  their animations and only notice you up close, say two comments.
- Ten players make it easier than solo: 1.
- Nothing is said about the mothership as a fight or about bosses.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- Never the same crash site, random loot and hidden areas: 2. Loot too random to plan around, says one
  comment on the empty world.
- One male and one female character, no way to change looks, and found clothes are lost with the
  match: 1.
- Nothing is said about progression between sessions.

### Runs, content and replay value
- The open world has no landmarks and is flat (4).
- Every escape run is over an hour of fun and it never gets old: 2.
- A bug stopped one player boarding the helicopter on the first map (part of 2 play-stopping bugs).
- Night, caves and music make it spooky: 3.
- The trailer promises a different game: 1.

### Money and price
- About $15 in early access. 6 bought it to fund the studio.
- Fair 4 (one says a thumbs down at $24.99, fun at the sale price). Only on sale 2; refunded 3; not
  worth paying for yet 2; good value while unfinished 1. One paid $1.39 in a sale and still felt ripped
  off. One tried the demo first.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- VR setup failures (8, 7 thumbs down): will not launch in VR, Index hands misaligned, launches in
  Windows Mixed Reality, height stuck.
- Bugs that stop play 2 (one could not board the helicopter); buggy 1.
- Frame rate drops in built-up areas 1; the grass setting makes it run badly 1; a steady 90 frames a
  second 1. Every update hangs one player's computer for hours 1.
- A patch fixed a CPU problem and, after the engine change, performance: 2.
- Looks dated 2; built from bought store parts 2.

### The studio, updates and community
- 21 of 74 reviews mention the two developers; 20 are warm. Listens and acts 7; known by name 3.
- The developers invite unhappy players to their Discord to talk (101695010).
- Players know them by name and played with them (3; 101584057).
- Steady updates 2; fixed what mattered 2; still supported 2 (2022 and a 2025 edit); never called
  abandoned (0).
- One support request went unanswered (1). 2 reviews are written to the studio rather than to buyers.
- The only block game whose biggest praise group is the studio itself; no other game in the study has
  the studio relationship as its largest praise group.

### What players asked for
- VR that launches and tracks correctly on every headset (8).
- Better VR grabbing, holsters and menus (10).
- Landmarks in the world (4).
- Comfort settings against motion sickness (2).
- More players, or easier ways to find a group (5).
- Manual reloading in VR (1).
- A way to change how characters look (1).
- A finished game (the "support it, but wait" reviews).

### Only in this game
Nothing is used by this game alone (the 2026-10-03 re-run agrees: 0). It is the first VR game in the
study, and the first where playing VR and normal-screen players together came up; it needed nothing new
to describe it. It is the one game in the block that kept being supported, and its reviews say so in
2025. Hours shown on reviews are low: median 1 hour; median 0 on thumbs-down reviews and 2 on
thumbs-up; none show 100 hours or more.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **A small studio that talks to players, by name, earns praise that outweighs rough edges.**
   Evidence: 21 of 74 reviews mention the two developers, 20 warmly; 7 say they listen and act; 3 name
   them; 6 bought to fund them.
   Dominion could: be present in a Discord, invite unhappy players to talk, play with the community,
   and let players know who the team is.
   Strength: medium.
2. **Keeping updates going is the difference from the rest of the block.**
   Evidence: 0 abandonment comments here against 9 in ArcRunner; "still supported" in 2022 and 2025.
   Dominion could: plan a long, steady update rhythm rather than a launch burst.
   Strength: medium (read with the block).
3. **Letting players on different setups play together is loved; a broken setup is fatal.**
   Evidence: crossplay of VR and normal screens praised 5 times (all thumbs up); setup failures 8
   times (7 thumbs down), rising from 8.0 to 18.2 per 100 reviews in the latest period.
   Dominion could: if it lets mixed inputs or platforms play together, test every combination; a
   player whose setup does not launch writes a thumbs down.
   Strength: medium.
4. **Open spaces need landmarks.**
   Evidence: 4 times it was said; the same complaint about empty spaces came up for ArcRunner's arenas
   and Zombie Girl's floors.
   Dominion could: give every arena and extraction map tall, unique landmarks to find your way and
   call out positions to teammates.
   Strength: weak to medium (small counts, three games).
5. **Players will pay to support a studio they trust, but say so in the thumb.**
   Evidence: 6 bought to support; one gave a thumbs down only because there is no middle option.
   Dominion could: be honest about early access state on the store page so supporters are not
   reviewing a promise.
   Strength: weak.
6. **A co-op game needs a way to find players, and must still work when there are few.**
   Evidence: 5 say nobody plays, so there are no groups; 1 says it is less fun alone; 1 friend vanished
   from the list after connecting; across the block, player culture is zero in all four co-op games
   (cross-game section 15).
   Dominion could: make public lobbies easy to find and join from the main menu, make a solo or
   two-player run worth playing, and test that friends who connect stay in the list.
   Strength: medium (block-wide reading).
7. **Getting to the way out must always work.**
   Evidence: a bug stopped one player boarding the helicopter on the first map; ZCREW's helicopter that
   would not come is the same moment.
   Dominion could: give extraction a backup path if the main trigger fails, and test boarding with
   every player count.
   Strength: weak by count, high stakes for an extraction game.
8. **Scale the danger with the number of players.**
   Evidence: one reviewer says ten players make it easier than going alone; 2 say reaching the way out
   alone is hard in a good way.
   Dominion could: raise enemy numbers or pressure with each added player, so a full team of four is not
   a walk-over and a solo player is not crushed.
   Strength: weak.
9. **Small co-op touches make the stories players tell.**
   Evidence: 2 say flare guns, voice chat and messing about with friends add fun; 1 names the
   walkie-talkie for finding each other; 1 tells of eating a downed teammate's food and leaving them.
   Dominion could: add cheap shared tools (flares, pings, nearby voice) and decide on purpose what
   teammates can take from each other.
   Strength: weak.
10. **Guns must point where the player aims, and feel solid.**
    Evidence: 2 say all guns point thirty degrees low in VR and shots spray; 2 say the guns feel like
    toys and enemies like paper bags.
    Dominion could: test aim against the crosshair on every weapon, and give hits clear feedback on the
    enemy.
    Strength: weak.
11. **Let players change how they look, and keep what they find.**
    Evidence: 1 complains of one male and one female character, no way to change looks, and found
    clothes lost with the match.
    Dominion could: let looks earned or found in a run carry over.
    Strength: weak.

### Limits
- Every English review was read: no margin of error. 63 of 74 reviews are from 2021 and 2022; the game
  today is covered by eleven reviews. No trend is claimed.
- 7 reviews (9.5%) were edited after posting; one moves praise from 2023 to 2025.
- 26 reviews in other languages were not read.
- 7 thumbs-up and 2 thumbs-down reviews say nothing.
- **What changed from the earlier entry:** the game now has its own ranked page,
  `findings/alien-dawn.md` (the earlier entry said there was none). The earlier entry had nothing on
  enemies, loot, price complaints, other tech problems or joining; the rebuilt pages count all of them,
  plus the three-period table and the empty player base (5). The re-run counts 67 complaints, not 68.
  On the cross-game recount the game is just above one praise per complaint (1.04 : 1), next to SCP:
  Abhorrent.
- The engine change, the 2025 update, the price and the ten-player figure are what reviewers say; the
  page did not check them.
- Personal details in some reviews (a reviewer's job, family) are left out; the developers' first
  names are kept only inside the quote that has them.
- The page does not give the hosting model, the flat-screen camera, or reviews written in another
  language under the English tag.

---

## Die After Sunset

**Read:** all 51 of 51 English reviews (100%). Every English review was read, so there is no margin of
error. Written February 2022 to August 2026 (19 months with reviews, every one read). **58.8% thumbs
up** in the English reviews, 30 up and 21 down (Steam, all languages: 65.0% of 103 reviews, Mixed).
**Pages:** `findings/die-after-sunset-english.md`, `findings/die-after-sunset.md`,
`findings/roguelike-block.md`, `findings/cross-game.md` section 15.

**What it is:** a third-person roguelite shooter (runs that end) from Playstark. In early access from
February 2022; full release (1.0) in August 2023 at about €20. Enemies get stronger as the sun sets, so
you keep the battlefield lit. Single-player: reviewers say co-op was promised and never came. Two
characters at first, each with their own weapons and abilities. One reviewer thanks the publisher PQube
for a free copy. The engine is not on the page. 40 of the 51 reviews were written in early access; the
busiest month is the early-access launch, February 2022, with 14. The page says Playstark later made
ArcRunner, but the ArcRunner page names a different studio (see Limits).

**How close to Dominion:** close in shape: third person, shooter, built on runs, with enemies that
grow stronger as the sun sets. The setting is a playful invasion, says one reviewer. It has no co-op
(players call that a broken promise) and no extraction.

### The short version (plain words)
The sharpest early-access fall in the block. 71% thumbs up during early access in 2022 (35 reviews),
40% in the year of full release (10 reviews), and 17% from 2024 to 2026 (6 reviews). Players say full
release made it worse, broke the first boss, and then the studio left. The broken promise is co-op: 5
reviews say less was delivered than promised, and 4 of them are about missing co-op. The top design
complaint is slow, clumsy movement (7), with a dodge that does not fire reliably (3), guns with no
punch (4) and upgrades that barely matter (4). What players like is the idea of keeping the battlefield
lit (4) and the cartoon look (4). It is the most negative text in the block: 80 complaints against 30
pieces of praise (154.9 complaints and 58.8 pieces of praise per 100 reviews).

### What players praised, most to least
1. **The bright cartoon look is great.** 4 times it was said.
2. **Keeping the battlefield lit as the sun sets is a fresh idea.** 4 times it was said.
3. **Thumbs up with no reason given.** 4 times.
4. **It has clear potential.** 2 times it was said.
5. **Upgrades and missions invite different builds.** 2 times it was said.
6. **Each character has its own weapons and abilities.** 2 times it was said.
7. **Devoted developers, in early access.** *"the devs are extremely devoted"* (2022). 1 review says
   the developers respond to bugs constantly; 1 that the developer adds content every month.
8. **Better than Risk of Rain 2, early on.** *"I prefer this to Risk of Rain 2 and I think it can be
   bigger!"* (110055549, 2022; the same reviewer later changed his mind).
9. One reviewer each: runs are short enough for a busy day; the shooting feels responsive; it is now
   stable; it runs on modest hardware; good value for early access; easy to pick up; lots of
   interesting powers and items; the currency progression is satisfying; great on Steam Deck; the
   playful invasion setting gives it personality.

Only 18 kinds of praise were used in this game; 30 pieces of praise in all.

### What players complained about, most to least
1. **Movement is slow and clumsy.** 7 times it was said, 5 from thumbs-up reviews (people who stayed),
   2 down. Moving up and down is tiring without upgrades. *"The movement is one of the weakest points
   of this experience, being clunky and inaccurate, I doubt it will be fixed."* (119558048, thumbs up).
2. **Co-op was promised and never came.** 5 times it was said that less was delivered than promised;
   4 of the 5 are about co-op (the fifth adds that there is no map either). *"Never gave us
   multiplayer"* (110085235). *"No Koop (YET)"* (134003294). *"Would be super cool to have the
   possibility to play with friends co-op."* (113712549). One more asks for it with a crude word
   (144492573).
3. **Builds barely change anything** (mostly elemental resistances). 4 times it was said, 2 up, 2
   down. *"The roguelike tag on the store page is really a stretch at this point as there is no real
   difference in builds or upgrades"* (118300691).
4. **Guns feel weightless.** 4 times it was said, 1 up, 3 down.
5. **A bug stopped play:** a soft-lock after the pirate-base event, a first boss you cannot reach or
   see, quests that fail by themselves and can freeze the game. 4 times it was said.
6. **The dodge does not work reliably** (useless or delayed). 3 times it was said, all 3 thumbs up.
7. **Early access never grew into what it promised.** 3 times it was said, all from 2023 on.
8. **It is buggy.** 3 times it was said.
9. **Little to do; three bosses;** a first run beaten in an hour. 2 times it was said.
10. **Hit from off screen with no warning;** the starting weapon is worthless and enemy shots hit at
    any range. 2 times it was said.
11. **Not worth it yet (wait; play the demo).** 2 times it was said, both 2022.
12. **Risk of Rain 2 or Gunfire Reborn does this better.** 2 times it was said.
13. **The levels look the same.** 2 times it was said.
14. **You cannot tell when you are being hit:** no health warning, no warning before attacks. 2 times
    it was said.
15. **The second character is weak and slow.** 2 times it was said.
16. **The tutorial and next steps are unclear** (nothing tells you that beating the boss starts a new
    level). 2 times it was said.
17. **It is not really a roguelite.** 2 times it was said.
18. One reviewer each: enemies spawn on top of you; a game pad feels like it pushes your aim away;
    mouse delay and forced acceleration; harder only means more enemies; the double jump has to be
    bought (and sometimes fails); the frame-rate lock and v-sync do nothing; menus overlap on an
    ultrawide screen; it crashes many times; too few enemy types; the bosses are too easy; the same
    three bosses every time; some events far harder than others; few items and most have no effect;
    grindy; fell through the ground; stuck on the scenery with no unstuck button; frame rate drops;
    paying coins to use a skill, with cooldowns not shown at the crosshair; the narrow view is a
    handicap; too high a price in 2023 against better games; a cash grab, two characters added and no
    fixes; refunded; working on the wrong thing first (characters and maps before the core play).
19. **Full release made it worse.** *"the game at 1.0 is worse than it was in early access"*; a patch
    made the first boss invisible. *"Within 5 minutes the gun i was holding was sideways... and i fell
    through the ground"*.
20. **Abandoned.** *"There are more bugs then features. Its also abandoned."* The block page counts 1
    review that updates stopped.

### How it changed over time
| | 2022 (early access) | 2023 (1.0 in August) | 2024 to 2026 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Reviews | 35 | 10 | 6 |
| Thumbs up | 71% (71.4%) | 40% | 17% (16.7%) |
| Praise per 100 | 60.0 | 10.0 | 66.7 |
| Complaints per 100 | 154.3 | 160.0 | 133.3 |
| What they say | *"the devs are extremely devoted"* | *"the game at 1.0 is worse than it was in early access"* | *"more bugs then features. Its also abandoned."* |

The same reviewer who wrote *"I prefer this to
Risk of Rain 2"* in 2022 edited it: *"2 years later, the content didn't pick up much whereas Risk of Rain
2 turned into a master piece, I wouldn't recommend it now, its a below average buggy game."*
(110055549). The turn players name is the 1.0 release. Later periods are 10 and 6 reviews, so the
percentages are shaky.

- **What fell:** the thumb, 71.4% to 40.0% to 16.7%; praise per 100 in 2023, to 10.0. Dull upgrades
  fell from 11.4 per 100 reviews in 2022 to 0 after.
- **What rose:** "never grew into its promise" rose from 2.9 to 20.0 per 100 in 2023 (the 2.9 in 2022
  is the 2024 edit of 110055549, counted on its 2022 date); the missing-co-op line from 8.6 to 20.0;
  bugs that stop play from 2.9 to 10.0 and 33.3.

### Co-op and online play
None in the game. Co-op was promised, players say, and never came: 4 of the 5 broken-promise
comments; one also asks for a horde mode. The page notes that the studio's roadmap is not in the study,
so "co-op was promised" is the reviewers' claim.

### Combat, movement and feel
- Slow, clumsy movement: 7 (5 from people who still recommend it).
- An unreliable dodge: 3 (all thumbs up). The double jump has to be bought (1) and sometimes fails.
- Weightless guns: 4 (3 thumbs down); the shooting feels responsive: 1.
- The starting weapon is worthless (part of 2 unfair-loss comments).
- One character is weak and slow next to the other: 2; each character plays its own way: 2.
- Paying coins to use a skill, and cooldowns not shown at the crosshair: 1.
- Controls: a game pad feels like it pushes your aim away 1; mouse delay with forced acceleration 1.
  A field-of-view slider and hold-to-sprint wanted (narrow view a handicap, 1).
- A gun held sideways after full release (bug).
- Nothing is said about stuns.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- Enemies strengthen as the sun sets (the core idea), praised 4 times as fresh.
- Hit from off screen with no direction marker, enemy shots hurting at any range: 2. You cannot tell
  when you are being hit (no health warning, no warning before attacks): 2.
- Enemies spawn on top of you: 1. Too few enemy types: 1. Difficulty only adds enemies: 1.
- Bosses: too easy 1; the same three bosses every time 1; some events far harder than others 1.
- A patch at full release broke the first boss (made it invisible, 1); one could not reach it.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- Builds and upgrades barely differ (mostly elemental resistances): 4. "Not really a roguelite": 2.
  Few items and most have no effect 1; grindy 1; shallow 1.
- Against: upgrades and missions invite different builds 2; lots of interesting powers 1; currency
  progression satisfying 1.
- The double jump bought with currency: 1.

### Runs, content and replay value
- Little to do, three bosses, a first run beaten in an hour: 2.
- The levels look the same: 2; fun for ten to fifteen hours before the single map repeats (one
  reviewer).
- Short runs fit a busy day: 1.
- Nothing tells you that beating the boss starts a new level (part of 2 "poorly explained").
- "The content didn't pick up much" over two years (110055549).

### Money and price
- About €20 at full release. Too high in 2023 against better games 1; a cash grab, two characters
  added and no fixes 1; refunded 1.
- Good value in early access 1; not worth it yet, play the demo and wait 2.
- One reviewer says the publisher gave them a free copy.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Bugs that stop play: 4 (a soft-lock after the pirate-base event, an unreachable first boss, quests
  that fail by themselves and can freeze the game). Buggy: 3.
- Falling through the floor: *"i fell through the ground to the endless void"* (the twelfth time in
  the study, in its seventh game). Stuck on the scenery, wants an unstuck button: 1.
- A gun held sideways; the first boss broken by the 1.0 patch.
- Crashes many times 1; frame rate drops 1; the frame-rate lock and v-sync do nothing 1; ultrawide
  menus overlap 1.
- Against: now stable 1; undemanding 1; great on Steam Deck 1.
- *"more bugs then features"*.

### The studio, updates and community
- Praised as devoted in early access; responds to bugs constantly (1); adds content every month (1).
  One reviewer says the developer commented on his review (118300691).
- One reviewer got a free copy from the publisher: *"Thanks Laurence from PQube for the copy!"*
  (119909633).
- Working on the wrong thing first, characters and maps before the core play: 1. Waiting for a
  campaign mode: 1.
- 1.0 made it worse; then called abandoned (1).
- 2 reviews are adverts for the reviewer's own curator page.
- The page says this studio went on to make ArcRunner, whose reviews repeat the same arc and the
  same two complaints (slow, thin upgrades) a year later. The ArcRunner page names a different studio.

### What players asked for
- Co-op (4 of 5 broken-promise comments), and a horde mode (1).
- Faster, cleaner movement and a reliable dodge (7 and 3).
- Builds that matter (4).
- Guns with punch (4).
- A hit warning and a marker showing where hits come from (2).
- A field-of-view slider and hold-to-sprint (1).
- An unstuck button (1).
- A campaign mode (1).
- A map (1).
- More content after release.

### Only in this game
Nothing is used by this game alone (the 2026-10-03 re-run agrees: 0). What stands out: the sharpest
fall from early access to full release in the block, and a missing co-op mode as the broken promise.
It is the most negative text in the block (0.38 praise for each complaint), level with Terminull
Brigade. Hours shown on reviews: median 1 hour for both thumbs; none show 100 hours or more.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Full release must be better than early access, not worse.**
   Evidence: 71% thumbs up in early access (35 reviews), 40% in the 1.0 year (10), 17% after (6);
   *"the game at 1.0 is worse than it was in early access"*; a 1.0 patch broke the first boss; bugs
   that stop play rose from 2.9 to 33.3 per 100 reviews.
   Dominion could: freeze features well before 1.0, test the first run and first boss above all, and
   do not ship 1.0 with new breaking changes.
   Strength: medium (small numbers, clear direction).
2. **If co-op is promised, it must ship, and early.**
   Evidence: 4 of 5 broken-promise comments are missing co-op.
   Dominion already is co-op; the lesson is to have four-player co-op and hosting working from the
   first public build, not added later.
   Strength: weak by count, high stakes for Dominion.
3. **Movement and dodge feel decide a roguelite shooter, again.**
   Evidence: slow movement 7 (5 from recommenders), unreliable dodge 3 (all recommenders), weightless
   guns 4. ArcRunner got the same complaint.
   Dominion could: make the dodge always fire when pressed, give clear hit feedback, and tune speed
   with playtests.
   Strength: medium (two games, same complaints).
4. **Builds must feel different, or players say it is not a roguelite.**
   Evidence: shallow builds 4, "not a roguelite" 2.
   Dominion could: make upgrades change how you play (new behaviour), not just small number bumps.
   Strength: weak to medium.
5. **The early-access-to-abandoned arc repeats across a studio's games.**
   Evidence: this game and ArcRunner show the same arc a year apart (the page says same studio).
   Dominion could: plan the after-launch years before launch; the block shows small studios are
   praised early and blamed later.
   Strength: medium (read with the block).
6. **Players must know when and from where they are being hit.**
   Evidence: 2 say they are hit from off screen with no warning; 2 say they cannot tell when they are
   being hit (no health warning, no warning before attacks).
   Dominion could: show a direction marker for every hit, a clear low-health warning, and a tell before
   big attacks.
   Strength: weak to medium.
7. **Fix the core before adding characters and maps.**
   Evidence: 1 says the studio worked on characters and maps before the core play; 1 calls two new
   characters with no fixes a cash grab; 2 say the second character is weak and slow.
   Dominion could: put feel, bugs and balance ahead of new content in the update plan, and say so.
   Strength: weak.
8. **Give players a way out when the game traps them.**
   Evidence: a soft-lock after an event, an unreachable first boss, quests that freeze the game (4 bugs
   that stop play); 1 stuck on the scenery asks for an unstuck button.
   Dominion could: add an unstuck command and a host command to skip a stuck objective, and log every
   time they are used.
   Strength: weak.
9. **Let players set their view and screen.**
   Evidence: 1 wants a field-of-view slider and hold-to-sprint; 1 says ultrawide menus overlap; 1 says
   the frame-rate lock and v-sync do nothing; 1 has mouse delay with forced acceleration.
   Dominion could: ship a field-of-view slider, raw mouse input, working frame-rate and v-sync settings,
   and menus that fit wide screens.
   Strength: weak.
10. **A fresh core idea and a clear look win praise even when the rest is thin.**
    Evidence: 4 praise keeping the battlefield lit as the sun sets; 4 praise the cartoon look.
    Dominion could: build each run around one clear idea players can name in a sentence.
    Strength: weak.

### Limits
- Every English review was read: no margin of error. 2023 is 10 reviews and 2024 to 2026 is 6, so the
  later percentages are shaky.
- 3 reviews (5.9%) were edited after posting; one moves from 2022 praise to later criticism.
- 52 reviews in other languages (50% of the audience) were not read.
- The first count of this game missed its 40 early-access reviews; it was fixed before reading.
- That co-op was promised is the reviewers' claim; the roadmap is not in the study.
- **What changed from the earlier entry:** the game now has its own ranked page,
  `findings/die-after-sunset.md` (the earlier entry said there was none). The earlier entry had no
  counted praise list, no price findings and little on enemies; the rebuilt pages count all of them.
  The page heading that said every review since 1.0 is a thumbs down has been corrected to follow its
  table (17% thumbs up for 2024 to 2026), as the earlier entry already did. The re-run counts 79
  complaints, not 80.
- The 1.0 date, the price and the patch that broke the first boss are what reviewers say; the page did
  not check them.
- Studio names do not match: this page says Playstark made this game and later ArcRunner; the
  ArcRunner page names Trickjump Games. Not settled here.
- The last review (232026981, August 2026) is a 700-word essay in a style unlike any other review of
  the game; the page flags it and tagged it on its content anyway.
- A crude remark in one co-op review is described, not quoted.

---

## Aliens: Fireteam Elite

**Read:** 1,501 of 17,551 English reviews (8.6%), ±3.56%, written 2021-08 to 2026-09 (62 months; the
last review read is dated 2026-09-01). **81.3% thumbs up** in the sample (83.42% when each month is
weighted back to its true size). Steam, all languages: 79.7%, Mostly Positive, on 27,056 reviews.
**Pages:** `findings/aliens-fireteam-elite-english.md`, `findings/aliens-fireteam-elite.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` section 13 (also the table at the top of the page, and short mentions in
sections 22 and 23).

**What it is:** A third-person, class-based, ability-driven co-op PvE shooter for three players, based
on the Aliens films. Made by Cold Iron Studios, published by Focus Entertainment. Released 2021-08-23,
with no Early Access. Paid, with no free tier, plus paid add-ons and a season pass. Online play is peer
to peer: one player hosts, with host migration. The campaign is twelve missions across four areas. Bots
fill empty seats. It shipped with no text chat and no voice chat. The studio later stopped updating it.
The pages do not give the engine.

**How close to Dominion:** The study calls it the closest match to Dominion in the whole corpus, and the
only close match that worked: third person, class-based, ability-driven PvE, paid, and hosted by one
player. It differs in team size (three, not four), in being missions rather than runs, in having no
extraction or arena mode, and in being a licensed game (Dominion's setting is its own).

### The short version (plain words)
This is the study's one "ordinary success": it was good, it sold, it ran five years and it stopped.
Players loved how faithful it was to the films (193 reviews) and nearly as many said there was not
enough of it (187). Praise is about the feel - the world, the friends, the builds, the guns; complaints
are about supply - how much there is, how fast it repeats, and whether anyone is left to play with. The
co-op broke three ways at once: nobody to match with, no way to talk to the people you got, and bots
that did not cover the gap. 137 reviews (9.1%) say wait for a sale, and the page reads that as the game
being worth about ten dollars, not forty. On the 2026-10-03 re-count, reviews carry 124.6 praises and
141.8 complaints per 100, 0.88 praise for each complaint - the same as Back 4 Blood (0.86) while ten
points higher on Steam.

### What players praised, most to least
Counts are the page's of 2026-09-04; where the 2026-10-03 re-count differs, both are given.
1. **Faithful to the films it adapts.** 193 reviews, the largest item after "good game, nothing else".
   One fan: *"You'll note this is not an Alien game. This is an Aliens game. The developers have really
   picked up on what made James Cameron's vision of the Alien universe unique."* (234223542). It fell
   from 16.1 per 100 in 2021 to 6.8 in 2026.
2. **Much better with friends.** 156. At 10.4 per 100 reviews, the highest in the corpus (cross-game
   section 22). 7 say it is how they play with their family.
3. **Builds are deep and varied.** 112.
4. **Shooting hits with weight.** 81.
5. **Keeps pulling you back.** 54.
6. **The atmosphere draws you in.** 51.
7. **Looks great.** 38.
8. **Each class plays its own way.** 38.
9. **Fair price.** 35 (6.8 per 100 in 2021, none in 2026).
10. **Hard in a good way.** 31.
11. **Random rule changes on a mission are welcome.** 29.
12. **A good mix of enemies.** 28. **Teamwork pays off.** 28.
13. **Bots make solo play possible.** 28. One reviewer (234027899) has 130 hours and clears Intense
    difficulty with one friend and a bot. 10 say bots help in a fight; 5 like that bots work offline.
14. **The story is worth following.** 24. **Better than its reviews say.** 24.
15. **The levels are well built.** 20. **Progress feels good.** 20.
16. **Difficulty steps are well spaced.** 19. **A game you can relax with.** 19.
17. **Made with care.** 18. **The music fits.** 18.
18. **Punchy sound effects.** 16. **The places feel real and moody.** 16.
19. **It runs well.** 12. **Crossplay works well.** 8.
20. **Paid add-ons are fair.** 11 (10 on the re-count; against 14 saying they are not worth it). 4 like
    that there are no small paid extras at all.
21. **Friendly fire makes funny stories.** 5: *"it helps you set new records for friendly fire damage.
    It's awesome."* (the last review read).
22. **Mods add to the game.** 5; 4 say a mod adds a mode the studio never shipped.
23. **The missing chat keeps it civil.** 3 (only in this game).
24. "Good game" with nothing else: 334 (22.3% of reviews; 333 on the re-count), the single largest item.
    With the negative version, 358 reviews (23.9%) say nothing about the game at all.

### What players complained about, most to least
Thumbs-up reviews complain too: the 1,221 thumbs-up reviews carry 1,187 complaints (and 1,759 praises);
the 280 thumbs-down reviews carry 918 complaints (and 98 praises). That is 0.97 complaints for each
recommending review (Immortal: Unchained page).
1. **Too little content.** 187 (12.5 per 100). Twelve missions; median play 13 hours. A thumbs-down that
   opens *"faithful recreation of aliens"* complains about the twelve-mission campaign (132762903). It
   fell from 13.5 per 100 in 2021 to 5.6 in 2026.
2. **Buy it on sale only.** 137 (9.1% of all reviews; 136 on the re-count), the fifth largest item and
   the second largest complaint. 9.1 per 100 reviews is second in the corpus after Redfall (17.0).
   *"This is a good horde shooter, for the $10 I paid. Heck, this is a good $40 horde shooter."*
   (224397410, a minority view). It rose from 4.8 per 100 in 2021 to a peak of 15.4 in 2024, then 9.9.
3. **Repetitive.** 101 (98 on the re-count).
4. **Only worth it if you already love the films.** 76, the fourth largest complaint, written mostly by
   people recommending the game ("for fans, everyone else stay away"). It rose from 1.7 per 100 in 2021
   to 6.8 in 2026.
5. **Dead game.** 73 (between 2.8 and 6.2 per 100 every year).
6. **Bots useless in combat.** 60. Also: you cannot set your bots' class (they default to Gunner), 7;
   they get in your way or hurt you - shoot you in the back, stand in your line of fire - 16; the game
   itself warns you not to rely on them above a certain difficulty, 3; bots fill the seats before people
   can join, 10 (only in this game); bots are forced on you 5; bots have no personality 5.
7. **No way to talk to teammates.** 51. *"why the hell is there no text or voice chat??"* (222497258,
   the whole review).
8. **Matchmaking finds no game.** 41. Slow to find a game: 16.
9. **The story is thin or forgettable.** 39.
10. **The reasons to replay run out fast.** 38 (4.5 per 100 in 2021, none in 2026).
11. **Nobody to play with / you need a group to enjoy it.** 37.
12. **Price too high for what it is.** 30 (4.5 per 100 in 2021, none in 2026).
13. **Three players is too small a team.** 29.
14. **Another game does it better.** 29.
15. **Does not feel like the films.** 28.
16. **Unlocks take too much grinding.** 27.
17. **Players keep getting disconnected.** 25, one of the three main 2026 complaints; it peaked in 2023
    (4.8 per 100).
18. **Not finished enough.** 24. A thumbs down with nothing else: 24.
19. **The levels are one straight path.** 21.
20. **The characters' faces do not move.** 19.
21. **It could have been more and settled for less.** 18.
22. **A mode players expected is missing.** 17.
23. **Paid add-ons not worth it.** 14. Paid add-ons and how the game makes money: 58 points across 15
    items, both ways. Also: the base game is too thin for add-ons 5; content behind a second purchase 5;
    it feels like a cash grab 4; not worth it at any price 9.
24. **Film details missing or fenced off:** the pulse rifle without its grenade launcher; the Queen you
    cannot fight (10 say there is no boss to fight); the power loader behind glass; the pulse rifle's
    99-round magazine locked behind a grind. An iconic thing that is there but never used: 3.
25. **The monsters are not scary any more.** 3. *"the aliens look as scary as a dog"*. 4 say your own
    character gives the scare away (only in this game).
26. **Other licence complaints:** it carries over a part of the films the reviewer dislikes (5); the
    additions do not belong (3); none of the films' characters are here (no count).
27. **Friendly fire is just a cost.** 2.
28. **Disconnects:** players freezing and dropping from the host (see Co-op).
29. **Achievements do not track what you actually did.** Seen here (3 sightings across this game,
    ZCREW and The Anacrusis; ZCREW page).

### How it changed over time
| Year written | Reviews read | % thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2021 | 355 | 81.4% | 113.8 | 151.3 |
| 2022 | 256 | 80.5% | 114.1 | 175.8 |
| 2023 | 248 | 81.9% | 92.7 | 136.7 |
| 2024 | 240 | 85.4% | 82.1 | 116.7 |
| 2025 | 240 | 81.7% | 108.8 | 130.8 |
| 2026 | 162 | 75.3% | 94.4 | 112.3 |
- Flat for five years, best in 2024, then a drop in 2026. Complaints per 100 were highest in 2022.
- The 2026 reviews are the least happy and the shortest (the last two batches are the second and seventh
  lowest of thirty on points per review).
- 2026 complaints cluster on "dead game", "can't find a game" and "frequent disconnects" - not on
  design. The page: "Nothing about the game got worse. The thing that got worse is that there is nobody
  in it."
- **What fell:** too little content, faithful to the films, runs out fast, price too high and fair
  price (numbers in the lists above). **What rose:** buy on sale only (peak 2024), only for fans, and a
  bare thumbs down (0.0 to 3.7 per 100).
- The cost of waiting for a sale: *"Enjoyable game when it reached my price point, too bad it took so
  long to be discounted. As the playerbase is dead now."* (229190054, thumbs down).
- The launch month (2021-08) read 88.9% thumbs up, but on only 72 reviews; it holds 25.4% of all
  English reviews.
- Edited reviews fall with age: 12.1% of 2021 reviews were edited later, 8.6% (2022), 8.9% (2023),
  5.8% (2024), 5.4% (2025), 1.9% (2026). Older reviews have simply had longer to be edited.

### Co-op and online play
- **Friends make it:** 156 say much better with friends (10.4 per 100, top of the corpus); 7 play it
  with family.
- **Three faults chained:**
  1. Nobody to match with: dead game 73, matchmaking finds no game 41, nobody to play with 37; slow to
     find games 16, the numbers are falling 15, players split across too many options 14, no server
     browser 8.
  2. No way to talk: 51. No text chat, no voice chat at launch. 9 say only the studio's outside chat
     fills a lobby; 4 cannot add friends; 3 say the missing chat keeps it civil.
  3. Bots do not fill in: useless 60, against 28 who say bots make solo work and 10 who say they help
     in a fight; bots harm you 16, cannot be set up 7, fill the seats before people can join 10, are
     forced on you 5; the game warns you off them at higher difficulty (3).
- One careful thumbs-down for newcomers (228598427) walks the whole chain: no lobby, or a lobby of
  over-levelled strangers who leave or steamroll, or bots worse than nothing. He says the bot option
  should be removed, because it promises a solo game that is not there.
- **Team size:** 29 say three players is too small; 7 say it needs a full team; 4 ask for a bigger team.
- **Hosting and drops** (a tag count over the summaries): frequent disconnects 25; peer to peer, not
  dedicated servers 7; cannot rejoin a match 7; lag 6; high lag 5; cannot connect 4; nobody replaces a
  player who leaves 4; cannot join a match already running 4; the host leaving ends everyone's run 3; a
  disconnect burns what you spent 3 (only in this game). The game has host migration; players freeze
  and drop *from the host*.
- **Strangers:** fine 6, poor 5; over-levelled strangers leave or steamroll new players (228598427).
- **Friendly fire:** makes stories 5, just a cost 2.
- **Crossplay:** works well 8, none at all 4.
- Across the whole corpus, bots being useless in combat is said 128 times and "no bots at all" only 2.

### Combat, movement and feel
- **Shooting hits with weight:** 81. Feels like every other shooter 12; weightless 6; sluggish weapon
  handling 3.
- **Each class plays its own way:** 38. A class is too weak 9; classes feel the same 5.
- **Atmosphere:** 51. **Looks great:** 38. **Punchy sound effects:** 16. Audio is 1.7% of all points
  (76).
- **Camera:** no choice of view 12 (4 ask for one); the camera hides the action 3; the narrow view is a
  handicap 3; it brings you close to the action 2.
- **Movement:** sluggish 2, missing modern moves 2, responsive 1.
- **Controls:** unresponsive 3, expected key bindings missing 3, aim sensitivity cannot be tuned 2,
  responsive and clear 1, you have to switch between controller and mouse to play well 1.
- **The monsters are not scary:** *"the aliens look as scary as a dog"*.
- Melee and stuns: nothing said.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- **Enemies:** good variety 28; variety lacking 7; poor enemy behaviour 15, good 4; bullet sponges 11;
  enemies arrive in the same places every run 10.
- **No boss to fight:** 10. The Queen is in the game but cannot be fought.
- **Difficulty:** hard in a good way 31; random rule changes welcome 29; well graded 19; too easy 9;
  a harder setting only changes the numbers 8; too hard 7; badly scaled 7; the lower settings are not
  worth playing 4.
- Higher difficulties: the game warns you off its bots; one player clears Intense with a friend and a
  bot (234027899).
- Strangers in matchmaking can be far stronger and steamroll new players (228598427).

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- **Builds deep and varied:** 112, the third largest praise. Change how you play 14; shallow 11; only a
  few builds work 3; only one build can be saved at a time 3 (only in this game).
- **Each class its own way:** 38.
- **Unlocks:** grindy 27, satisfying 20, locked behind a different mode 9, behind farming 6, respects
  your time 6, nothing left to chase 6, slow start 5.
- **An iconic gun part locked behind a grind:** the pulse rifle's 99-round magazine.
- **Over-levelled strangers** in public games (228598427).
- Nothing on random drops on the pages; the study notes it has no heading for loot the game hands you
  (3 sightings across games, parked in three places).

### Runs, content and replay value
- **Too little:** 187. **Repetitive:** 101. **Keeps pulling you back:** 54. **Runs out fast:** 38.
- **Levels:** one straight path 21, well built 20, repetitive layouts 13, the campaign just stops 7; 5
  say the maps should have been generated (only in this game).
- **Modes:** an expected mode is missing 17 (8 ask for one); a good choice of modes 11.
- Twelve missions across four areas. Median hours 13 (about the campaign's length), 90th percentile 69,
  longest 2,928. 95 reviews show 100 hours or more and 3 show 1,000 or more. The median is 7 hours for
  thumbs down and 15 for thumbs up.

### Money and price
- **Wait for a sale:** 137 (9.1%). **Fair price:** 35. **Too high:** 30. **Not worth it at any
  price:** 9. The verdict: worth about ten dollars, not forty.
