- **A co-op game that waits for sales loses its players:** 229190054 waited, and by then the lobbies were
  empty.
- **Paid add-ons:** 58 points across 15 items; not worth it 14, fair 11 (10 on the re-count); base game
  too thin for add-ons 5; content behind a second purchase 5; no small paid extras at all 4 (praise);
  feels like a cash grab 4. No review reads as a full verdict on the season pass.
- 26 reviews (1.7%) were marked as received free. 52 (3.5%) have zero hours (Terminull Brigade, a free
  game: 30.4%).

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Engineering is 6.8% of all points (301).
- **Disconnects:** 25, and freezes dropping players from the host; "frequent disconnects" is one of the
  three main 2026 complaints.
- **Bugs:** break play 16, buggy 15, rare and minor 10, the sound breaks and stays broken 5.
- **Crashes:** repeatedly 8, at a set moment 5. Progress not saved: 4.
- **Performance:** well optimised 12, runs on a modest computer 5, stutter 5.

### The studio, updates and community
- **Updates stopped and nobody talked about it:** live service talk is 1.7% of points (73), the smallest
  share of any game that ran five years. A finished game makes no update talk, good or bad.
- **What players say about updates** (tag count): updates stopped 13, known bugs never fixed 5, waiting
  on promised content 5; a steady stream of updates 5, still supported 3; a patch fixed what mattered 15,
  made it better 7, made it worse 4; the studio listens and acts 4, ignores feedback 4.
- **Mods:** they extend the game 5; a mod adds a mode the studio never shipped 4; no mod support 5.
- **Players help each other:** players teach each other the fix 6; the review teaches you how to play 5.
- **Community talk** is 10.4% of points (459).
- **Some players want a sequel** (a heading built for this game; no count on the pages).
- 3 say they play it as revenge on a film that once frightened them (only in this game).

### What players asked for
A word search of the review summaries finds 111 points worded as a request. By subject: more content
24; an expected mode 8; a choice of camera view 4; a bigger team 4; better bots 4; a way to talk 4;
matchmaking that finds a game 3; and 2 each for: a fairer verdict on an "average" game, the add-ons,
generated maps, more for people who are not fans, more of the films, better explanations for new
players, a boss to fight, bots with personality, mod support and more players; plus 40 single requests.
The page's own list:
- **Text and voice chat** (51 complain it is missing).
- **More missions and more variety** (187 too little, 101 repetitive).
- **Bots you can set up and trust** (60 useless; can't choose their class).
- **The film things they expected:** the grenade launcher on the pulse rifle, a Queen fight, the power
  loader in use.
- **A lower price or a sale** (137).
- **A sequel** (present, no count).

### Only in this game
- **The only licensed game in the corpus.** Nine headings exist only because of it: faithful (193) / not
  faithful (28); only for fans (76); an iconic thing is missing; an iconic thing is there but unused; the
  monster is no longer scary; faithful to one part of the series; carries over a disliked part; none of
  the characters are here; the additions do not belong.
- **"The game itself warns you off its bots."**
- **The only "ordinary success":** good, sold, ran five years, stopped. Its ending was the population,
  not the design.
- Same praise-to-complaint balance as Back 4 Blood (0.88 to 1 against 0.86 to 1) while ten points
  higher on Steam: the thumb records "would buy again"; the text records "what I would change".
- **62 points no other game has**, the most used: bots fill the seats before people can join (10), the
  maps should have been generated (5), carries over the disliked part of the films (5), bots have no
  personality (5), bots work offline (5), your own character gives the scare away (4), and 3 each for
  the monster no longer frightening, the additions do not belong, an iconic thing there but unused,
  playing as revenge on a frightening film, only one build saved at a time, the game warns you off its
  bots, a disconnect burns what you spent, and the missing chat keeps it civil.

### For Dominion - our reading
These include every lesson the game's own "for Dominion" page gives, marked (page).
1. **You can nail the feel and still lose on content and population.** (page) Evidence: faithful 193,
   too little content 187; the 2026 drop is about empty lobbies, not design. Dominion could: plan content
   volume and repeat-play (runs, arenas, extraction maps, modifiers) as hard as the feel; for a small
   team, systems that make many runs from few parts matter more than hand-built missions. Strength:
   strong.
2. **Build chat with the session, not after it.** (page) Evidence: 51 say there is no way to talk; no
   text or voice chat at launch; chat is the cheapest of the three co-op faults to fix and the one this
   game skipped. Dominion could: ship text chat, voice chat and a ping/marker system on day one, with
   mute and report. Strength: strong.
3. **A three- or four-player game must win its matchmaking.** (page) Evidence: dead game 73, can't find
   a game 41, nobody to play with 37; 2026 complaints are about this. Dominion could: make joining easy
   (quick join, open lobbies, friend invites, joining a run in progress), keep one shared pool rather
   than many split queues, and add crossplay if possible. Strength: strong.
4. **A bad bot is worse than no bot; decide early what bots are for.** (page) Evidence: useless 60
   against 28 who say bots make solo work; bots can't be set (7), shoot you (16), and the game warns you
   off them (3); across the corpus bad bots 128, missing bots 2; 228598427 wants them removed. Dominion
   could: either make bots a real supported way to play (choose their class, they revive, they never
   hurt you, they hold up on high difficulty) or scale the run to the number of players and leave bots
   out. Say which in the design, not in a warning box. Strength: strong.
5. **A co-op game's price plan is a population plan.** (page) Evidence: 137 say wait for a sale (9.1%);
   the verdict is "worth ten, not forty"; 229190054 waited and found the game empty. Dominion could:
   price so launch buyers feel it is fair, and plan early discounts, bundles or friend passes to keep
   lobbies full, rather than a long wait for a deep sale. Strength: strong.
6. **Expect a quarter of reviews to say nothing.** (page) Evidence: 358 (23.9%) carry no detail; good
   games get short reviews (2.93 points each). Dominion could: plan feedback reading knowing about one in
   four reviews is noise. Strength: strong.
7. **A licence buys an audience and narrows it; an original setting pays neither cost.** (page)
   Evidence: 193 faithful, 76 "only for fans", plus the missing-iconic-thing complaints. Dominion could:
   earn the attention a licence hands out - a clear look, a strong first hour and a reason to tell
   friends - since it has no film to lean on. Strength: medium.
8. **Fans ask "which part were you faithful to".** (page) Evidence: "not an Alien game, an Aliens
   game" (234223542). Dominion could: since it draws on its own source books for lore, pick which part of
   them it follows and say so. Strength: weak (one review), but sharp.
9. **Do not fence off the iconic thing.** Evidence: the Queen you cannot fight (10 say there is no boss
   to fight), the power loader behind glass, the grenade launcher missing, the 99-round magazine locked
   behind a grind; an iconic thing there but never used, 3. Dominion could: if the world has a famous
   threat or tool, let players fight it or use it, and do not lock the signature version of a gun behind
   a long grind. Strength: weak to medium (small counts), clear pattern.
10. **Thumbs up does not mean nothing is wrong.** Evidence: thumbs-up reviews carry 1,187 complaints
    (about one each). Dominion could: read the complaints in positive reviews as the to-do list. Strength:
    strong.
11. **Peer-to-peer hosting needs good drop handling.** Evidence: players freeze and drop from the host;
    frequent disconnects (25) is a main 2026 complaint; the game has host migration. Dominion could, as a
    listen-server game: plan host migration or a quick rejoin, save run progress so a dropped player can
    come back, and test bad connections. Strength: medium.
12. **Strangers far above your level ruin the first games.** Evidence: 228598427 - over-levelled
    strangers who leave or steamroll. Dominion could: match by level or difficulty, and let new players
    find a team at their level. Strength: weak to medium.
13. **Deep builds and distinct classes are what players praise.** Evidence: builds 112, guns 81, classes
    38. Dominion could: keep classes clearly different and builds deep. Strength: strong.
14. **Monsters must stay scary.** Evidence: "as scary as a dog" (3 say the monster is no longer
    frightening; 4 say their own character gives the scare away). Dominion could: keep the main enemy's
    threat clear with sound, size and behaviour, and do not let it become just a crowd. Strength: weak.
15. **When updates stop, the game goes quiet, not angry.** Evidence: live service talk only 1.7%.
    Dominion could: expect that ending a game's support will not draw a storm, but the population will
    fade. Strength: medium.
16. **Paid add-ons split players.** Evidence: 14 not worth it, 11 fair, 58 points in all. Dominion could:
    if it sells add-ons, keep maps and modes free so the player pool stays whole. Strength: weak.
17. **Friendly fire splits people.** Evidence: makes stories 5, just a cost 2. Dominion could: make it
    a lobby option or a difficulty rule. Strength: weak.
18. **The page's network note:** the game's page wrote its lessons for a Dominion with a 200-player
    dedicated-server target, and says nothing in the corpus speaks to that. Dominion starts as a
    four-player listen server, so this game's peer-to-peer complaints are the right comparison now; the
    200-player lessons are kept for the possible larger version later (Rico, 2026-10-03).
19. **Every way back into a run must work.** Evidence: cannot rejoin a match 7; nobody replaces a
    player who leaves 4; cannot join a match already running 4; the host leaving ends everyone's run 3;
    a disconnect burns what you spent 3. Dominion could: let a dropped player rejoin the same run with
    their gear, let new players fill an empty seat mid-run, and refund anything spent on a run lost to a
    disconnect. Strength: medium (small counts, but they sit on Dominion's exact hosting model).
20. **Bots must give their seat to a human.** Evidence: 10 say bots fill the seats before people can
    join. Dominion could: keep lobby seats open to players until the run starts, and swap a bot out the
    moment a player joins. Strength: medium.
21. **Four is the right team size; three felt small.** Evidence: 29 say three players is too small; 4
    ask for a bigger team. Dominion could: keep four as the full team. Strength: medium.
22. **Random rule changes are welcome.** Evidence: 29 say random rule changes on a mission are welcome.
    Dominion could: add per-run modifiers to arenas and extraction runs. Strength: medium.
23. **Fixed maps get learned; vary them.** Evidence: levels one straight path 21 (against well built
    20); enemies arrive in the same places every run 10; repetitive layouts 13; 5 say the maps should
    have been generated. Dominion could: vary routes, spawn points and objectives between runs, by
    hand-made pieces or generated layouts. Strength: medium.
24. **Players want a fight at the end.** Evidence: no boss to fight 10 (2 ask for one). Dominion could:
    give runs a clear climax enemy or event. Strength: weak to medium.
25. **Let players pick the camera.** Evidence: no choice of view 12, 4 ask for one; the camera hides the
    action 3. Dominion could: offer a shoulder swap and a field-of-view setting in its third-person
    camera. Strength: medium.
26. **Close-ups show what is not animated.** Evidence: 19 say the characters' faces do not move.
    Dominion could: keep the camera off faces it cannot animate, or animate the ones it shows. Strength:
    weak to medium.
27. **A story that is there must be worth following.** Evidence: thin or forgettable 39, against worth
    following 24. Dominion could: keep story short and tied to each run, rather than a thin campaign.
    Strength: medium.
28. **Harder should mean smarter, not just bigger numbers.** Evidence: a harder setting only changes
    the numbers 8; bullet sponges 11; poor enemy behaviour 15. Dominion could: add new enemy behaviour
    or rules at higher difficulty. Strength: weak to medium.
29. **Mods can carry a finished game.** Evidence: mods extend the game 5, a mod adds a mode the studio
    never shipped 4, no mod support 5. Dominion could: plan custom rules or mod support for the day
    updates stop. Strength: weak.

### Limits
- 1,501 reviews, ±3.56%, not ±2.5%. The launch month (2021-08) holds 25.4% of English reviews and was
  read at 1.62% (72 reviews); it carries two thirds of the uncertainty. Nothing here speaks with
  confidence about the first weeks. Rico chose not to re-pull it.
- English only: 9,505 other-language reviews unread.
- 117 reviews (7.8%) were edited more than a day after posting.
- The study cannot tell "the matchmaking is bad" from "not enough people bought it".
- Nothing measures sales; "sold well" comes from the review count against its rating.
- It cannot see people who stopped without reviewing.
- **Changed since the last entry:** several sharp complaints that had no counts now have them, from a
  tag count over the summaries on 2026-10-03: bots cannot be set 7, bots harm you 16, the game warns you
  off its bots 3, frequent disconnects 25, friendly fire makes stories 5 and is just a cost 2, no boss
  to fight 10, the monster is no longer frightening 3. "None of the films' characters are here" and "a
  sequel" still have no count.
- Counts moved slightly on the 2026-10-03 re-count: points 4,400 to 4,406; buy on sale 137 to 136;
  repetitive 101 to 98; bare thumbs up 334 to 333; add-ons fair 11 to 10. The weighted 83.42% was not
  re-run. The old figures are kept where the old page used them.
- The request list is a word search, so a few hits are wishes a reviewer describes rather than asks
  for. Per-period margins are not counted.
- The game's page compares it to a Dominion of "3-4" players with a 200-player dedicated-server target;
  Rico ruled on 2026-10-03 that Dominion starts at four players on a listen server, with a larger
  version (possibly 200) only as a later maybe.
- Not checked against a primary source: what the add-ons contained, when sales ran, the studio's update
  history, and prices quoted by reviewers.

---

## The Anacrusis

**Read:** 611 of 1,290 English reviews (47.4%), ±2.85%, written January 2022 to August 2026 (55 of
the 56 months; 49 of those 55 months were read in full, every review). **57.45% thumbs up** in the
sample (351 of 611); weighted to each month's true size, 58.49% (Steam, all languages: 46.6%, Mixed, of
1,886 reviews). The English figure and Steam's all-language, all-time figure are not the same thing; do
not set them side by side.
**Size of the read, in numbers:** 2,424 things said by the script count on 2026-10-03 (3.97 per
review); the earlier game page counted 2,411 (3.95) and the cross-game table 2,419 (3.96). 186 of them
(7.7%) say nothing specific (the earlier page: 8%). 381 of the 611 reviews were written during early
access. 376 different labels were needed, 18 of them used in no other game. Hours on the review: median
2; thumbs-down reviewers played a median of 1 hour, thumbs-up reviewers 3; only 2 reviews show 100
hours or more.
**Pages:** `findings/the-anacrusis-english.md`, `findings/the-anacrusis.md`, `findings/cross-game.md`
section 11.

**What it is:** A co-op horde shooter for one to four players, built on purpose as a rival to Left 4
Dead, by Stray Bombay, a small independent studio whose lead wrote Left 4 Dead. Set on a huge space
ship overrun by aliens. Played as episodes: reviewers name 5 episodes and 4 to 6 hours of content.
An AI "director" moves the difficulty up or down by how well you are doing. Bots take over a player's
character when they step away. Custom maps through a map workshop (one reviewer says the map tools
taught him Unreal Engine). Paid, and also shipped on Game Pass. Early access from January 2022, full
release (1.0) December 2023. Reviewers say games run peer to peer, not on dedicated servers (3 times
said), and the studio's chat server (Discord) is how many players find a full team. The Steam store page calls it *"a 1-8 player, sci-fi first-person shooter"* with *"4 player co-op"*
(checked 2026-10-03). It was picked as the closest sibling to Back 4 Blood: the same pedigree (built
against Left 4 Dead, by people tied to it) with a small budget instead of a big publisher. Both lost
the comparison.

**How close to Dominion:** Close. It is a four-player co-op PvE shooter, sci-fi, on a space ship,
made by a small team, paid, with bots and an adaptive director. It differs in camera (first person,
not third), in shape (fixed episodes in a horde game, not runs, arenas or extraction), and its build
choices are small (character choices that change how you play are praised 19 times). It is a failure
case: a small game that was liked, defended, released, and then slowly emptied.

### The short version (plain words)
The thing players do most - shoot - feels like it hits nothing, and that never got fixed. "Shots feel
weightless" is the biggest single complaint (81 times), and its share of each year's complaints grew
every year: 4.8%, 5.9%, 6.6%, 8.6%. At first, players defended the game: "the potential is still
there" was said 74 times, 68 of them in 2022 and 2023. After full release that hope just stopped (4,
then 1, then 1). The players left, the lobbies emptied ("dead game" 46 times on the 2026-10-03 count,
47 when first counted), and the studio argued with players about the player count. The art and the
map-making tools were liked the whole time. Thumbs up fell every period, from 69.9% in 2022 to 42.0% in
2025-26, and praise per 100 reviews halved (155.4 to 74.1). Overall reviewers write 224.2 complaints
and 129.3 praises per 100 reviews (the cross-game table: 223.6 and 129.6, 0.58 praise per complaint),
the second-highest complaint rate of the nine games in that table, after Redfall.

### What players praised, most to least
Counts are the 2026-10-03 script count; where the earlier page differs, both are given. Per 100 reviews
by year is given where the page has it (2022, 2023, 2024, 2025-26).
1. **"The potential is still there"** - 74 times, the third biggest single thing said. This is hope
   more than praise of what is there: the design works and the missing parts can still come. 35 in
   2022, 33 in 2023, 4 in 2024, 1 in 2025, 1 in 2026 (18.1, 15.1, 3.4, 2.5 per 100).
2. **The art style looks well directed** - 58 times (13.0, 9.2, 8.4, 3.7 per 100: it faded too).
3. **The studio listens and acts, and is open about its plans** - 34 times together (16 + 18), mostly
   in 2022 and 2023. "Listens and acts" went from 5.7 per 100 in 2022 to 0.0 in 2024 and after.
4. **The game is better than its rivals** - 34 times. As a rate, 5.6 reviews per 100; when Escape from
   Duckov was read, only Duckov (6.1) was higher.
5. **The map workshop** (custom maps) - 29 times (30 when first counted). Nobody argues about it.
   *"honestly helped me out with my career in making custom maps & level design in general! This was
   one of my first ways of going into Unreal Engine."* (197189537, 110 hours)
6. **The special enemies** - 25 times.
7. **The environment art** - 23 times.
8. **Playing with friends** - 23 times.
9. **Shooting hits hard** - 21 times (against 137 saying it does not).
10. **Character choices change how you play** - 19 times.
11. **The price is fair** - 18 times (6.7 per 100 in 2024).
12. **The levels are well built** - 18 times.
13. **Glad they could try it first** (the free weekend or a demo) - 18 times. 6.7 per 100 in 2022, 0.0
    from 2024 on.
14. **The music fits** - 15 times.
15. **Worth it even while unfinished** - 13 times (against 28 that say early access was not worth it
    yet).
16. **Many ways to build your character** - 12 times.
17. **Updates keep coming** - 12 times, mostly 2023.
18. **The mood pulls you in** - 11 times.
19. **It keeps pulling you back** - 10 times.
20. **The best game of its kind** - 9 times.
21. **The cast is funny or memorable** - 9 times (these reviewers played a median of 6 hours).
22. **Each run plays differently** - 9 times.
23. **The updates made it better** - 9 times, all 2022.
24. **Nothing else plays like it** - 8 times.
25. **Bots take over when you step away** - 3 times, all praise, even from one reviewer who does not
    recommend the game: *"It has one good innovation that when you go afk in co-op session AI takes
    control of the character."*
26. Players who buy extra things just to support the studio: 5 times, 0.82 reviews per 100. When
    Warframe was read, only Warframe (0.93) was higher. 5 more praise that there is no in-game shop at
    all.

Also: 60 thumbs-up reviews say nothing about why.

### What players complained about, most to least
The pages do not give a thumbs-up / thumbs-down split for each complaint. They do say a thumbs-up
reviewer here writes 1.16 complaints and 1.93 praises on average. Counts are the 2026-10-03 script;
per 100 reviews by year (2022, 2023, 2024, 2025-26) where the page gives it.
1. **Shooting feels weightless** - 81 times (3.4% of everything said), the biggest single complaint
   (11.4, 12.4, 16.0, 16.0 per 100). With **hits look weak** (32) and **weak, thin sound** (24) it
   makes a group of **137 (5.7% of everything said) against 21 that say shooting hits hard: six and a
   half to one.** *"this game is 99% shooting and that aspect feels worse in every way than L4D, so I
   can't recommend it over that."* (131738483, 30 helpful) *"They have received complaints about this
   since it arrived on Early Access and have done absolutely nothing to rectify it."* (154491765)
2. **Another named game does it better** - 77 times (9.3, 14.7, 11.8, 16.0). (The First Descendant
   page gives 73 for this.)
3. **It copies an older game** - 63 times (10.9, 12.8, 8.4, 4.9: this one faded).
4. **Dead game, nobody to play with** - 46 times (47 when first counted) (4.1, 6.4, 11.8, 12.3). With
   "only the studio's chat server fills a lobby", "nobody ever heard of it", "dead in my region" and
   "the numbers are falling", the group was 70 when first counted. The 2026-10-03 script counts those
   parts as 46, 9, 10, 3 and 1, plus 1 review saying most owners never played it.
5. **Too little content** - 42 times (reviewers name 4-6 hours, 5 episodes). Steady every year (6.7,
   7.3, 6.7, 6.2).
6. **It needs more work** - 33 times. It tripled late: 4.1 per 100 in 2022, 13.6 in 2025-26.
7. **Hits look weak** - 32 times.
8. **The characters are flat or annoying** - 31 times (32 when first counted). But see below: these
   reviewers played a median of 1 hour.
9. **Early access is not worth it yet** - 28 times (gone once the game left early access).
10. **Costs too much for what it is** - 26 times (2.6 per 100 in 2022, 6.2 in 2025-26).
11. **Weak, thin sound** - 24 times.
12. **Wayfinding: cannot tell where to go** - 22 times on its own. With "cannot spot what you need",
    "the screen hides information" and "nothing in the level shows what happened here", the group was
    45 when first counted; the 2026-10-03 script counts the parts as 22, 7, 12 and 4.
13. **It is buggy** - 22 times.
14. **It never became what it promised** - 22 times.
15. **Few real ways to build your character** - 19 times, almost all in 2022.
16. **The animation is stiff** - 19 times.
17. **The special enemies are forgettable** - 18 times (against 25 that say they stand out).
18. **The frame rate jumps around** - 17 times.
19. **Too few kinds of enemy** - 16 times.
20. **Only buy it on sale** - 16 times.
21. **No sound warns you of danger** - 15 times.
22. **Every session feels the same** - 14 times.
23. **Nothing in the levels stands out** - 13 times.
24. **The mood falls flat** - 13 times.
25. **The studio talks badly to players** (punishes criticism, disputes the player count, fights
    players, ignores feedback, and more) - 28 times together, almost all from 2024 on.
26. **The successor claim backfired** (calling it the heir to Left 4 Dead hurt it) - 10 times.
27. **The studio lead's politics drove me away** - 4 reviews in 2025, two of them the most helpful of
    their batch (41 and 45 helpful). Neither talks about play.
28. **The game's world politics put me off** (cosmetic banners) - 3 times. One reviewer says the
    opposite: *"I love the pride banners"* (181915055); by 2026-10-03 the study can count that side
    too, and finds it once.
29. Other games do it better, with none named - 1 time.

Also: 46 thumbs-down reviews give no reason (4.7 per 100 in 2022, 13.6 in 2025-26).

### How it changed over time
| Period | Reviews read | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2022 | 193 | 69.9% | 155.4 | 194.8 |
| 2023 | 218 | 55.5% | 116.1 | 217.9 |
| 2024 | 119 | 51.3% | 98.3 | 258.0 |
| 2025-26 | 81 | 42.0% | 74.1 | 200.0 |

- **Thumbs up fell every period, and praise halved.** What fell, per 100 reviews: the potential is
  still there 18.1 to 2.5; glad I could try it first 6.7 to 0.0; listens and acts 5.7 to 0.0; early
  access not worth it yet 6.7 to 0.0 (the game left early access). What rose: dead game 4.1 to 12.3;
  weightless 11.4 to 16.0; needs more work 4.1 to 13.6; bare thumbs down 4.7 to 13.6.
- **Hope died, and the complaint stayed.** "The potential is still there" by year: 2022 **35**,
  2023 **33**, 2024 **4**, 2025 **1**, 2026 **1**. "Dead game" by year: 8, 14, 15, 5, 5. After the
  December 2023 full release, people stopped saying it would get better. They did not say it got
  worse.
- **Weightless shooting grew as a share** of each year's complaints: 2022 37 of 767 (4.8%), 2023 51 of
  869 (5.9%), 2024 33 of 502 (6.6%), 2025 14 of 162 (8.6%). Named in the first month of early access,
  still the top complaint two years after full release.
- **The studio's talk turned.** Praise for how the studio talks (34) clusters in 2022-2023. Almost all
  the negative talk (28) is 2024 or later. Three reviewers tell the same story on their own: the
  public charts show almost nobody, the studio says the charts are wrong, the studio removes people
  who post them. One quotes a ban notice for linking the game's own public player-count page, with no
  reason given (139919128, the reviewer says). Later: *"the devs have now finally admitted that there
  is a low player count."* (141244492)
- **2025 is mostly not about the game.** 2025 alone reads 37.7% up, and its two most-helpful reviews
  (41 and 45 helpful) are about the studio lead's public politics.
- The months read least were 2022-06 (64 of 345, 18.6%) and 2023-01 (79 of 299, 26.4%). The page
  calls these the early access launch month and the free weekend: months where the game reached
  people who had not chosen it.
- Edited reviews: the earlier page counted 125 (20.5%); the 2026-10-03 script's edit marker finds 66
  (10.8%). Some reviewers kept their review as a running list of what the studio fixed (the page gives
  no count).

### Co-op and online play
- **Not enough players.** 70 times said in the population group when first counted: dead game 47,
  only the studio chat fills a lobby 8, nobody ever heard of it 7, plus dead in my region and numbers
  falling. The 2026-10-03 script: dead game 46, studio chat 9, nobody heard of it 10, dead in my region
  3, numbers falling 1, most owners never played it 1. Also: needs a group 11; cannot find games 5.
  Reviewers bring their own numbers (what they say, not checked):
  - *"the monthly average players is 3.4 per day and even on a Saturday night it only peaks at 7"*
    (157013622)
  - *"the player count displayed a pitiful '1.' Yes, you guessed it, that lone soul was none other
    than myself."* (160509575)
  - *"This game had maybe 11 players before the f2p weekend"* (130514365)
- **The studio's Discord became the matchmaker.** 8 times when first counted (9 by the 2026-10-03
  script) it is said to be the only sure way to fill a lobby, one as friendly advice, one quoting the
  studio telling players to arrange games there. The cost: *"Their Discord server members are not
  very mature, and people get easily pissed when you have different opinions."* (219373901) A player
  who cannot get along there cannot play.
- **Friends and strangers:** much better with friends 23; poor with strangers 4; fine with strangers
  2; it is how I play with my family 6 (first named here; the first version of this entry had no
  count); the bots are better company than the strangers 1.
- **Bots:** they make solo play possible 9; but they are useless in a fight 11, get stuck 7, fail to
  revive you 3, and you cannot give them orders 2; they help in a fight 3; they follow well 1. **Bots
  take over when someone steps away**: 3 times, all praise. The least argued-about feature.
- **Hosting and servers:** peer to peer, not dedicated servers 3; high lag 3; lag and out-of-step play
  7; frequent disconnects 3; no private games 7; no local servers 2; the studio limits who can host 1
  (seen in no other game); no player hosting 1.
- **Joining:** easy to drop in and out 3; cannot rejoin a match 2; no one fills a leaver's place 1; no
  server browser 3.
- **Scaling:** scales to the number of players 1; needs a full team 1.
- **Friendly fire** lets players grief each other: 2.
- **Social tools:** cannot talk to other players 4; cannot add friends 3; welcoming community 7.
- **Who you play:** 3 say you do not get to choose your character (seen in no other game). Reviewers
  disagree: two say the game picks your character for you even in single player (153599609,
  194024444); another, fourteen months apart, says *"you get to pick your character from the menu"*
  (159107507). The page says to check the patch notes.
- One reviewer says fewer than 10% of owners hold the achievement for opening the game (176679912,
  the reviewer's reading of a public stat).
- Crossplay: nothing on the pages.

### Combat, movement and feel
- **Shooting is the core, and it feels like nothing.** Weightless 81, hits look weak 32, weak sound 24
  = 137, against 21 for "hits hard", 2 for punchy sound and 1 for hits that look powerful. The payoff
  of a hit lands flat: 2. No melee attack: 5.
- **Only one reviewer of 611 gives a fix list instead of a verdict** (165340291): *"add viewbob, do a
  whole pass on animations, remake Commons from scratch, add bullet penetration on pellets / consider
  replacing hitscan, either implement physics or just death animations (enemies just sort of slowly
  lump on ground, no momentum on ragdolls)"*. He also argues that the bad sound is a red herring:
  fix only the audio, and *"The game would still have a lot more important problems at hand."* His
  view: sound (24) and visual impact (32) are both symptoms of one missing layer - no hit reaction,
  no variety in death animations, no momentum when enemies fall. One reviewer, but the only cause
  anyone gives.
- **Stiff animation**: 19.
- **Movement:** missing modern moves 9; responsive 3; choppy 1.
- **Controls:** rebind anything 2; an expected button missing 1; unresponsive 1. Controller support:
  nothing said.
- **Too bright to look at**: 6 (filed under access options). One review says a phobia setting let them
  play (seen in no other game).

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- **Special enemies:** stand out 25 against forgettable 18. Too few kinds of enemy 16.
- **Behaviour:** dumb enemies 10; unfair spawns 5; overwhelming numbers 4; attacks with no answer 3.
- **The basic enemies ("Commons") need remaking**, says the one fix-list reviewer (165340291).
- **Reading danger:** no sound warns you of danger 15; threats are unclear 10.
- **Bosses:** no boss to fight 1; the final fight is a pushover 1.
- **Difficulty:** too easy 10; badly scaled 5; too hard 1; well graded 2.
- **The AI director** that moves the difficulty by how you are doing was first named in this game:
  7 times, judged both ways, and used in no other game. (The first version of this entry had no count.)

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
(The first version of this entry said the pages had nothing here. The rebuilt pages now count it.)
- **Builds:** few real ways to build your character 19 (almost all 2022) against character choices
  that change how you play 19 and many ways to build 12.
- **Unlocks:** nothing builds up between games 5; you cannot choose what you unlock 3; the reward only
  buys looks 1.
- **Randomness:** not random enough 3; luck decides the outcome 2; randomness keeps it fresh 2.
- Grind: nothing said beyond these.

### Runs, content and replay value
- **Too little content**: 42 times. Reviewers name **4-6 hours** and **5 episodes**. Levels too short
  6; runs out fast 12; the campaign just stops 2. Against: it ends before it wears out 5.
- **Sameness:** every session the same 14; layouts repeat 11; nothing in the levels stands out 13.
  Against: each run plays differently 9; levels change from run to run 6; keeps pulling you back 10.
- **Wayfinding fails twice.** 45 times about not knowing where to go when first counted. The sharpest
  one lists what is missing: *"there are no indications of other survivors than yourselves, no
  gore-splatter, no signage, no unpossessed corpses, no hi-vis paint, no HUD element"* (159128455, 19
  helpful). The same empty rooms fail to tell the story and fail to show the way. Another reviewer
  (156652417) reaches the same point from the story side. "Nothing in the level shows what happened
  here": 4, seen in no other game.
- **The game's own fix became a complaint.** The game auto-paths you when you wander. The reviewer
  who could not find his way then said: *"the game will just path you to where you need to go... this
  felt more like an annoyance and like the game was treating me like an idiot."* (130889109) That
  "the help feels like being talked down to" is seen in no other game.
- **Custom maps** (29 praise, 30 when first counted) add to what there is to play.
- Median playtime across all 611 reviews: **2 hours**; 46.2% under two hours.

### Money and price
(The first version of this entry said the pages had no price counts. The rebuilt pages now count them.)
- **Price verdicts:** too much for what it is 26; only buy it on sale 16; not worth it at any price 5;
  against the price is fair 18.
- **Early access:** not worth it yet 28 against worth it even unfinished 13.
- **Trying first:** glad they could try it first (the free weekend) 18, all in 2022-23.
- **Shops:** no in-game shop at all 5 (praise); players buy extras to support the studio 5 (0.82 per
  100 reviews, second in the study when Warframe was read); a shop in a paid game 2; feels like a cash
  grab 3; something that was free is now paid 1.
- One purchase covers two platforms: 1 (seen in no other game).
- Game Pass: the game shipped there, so Steam's hours do not show everyone's play.
- 23 reviews say outright they are grading the game against the team's small size: three lower the
  bar for that, one raises it because of who the lead is, and one refuses the excuse: *"I understand
  that the team working on this game is small, but there are incredible games made by small teams."*

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
(The first version of this entry said nothing was counted here. The rebuilt pages now count it.)
- Buggy 22; bugs break play 9; frame rate jumps 17; stutter 6; crashes 2; runs smoothly 6.
- Too bright to look at 6.
- **Tech is not among the game's named problems**: it is 5.2% of everything said.
- One review says the achievements do not track what you actually did (one of three such reviews in
  the study, each from a different game). One says a default setting is not what the studio says it
  is, and one says the translation reads as machine-made (each seen in no other game).

### The studio, updates and community
- **The studio listened early and fought later.** Praise: listens and acts 16, open about what it is
  doing 18, updates keep coming 12 (mostly 2023), the updates made it better 9 (all 2022). Then:
  punishes criticism 8, working on the wrong thing first 6, disputes the player count 5 (seen in no
  other game), fights players 4, ignores feedback 3, misreads what players want 2 - 28 negative in
  all; updates too slow 8; updates stopped 8. The praise is 2022-2023; the negatives are almost all
  2024 on.
- **Disputing the player count** was first named here: the studio said the public numbers were
  wrong; players could check them.
- **Bans for posting player counts** (reviewer says, 139919128).
- **The big complaint was never fixed**, and reviewers said so (154491765).
- **The workshop and the community chat are the two things nobody argues about** - though the chat
  is also a trap, see co-op. One reviewer says the map tools taught him to make games (seen in no other
  game).
- **A political row in 2025** about the studio lead: 4 reviews, top helpful counts (41, 45).
- **Some reviews say the game reads as machine-made**: 6 times. A new idea first seen here.
- **Some reviewers say the gap between what they expected and what they got was their own fault**,
  another new idea here (no count).
- **"Average, not good or bad"**: 24 reviews say this instead of a verdict.
- **Comparison talk is huge.** 263 of 2,411 things said (10.9%) name another game, nearly three
  times Redfall (3.8%). 141 say it loses or copies; 51 say it wins or stands alone; 71 just use Left 4
  Dead to say what the game is (*"Left 4 Dead in space"*), with no verdict. Marketing and comparison
  talk is 14.4% of everything said (14.3% on the 2026-10-03 count), the highest of any game when read.
- What is talked about, by area (2026-10-03 script): how the game plays 26.9%; reputation and
  comparison 14.3%; how it was made and shipped 12.3%; other players and the studio 10.6%; art 7.9%;
  the review itself 7.4%; tech 5.2%; sound 4.6%; story 3.8%; price and selling 3.7%; updates 2.8%;
  access options 0.4%.
- Other single sightings seen in no other game: the best character lines rarely play 1; in-jokes
  replace the writing 1; the music jumps between styles with no theme 1; the art style looks like a
  budget choice 1.

### What players asked for
- **Make the shooting land**: one detailed list (165340291): view bob, an animation pass, remade
  common enemies, bullet penetration for shotgun pellets or moving off hitscan, physics or death
  animations, ragdoll momentum.
- **Signs and set dressing so the place tells you where to go**: survivors, gore, signage, corpses,
  hi-vis paint, a screen marker (159128455).
- **More content**: 42 say too little.
- **Honesty about the player count** (implied by the 28 negative studio-talk mentions and the
  dispute story).
- **A mode they expected is missing**: 4.
- **Private games** (7 say there are none), **a way to add friends** (3) and **a way to talk to other
  players** (4), read from the complaints.
The pages give no other counted requests.

### Only in this game
- **Hope as its own measurable thing, and its collapse.** No other game shows optimism dying apart
  from the score falling.
- **The studio's chat server as the only real matchmaker**, and its social mood as a gate to play.
- **The studio publicly disputing the player count** (5).
- **Bots taking over when you step away** (3) - praised by everyone who mentions it.
- **The AI director** (7) judged both ways.
- **Grading against the studio's size**, a review kept as a ledger of fixes, a game that "reads as
  machine-made", "the mismatch was my fault", playing with family - all first named here.
- **The character complaint may be about delivery, not writing.** 31 say the cast is flat (32 when
  first counted). One reviewer (166892773, 21 hours) says the lines that show character are weighted
  to play rarely, so *"you're not gonna get this all from just one hour"*. The test fits: the 31 "flat"
  reviewers played a median of **1 hour** (52% under 2 hours); the 9 "funny or memorable" reviewers
  played a median of **6** (22% under 2 hours). The study now has a label for "the best lines rarely
  play", used once, here only.
- **The highest comparison rate** of any game when it was read (10.9%).
- **The study could at first record only one side of a political argument** (dislike of the banners,
  not liking them). The cross-game page called this the first fault found in the sorting system rather
  than in a game; by 2026-10-03 the approving side has a label, used once here.
- **18 things said here and in no other game**, the ones above plus: you do not get to choose who you
  play 3; nothing in the level shows what happened here 4; one purchase covers two platforms 1; in-jokes
  replace the writing 1; machine-translated text 1; a default setting is not what the studio says 1;
  the help feels patronising 1; bots follow well 1; the studio limits who can host 1; the tools taught
  me to make games 1; most owners never played it 1; the music jumps between styles 1; the style looks
  like a budget choice 1; a phobia setting let me play it 1.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **The action players do most must feel great first, before anything else.**
   Evidence: weightless shooting 81 times, the top complaint; with weak hits (32) and weak sound (24),
   137 against 21 for "hits hard". Its share grew every year (4.8% to 8.6%). *"this game is 99%
   shooting"* (131738483).
   Dominion could: make gun feel a gate for any public build. Give every hit a reaction (flinch,
   stagger, part damage), several death animations, and ragdolls that keep the bullet's momentum.
   Then tune sound and effects on top of that, not instead of it.
   Strength: **strong**.
2. **Sound and visual impact may be symptoms, not the cause.**
   Evidence: one reviewer (165340291) argues sound (24) and visual (32) complaints both come from no
   hit reaction, no death variety, no momentum; fixing audio alone would not help.
   Dominion could: when "feels weak" comes in, check enemy hit reactions and death motion first, then
   audio.
   Strength: **weak** (one reviewer), but it is the only cause given.
3. **An unanswered top complaint slowly turns defenders into ex-players.**
   Evidence: "the potential is still there" fell from 35 and 33 a year to 4, 1, 1 after full release,
   while the weightless complaint grew. *"They have received complaints about this since it arrived on
   Early Access and have done absolutely nothing"* (154491765).
   Dominion could: if early access or playtests name one top complaint, fix it or say clearly why not,
   before full release. Track hope ("it could be great") as a signal; when it drops, time is short.
   The cross-game page's reading: in early access, players' patience is measured in about two years,
   and the promised content has to arrive inside that window.
   Strength: **strong**.
4. **A four-player co-op game needs players or a way to play without them.**
   Evidence: 70 times about too few players when first counted; 46 "dead game" (47 when first
   counted); needs a group 11; cannot find games 5; reviewers quoting player counts of 1 to 11.
   Dominion could: make solo with bots and two- or three-player runs fully good, so an empty
   matchmaking pool does not end the game; scale enemies to party size.
   Strength: **strong**.
5. **Let bots take over a player who steps away.**
   Evidence: 3 mentions, all praise, even in a thumbs-down review; the least argued-about feature.
   Dominion could: when a player goes idle or drops on a listen server, let a bot run their character
   and hand it back when they return.
   Strength: **weak to medium** (few mentions, but none against).
6. **Do not make a chat server the matchmaker.**
   Evidence: 8 say the studio Discord is the only way to fill a lobby (9 on the 2026-10-03 count); one
   says its members are unpleasant; a player who cannot fit in there cannot play.
   Dominion could: build in-game public lobbies and quick join, so outside chat is a bonus, not a
   requirement.
   Strength: **medium**.
7. **Never argue with players about the player count; never ban for posting it.**
   Evidence: 28 negative studio-talk mentions, almost all 2024 on; disputes the player count 5; three
   reviewers tell the same story of denial and bans; the studio later admitted the low count
   (141244492).
   Dominion could: be honest about numbers; answer criticism, do not remove it.
   Strength: **medium**.
8. **Levels should show the way with props and story, not with a nav arrow.**
   Evidence: 45 times about wayfinding when first counted; *"no signage, no unpossessed corpses, no
   hi-vis paint, no HUD element"* (159128455); the auto-path felt like *"treating me like an idiot"*
   (130889109).
   Dominion could: use lighting, signs, damage, bodies and paint to lead players through each arena
   and extraction route; keep any marker as a light help, not the main guide.
   Strength: **medium**.
9. **Small-team excuses do not save a core flaw.**
   Evidence: 23 reviews grade against the team's size, split both ways; one says great small-team
   games exist.
   Dominion could: expect players to judge the shooting by the standard of the best in the genre, not
   by team size.
   Strength: **medium**.
10. **Content length matters for a paid co-op game.**
    Evidence: 42 say too little; 4-6 hours, 5 episodes named; runs out fast 12; levels too short 6.
    Dominion could: lean on runs that play out differently each time, so a small number of arenas
    gives many hours.
    Strength: **medium**.
11. **Count a complaint, then check who is making it.**
    Evidence: 31 say the cast is flat (32 when first counted); those reviewers played a median of 1
    hour, the fans played 6. One reviewer says key lines are set to play rarely.
    Dominion could: let the best character lines play early and often enough to be heard in a first
    hour; when reading feedback, look at hours played before rewriting.
    Strength: **weak to medium**.
12. **A custom map workshop builds goodwill.**
    Evidence: 29 praise (30 when first counted), uncontested; one reviewer says it started his Unreal
    level-design career.
    Dominion could: later, consider letting players make arenas (one reviewer says the map tools taught
    him Unreal Engine). A long-term nice to have, not a launch need.
    Strength: **medium**.
13. **Art direction is the safest praise, but it does not hold players.**
    Evidence: art direction 58, environment art 23 - yet the game still emptied, and art praise itself
    faded (13.0 per 100 in 2022, 3.7 in 2025-26).
    Dominion could: keep a strong look, but spend first on feel.
    Strength: **medium**.
14. **Adaptive difficulty splits players.**
    Evidence: the AI director was judged both ways, 7 times said (the first version of this entry had
    no count).
    Dominion could: if difficulty moves under players, let them see or choose it.
    Strength: **weak**.
15. **Studio politics can swamp reviews.**
    Evidence: 4 reviews in 2025 with the top helpful votes (41, 45), about the studio lead, none about
    play; 3 about the game's banners, and 1 the other way.
    Dominion could: know that the studio's public voice is part of the product page.
    Strength: **weak**.
16. **Being compared to a giant is unavoidable; make it a short label, not a contest.**
    Evidence: 263 of 2,411 things said (10.9%) name another game; 77 say a named game is better; 63
    say it copies; 71 just say "Left 4 Dead in space"; calling itself the heir backfired 10 times.
    Dominion could: know which games it will be measured against and beat them on at least one clear
    thing; describe itself in its own words rather than as "the next" famous game.
    Strength: **medium**.
17. **Bots must fight and revive well, or they become a complaint of their own.**
    Evidence: bots make solo possible 9; but useless in a fight 11, get stuck 7, fail to revive 3,
    cannot be given orders 2; help in a fight only 3.
    Dominion could: if Dominion has bots, test that they shoot, move through every arena without
    sticking, and revive the player reliably; consider simple orders (follow, hold, revive).
    Strength: **medium**.
18. **Danger must be heard and seen.**
    Evidence: no sound warns you of danger 15; threats are unclear 10.
    Dominion could: give every special enemy a sound cue before it acts and a clear look, so a squad
    can call it out.
    Strength: **medium**.
19. **Early access must show steady progress, or "needs more work" grows.**
    Evidence: "needs more work" rose from 4.1 to 13.6 per 100; "never became what it promised" 22;
    early access not worth it yet 28 against worth it even unfinished 13; updates too slow 8; updates
    stopped 8.
    Dominion could: if it uses early access, publish a short plan, ship on it, and say plainly when it
    slips.
    Strength: **medium**.
20. **A free weekend or demo wins goodwill.**
    Evidence: glad they could try it first 18, all in 2022-23 (6.7 per 100 in 2022).
    Dominion could: offer a demo or free weekend so players can test the gun feel before they pay.
    Strength: **medium** (but the page notes the free-weekend month also brought people who had not
    chosen the game).
21. **Give players private games and ways to reach each other.**
    Evidence: no private games 7; cannot talk to other players 4; cannot add friends 3; no server
    browser 3; cannot rejoin a match 2.
    Dominion could: offer friends-only and invite-only lobbies, in-game voice or text and quick
    signals, and rejoin after a drop.
    Strength: **weak to medium**.
22. **Give each level something people remember.**
    Evidence: nothing in the levels stands out 13; layouts repeat 11; forgettable special enemies 18;
    too few kinds of enemy 16.
    Dominion could: give each arena or route one set piece or landmark, and keep adding enemy kinds.
    Strength: **weak to medium**.

### Limits
- English only: 596 reviews in other languages are unread. Spanish reviews showed up in the English
  group, so Steam's language tag is imperfect.
- 611 of 1,290 read; ±2.85%. Two big months were read thin: 2022-06 at 18.6% and 2023-01 at 26.4%.
  Margins per year were not computed.
- **Edited reviews:** the 2026-09-03 page counted 125 (20.5%) carrying more than one date; the
  2026-10-03 script's edit marker finds 66 (10.8%). The pages do not reconcile the two.
- **The weighted file is stale:** the 58.49% weighted figure is the 2026-09-03 page's; the stats file
  behind it was built from only 150 reviews.
- **Playtime is a weak guide here.** Median 2 hours; 46.2% under 2 hours; **163 reviews (26.7%) show
  zero hours, 113 of them thumbs down.** Game Pass play is not counted by Steam.
- 2025's 37.7% is a political row, not a verdict on the game.
- The line-weighting claim (why the cast seems flat) is one reviewer; the playtime test fits it but
  does not prove it.
- Player counts, bans and the character-pick question are what reviewers say; none was checked.
- **Counts moved slightly between the two counts** (2026-09-03 page, then the 2026-10-03 script): dead
  game 47 then 46; studio chat fills a lobby 8 then 9; nobody ever heard of it 7 then 10; workshop 30
  then 29; flat cast 32 then 31; things said 2,411 (game page) or 2,419 (cross-game table) then 2,424.
  The year tables, the comparison and population groupings and the 2025 rate are from the 2026-09-03
  page and were not re-run.
- **What changed from the first version of this entry:** the rebuilt pages now count tech, price,
  progression, movement, hosting and bots, which the entry said the pages did not; the AI director (7),
  "reads as machine-made" (6) and playing with family (6) now have counts; and the approving side of
  the banner argument now has a label (1).

---

## Terminull Brigade

**Read:** 737 of 2,818 English reviews (26.2%), ±3.05%, written 2025-07 to 2026-09 (15 of 15 months;
the 8 months from 2026-02 on read in full). **41.0% thumbs up** in the sample (40.63% when each month
is weighted back to its true size). Steam, all languages: 50.9%, Mixed, on 5,673 reviews. The page warns
the English 41.0% and Steam's all-language 50.9% are not the same measure and should not be set side by
side.
**Pages:** `findings/terminull-brigade-english.md`, `findings/terminull-brigade.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` section 12 (also short mentions in sections 13, 21, 22 and 25).

**What it is:** A free-to-play third-person roguelike shooter for four players, built on runs and on
character abilities. Made by Pew Pew Games, published by Level Infinite. Released July 2025; a public
test came before launch. No review read carries the Early Access mark. It is the only free game in the
first part of the study. Online play goes through the studio's servers: reviewers report the studio
saying its stutter is a server-side problem. Its second season changed the main mode from runs to
farming gear with a fixed build. The pages do not give the engine.

**How close to Dominion:** The closest in shape of any game read: run-based, third person, ability-
driven, four players - and failing. It differs in price model (free, with a shop for looks) and, as far
as reviewers say, in hosting (studio servers, not one player's machine). No extraction mode is described.

### The short version (plain words)
One fault decided this game: a stutter that freezes play. It is named 165 times, more than three times
the next real complaint, and with its close neighbours (frame rate swings, freezes, lag) it is 225 of
2,248 points made (10.0%). It was there in the first month, the last month and, two reviewers say, the
public test. Yet 40 of the 165 who name it still recommend the game, and 18 say they will flip their
verdict the day it is fixed. People who named the stutter recommend the game 25.0% of the time; people
who named neither the stutter nor the install reward recommend it 50.8%. A paid install campaign and a
season that replaced the run mode added smaller, sharper damage. It is the most negative writing in
the study: 65.4 praises and 183.6 complaints per 100 reviews on the 2026-10-03 re-count, 0.36 praise for
each complaint (Redfall 0.48, Rogue Core 0.60, Back 4 Blood 0.86).

### What players praised, most to least
(The share that are thumbs up is in brackets where the page gives it. Counts are the page's of
2026-09-03; where the 2026-10-03 re-count differs, both are given.)
1. **The idea is good and could still come good.** 31 times on the re-count - the most-said praise
   after "good game". It fell from 28 times in 2025 to 3 in 2026.
2. **Builds are deep and varied.** 27 times (77% thumbs up). The strongest praise about play.
3. **The game is better than its reviews say.** 19 times, all 19 thumbs up (18 on the re-count).
4. **Shooting hits with weight.** 18 times (66% up). The second-strongest praise about play.
5. **Explained by naming other games** ("X meets Y"), used as a plain description. 17 times (76% up).
6. **Looks great.** 15 times (86% up).
7. **Keeps pulling you back for another run.** 15 times (73% up).
8. **The art style works.** 15 times (73% up).
9. **Paid extras are only looks; they never change play.** 13 times (76% up).
10. **Free, so easy to try first.** 13 times (61% up).
11. **Fair price.** 12 times. **The characters are appealing.** 12 times.
12. **It runs well.** 11 times. **Runs on a modest computer.** 7 times.
13. **Everything can be earned by playing.** 10 times. **The shop money can be earned by playing.** 8
    times. **Paying does not touch the grind.** 5 times.
14. **Much better with friends.** 10 times.
15. **Progress feels good.** 9 times. **Build choices change how you play.** 8 times.
16. **Difficulty steps are well spaced.** 6 times. **Each character plays its own way.** 6 times.
    **Memorable special enemies.** 6 times. **Easy to understand.** 5 times.
17. **No player-versus-player is a plus.** 6 times, 0.8 per 100 reviews (cross-game section 21; the
    highest after Escape from Duckov).
18. **Fun under the stutter.** Many stutter reports are conditional praise: *"Besides the stutter
    issuees its a reeally fun rogue like game"*, *"lotta stuttering but kinda fun nonetheless"*,
    *"Excellent free roguelike 3PS, once they fix those lag spikes!"*, *"The bloody Stuttering in this
    game. Otherwise good."* (no ids given on the page).
19. **A reward moment gives a rush.** 5 times (against 6 saying the payout lands flat).
20. **The game's politics (or lack of them) drew them in.** A praise built during this read; no count
    given.
21. **Checked the "spyware" claim and found it false.** 3 reviewers (see Tech).
22. "Good game" with nothing else: 97 times (13.2 per 100).
Overall, only 21.7% of all points made were praise once weighted (59.7% complaints, 18.6% neutral).

### What players complained about, most to least
1. **Stutter.** 165 times, the largest single complaint of any game in the study (7.3% of everything
   said about this game; 22.4 per 100 reviews). 40 of the 165 are thumbs up. Neighbours: frame rate
   swings 23, freezes or hangs 17, lag and desync 20. All together 225 (10.0%).
2. **Repetitive.** 50 times (48 on the re-count) - the next real complaint, a third the size of the
   stutter.
3. **Paid extras and the shop.** Paying gives you an edge in play 32; what you buy is a random draw 29;
   the shop pushes itself at you 25; you cannot work out what things really cost 18; content behind a
   second purchase 5; it asks for a credit card just to start 3. Reviews with a complaint about how the
   game makes money are 13.2 per 100 here, the second highest in the study after The First Descendant
   (14.6) (cross-game section 25). How the game sells itself is 10.6% of all points made.
4. **Too little to do.** 31 times.
5. **Too easy.** 29 times.
6. **Unlocks take too much grinding.** 22 times.
7. **Spyware fears.** 21 times (18 on the re-count): admin prompts, an anti-cheat driver, claims of
   spying or mining.
8. **Matchmaking finds no game.** 21 times.
9. **It looks like a cheap free-to-play template.** 20 times.
10. **A copy of an older game.** 19 times (21% up). *"a copy of a copy of a copy with nothing interesting
   carried over"*, *"a clone of five other games"*, *"the characters look like knock-offs of two named
   popular games"*.
11. **Nobody is left playing.** 18 times, rising to 6.2 per 100 in 2026.
12. **Not finished enough.** 17 times. **The menus are hard to find your way around.** 17 times.
13. **Another game does it better.** 15 times (13% up). *"a named competitor twenty dollars cheaper"*.
14. **Known bugs are never fixed.** 15 times. **It could have been more and settled for less.** 15.
15. **Buggy.** 14 times. **Build choices barely change anything.** 14 times.
16. **Expects the game to be switched off.** 12 times.
17. **The studio ignores feedback.** 10 times.
18. **Reward pop-ups get in the way.** 7 times (only in this game).
19. **The season replaced the run mode.** 6 reviewers, 5 of them thumbs down, 4 with over 70 hours
    (7 on the re-count; see Studio).
20. **The crossover came before the fix.** 3 reviewers wrote to the licence holder, not the studio:
    *"the fact these devs decided to do a crossover without fixing the biggest thing that so many
    negatives are pointing it out is just bad"* (217246854).
21. **Updates stopped.** Appears in 2026; 5 times on a tag count.
22. **The characters are designed to be sexy.** 24 times; the page files this as neither praise nor
    complaint. 3.3 reviews per 100 (cross-game section 25).
23. **Sorting worthless drops.** 2 reviewers.
24. **Motion sickness and headache from the stutter.** 2 reviewers (217246854, 219828133).
25. **The first stages of every run are dull.** 1 review here (Risk of Rain 2 page).
26. "Bad game" with nothing else: 52 times.
Each thumbs-up review carries 0.96 complaints and 1.25 praises (Immortal: Unchained page).
Thumbs split by group:
| Group | Reviews | Share | % up |
|---|---|---|---|
| Named the stutter, not the reward | 188 | 25.5% | 25.0% |
| Named the outside reward, not the stutter | 80 | 10.9% | 25.0% |
| Named both | 10 | 1.4% | 20.0% |
| Named neither | 459 | 62.3% | 50.8% |
This split held across nine batches: the stutter group stayed 24.7% to 27.3% of reviews and its
thumbs-up 25.0% to 29.0%. The page warns the 50.8% is not the game's score.

### How it changed over time
| Month | Reviews read | Named the stutter | Share |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2025-07 | 50 | 7 | 14.0% |
| 2025-08 | 424 | 96 | 22.6% |
| 2025-09 | 45 | 21 | 46.7% |
| 2025-12 | 97 | 12 | 12.4% |
| 2026-01 | 19 | 6 | 31.6% |
| 2026-02 | 16 | 5 | 31.2% |

Praise and complaints per 100 reviews, by period (re-count of 2026-10-03):

| Period | Reviews read | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Launch (2025-07 to 2025-08) | 474 | 43.2% | 58.6 | 182.9 |
| Late 2025 (2025-09 to 2025-12) | 182 | 37.9% | 44.5 | 153.8 |
| 2026 (2026-01 to 2026-09) | 81 | 34.6% | 32.1 | 190.1 |

- December's drop is not a fix: 33 of its 97 reviews were reward arrivals with zero hours, who never
  played long enough to see a stutter. January and February go back to about 31%.
- Two reviewers say the stutter was there from the public test (218520118, 217246854) - nine months.
- Praise fell every period (58.6, 44.5, 32.1 per 100). The stutter did not move: 21.7, 24.2, 22.2 per
  100.
- **What rose in 2026:** repetitive 6.8, 3.3, then 12.3 per 100; dead game 0.8, 4.9, 6.2; known bugs
  never fixed 2.1, 0.5, 4.9; buggy 2.1, 0.0, 4.9; a bare thumbs up 11.0, 12.6, 27.2.
- **What fell:** "the potential is still there" 5.1, 2.2, 3.7; too little 5.1, 3.3, 1.2; too easy 4.0,
  4.9, 1.2. Spying fears peaked in late 2025 (1.1, 6.0, 2.5).
- **2026 is worse than the lifetime figure.** The 62 reviews from 2026-02 to 2026-09 (every one read)
  are 32.3% thumbs up; all 81 reviews from 2026 are 34.6%; the whole read is 41.0%.
- "The design works, the content will come" fell from 28 times in 2025 to 3 in 2026, and "updates
  stopped" appeared.
- The install-reward campaign hit twice: 2025-08 (41 reviews, 36.6% up, 31 at zero hours) and 2025-12
  (33 reviews, 9.1% up, 32 at zero hours). A smaller first wave in 2025-07: 12 reviews, 25.0% up, 10 at
  zero hours.
- Season 2 changed the main mode (see Studio).
- 2025-07 and 2025-08 hold 2,193 of the 2,818 English reviews (78%).
- Population (reviewer claims from an outside tracker, not checked): four reviewers over five weeks put
  live players between 100 and 657; one (202932618) gives 466 now, 657 peak, just under 6,000 at launch.

### Co-op and online play
- **Lag and desync:** 20 times. **Matchmaking finds no game:** 21 times. The page says to lean on this
  count, not on player-count claims, when talking about population.
- **Studio servers** (a tag count over the summaries): high lag 9, cannot connect 6, frequent
  disconnects 5, no servers near me 4, a disconnect loses the run 2. One reviewer says the party
  leader's region is forced on everyone.
- **Too many ways to queue:** players split across too many options 11; slow to find a game 7.
- **Friends:** much better with friends 10; needs a group 8; rewards teamwork 4.
- **Alone:** punishing alone 4, works alone 3, unplayable alone 1. One point says bots make solo
  possible.
- **Crossplay:** none at all, 2.
- **Nobody agrees what causes the stutter.** Reviewers name the servers, frame rate tied to network
  delay, the netcode, and the studio's own server-side statement. One reviewer (202002088) reports the
  studio saying so, and that the stutter freezes the whole world at once, *"so it does not put anyone at
  a disadvantage."*
- Hosting by a player, joining mid-run and friendly fire: nothing said.

### Combat, movement and feel
- **Shooting hits with weight:** 18 (66% up). Weightless 3; feels like every other shooter 3.
- **Stutter freezes play:** 165, plus frame rate swings 23 and freezes 17.
- **Reward moments:** the payout lands flat 6; it gives a rush 5.
- **Abilities:** no fun to use, 3. No melee attack, 1.
- **Controls:** expected key bindings missing 4, unresponsive 3, responsive and clear 3, cannot rebind
  1, can rebind anything 1, one control layout only 1, aim assist helps 1, actions trigger by
  themselves 1.
- **Movement:** responsive 2, sluggish 1, missing modern moves 1.
- **Motion sickness and headache:** 2 reviewers, both tied to the stutter.
- Camera: nothing on the pages.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- **Too easy:** 29 - the seventh complaint. **Well graded:** 6. **Badly scaled:** 2.
- **Enemies:** bullet sponges 11; memorable special enemies 6; bosses are a chore 6; not enough variety
  5; poor enemy behaviour 3; no counterplay 3.
- One reviewer says you must beat a time to move on (4 points, only in this game).

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- **Deep and varied builds:** 27, the top praise about play (77% up). **Shallow:** 14; **only a few
  builds work:** 8; **builds change how you play:** 8.
- **Unlocks:** grindy 22; everything earnable 10; satisfying 9; nothing left to chase 6; locked behind
  real-world time 5, behind farming 4, behind a different mode 4.
- **Luck decides the outcome:** 11.
- **Season 2 locked earned gear to the new modes.** *"The first season you could equip the gear you've
  earned and play the now 'classic' mode with it... Now the gear you earn can only be used in the
  repetitive modes."* (220803108, 402 hours).
- **Worthless drops to sort:** 2 reviewers (216458142, 223751951).
- One reviewer says upgrading an item can ruin it.

### Runs, content and replay value
- **Keeps pulling you back:** 15 (73% up). **Repetitive:** 50 (48 on the re-count). **Too little:** 31.
  **Runs out fast:** 11.
- **Levels too short:** 5. **Repetitive layouts:** 5. **Needs long sessions:** 6. **Good in short
  sittings:** 2.
- **Season 2 replaced runs with gear farming:** *"In S2, they replaced the run-based format with a
  build-based format where you play too-short levels with a fixed build to farm gear. The whole gameplay
  loop that made the game fun for me is gone - it's not the same game any more."* (219464772, 102 hours).
- **Early stages of a run are dull:** 1 review.
- **Hours on the review:** median 4 on a count of 2026-10-03 (the page's own figure is 5); 23 show 100
  or more and 1 shows 1,000 or more; 224 show zero (30.4%). The median is 1 for thumbs down and 6 for
  thumbs up.
- Level generation: nothing said.

### Money and price
- Zero-hour reviews are 30.4% of the read (Aliens: Fireteam Elite page; that game, which is paid, has
  3.5%).
- Free to play. **Easy to try first:** 13 (61% up). **Paid extras only change looks:** 13 (76% up).
  **Shop money earnable by playing:** 8.
- **But complaints about the shop are high:** 13.2 reviews per 100, second in the study. Paying gives
  an edge 32; random draws 29; pushy shop 25; prices hard to work out 18; content behind a second
  purchase 5; a credit card asked for just to start 3 (only in this game).
- **Price:** fair 12; too high 6; not worth it at any price 3. Two reviewers say their country is
  missing from the in-game list.
- **The install-reward campaign:** a chat platform paid its users to install and play fifteen minutes.
  It put 90 reviews in the read (the second largest single item; 89 on the re-count), 76 of them at zero
  hours. Reward reviews: 24.4% up, median 0 hours. Everyone else: 43.3% up, median 5 hours. Removing all
  90 lifts the score from 40.98% to 43.3% - 2.3 points - and the praise-to-complaint balance from 0.36
  to 1 only to 0.37 to 1. The campaign is not why the game scores 41%.
- **A crossover brought people in:** 14 times, 8 up and 6 down. A door, not a verdict.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Engineering is 19.7% of everything said (444 points on the re-count) - second only to game design.
- **Stutter:** 165; frame rate swings 23; freezes 17; lag 20. 225 in all (10.0% of all points).
- **Other faults:** upsets the whole computer 10, crashes repeatedly 4, crashes at a set moment 3, buggy
  14, bugs that break play 6, needs strong hardware 9, cannot lower the settings 5, long loads 3. On the
  good side: runs well 11, runs on a modest computer 7.
- **18 reviewers say the verdict flips the day it is fixed.** One asks only to be told: *"tell me when
  you fix the stutters, or at least drop some english patch notes... so that i can better know when that
  deal braking problem for this otherwise good game is fixed"* (234031384).
- **Spyware fears:** 21 times, from an admin prompt, an anti-cheat driver and a launcher file with a
  confusing name; 3 note the anti-cheat runs at the deepest level of the system. Three reviewers
  checked: *"It asks for admin to access its own launcher, which is
  unfortunately names 'rs_launcher.exe'. If you actually look at the details you can see that it is their
  own launcher inside of their own directory."* (214226524). The page's reading: a badly named file is a
  support cost.

### The studio, updates and community
- **The season replaced the game, and only the long-time players saw it.** 6 reviewers: 4, 13, 73,
  102, 147 and 402 hours; the median for all reviewers is 5 hours. Five of the six are thumbs down.
  Nobody at zero hours raised it. Six reviews against 165 for the stutter: a simple count buries it.
- **The crossover on top of an unfixed fault** read to 3 reviewers as choosing marketing over repair; 3
  say the crossover partner should not have lent its name.
- **No English patch notes** is named by the reviewer above (234031384); 3 say the updates are not in a
  language they can read.
- **What players say about the studio** (tag count): known bugs never fixed 15; expects the game to be
  switched off 12; ignores feedback 10; working on the wrong thing first 7; a patch made it worse 7;
  updates stopped 5; talks, but never about the problem 5; the patch notes do not match the patch 4. On
  the other side: listens and acts 4, a patch made it better 4, fixed what mattered 4, open about what it
  is doing 4, and players teach each other the fix 4.
- **Dead game:** 18, rising to 6.2 per 100 in 2026.
- **Defenders are real fans:** all 19 "judged unfairly" reviews are thumbs up.
- **Copied reviews:** a script found five copies across four months; the earliest is the original.
- **"The beta was better":** five hits, two of them say the opposite.

### What players asked for
A word search of the review summaries finds 41 points worded as a request. By subject: fix the stutter
4; a complaint with a wish attached 2; the potential is still there 2; missing comforts 2; servers near
them 2; reviews written to the studio rather than to buyers 2; and 27 single requests, among them to be
sold items outright (not by random draw) and a neutral review option on Steam. The clearest ask: fix the
stutter, and say when it is fixed (18 say their verdict flips on the fix; 234031384 asks for English
patch notes). The long-time players ask for the run mode back and for earned gear to work in it
(219464772, 220803108).

### Only in this game
- **The largest single complaint of any game in the study** (stutter, 165, 7.3%).
- **The install-for-a-reward campaign** (90 reviews, 76 at zero hours) - built as a new item here.
- **Players comparing the game to its own past self** (the season change), not to another game.
- **Spyware fears from a badly named launcher file**, with the corrections in the same pile.
- **The most negative text in the study** at 0.36 praise per complaint, yet only the third least
  detailed (3.05 points per review): one complaint does not take many words.
- **The only free-to-play game in the corpus** at the time it was read.
- **32 points no other game has**, the most used: the reward installs (89 on the re-count), came for a
  crossover (14), reward pop-ups get in the way (7), copied reviews (5), you must beat a time to move on
  (4), a credit card asked for just to start (3), the crossover partner should not have lent its name
  (3), the updates are not in a language I can read (3), my country is missing from the in-game list
  (2). The other 23 were used once each.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **A single unfixed performance fault can become the whole game.** Evidence: stutter 165 times, 225
   with its neighbours (10.0% of all points), from public test to the last month; stutter-namers
   recommend the game 25.0% of the time against 50.8% for those who named neither. Dominion could: treat
   hitches and freezes as a launch blocker; test a full four-player match with the host on a mid-range
   machine, since a listen server puts the host's frame rate on everyone; hunt hitches (shader
   compiling, loading, garbage collection, network spikes) before content. Strength: strong.
2. **Players who report a fault are often fans waiting to come back - tell them when it is fixed.**
   Evidence: 40 of 165 stutter reviews are thumbs up; 18 say the verdict flips on the fix; 234031384
   asks only to be told. Dominion could: keep a public known-issues list and write short, plain patch
   notes that say "fixed: X". Strength: strong.
3. **Know and say where a hitch comes from.** Evidence: reviewers name four causes and the studio a
   fifth; no one knows. Dominion could: build in its own timing and network logs so the team can say
   whether a hitch is the host, the client or the network, and tell players. Strength: medium.
4. **Do not replace the loop players came for; add beside it.** Evidence: 6 reviewers, 4 with over 70
   hours, 5 thumbs down, say Season 2 swapped runs for gear farming and locked earned gear out of the old
   mode. Dominion could: keep the run mode as the home mode, let earned gear or unlocks work in every
   mode, and weigh feedback by hours played after any big change. Strength: medium (few reviews, but the
   most committed players).
5. **Do not pay for installs.** Evidence: 90 reward reviews, 76 at zero hours, 24.4% up; the second wave
   was 9.1% up with 32 of 33 at zero hours. Dominion could: skip "install and play 15 minutes" deals;
   if it runs promotions, aim them at people who will play. Strength: strong.
6. **But do not blame the score on outsiders.** Evidence: removing every reward review moves the score
   only 2.3 points. Dominion could: always check how much a suspected cause really moves the number
   before acting on it. Strength: strong.
7. **A crossover is a door, not a fix.** Evidence: 14 crossover reviews, 8 up and 6 down; 3 say a
   crossover on top of an unfixed fault reads as choosing marketing over repair. Dominion could: run
   collaborations or events only after the main faults are fixed. Strength: weak to medium.
8. **Name every file and permission prompt plainly.** Evidence: 21 spyware fears from an admin prompt,
   an anti-cheat driver and a launcher file with a confusing name; 3 found it was harmless. Dominion
   could: name its files after the game, avoid admin prompts, explain any anti-cheat on the store page.
   Strength: medium.
9. **Builds and shooting are what players will praise if the game runs.** Evidence: deep builds 27
   (77% up), shooting weight 18 (66% up). Dominion could: keep run builds deep and hits weighty; they
   were the strongest praise even here. Strength: medium.
10. **Do not let the game read as a copy.** Evidence: 19 call it a copy of an older game (21% up), 15
    send people to a rival (13% up); when players name other games just to describe it, 76% are up.
    Dominion could: have one clear thing of its own (for example extraction plus arena runs in a
    listen-server team) and lead with it. Strength: medium.
11. **Matchmaking must find a game even when few are online.** Evidence: 21 say they could not find a
    game. Dominion could: let players host and start alone or with bots, and fill games as people join.
    Strength: medium.
12. **Shop praise and shop complaints can live side by side.** Evidence: 13 praise looks-only extras,
    yet shop complaints run 13.2 reviews per 100. Dominion could: if it sells looks, keep prices fair and
    never tie them to play. Strength: medium.
13. **Watch the late years, not only the lifetime score.** Evidence: 2026 reads 32.3% up against 41.0%
    lifetime; "the potential is still there" fell from 28 to 3. Dominion could: track recent reviews and
    hope-type comments as an early sign that players are giving up. Strength: medium.
14. **Screen motion can make players sick.** Evidence: 2 reviewers report motion sickness or headache.
    Dominion could: offer camera shake and blur toggles and a field-of-view slider. Strength: weak.
15. **Loot nobody wants is a chore.** Evidence: 2 reviewers complain about sorting worthless drops.
    Dominion could: auto-sell or auto-skip junk. Strength: weak.
16. **Whatever is sold, sell it plainly.** Evidence: paying gives an edge 32, random draws 29, a pushy shop 25, prices you cannot work out
    18, content behind a second purchase 5, a credit card asked for just to start 3; one request asks to
    buy items outright. Dominion could: sell nothing that changes play, no random draws, one clear price
    in real money, and no shop pop-ups during a run. Strength: strong.
17. **A run must have bite.** Evidence: too easy 29, the seventh complaint, against well graded 6; enemy
    bullet sponges 11. Dominion could: offer harder settings from the start and make challenge come
    from enemy behaviour, not bigger health bars. Strength: medium.
18. **Keep the screens clean and the menus simple.** Evidence: menus hard to find your way around 17;
    reward pop-ups get in the way 7. Dominion could: keep reward screens short and skippable, and test
    the menus on new players. Strength: medium.
19. **Talk to players about the problem they are raising.** Evidence: known bugs never fixed 15; expects
    the game to be switched off 12; ignores feedback 10; working on the wrong thing first 7; talks, but
    never about the problem 5; patch notes that do not match the patch 4; updates not in a language
    they can read 3. Dominion could: lead each update note with the top known problem and its status,
    keep notes accurate, and post them in English at least. Strength: medium.
20. **Where the host is matters.** Evidence: high lag 9, no servers near them 4, the leader's region
    forced on everyone 1. Dominion could: as a listen-server game, show each player's ping to the host
    before a run and let groups pick the best-placed host. Strength: weak to medium.
21. **Too many queues empty every queue.** Evidence: players split across too many options 11; slow to
    find a game 7; matchmaking finds no game 21. Dominion could: launch with few modes and difficulty
    choices in public matchmaking, and widen the search before showing "no game found". Strength: medium.
22. **Looking cheap costs trust.** Evidence: 20 say it looks like a cheap free-to-play template.
    Dominion could: make menus, the shop and the first hour look and feel made for this game. Strength:
    medium.

### Limits
- 737 of 2,818 English reviews, ±3.05%. The two launch months hold 78% of English reviews and were read
  at 21.6% together (July at 8.7%, so one July review counts for 11.6). The weighted and raw scores agree
  to 0.35 points.
