- Free to play: hours, zero-hour share and population talk are not comparable to paid games. Nothing here
  separates "free" from "this game".
- English only: 2,855 other-language reviews unread.
- 46 reviews (6.2%) have more than one date.
- The cause of the stutter is not known; the corpus records claims only.
- Player counts are reviewer claims from an outside tracker.
- The reward wave's December figure was printed wrong twice before it settled (5.3%, then 10.3%, then
  9.1%); the gap between waves is the safe finding.
- One review (229578207, 1,470 hours) was about a different game; only its two stutter sentences were
  used. How often this happens is unknown.
- Some numbers here come from other games' pages (shop complaints 13.2 per 100 and the "designed to be
  sexy" 3.3 per 100 from The First Descendant's section; zero-hour share 30.4% from Aliens: Fireteam
  Elite's page; no-PvP 0.8 from section 21).
- **Changed since the last entry:** the rebuilt page gives counts where the old entry had none - "the
  potential is still there" 31, "updates stopped" 5, the cast built to be sexy 24 (filed as neither
  praise nor complaint), and the shop's own complaints (32, 29, 25, 18). The old entry said the pages
  had nothing on enemies, difficulty, movement or controls; the rebuilt page has counts for each.
- Counts moved slightly on the 2026-10-03 re-count: points 2,248 to 2,250; reward installs 90 to 89;
  spying fears 21 to 18; repetitive 50 to 48; "judged unfairly" 19 to 18; "the loop was replaced" 6 to
  7. Median hours: the page says 5, the count of 2026-10-03 says 4; not reconciled. The old figures are
  kept where the old page used them.
- Many smaller counts (servers, controls, enemies, unlocks, studio talk) come from a tag count over the
  summaries on 2026-10-03. The request list is a word search, so a few hits are wishes a reviewer
  describes rather than asks for. Per-period margins are not counted.
- Not checked against a primary source: the stutter's cause, the studio's server-side statement, player
  counts, the reward campaign's terms, the season change, prices.

---

## Redfall

**Read:** 1,015 of 3,071 English reviews (33.0%), ±2.52 points, written May 2023 to August 2026.
**48.6% thumbs up** in the sample (493 of 1,014) (Steam, all languages: 38.5%, Mostly Negative, of
4,716 reviews). The sample reads high on purpose: the method reads every month at about the same depth,
and 65% of all English reviews came in the launch month. Weighted back to each month's real size, the
sample reads **42.9%** up. Never quote the 48.6% as Steam's number.
**Size of the read, in numbers:** 3,819 things said by the script count on 2026-10-03 (3.77 per
review); the earlier page counted 3,812 (3.76 per review), the most detailed of any game when it was
counted. Only 267 of them (7.0%) say nothing specific (the earlier page: 275, 7.2%), tied for the lowest
in the study when counted. Reviewers here write long and list things. 356 different labels were needed,
13 of them used in no other game. Hours on the review: median 9; thumbs-down reviewers played a median
of 5 hours, thumbs-up reviewers 13; 12 reviews show 100 hours or more, 1 shows 1,000 or more.
**Pages:** `findings/redfall-english.md`, `findings/redfall.md`, `findings/cross-game.md` section 10.

**What it is:** A first-person shooter for one to four players, played online (the Steam store page: *"an open-world, single player and co-op FPS"*, *"up to four player co-op"*, checked 2026-10-03). Vampires take over a
small island town, and you clear it in an open world. Made by Arkane Austin, a studio owned by
Microsoft. Paid, and also shipped on Game Pass and given away with graphics cards; a "Bite Back
Edition" was sold too. Released 2 May 2023. The game checks in with a server when it first starts.
An offline mode was added on 30 May 2024. Microsoft closed the studio on 7 May 2024. The engine is
not named on the pages. Reviewers say co-op has no public matchmaking (you can only join by invite):
14 times said; and that only the host keeps story progress: 8 times said. (The first version of this
entry had no count for either; the rebuilt pages count them.)

**How close to Dominion:** Close in the parts that hurt most. It is a four-player co-op shooter with
PvE enemies, a paid game, built by a studio for friends to play online, and it failed. It differs in
camera (first person, not third), shape (an open world, not runs, arenas or extraction), setting
(vampires in a modern town, not sci-fi) and size (a big studio owned by a giant company). It is the
corpus's disaster case, so most lessons here are "what went wrong".

### The short version (plain words)
Redfall's reviews are mostly about a purchase, a reputation and a company, not about how the game
plays. The two most common things said are "buy it on sale" (172 times) and "it costs too much for
what it is" (143 times on the 2026-10-03 count, 144 when first counted). The game got much better after
a patch in October 2023: reviews from May to August 2023 are 34.7% thumbs up, and reviews from
September 2023 on are 60.7% up. But Steam shows one score for both, and that score is built mostly out
of launch month. Then Microsoft closed the studio, and today the game often will not start because it
waits for a server: one in ten of the 2026 reviews read say so. The biggest complaint about the game
itself is dumb enemies (126 times). Overall, reviewers write 241.6 complaints and 116.7 praises per 100
reviews: the highest complaint rate of the nine games in the cross-game page's first table, and a
praise-to-complaint ratio of 0.48 to 1, second lowest after Terminull Brigade (0.36 to 1).

### What players praised, most to least
Counts are the 2026-10-03 script count; where the earlier page differs, both are given. "Launch" is May
to August 2023, "after" is September 2023 on, both per 100 reviews in that half.
1. **"The game is better than its bad name"** - 124 times. This is the top praise, and it is not
   about the game. It is people arguing that other reviewers are wrong. Only 9 times does someone
   say the bad name is fair. That is 14 to 1. It ran at 13.0% of reviews in the launch half and 11.6%
   after, so people argued it during the pile-on, not just years later.
2. **The patches made it better** - 61 times (launch 0.6, after 10.7 per 100).
3. **Much better with friends** - 54 times (5.5 then 5.1: flat).
4. **The story is worth following** - 53 times (3.6 then 6.6).
5. **Shooting hits hard and feels good** - 53 times (3.0 then 7.2).
6. **The world is worth exploring** - 52 times (2.8 then 7.2).
7. **The mood pulls you in** - 51 times (3.6 then 6.2).
8. **The art style looks well directed** - 43 times (44 when first counted).
9. **Bugs are rare and small** - 39 times.
10. **The places are worth looking at** - 39 times. This one *fell* over time: 6.0 per 100 at launch,
    2.0 after.
11. **Many ways to build your character** - 24 times.
12. **The levels are well built** - 24 times.
13. **It works fine alone** - 22 times.
14. **It can now be played without the internet** - 21 times, none before the offline patch.
15. **It looks great** - 20 times.
16. **Low expectations made it feel better** - 18 times.
17. **Each character plays differently** - 17 times.
18. **The updates fixed what mattered** - 17 times.
19. **The characters are funny or memorable** - 16 times.
20. **It runs smoothly** - 15 times.
21. **More than one way into a place** - 14 times.
22. **It keeps pulling you back** - 14 times.
23. **As good as the studio's older games** - 13 times (against 65 that say it falls short).
24. **Runs well on my machine** - 11 times.

Four of the top ten (items 6, 7, 8, 10) are about the town and how it looks. **The art held up when
nothing else did**, and it was praised even inside thumbs-down reviews at launch. Also: 128 thumbs-up
reviews say nothing about why (129 when first counted).

### What players complained about, most to least
Raw count first (2026-10-03 script; the earlier page's count in brackets where it differs). Where the
earlier page gives it, the "weighted" share is the share of all English reviewers once the launch month
is counted at its true size. "Launch then after" is per 100 reviews in each half.
1. **Only buy it on sale** - 172 times (12.7% weighted; 8.7 then 24.1). Said most often, but voted
   least helpful: 15 helpful votes per time said.
2. **Costs too much for what it is** - 143 (144) times (18.1% weighted, the top complaint once
   weighted; 21.3 then 7.9). It carries 6,107 helpful votes, 42 per time said.
3. **The enemies are dumb** - 126 times (15.2% weighted; 17.0 then 8.5). The top complaint about the
   game itself. It carries 3,448 helpful votes. *"how are vampires so stupid in A VAMPIRE GAME"*.
   *"Rather than stalking, flanking, or overwhelming players in clever ways, many enemies simply run
   forward or stand in place."*
4. **It shipped broken** - 98 (99) times (12.0% weighted; 14.7 then 5.3). 4,289 helpful votes, 43 per
   time said.
5. **The story is thin and forgettable** - 71 (72) times (7.2% weighted; 7.2 then 6.8: it never fell).
6. **It is buggy** - 68 (70) times (8.5% weighted; 10.2 then 3.7).
7. **It falls short of the studio's earlier games** - 65 times (5.9% weighted; 5.3 then 7.4: it rose).
8. **Bugs break the session** - 59 (60) times (5.1% weighted; 5.3 then 6.2).
9. **The owner pulled the plug** (Microsoft closed the studio) - 59 (60) times (0.2 then 10.7).
10. **Every session feels the same** - 51 (52) times (4.9% weighted).
11. **The frame rate jumps around** - 49 times (6.4% weighted; 8.5 then 1.7).
12. **It wasted its potential** - 49 (53) times (3.2 then 6.2).
13. **Too little content** - 41 times.
14. **Another game does this better** - 41 times (5.7 then 2.6).
15. **The cutscenes are badly made** - 41 times (3.4 then 4.6).
16. **It is too easy** - 39 times.
17. **Not worth buying at any price** - 39 times.
18. **It crashes again and again** - 38 times (rose after launch, see below).
19. **Wanted a refund and could not get one** - 34 times.
20. **Few real ways to build your character** - 34 times.
21. **Too few enemies at once** - 34 times.
22. **The characters are flat or annoying** - 33 times.
23. **The owning company pushed it out too early** - 33 times. (The first version of this entry had
    no count for this.)
24. **Hard going if you play alone** - 31 times.

Also: 103 thumbs-down reviews give no reason (10.2 per 100). The store page hides a deal-breaker (8
times); two reviewers ask for the game to be pulled from sale.

The page does not give the thumbs-up / thumbs-down split for each complaint. It does say a
thumbs-up reviewer here writes 1.80 complaints and 2.10 praises on average, the most complaints per
thumbs-up review of the nine games compared on the Immortal: Unchained page.

**The most helpful-voted complaint per time said is about a free copy.** "It came free with my
graphics card" is said only 50 times, but carries 3,935 helpful votes, 79 per time said, the highest
of anything said in the Redfall read. (It is a fact about how the reviewer got the game, so the
script's praise and complaint lists leave it out.) The single most helpful review in the whole Redfall
read (1,070 helpful, thumbs down, one hour played) says three things: it shipped broken, it costs too
much, and it came free with hardware.

### How it changed over time
Reviews by period (patch dates are from Steam's own news feed, so they are checked):

| When | What happened | Reviews |
|---|---|---|
| 2 May 2023 | Release | May 2023: 34.9% up, 404 reviews read |
| 8 May 2023 | Small hotfix | no change |
| 12 June 2023 | First big patch: enemy AI, graphics, stability | June 34.6%, July 30.0%, August 35.0%: **flat** |
| Sept 2023 | - | 18.8% up, the worst month (only 16 reviews) |
| 6 Oct 2023 | **Game Update 2**: performance mode, open-world rework | October jumps to 55.6% and **never goes back** |
| 16 Nov 2023 | Game Update 3: weapons, character animation | November 64.3%, December 67.9% |
| Jan 2024 | two hotfixes | holds around 60% |
| 7 May 2024 | **Microsoft closes the studio** | "the owner pulled the plug" appears, reaching 60 times said (59 on the 2026-10-03 count) |
| 30 May 2024 | **Game Update 4**: pause, **offline mode**, DLSS 3 | "it plays offline" appears: 21 times, none before |
| 8 June 2024 | Refunds of the price gap for the Bite Back Edition | refund talk reaches 55 times |
| 1 July 2026 | Reports that Arkane Lyon is next to close | outside the review window; a report, not checked |

**Two halves, two different games.** May-August 2023: 470 reviews, 34.7% up (range 30.4 to 39.0).
September 2023 to August 2026: 544 reviews, 60.7% up (range 56.6 to 64.8). The ranges never touched
in nine rounds of reading.

**Praise per 100 reviews rose from 80.9 to 124.1; complaints per 100 fell only from 249.1 to 215.6.**
The later reviews like more things but still list nearly as many faults.

Share of reviews, launch half then later half (the 2026-09-03 page; the 2026-10-03 script differs by up
to 0.2 points, and its figure is in brackets where it does):
- Price too high: 21.3% then 8.1% (7.9) (fell by two thirds).
- Dumb enemies: 17.0% then 8.5% (halved).
- Shipped broken: 14.7% then 5.5% (5.3).
- Buggy: 10.2 then 3.7 per 100 (script only).
- Frame rate jumps: 8.5% then 1.7% (nearly gone).
- Buy on sale only: 8.7% then 24.1% (tripled).
- The owner pulled the plug: 0.2% then 10.8% (10.7).
- The updates made it better: 0.6 then 10.7 per 100 (script only).
- Crashes again and again: 1.7% then 5.5% (tripled).
- Plays offline (praise): 0.0% then 3.9%.
- Cannot reach the server: 0.6% then 2.8%.

**The game that exists now crashes more than the one that shipped.** Frame rate was fixed; stability
was not, and then the people who could fix it were let go.

Two plain points from the timeline: **the first big patch did nothing to the score**; the turn came
four months later, at Game Update 2 (five months after release). And **the best update shipped three
weeks after the studio was closed**: the offline mode is now the only reason a new buyer can play at
all. The cross-game page says that by the time of that patch the number of people playing at once was
in single figures (it does not say how it checked this), so the recovery changed the reviews and not
the business.

The helpful vote keeps the old verdict on top: the launch-week price and broken-launch complaints are
voted about three times harder per time said than the later "buy on sale" advice. That is one reason
Steam's score stays at 38.5% while the reviews under it move.

### Co-op and online play
- **Players barely talk about each other.** Only 2.7% of everything said is about other players or
  the community. Deep Rock Galactic is 28.8% and Helldivers 2 is 28.5%; the next lowest co-op game,
  Rogue Core, is 9.2%. **A four-player co-op game whose reviewers almost never mention playing with
  anyone.** The cross-game page notes the two games with lots of "other people" talk survived, and
  the low ones did not, and that across five games the measure separates the two survivors from the
  three failures; it says this does not prove which way it runs.
- **Friends help a lot when it happens:** "much better with friends" 54 times; "needs a group" 7.
- **Joining:** no public matchmaking 14; no server browser 8; easy to drop in and out 1; cannot rejoin
  a match 1.
- **Hosting and shared progress:** only the host keeps the progress 8; teammates cannot share progress
  3; cannot give a teammate your spare 2; dragged into content above your level 1.
- **Scaling to the group:** more players makes it trivial 6; scales to the number of players 2.
- **Teamwork:** demands coordination 3; rewards teamwork 2; friendly fire makes stories 1.
- **Solo:** hard going alone 31 against works fine alone 22. No bots are provided: 2.
- **Servers and lag:** cannot connect 18 (below); requires internet 21; frequent disconnects 5; lag
  and out-of-step play 5; a disconnect loses the run 2; smooth online 1; servers stable 1.
- **The online check at first start is the big online story.** Cannot reach the server: 18 reviews.
  3 at launch in 2023 (none at zero hours, 56 helpful votes). **13 after the studio closed (from
  November 2024), 7 of them with zero hours played, carrying 593 helpful votes.** Seven people
  reviewed a game they could never start.
  - *"Don't buy, game just is stuck on a connecting to server page."* (39 helpful, 0 hours)
  - *"offline mode doesn't exist? it won't let me start the game just gets stuck trying to connect
    to non existent servers"* (54 helpful, 0 hours)
  - *"It still tries to connect to a server that isn't there anymore and doesn't boot up cuz of it...
    Was refused a refund and the game is still for sale despite being unplayable."* (77 helpful)
- **How it breaks, from the reviews:** on first start a "connecting to server" box sits on top of the
  licence agreement. You must accept the agreement to connect, but the box covers it. The offline
  mode that would skip all of this is behind the same screen for anyone who did not turn it on
  before the servers went quiet. One review says exactly that: the offline mode works only if you
  already had it on (a point seen in no other game).
- Crossplay: nothing on the pages.

### Combat, movement and feel
- **Shooting:** hits hard 53 against feels weightless 17; shots go where they want 9; slow weapon
  handling 3; feels like every other shooter 4. The payoff of a kill or a hit lands flat 16 against
  lands well 10.
- Game Update 3 (November 2023) changed weapons and character animation; the months after it read
  64.3% and 67.9% up.
- **Abilities and characters:** each character plays its own way 17; a character is underpowered 7;
  the characters feel the same 4; the abilities are no fun to use 2. Stuns that take control away: 2.
- **Movement:** unreliable 8, slow 5, missing modern moves 3, against responsive 5.
- **Controls:** aim sensitivity cannot be tuned 7; controls unresponsive 5; an expected button missing
  1; abilities awkward to trigger 1. Controller support: nothing said.
- Camera: nothing on the pages.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- **Dumb enemies are the top complaint about the game itself**: 126 times, 15.2% weighted, 3,448
  helpful votes. It halved after the patches (17.0% of launch-half reviews, 8.5% after) **but did not
  go away.** It survives every reweighting.
- **Too few enemies on screen**: 34. One review says there are too many enemies for the town to keep
  its mystery (a point seen in no other game).
- **Difficulty:** too easy 39 against hard in a good way 10; badly scaled 14; well graded 4; content
  locked to harder settings 3; harder is not worth it 2; harder pays better 1.
- **Bosses are a chore**: 3.
- **The final fight is a pushover** (easier than what came before it): 2 times. This complaint was
  first named in this game. (The first version of this entry had no count.)

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
(The first version of this entry said the pages had nothing here. The rebuilt pages now count it.)
- **Builds:** few real ways to build your character 34 against many ways 24; some options are useless
  21; levelling up changes nothing 9.
- **Pace:** slow start 7; progression outgrows the challenge 5; grindy 3.
- **Resources:** too scarce 6, too plentiful 3; the currency stops being worth anything 4.
- **Carry-over:** progress does not carry over 1. Achievements are easy to miss 3 (seen in no other
  game).
- **Luck:** luck decides the outcome 1.

### Runs, content and replay value
- Every session feels the same: 51 (52) times (4.9% weighted).
- Too little content: 41 times. Runs out fast 9 against keeps pulling you back 14.
- It wasted its potential: 49 (53) times.
- The world is worth exploring: 52 times; the places are worth looking at: 39; the levels are well
  built 24; more than one way into a place 14.
- Buildings you cannot enter: 2 (seen in no other game).
- The open world was reworked in Game Update 2 (October 2023), the patch that turned the score.
- Generated levels: nothing said.

### Money and price
- **"Only buy it on sale"** is the most common thing said: 172 times, and 17.0 per 100 reviews, the
  highest rate in the whole study (next are Aliens: Fireteam Elite at 9.1 and Back 4 Blood at 8.6).
  It tripled after launch (8.7% to 24.1% of reviews).
- **"Costs too much for what it is"**: 143 (144) times, the top complaint once weighted (18.1%), and
  the most helpful-voted (6,107 votes). It fell from 21.3% to 8.1% of reviews as the price fell.
- **Not worth it at any price**: 39 times.
- **Price moves:** never put on sale despite its state 2; the price dropped so fast that launch buyers
  lost out 1 (seen in no other game).
- **Play it on the subscription (Game Pass) instead**: 4 (seen in no other game).
- Price and selling are 11.9% of everything said, the most of any game in that comparison.
- **Free copies:** "came free with my graphics card" 50 times, the highest helpful votes per time said
  (79). Game Pass and bundles mean many players never paid and never reviewed.
- **Refunds:** wanted a refund and could not get one, 34 times; got one, 19 times. Bite Back Edition
  price-gap refunds (June 2024) push refund talk to 55 times.
- **Paid add-ons (DLC):** sold before anyone knows what is in it 10; not worth it 7.
- **The store page hides a deal-breaker** (the game may not start; support ended): 8 times. Two
  reviewers ask for the game to be taken off sale.
- **101 reviews (10.0%) show zero hours played, and 89 of them are thumbs down.** The game keeps
  selling, and each new buyer who cannot start it becomes another bad review.
- In-game shops: nothing said.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Shipped broken: 98 (99) times (12.0% weighted). 14.7% of launch-half reviews, 5.5% after.
- Buggy: 68 (70) times. Bugs break the session: 59 (60) times. Bugs rare and small (praise): 39 times.
- Frame rate jumps: 49 times (6.4% weighted). 8.5% of launch-half reviews, 1.7% after. Game Update 2
  added a performance mode. Stutter 22; needs strong hardware 12; runs smoothly 15; runs well on my
  machine 11.
- **Crashes rose after launch:** crashes again and again 38 times, 1.7% to 5.5% of reviews. Crashes on
  one certain event 7.
- **Progress not saved**: 12. Huge install 3; long loads 1.
- **My friends cannot run it**: 1 (seen in no other game) - one player's weak machine stops the group.
- **Players found the crash cause themselves:** two reviewers each found one setting behind the
  crashes: *"switching off DLSS cured ALL the crashing"*, *"lower your shadow quality and turn on
  FXA."*
- **Players taught each other how to get past the server screen:** 8 times. *"DON'T use the mouse
  is the key, use the keyboard and hit enter."* *"press the space bar multiple times while pressing
  cancel and voilà - you're in."* *"if you spam enter, you can get it to work. if you do not do this,
  it will never connect."*
- **One in ten of the 2026 reviews read say the game will not start.**
- The engine is not blamed on the pages.

### The studio, updates and community
- **The studio was closed by its owner**: 59 (60) times. Reviewers blame Microsoft, not the team.
  **The owner rushed it out**: 33 times. The study had to add a way to say "the owner, not the
  studio, ended it" and its partner, "the owner rushed it out", because no game before Redfall needed
  that split. (The first version of this entry had no count for "rushed it out".)
- **After the closure:** updates stopped 18; known bugs never fixed 15; went silent after a bad launch
  6; the studio admitted the game was bad 4 (seen in no other game); dead game 7. One review says the
  team was too small, and one says the game's failure cancelled other games (each seen in no other
  game).
- **Patches:** the updates made it better 61; fixed what mattered 17; made it worse 2. The first big
  patch (June 2023) moved nothing; the October 2023 one moved everything; the last one (offline mode)
  came after the studio was gone.
- **"The people who made the old games have left; only the studio name is the same"**: 5 times, seen
  in no other game. (The first version of this entry had no count.) "It falls short of the studio's
  earlier games": 65 times, against 13 that say it lives up to them.
- **Reputation is the biggest subject of all**: 356 times (9.3% of everything said). People argue
  about the game's name more than they describe it. 124 say it is judged unfairly; 9 say the bad
  name is earned. 18 say low expectations made it feel better.
- **Attacked for writing the review**: 2 (seen in no other game).
- A review that calls the game simply "average, not good or bad" was first named here (no count).
- Some reviewers say the bad name set the bar so low that the game cleared it. The page says this is
  why it cannot tell "the game got better" from "cheaper, warned buyers arrived".
- Buyers did the support work: 8 reviews pass on the start-up trick and 2 pass on the crash setting.
- What is talked about, by area (2026-10-03 script, with the earlier page in brackets where it
  differs): how the game plays 25.7% (25.6%); reputation and marketing 12.6% (12.5%); tech 12.2%
  (12.3%); price and selling 11.9%; how it was made and shipped 8.3% (8.4%); story 8.2%; the review
  itself 6.6%; art 5.7%; updates and support 5.2%; other players and the studio 2.7%; sound 0.9%;
  access options 0.1%; the store 0.0%.
- Story is 8.2% of everything said, second only to Space Marine 2 (9.2%); the co-op games are under 3%.
  Lines worth quoting are rare: 0.6 reviews per 100. "Looks dated": 1.9 reviews per 100. One review
  says the world sounds alive (seen in no other game).

### What players asked for
The pages count no request as its own item:
- Two reviewers ask for the game to be **delisted** (taken off sale) because it does not start.
- Single reviewers ask for: more guns; melee weapons; a harder top difficulty; more kinds of enemy;
  more things to loot in the world; swapping character at the hub; objectives shown on the radar; a
  sequel. (Each seen once, from a word search of the summaries.)
- The fixes players wanted most are clear from what changed the score: an **offline mode / no server
  check** (21 times praised once it came), a **pause**, **steady frame rate**, and **smarter enemies**.
  These come from what was praised after it arrived, not from a counted list of requests.

### Only in this game
- **The reputation argument.** Reputation as the biggest subject, and a 124-to-9 argument that the
  game is better than its name. No other game in the corpus has this.
- **A server check that outlived the company.** The game still asks a server for permission at first
  start, the studio is gone, and the store still sells it. "The offline mode works only if you already
  had it on": 1, seen nowhere else.
- **The owner, not the studio, killed it** (59), and **the owner rushed it out** (33): first needed
  here.
- **"It came free with my graphics card"** carries the most helpful votes per time said in the read.
- **Buyers as the support desk and the crash testers.**
- **Only the host keeps progress** (8) and **no public matchmaking** (14) were first named in this game.
- **The final fight is a pushover** (2) was first named here.
- Story share (8.2%) is the highest of any co-op game read.
- **13 things said here and in no other game:** only the studio name is the same 5; play it on the
  subscription instead 4; the studio admitted the game was bad 4; achievements are easy to miss 3;
  buildings you cannot enter 2; attacked for writing the review 2; the price dropped so fast launch
  buyers lost out 1; the team was too small 1; its failure cancelled other games 1; my friends cannot
  run it 1; the offline mode works only if you already had it 1; the world sounds alive 1; too many
  enemies for the town to keep its mystery 1.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Do not lock the game behind an online check that can die.**
   Evidence: 18 reviews cannot reach the server; 13 came after the studio closed, 7 at zero hours,
   593 helpful votes; one in ten 2026 reviews say it will not start; 101 zero-hour reviews, 89 of them
   thumbs down. "Requires internet" is said 21 times. The offline mode came three weeks after the
   studio closed, behind the same broken screen, and one review says it only works if you already had
   it on.
   Dominion could: let the game start and reach the menu with no server at all. Let solo and
   friends-only (one player hosts) play run with no online service needed. If any online check is
   added (Steam, EOS, store), make it fail open: show a clear message and keep going. Test a start with
   the network cable pulled before every release.
   Strength: **strong** for the outcome; the cause is told by reviewers.
2. **The first screen must never block itself.**
   Evidence: the "connecting" box sits on top of the licence agreement you must accept; 8 reviews teach
   others to press Enter or Space to get through.
   Dominion could: never stack two pop-ups that both need an answer. Every first-run screen should
   work with mouse, keyboard and pad, and time out to something you can use.
   Strength: **medium** (8 reviews plus the 18 cannot-connect reviews).
3. **Smart enemies are the design fix that matters most in a PvE shooter.**
   Evidence: dumb enemies is the top complaint about the game itself, 126 times, 15.2% weighted,
   3,448 helpful votes. It halved after patches but stayed. *"how are vampires so stupid in A VAMPIRE
   GAME"*.
   Dominion could: give each enemy type a clear plan (rush, flank, hold, ambush) and test that the
   team sees it happen. Avoid enemies that only run forward or stand still. Budget for AI before
   launch, not after.
   Strength: **strong**.
4. **Launch is the review that sticks. A late fix changes reviews, not the score or the sales.**
   Evidence: 65% of English reviews came in launch month; the score went from 34.7% to 60.7% up after
   October 2023, but Steam still shows 38.5%. The first big patch (June 2023) did nothing. The
   cross-game page says few people were playing by the time the fix landed.
   Dominion could: hold the launch until frame rate, stability and enemy AI are solid. Use an early
   access or a playtest with honest labels instead of a big launch of an unfinished game. Plan the
   first-month fix cycle before launch, not after the reviews arrive (the cross-game page's reading).
   Strength: **strong**.
5. **A big patch must fix what players actually feel, not a list of systems.**
   Evidence: v1.1 ("enemy AI, graphics, stability") left June-August flat; Game Update 2 (a
   performance mode and an open-world rework) turned the score at once and for good.
   Dominion could: aim patches at the top two or three complaints players name, and say in the notes
   exactly what changes for them.
   Strength: **medium** (one game, clear timing).
6. **Fixing frame rate is not the same as fixing crashes.**
   Evidence: frame rate complaints fell from 8.5% to 1.7%, but crashes rose from 1.7% to 5.5% (38
   times said in all); two players found one setting (DLSS, shadows) behind the crashes; 7 say it
   crashes on one certain event.
   Dominion could: track crashes as their own number; test each upscaler and each high setting for
   crashes; give a safe-mode start (low settings, no upscaler) if the last run crashed.
   Strength: **medium**.
7. **Price must match what is in the box, on day one.**
   Evidence: "costs too much for what it is" 143 times (144 when first counted), 18.1% weighted, 6,107
   helpful votes; "only on sale" 172 times and 17.0 per 100 reviews, the highest in the study; not
   worth it at any price 39; too little content 41 times.
   Dominion could: set the launch price to the amount of content and polish there really is; a lower
   price at launch costs less than years of "wait for a sale".
   Strength: **strong**.
8. **Free copies bring the angriest, most-voted reviews.**
   Evidence: "came free with my graphics card" 50 times, 79 helpful votes per time said, the top rate;
   the most helpful review (1,070 votes) was a one-hour, thumbs-down review that says it came free
   with hardware.
   Dominion could: be careful with bundles and giveaways while the game is rough; a free player who
   bounces still writes a review.
   Strength: **medium**.
9. **Co-op needs to be easy to get into, or players stop talking about each other.**
   Evidence: only 2.7% of everything said is about other players, against 28.5-28.8% in the two big
   co-op hits; no public matchmaking 14, no server browser 8, only the host keeps the progress 8,
   teammates cannot share progress 3; yet "much better with friends" is the third praise (54 times).
   Dominion could: offer quick join with strangers as well as friend invites; make sure every player,
   not just the host, keeps what they earn in a run (this matters doubly on a listen server where one
   player hosts).
   Strength: **medium** (the 2.7% is strong; the joining and host-progress counts are small, 3 to 14).
10. **A strong world and look will not save weak play, but players do notice them.**
    Evidence: world worth exploring 52, mood pulls you in 51, art well directed 43 (44 when first
    counted), places worth looking at 39, looks great 20; praised even in thumbs-down launch reviews.
    Dominion could: keep investing in a clear sci-fi look and mood, but never in place of enemy AI,
    stability and feel.
    Strength: **medium**.
11. **A story that matters to players needs good cutscenes.**
    Evidence: story worth following 53 vs thin and forgettable 71 (72 when first counted); cutscenes
    badly made 41; characters flat or annoying 33 vs funny or memorable 16.
    Dominion could: if it tells a story, keep cutscenes short and well staged, or tell it in play
    instead.
    Strength: **medium**.
12. **Have a pause and an offline mode from day one.**
    Evidence: Game Update 4 added a pause and offline mode; "it plays offline" went from 0 to 21 times
    said, all praise.
    Dominion could: pause in solo; offline solo play from launch.
    Strength: **medium**.
13. **Write the store page honestly, and keep it honest as things change.**
    Evidence: "the store page hides a deal-breaker" 8 times; refunds refused 34 times; two ask for
    delisting.
    Dominion could: if support ever ends or servers change, say so at the top of the store page.
    Strength: **weak to medium**.
14. **The climax must be the hardest fight, not the easiest.**
    Evidence: "the final fight is a pushover" was first named in this game, 2 times said; "too easy" is
    said 39 times against 10 for "hard in a good way".
    Dominion could: make sure the last arena or boss of a run is the peak of the run.
    Strength: **weak** (2 reviews for the final fight; the wider "too easy" count is medium).
15. **A bad name sticks, and the people who come later spend their review arguing with it.**
    Evidence: reputation is the biggest subject (356 times, 9.3%); 124 say it is judged unfairly vs 9
    who say it is fair; 18 say low expectations made it feel better.
    Dominion could: protect the launch reputation above all; a strong launch is cheaper than any
    rescue.
    Strength: **strong** for the pattern.
16. **Fill the arena, and make the fight hard enough.**
    Evidence: too few enemies on screen 34; too easy 39 against hard in a good way 10; badly scaled 14;
    more players makes it trivial 6.
    Dominion could: keep enemy counts high enough that the squad feels pressed, and scale both numbers
    and toughness to the number of players so a full team of four is still pushed.
    Strength: **medium**.
17. **Solo must be a real way to play, not a punishment.**
    Evidence: hard going alone 31 against works fine alone 22; "needs a group" 7; no bots provided 2.
    Dominion could: tune solo runs on their own (enemy count, revive rules, or bots) so a player
    without friends online is not shut out.
    Strength: **medium**.
18. **Every build choice should change something.**
    Evidence: few real ways to build your character 34 against many ways 24; some options useless 21;
    levelling up changes nothing 9; the currency stops being worth anything 4.
    Dominion could: make each upgrade or perk change how a run plays, not just add a small number; cut
    or rework picks that nobody takes.
    Strength: **medium**.
19. **Never lose a player's progress.**
    Evidence: progress not saved 12; a disconnect loses the run 2; only the host keeps the progress 8;
    teammates cannot share progress 3.
    Dominion could: save what each player earns on their own machine as it is earned, so a crash, a
    dropped connection or a host leaving does not wipe a client's run rewards.
    Strength: **medium**.
20. **Let players tune aim and controls.**
    Evidence: aim sensitivity cannot be tuned 7; movement unreliable 8; controls unresponsive 5; an
    expected button missing 1.
    Dominion could: ship sensitivity sliders (hip, aim, pad and mouse apart), full rebinding and quick,
    reliable movement from day one.
    Strength: **weak to medium**.
21. **Do not sell add-ons before players can see them.**
    Evidence: paid add-ons sold before anyone knows what is in them 10; not worth it 7; the price
    dropped so fast launch buyers lost out 1.
    Dominion could: sell any extra content only once it is shown, and avoid deep price cuts soon after
    launch that punish the first buyers.
    Strength: **weak to medium**.
22. **When updates stop, say so; do not leave known bugs hanging.**
    Evidence: updates stopped 18; known bugs never fixed 15; went silent after a bad launch 6; the
    studio admitted the game was bad 4.
    Dominion could: keep a public list of known bugs and their state; if support ever ends, say so
    plainly and leave the game in a state that works offline.
    Strength: **medium**.

### Limits
- English only: 1,645 reviews in other languages were never read.
- The sample is 48.6% up; Steam is 38.5%. Weighted it is 42.9%. The launch month (1,986 reviews,
  65%) was read at 20.3%; the other 39 months (1,085 reviews) at 56.3%. Even inside May 2023 the
  first, worst days are read thinner. September 2023 is only 16 reviews.
- **Edited reviews:** the 2026-09-03 page found 175 reviews (17.3%) carrying more than one date; the
  2026-10-03 script's edit marker finds 102 (10.1%). The pages do not reconcile the two. Each is filed
  under the month it was first written, and that is still an open question with Rico.
- **Counts moved slightly between the two counts** (2026-09-03 page, then the 2026-10-03 script): too
  high for what it is 144 then 143; shipped broken 99 then 98; thin story 72 then 71; buggy 70 then 68;
  bugs break play 60 then 59; owner pulled the plug 60 then 59; same every session 52 then 51; wasted
  potential 53 then 49; art well directed 44 then 43; bare thumbs up 129 then 128; things said 3,812
  then 3,819. This entry gives both where they differ.
- **What changed from the first version of this entry:** the rebuilt pages now give counts where the
  entry said "no count" (no public matchmaking 14, only the host keeps progress 8, the final fight is a
  pushover 2, rushed out by the owner 33, only the studio name is the same 5), and they now count
  progression, loot, movement and controls, which the entry said the pages did not.
- Helpful votes, the cannot-connect split, the subjects table and the weighted shares come from the
  2026-09-03 page and were not re-run.
- Steam's "English" label is the reader's client language: at least one review is in Portuguese.
- The pages cannot split "the game got better" from "cheaper, warned buyers arrived".
- Game Pass and bundles hide most players; only 50 reviews show the bundle.
- Server, refund, delisting and ban claims are what reviewers say, except the patch dates and the
  studio closure date, which the page took from Steam's news feed. The 2026 report about Arkane Lyon is
  not checked.
- The single-reviewer requests come from a word search of the summaries, each seen once.
- One review in the read was not about the game and was left out.

---

## Helldivers 2

**Read:** 1,626 of 819,840 English reviews (0.20%); the pages give no margin of error, and call this the
thinnest sample in the study. Written February 2024 to August 2026 (31 months). **83.3% thumbs up** in
the sample, 1,355 of 1,626 (Steam's own English figure, on the cross-game page: 83.3%; the pages give no
all-languages figure and no Steam rating word). 4 reviews were not about the game and were left out.
Hours on the review: median 44; 464 reviews show 100 hours or more and 11 show 1,000 or more; the median
is **73 hours for the 271 thumbs-down reviews and 40 for the 1,351 thumbs-up ones** (the 1,622 kept
reviews).
**Pages:** `findings/helldivers-2-english.md`, `findings/helldivers-2.md`, `findings/cross-game.md`
sections 1 to 8 (and passing mentions in later sections).

**What it is:** A co-op PvE shooter with friendly fire, a satirical sci-fi setting (players fight "for
Super Earth" and "for Democracy"), sold as a paid game with a paid currency that players can also earn
by playing, and run as a live service with frequent patches and content updates (Escalation of Freedom,
an Illuminate invasion, Machinery of Oppression). Reviewers say a publisher account became required in
May 2024 and that the game then stopped being sold in many countries; the page calls it "the
publisher-account fight" and does not check the details. Some players object to extra software it
installs. Reviews start in February 2024; the Steam patch
record starts in September 2023. The Steam store page calls it *"a fast, frantic, and ferocious third-person shooter"* with *"squads of up
to four"* (checked 2026-10-03). The pages do not say who hosts a match, the engine, the price, the
developer or publisher by name, or how missions are structured.

**How close to Dominion:** Very close in what players do: third person, four-player co-op, sci-fi, PvE against alien hordes, friendly fire, and a strong in-world voice. It differs in being a big live service with a big publisher, a
required outside account and a paid currency, and the pages say nothing about hosting, so nothing here
speaks to a listen server directly (one review, seen once, says enemies go for the host). Most of what
this game teaches is about running a game after launch, not about its design.

### The short version (plain words)
Almost nobody complains about how the game plays. They complain about the people running it. Four of the
top five complaints are choices someone made: a patch made it worse (said 47 times), the publisher puts
players off (45), it is not sold in my country (39), and the studio weakened things players liked (39);
the fifth is crashes (37). The first complaint about the game itself, "it repeats itself", is seventh at
22. The game got better (crash complaints fell from 2.5 to 1.7 per 100 reviews from 2024 to 2026) while
the relationship collapsed: thumbs down tripled to 36.2% in 2026, and good things said about the studio
went from beating bad things 3.3 to 1 in 2024 to losing 1 to 9 in 2026. The people who complain are the
people who stayed: the middle thumbs-down reviewer has played 73 hours, the middle thumbs-up reviewer 40.
What stays loved all three years is the war cry (398 times, one review in four), playing with friends
(62), the satire (53), coming back again and again (47), and friendly fire that makes funny stories (37).

### What players praised, most to least
Counts are times it was said (one review can say more than one thing). Per 100 = per 100 reviews read.
In all, praise was said 1,752 times (108.0 per 100) and complaints 900 times (55.5 per 100) on the
2026-10-03 re-run.

1. **The war cry.** Reviews say nothing but the game's slogan - "FOR SUPER EARTH", "FOR DEMOCRACY",
   "Liber-tea" - 398 times, 24.5 per 100, one review in four, 13.7% of everything said. Two reviewers wrote
   another game's chant by mistake ("FOR ROCK AND STONE ...wait wrong game" from Deep Rock Galactic, and
   "SONS AND DAUGHTERS OF HELGHAN RISE" from Killzone). The page reads this as a habit tied to having a
   slogan at all. It is fading: 26.3 per 100 in 2024, 24.2 in 2025, 16.3 in 2026 (16.4 on the re-run).
   The war cry and the bare thumbs up together are 764 of the things said, 26.3% - and neither says
   anything about the game.
2. **The studio listens and acts** - 78 times (4.8 per 100; 75 on the 2026-10-03 re-run). 4.8 per 100 in
   2024 (4.6 on the re-run), 7.1 in 2025, then 1.7 in 2026. Publisher reversals were filed here too.
3. **Much better with friends** - 62 times (3.8 per 100; 61 on the re-run), steady across all three
   years.
4. **Players take on the game's identity** - they call themselves by the game's own names - 54 times (3.3
   per 100).
5. **The satire lands** - 53 times (3.3 per 100), steady across all three years.
6. **It keeps pulling you back** - 47 times (2.9 per 100), steady.
7. **Best game of its kind** - 46 times (2.8 per 100), steady.
8. **Friendly fire makes stories** - 37 times (2.3 per 100), steady. Against only 3 negative ones in the
   whole study. The positives are self-inflicted, against friends, and funny: "dropping a bomb on his own
   squad and then apologising insincerely."
9. **A welcoming community** - 33 times (2.0 per 100).
10. **Patches fixed what mattered** - 26 times (1.6 per 100).
11. **The price is fair** - 25 times (1.5 per 100).
12. **Combat hits hard** - 22 times (1.4 per 100).
13. **The game is judged unfairly** - its reputation is worse than the game - 21 times (1.3 per 100). This
    became the most common opinion in the last batch read: the argument itself became the subject.
14. **The paid currency can be earned by playing** - 20 times (1.2 per 100).
15. **A patch made it better** - 20 times (1.2 per 100).
16. **Builds are deep and varied** - 16 times (1.0 per 100). 0.9 per 100 in 2024, 2.0 in 2025, 0.0 in
    2026 (re-run).
17. **Hard in a good way** - 16 times (1.0 per 100).
18. **It looks great** - 16 times (1.0 per 100). 1.3, 0.7, 0.0 per 100 by year (re-run).
19. **It works solo** - 15 times (0.9 per 100).
20. **The studio earned my trust** - 14 times (0.9 per 100). Deep Rock Galactic 3.0 per 100, Back 4 Blood
    0.0.
21. **A steady stream of updates** - 14 times (0.9 per 100).
22. **The difficulty ladder is well built** - 14 times (0.9 per 100).
23. **Fine with strangers** - 14 times (0.9 per 100).
24. **The mission falling apart is the fun** - a swarm, a collapse, a panicked escape - 13 times (0.8 per
    100).
25. **Nothing else plays like it** - 10 times (0.6 per 100). (On the 2026-10-03 re-run this one falls just
    out of the top 25; the other 24 are the same in a slightly different order.)
26. **Silly actions with no use that players love** (emotes and the like) - 6 times (0.4 per 100), against
    111 in Deep Rock Galactic.

Also counted on the 2026-10-03 re-run, and used in no other game - all neither praise nor complaint
unless marked:
- **A running story players follow** (the war the whole player base fights, a world that changes for
  everyone at once) - 20 times.
- **The shared war counts my play** - the reviewer talks about how their own missions feed a total
  everyone shares - 8 times.
- **The game's fiction organised something real** among players - 5 times.
- **The studio stood with us against the owner** - 2 times (praise).
- **The studio answered in character**, in the game's own voice - 2 times.
- Seen once each: the community completes the game; the studio stopped punishing criticism; good
  material for a video.

Also: 24 reviews explain the game by naming another game (re-run). Where the praise comes from: 37.3% of
this game's praise is about the community, 18.6% about game design, 7.1% about marketing and reputation
(Back 4 Blood, the game that did not last, puts 18.5% of its praise on marketing and 8.3% on the
community: "the failure praises the marketing; the survivors praise each other").

### What players complained about, most to least
Counts are times it was said. Per 100 = per 100 reviews read. The pages do not split complaints by thumbs
up or down, except where noted. Year figures are per 100 reviews in 2024, 2025 and 2026, from the
2026-10-03 re-run.

1. **A patch made it worse** - 47 times (2.9 per 100). 2.0, 3.0, then 6.9 per 100 by year.
2. **The publisher (the owner) puts players off** - 45 times (2.8 per 100). 30 of the 45 in May 2024.
   3.4, 1.3, 1.7 per 100 by year.
3. **Not sold in my country** - 39 times (2.4 per 100). 30 of the 39 in May 2024. Reviewers could not
   agree how many countries: they say 176, 177, 180, "over 200" and "about 170". Most of these English
   reviewers write about countries they do not live in.
4. **The studio weakened things players liked** - 39 times (2.4 per 100; 37 on the re-run). 1.6 per 100
   in 2024, 1.7 in 2025, 7.3 in 2026 - up 4.7 times (the re-run: 1.5, 1.7, 6.9). It becomes the game's
   defining complaint.
5. **It crashes again and again** - 37 times (2.3 per 100). Falling: 2.5 per 100 in 2024, 2.0 in 2025,
   1.7 in 2026.
6. **A bug stops a mission, loses progress or spoils a run** - 28 times (1.7 per 100; 26 on the re-run).
   1.6, 0.3, then 3.0 per 100 by year.
7. **It repeats itself** - 22 times (1.4 per 100). The first complaint about the game itself. 1.6, 1.3,
   then **0.0** in 2026: by 2026 nobody complains about the game repeating.
8. **Other players put me off** - 22 times (1.4 per 100). 0.8, 1.0, 4.3 per 100 by year. By 2026 it comes
   from both sides: one reviewer objects to players attacking the studio, another to players defending it.
9. **The thumbs down is a protest, not about the game** - 21 times (1.3 per 100). 14 of the 21 in May
   2024. "21 people said outright that their thumb was aimed at the company, not at the game."
10. **Buggy** - 19 times (1.2 per 100). (The re-run also counts 19 bare thumbs down with no reason, which
    it ranks at row 11.)
11. **The studio misreads what players want** - its idea of fun is not theirs - 19 times (1.2 per 100; 18
    on the re-run). 0.7, 0.3, 3.9 per 100 by year.
12. **The studio fights its own players** - 17 times (1.0 per 100). 0.4, 1.0, 4.3 by year.
13. **The studio lost my trust** - 16 times (1.0 per 100). 0.1, 1.3, 4.7 by year.
14. **The studio ignores feedback** - 15 times (0.9 per 100). 0.3, 0.3, 4.7 by year.
15. **A required account or platform link blocks or annoys** - 15 times. 13 of the 15 in May 2024.
16. **Disconnects** - 14 times (0.9 per 100).
17. **It damages things outside the game** - the computer, the sound or the system needs fixing after - 12
    times (0.7 per 100).
18. **It installs extra software I did not want** - 12 times (0.7 per 100). The label covers anti-cheat,
    launchers and companion apps; the page does not say which.
19. **Selling more while the game is still broken** - 11 times (0.7 per 100). 0.3, 0.0, then 3.4 per 100
    in 2026.
20. **Known bugs never fixed** - 11 times (0.7 per 100).
21. **Punishing solo** - 11 times (0.7 per 100).
22. **Too grindy** - 11 times (0.7 per 100).
23. **Builds barely change anything** - 11 times (0.7 per 100; 10 on the re-run).
24. **Trolls and griefers** - 11 times (0.7 per 100).
25. **A marketing claim was untrue** - 10 times (0.6 per 100).
26. **Updates arrive but add little** - 10 times (0.6 per 100).
27. **It needs a stronger computer than I have** - 10 times (new: the 2026-10-03 re-run puts it in its
    top 25).
28. **It stopped working on my setup** - 10 times (new: also in the re-run's top 25).
29. **The studio punishes criticism** - 0 in 2024, 1 in 2025, 6 in 2026. The 2026 ones are four separate
    reviewers, none involved, reporting the same story: that a player who offered the studio help had
    private details posted online. This is hearsay in every case - the page records that four people
    believed it, not that it happened.
30. **The fights drove me away** - built from this game: "I game to relax and enjoy myself and I find there
    is more drama with this game than anything else I do in my life." (count not given)
31. **Friendly fire as a cost** - 3 negative reviews in the whole study, and all of them are done TO the
    reviewer by strangers, again and again: "I get teamkilled about 85% of my games."
32. **Shots stopped by scenery that should let them pass** - seen once here (and once in another game)
    before a later game made it a full label.
33. Seen once each and used in no other game (re-run): **enemies go for the player who hosts**, so
    hosting costs that player; **too many buttons to manage**.

**Who could have stopped these?** Of the top 25 complaints: live-ops and publisher decisions, 202 times;
community and reputation, 131; engineering, 122; game design and production, 55 - the smallest group, by
a factor of four. As a share of all this game's complaints: engineering 21.7%, game design 19.9%,
community 15.8%, publishing 15.1%, live-ops 13.3%, marketing 4.2%, production 3.8% (898 complaints in all).
It is the only one of the first three games where the game itself is not the main target. Only two
design complaints reach the top fifteen, and one of them is about other players. Complaints about money
practices are 1.8 per 100 reviews (Warframe 2.8, The First Descendant 14.6).

**What players talk about overall.** Talk about the community is 28.5% of everything said (828 times),
game design only 18.6% (540) - the lowest design share of the first three games. Engineering is 9.3%
(269), three times Deep Rock Galactic's share. The running of the game after launch (patches, updates)
and the publisher's choices together are 15.0% - more than engineering, art and audio put together
(337). Sound, accessibility, the store page and translation together are 19 times: not a sign they are
fine, only that English reviews of a co-op shooter do not talk about them.

### How it changed over time

| Year | Reviews | Thumbs down | Things said per review | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2024 | 1,093 | 13.9% | 1.72 | 110.2 | 48.0 |
| 2025 | 297 | 11.8% | 1.73 | 114.1 | 46.1 |
| 2026 | 232 | 36.2% | 2.17 | 89.7 | 101.7 |

The 2026-10-03 re-run leaves out every line about the review itself (a bare thumbs up or down, and notes
on how the review is written) and gives thumbs up 86.1%, 88.2%, 63.8%; praise per 100 86.1, 94.6, 70.7;
complaints per 100 46.0, 43.4, 97.8. Both counts tell the same story.

- **2026 is the break.** Thumbs down tripled. Complaints per review doubled and passed praise for the
  first time. People wrote more (2.17 things each).
- **What players said about the studio, per 100 reviews:** "listens and acts" 4.8, 7.1, 1.7; the four bad
  ones together (misreads, fights its players, ignores feedback, punishes criticism) 1.5, 2.0, 15.5. Good
  to bad went from 3.3 : 1 (2024) and 3.5 : 1 (2025, the studio's best year) to 1 : 9 (2026) - a
  thirty-fold swing in a year. "Weakened what players liked" 1.6, 1.7, 7.3. Crashes 2.5, 2.0, 1.7.
- **What rose into 2026 (re-run, per 100, 2024 then 2025 then 2026):** a patch made it worse 2.0, 3.0,
  6.9; weakened what players liked 1.5, 1.7, 6.9; lost my trust 0.1, 1.3, 4.7; ignores feedback 0.3, 0.3,
  4.7; fights its own players 0.4, 1.0, 4.3; other players put me off 0.8, 1.0, 4.3; misreads what
  players want 0.7, 0.3, 3.9; selling while broken 0.3, 0.0, 3.4; a bug breaks play 1.6, 0.3, 3.0.
- **What fell:** crashes 2.5, 2.0, 1.7; the war cry 26.3, 24.2, 16.4; "listens and acts" 4.6, 7.1, 1.7;
  "it repeats itself" 1.6, 1.3, 0.0; "builds are deep and varied" 0.9, 2.0, 0.0; "looks great" 1.3, 0.7,
  0.0; the publisher puts players off 3.4, 1.3, 1.7. In 2026 the complaints about the game itself went
  quiet and the complaints about the people running it took over.
- **May 2024 is the loudest month, not the angriest.** 309,103 English reviews on Steam that month, more
  than twice the launch month's 147,461 (510 read). It is the publisher-account fight: 30 of 45 "publisher
  puts me off", 30 of 39 "not sold in my country", 14 of 21 "protest vote", 13 of 15 "account blocks me"
  all land in that one month - between 67% and 87% of each. Yet its share of negative reviews read was
  30.9%, not among the worst.
- **The three low points** (share of reviews read that were negative): August 2024, 48.1% (on only 10,632
  Steam reviews - a quiet, unhappy month, not a review bomb); May 2025, 50.8% (11,504 reviews); March to
  May 2026, 52.3%, 62.3%, 56.2%. April 2026 is the most negative month in the whole 31-month record, with
  8,004 reviews, two years after launch.
- **Other unhappy months** on the page: June and July 2024 (38.2%, 39.5%), October and November 2025
  (42.1%, 41.9%), February 2026 (41.7%), August 2026 (47.4%). Each rests on 19 to 32 reviews read.
- **The best month:** March 2025, 4.2% negative.
- **Lined up against the Steam patch notes** (799 Steam news items from 2023-09-14 to 2026-08-25, 103
  with a version number, pulled 2026-09-02; quotes are from that record):
  - February 2024: eleven versioned patches in one month, from 01.000.004 on the 9th to 1.000.13 on the
    28th - patches every two to three days. The least negative month of the launch period, 14.1%.
  - March 2024: 1.000.100 "nerfs its best weapons, adds new hazards" (03-06), 1.000.102 "nerfs hardest
    enemy and spawn rates" (03-12), 1.000.103 "sadly doesn't fix the highest priority issue" (03-20).
    Negative share nearly doubled, 14.1% to 25.1%. The earliest dated start of the weakening complaint.
  - April 2024: 01.000.202 "finally fixes those many crashes" (04-09); 01.000.300 "makes some massive
    changes to ammo" (04-29). Negative share 35.2%.
  - August 2024: 1.001.002 Escalation of Freedom (08-06), then 1.001.005, an Emergency Hot Fix (08-21).
    48.1% negative. A content release, not a removal - "a studio can ship the thing it promised and lose
    the month anyway."
  - May 2025: 01.003.000, the Illuminate invasion (05-13), 6 patches that month. 50.8% negative.
  - March to May 2026: 6.1.0 to 6.2.5, Machinery of Oppression, 6 patches at an ordinary pace of one to
    three a month, the same rate as 2025. 52.3%, 62.3%, 56.2% negative. By 2026 the complaint is no longer
    about any one patch.
- **Edits:** many reviews carry several dated updates across years and are counted on one date (2 to 15
  per batch of 50). The re-run counts 299 edited reviews, 18.4%, the highest in the study. This affects
  the 2026 numbers: a review begun in 2024 and rewritten in 2026 counts as 2024.
- **Where it sits among later games:** the cross-game page names this as one of five shapes a complaint
  line can take - here, the relationship with the studio. (Space Marine 2: the price; Remnant II: the
  reviewer; Risk of Rain 2: the owner; Warframe: a flat line.)

### Co-op and online play
- **Friends:** much better with friends, 62 (3.8 per 100). Lower than Back 4 Blood (7.5) and Deep Rock
  Galactic (10.1).
- **Strangers:** fine with strangers, 14; a welcoming community, 33; trolls and griefers, 11; other
  players put me off, 22 (by 2026 mostly about the fight over the studio, 4.3 per 100 that year).
- **Friendly fire is the eighth best thing about the game** (37 praise, 3 against in the whole study).
  The split is not the mechanic or how much it hurts; it is who does it and how often. Praise: you did it
  to your friends, by your own hand, and it was funny. Complaint: strangers did it to you, again and again
  ("teamkilled about 85% of my games"). The page: "Removing friendly fire would delete the game's
  most-loved feature; the lever is who can do it to you and how often." Friendly fire is 2.3 per 100 here,
  0.6 in Deep Rock Galactic, 0.0 in Back 4 Blood. Across the whole study, when two friendly-fire labels were merged
  (2026-09-02), friendly fire held 72 times in one family, 5 of them "friendly fire lets people grief".
- **The mission falling apart is the fun:** 13.
- **Solo:** works solo 15, punishing solo 11. Both sides show up in all three of the first games.
- **Every role needed:** 0.0 per 100 here (4.6 in Deep Rock Galactic).
- **Bots:** "computer teammates useless" is 0.0 per 100 here (Back 4 Blood 2.6).
- **Hosting:** seen once - enemies go for the player who hosts, so hosting costs that player.
- **Disconnects:** 14.
- **Account:** a required account or platform link, 15 (13 in May 2024).
- **The shared war:** a running story players follow (20) and the shared war counting my play (8) - a
  co-op game where every squad's missions feed one total that the whole player base sees.
- The pages do not cover joining a game already running, rejoining, scaling, matchmaking, crossplay or
  loot sharing.

### Combat, movement and feel
- **Combat hits hard:** 22 (1.4 per 100). Lower than Back 4 Blood's 5.8.
- **Weakening weapons:** the March 2024 patch notes say a patch "nerfs its best weapons"; "the studio
  weakened things players liked" is 39 and rises to 7.3 per 100 by 2026. An ammo change in April 2024
  ("massive changes to ammo").
- **Shots stopped by scenery:** seen once here.
- **Too many buttons to manage:** seen once, used in no other game.
- **Silly actions with no use** (emotes and the like): 6, far below Deep Rock Galactic.
- The pages say nothing else about movement, controls, camera or stuns.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- **Hard in a good way:** 16. **The difficulty ladder is well built:** 14.
- A March 2024 patch "nerfs hardest enemy and spawn rates" and another "adds new hazards" (patch notes).
- New enemy factions arrive as content: the Illuminate invasion in May 2025 (50.8% negative that month).
- Enemies going for the host: seen once.
- The pages name no bosses and no unfair attacks.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- **Builds:** deep and varied, 16; barely change anything, 11 (10 on the re-run). Both sides of one design
  choice. "Deep and varied" peaked at 2.0 per 100 in 2025 and was 0.0 in 2026.
- **Grind:** 11 (0.7 per 100). The same low rate in all three of the first games (0.9, 0.7, 0.7).
- **Paid currency earnable by playing:** 20 praise.
- The pages say nothing about loot drops, luck or time gates.

### Runs, content and replay value
- **Keeps pulling you back:** 47, steady all three years.
- **Repeats itself:** 22 (1.4 per 100). This rate is nearly the same in all three of the first games (1.8,
  1.4, 1.4) - the tightest match in the whole study. In 2026 it was 0.0 here.
- **Updates add little:** 10. **A steady stream of updates:** 14.
- **Nothing else plays like it:** 10. **The mission falling apart is the fun:** 13.
- **The war as content:** a running story players follow, 20; the shared war counts my play, 8.
- Content updates did not protect a month: Escalation of Freedom (August 2024) and the Illuminate invasion
  (May 2025) both sit in the worst months.

### Money and price
- **Fair price:** 25 (1.5 per 100). **Paid currency can be earned by playing:** 20.
- **Selling more while the game is broken:** 11 - and 3.4 per 100 in 2026, up from 0.3 and 0.0.
- **Buy on sale only:** 0.0 per 100 (Back 4 Blood 8.6).
- **Complaints about money practices** overall: 1.8 per 100 reviews (cross-game page; Warframe 2.8, The
  First Descendant 14.6).
- **Free updates after launch:** 0.1 per 100 say so (Deep Rock Galactic 1.5).
- **Region lock:** 39 "not sold in my country" (30 in May 2024) - a publisher decision, not a price.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Engineering is 9.3% of everything said - three times Deep Rock Galactic's share and more than twice
  Back 4 Blood's - and 21.7% of all complaints, more than game design.
- **Crashes again and again:** 37, falling 2.5 to 1.7 per 100 from 2024 to 2026. Patch 01.000.202 on
  2024-04-09 "finally fixes those many crashes" (patch notes).
- **A bug breaks the mission or loses progress:** 28 (26 on the re-run), 3.0 per 100 in 2026.
  **Buggy:** 19. **Known bugs never fixed:** 11.
- **Damages the computer or system:** 12.
- **Needs a stronger computer:** 10. **Stopped working on my setup:** 10 (both re-run counts, new).
- **Unwanted extra software** (anti-cheat, a launcher or a companion app - the page does not say which): 12.
- **Disconnects:** 14.
- **Required account or platform:** 15.
- An Emergency Hot Fix followed the August 2024 content patch (patch notes).
- The engine is not named on the pages.

### The studio, updates and community
- **The whole finding:** "The game got better and the relationship got worse." A live-service game "is not
  judged on its build quality after the first year. It is judged on whether the studio is felt to be on the
  player's side."
- **Listens and acts** was the second biggest praise (78), yet the bad studio labels add up to 51 (misreads
  19, fights its players 17, ignores feedback 15) plus punishes criticism (7, mostly 2026).
- **Weakening favourite things** (39) and **patches that made it worse** (47) are the top live-ops
  complaints; **fixed what mattered** (26) and **made it better** (20) the praise.
- **Trust:** the studio earned my trust 14; lost my trust 16 (4.7 per 100 in 2026).
- **The studio in the story:** the studio answered in character, in the game's voice (2); the studio stood
  with us against the owner (2); the studio stopped punishing criticism (seen once).
- **The publisher:** the owner puts players off 45; not sold in my country 39; protest vote 21; account
  required 15 - one dated event in May 2024. The page notes that publisher reversals were filed as the
  studio listening, and whether to split studio from publisher is an open question with Rico. One review
  (225615617) carries two "listens and acts" notes about two different actors.
- **The community:** the war cry (398), the identity players take on (54), the satire (53), a welcoming
  community (33), the fiction organised something real (5), the community completes the game (seen once).
  Community is 28.5% of everything said - the biggest part - and game design only 18.6%. All talk of the
  players' own culture is 29.7 per 100 here, 35.7 in Deep Rock Galactic and 0.0 in Back 4 Blood; the
  cross-game page calls this "the culture line" between the games that lasted and the one that did not.
- **The argument took over:** other players put me off (22, from both sides); judged unfairly (21, the most
  common opinion in the last batch); three reviewers in one 2026 batch read the same community three
  incompatible ways; "the fights drove me away".
- **Updates:** eleven patches in February 2024; one to three a month by 2026.
- **A yardstick for others:** later games are measured against it - in Earth Defense Force 5, no review
  names Helldivers before 2024 and 36 do after (cross-game section 23).

### What players asked for
The pages give no list of requests for this game. What the complaints ask for, without new counts: stop
weakening favourite weapons and enemies' challenge (39); sell the game in every country and drop the
required account (39 and 15); fix crashes and game-breaking bugs (37 and 28); more variety (22); do not
sell more while it is broken (11); drop the unwanted extra software (12); protect players from
strangers who teamkill (3 in the whole study, and trolls and griefers 11); run on more machines and keep
working after updates (10 and 10).

### Only in this game
The 2026-10-03 re-run counts 344 labels used here, 13 of them in no other game:
- A running story players follow - 20.
- The shared war counts my play - 8.
- The game's fiction organised something real - 5.
- The studio stood with us against the owner - 2.
- The studio answered in character - 2.
- Seen once each: enemies go for the host; too many buttons to manage; the community completes the
  game; the studio stopped punishing criticism; good material for a video; a note on which languages it
  comes in (unexplained); a note on running the game after launch (unexplained); and one personal note
  left out here.
What the cross-game page shows as almost only here, among the first three games:
- The publisher puts players off - 2.8 per 100 (Back 4 Blood 0.5, Deep Rock Galactic 0.0).
- Not sold in my country - 2.4 per 100 (0.0 in the other two).
- Friendly fire makes stories - 2.3 per 100 (Deep Rock Galactic 0.6, Back 4 Blood 0.0).
- The thumbs down as a protest vote (21), and "the fights drove me away" - both built from this game.
- The 30-fold swing in how players see the studio in one year.

### For Dominion - our reading

1. **Lesson:** Do not weaken what players love; it becomes the complaint that defines the game.
   **Evidence:** "the studio weakened things players liked" 39 times, rising from 1.6 to 7.3 per 100 by 2026;
   the March 2024 patches that "nerf its best weapons" and "nerf hardest enemy" came in the month the
   negative share nearly doubled (14.1% to 25.1%); "a patch made it worse" is the top complaint (47).
   **Dominion could:** balance by raising weak options rather than cutting strong ones; when a cut is
   needed, explain it in the patch note and give something back in the same patch; test balance changes
   with players first. **Strength:** strong.

2. **Lesson:** After the first year, players judge the studio, not the build.
   **Evidence:** crash complaints fell (2.5 to 1.7 per 100) while thumbs down tripled (11.8% to 36.2%) and
   good-to-bad talk about the studio swung from 3.3 : 1 to 1 : 9. "The studio listens and acts" is still
   the second biggest praise (78). In 2026 "it repeats itself" fell to 0.0 while lost trust (4.7 per 100),
   ignores feedback (4.7) and fights its own players (4.3) rose from near zero. **Dominion could:** keep a
   visible loop - "you said, we did" in patch notes; never argue with players in public; admit mistakes
   fast. **Strength:** strong.

3. **Lesson:** Friendly fire is loved when you do it to friends and hated when strangers do it to you.
   **Evidence:** 37 praise it, 3 complain in the whole study; the praise is self-inflicted and funny, the
   complaints are strangers teamkilling "about 85% of my games"; trolls and griefers 11; "the mission
   falling apart is the fun" 13. **Dominion could:** keep friendly fire as a feature; give the host a
   setting for it; soften or limit it in public matchmaking; let the squad vote out a griefer; make funny
   friendly kills easy to share. **Strength:** medium-strong.

4. **Lesson:** A required outside account or a region lock can cause the biggest review wave the game ever
   gets.
   **Evidence:** May 2024 had 309,103 English reviews, twice launch; 30 of 45 publisher complaints, 30 of 39
   "not sold in my country", 14 of 21 protest votes and 13 of 15 account complaints landed that month.
   **Dominion could:** use only Steam login; if an outside account is ever needed, make it optional and
   decide it before launch, never after; sell everywhere Steam sells. **Strength:** strong (one event, huge
   volume).

5. **Lesson:** Crashes and game-breaking bugs lead the engineering complaints, and some bugs hurt the
   player's computer.
   **Evidence:** crashes 37, bugs that end a mission or lose progress 28, buggy 19, damages the system 12,
   known bugs never fixed 11; engineering is 21.7% of all complaints. Game-breaking bugs came back in
   2026 (1.6, 0.3, 3.0 per 100). **Dominion could:** on a listen server a host crash ends the run for all
   four, so save each player's run rewards often and let the squad recover after a host drop; keep a
   public known-bugs list and close it out. **Strength:** strong.

6. **Lesson:** Unwanted extra software (anti-cheat, a launcher, a companion app) is a complaint of its own.
   **Evidence:** 12 times. **Dominion could:** a PvE co-op game may not need deep anti-cheat; if it uses
   any, say what and why on the store page. **Strength:** medium.

7. **Lesson:** A slogan and an identity give players a voice, and it spreads.
   **Evidence:** the war cry 398 times (one review in four); players take on the game's identity 54; the
   satire lands 53; two reviewers wrote another game's chant by mistake. It faded from 26.3 to 16.3 per
   100 as the relationship soured. The cross-game page ties having this culture to the games that lasted
   (29.7 per 100 here, 35.7 in Deep Rock Galactic, 0.0 in Back 4 Blood). **Dominion could:** give the
   player's side a short war cry and a name players can call themselves, and put both in voice lines,
   emotes and the end-of-run screen; a tone with some humour helps. **Strength:** medium-strong.

8. **Lesson:** A content update can still lose the month if it lands badly.
   **Evidence:** Escalation of Freedom (August 2024) came with the worst month of the launch year (48.1%
   negative) and needed an Emergency Hot Fix; the Illuminate invasion month was 50.8% negative.
   **Dominion could:** test new content with a small group before release; do not pair new content with
   big balance cuts; have a hotfix plan ready. **Strength:** medium (the months are thinly read).

9. **Lesson:** Fast patching at launch helps.
   **Evidence:** eleven versioned patches in February 2024, the least negative month of the launch period
   (14.1%). **Dominion could:** plan a launch team that can ship fixes every few days in the first month.
   **Strength:** medium-weak (the months line up; cause is not proven).

10. **Lesson:** Play with friends is the core, and strangers need to be safe too.
    **Evidence:** much better with friends 62; fine with strangers 14; welcoming community 33; trolls and
    griefers 11. **Dominion could:** make inviting friends and joining friends' games one click; give public
    lobbies a kick vote and a mute. **Strength:** medium.

11. **Lesson:** Solo play is a real trade-off: some say it works, some say it punishes.
    **Evidence:** works solo 15, punishing solo 11; both sides show up in all three of the first games.
    **Dominion could:** decide on purpose how a solo run scales (fewer enemies, or help), and say so in
    game. **Strength:** medium.

12. **Lesson:** "It repeats itself" shows up at the same rate in every co-op game.
    **Evidence:** 22 here (1.4 per 100); 1.8, 1.4, 1.4 across the first three games. **Dominion could:**
    plan mission and arena variety from the start, mixing objectives, places and enemy sets. **Strength:**
    medium-strong (across games).

13. **Lesson:** A fair price and a currency you can earn keep money complaints low.
    **Evidence:** fair price 25; paid currency earnable by playing 20; money-practice complaints only 1.8
    per 100; "buy on sale only" 0.0. But "selling more while broken" 11, and it jumped to 3.4 per 100 in
    2026. **Dominion could:** let any paid currency be earned in play; do not launch a new paid item in a
    week when a big bug is still open. **Strength:** medium.

14. **Lesson:** Builds must feel different, or players say they do not matter.
    **Evidence:** deep and varied 16; barely change anything 11; grindy 11. **Dominion could:** make every
    loadout choice change how a fight goes, not just a number. **Strength:** weak-medium.

15. **Lesson:** Fights among players about the studio can drive people away.
    **Evidence:** other players put me off 22, from both sides by 2026; "the fights drove me away" built
    from "there is more drama with this game than anything else I do in my life"; the doxxing story told by
    four reviewers (hearsay). **Dominion could:** keep official spaces moderated and calm; never single out
    a player in public. **Strength:** weak-medium.

16. **Lesson:** Marketing claims get checked.
    **Evidence:** "a marketing claim was untrue" 10. **Dominion could:** promise only what is in the build.
    **Strength:** weak.

17. **Lesson:** Updates that add little are noticed, and a steady stream is praised.
    **Evidence:** updates add little 10; steady stream 14; "patches fixed what mattered" 26, "made it better"
    20. **Dominion could:** make each update carry one thing players can feel. **Strength:** weak.

18. **Lesson:** The angriest reviewers are the most invested players - read them as the core audience,
    not as passers-by.
    **Evidence:** the middle thumbs-down reviewer has played 73 hours; the middle thumbs-up reviewer 40.
    464 of 1,622 kept reviews show 100 hours or more. The complaints that rise in 2026 are about the studio,
    not a broken game. **Dominion could:** weight feedback from long-time players heavily, and check
    every balance or business change against "what does this do to someone with 100 hours?".
    **Strength:** medium-strong (a whole-sample median, but one game).

19. **Lesson:** A shared, running war that counts each squad's missions gives players something to follow
    and talk about.
    **Evidence:** "a running story players follow" 20 and "the shared war counts my play" 8 - both used in
    no other game; the game's fiction organised something real among players 5; the studio answered in
    character 2. The tree marks these as neither praise nor complaint: the same thing keeps one player
    checking in and makes another feel behind. **Dominion could:** consider a shared front where every
    run's result adds to one total all players see, with story beats that move over weeks; make sure a
    player's own runs visibly count; speak to players in the world's voice now and then. **Strength:**
    medium (only this game shows it, but 20 is a solid count at this sample size).

20. **Lesson:** On a listen server, hosting must not cost the host.
    **Evidence:** seen once, and used in no other game: enemies go for the player who hosts, so hosting
    costs that player. **Dominion could:** check that enemy targeting and spawning do not favour the host
    in a listen-server game. **Strength:** weak (one review), but cheap to test.

21. **Lesson:** Keep the game running on the machines it ran on at launch.
    **Evidence:** "needs a stronger computer than I have" 10 and "stopped working on my setup" 10 (both
    new in the 2026-10-03 re-run's top 25); damages the system 12. **Dominion could:** publish honest
    minimum specs; test each patch on the minimum machine as well as the target one. **Strength:**
    medium-weak.

### Limits
- 0.20% of the English reviews - the thinnest sample in the study, five times thinner than Deep Rock
  Galactic's. Month weights reach 579 to 1; one bullet in a quiet month can move a weighted share by a full
  point. Weighted shares are direction, not measurement. A label with 10 bullets is 10 people out of
  819,840. The counts in this entry are not weighted.
- 2026 rests on about 232 reviews for a whole year; single months read 19 to 510 reviews.
- English only. Four other language groups (184,243 reviews: Simplified Chinese 116,313, Russian 30,779,
  European Spanish 29,201, LatAm Spanish 7,950) were pulled and not read - 113 times what was read here.
  The country-availability complaint is exactly where they might read differently. Reviews written in
  another language under the English label were not counted.
- Many reviews were rewritten across years and counted on one date (299 reviews, 18.4%, on the re-run);
  this affects the 2026 figures. Open with Rico.
- Publisher actions were filed under "the studio"; whether to split them is open with Rico.
- The doxxing story is hearsay from four reviewers. The account requirement, the country count and the
  extra software are also what reviewers say; only the patch dates and titles come from a primary source
  (Steam news).
- Reviews do not measure how many people play; the worst 2026 months also have few reviews.
- **Two counts (new since the old entry).** The pages' own counts and a 2026-10-03 re-run differ by a
  few: 2,901 things said against 2,908 on the re-run (and 2,905 on the cross-game table); 1,626 reviews
