  against 1,622 kept; 504 too vague to file (17%; 17.4% on the master page) against 493 (17.0%, 366 of
  them a bare thumbs up); and a handful of labels by one to three (weakened favourites 37, bug breaks play
  26, misreads 18, builds barely matter 10, listens and acts 75, better with friends 61). This entry gives
  the page's count first and the re-run beside it. The re-run's per-year figures leave out lines about
  the review itself.
- Small mismatches between pages: the year table gives 1,093 / 297 / 232 reviews on one page and 1,096 /
  297 / 233 on the other; the crash fall is called 32% on one page and 30% on the other; the
  first-three-games table on the cross-game page gives 107.7 praise and 55.2 complaints per 100 on an
  earlier count; the recomputed figures are 108.0 and 55.5 (1.95 : 1, above Space Marine 2's 1.87 : 1 and
  below Warframe's 2.5 : 1).
- The cross-game page says its friendly-fire rates for the first three games were taken before two
  labels were merged and so are a floor, not a total; this game's own count barely moves (one bullet).
- The pages are tags, not quotes; few direct quotes are given.

---

## Deep Rock Galactic: Rogue Core

**Read:** 814 of 7,990 English reviews (10.2%), ±3.26%, written 2026-05 to 2026-09 (September only
part of the month). **70.3% thumbs up** in the sample (Steam, all languages: 60.1%, Mixed, on 15,210
reviews).
**Pages:** `findings/drg-rogue-core-english.md`, `findings/drg-rogue-core.md`, `findings/cross-game.md`
section 9 (also the table at the top of the page, and short mentions in sections 10, 23, 24 and 25).

**What it is:** A spin-off of Deep Rock Galactic, by the same studio, in the same world, played by the
same fans. A co-op shooter built on runs: a team goes in, picks up upgrades from a pool the whole team
shares, and a timer inside the run decides when the run ends. Paid game (one reviewer complains about
small paid extras "in a paid game"). In Early Access since 2026-05-20; every review read was written
inside Early Access, in its first five months. The camera is not stated on the pages or in the Steam store's short description. The pages do not say who hosts a
game, how many play at once, or the engine (the store was not asked for this rebuild). Reviewers do
describe how online games run: some say games are peer to peer rather than on dedicated servers, and
that the host leaving ends everyone's run (2 reviews each; see Co-op).

**How close to Dominion:** Close on the core loop: a co-op team, sci-fi setting, runs with upgrades
picked up along the way, and a clock that pushes the team out. It is the nearest thing in the study to
"a loved co-op game makes a run-based follow-up", which is a useful mirror for Dominion's runs. It is
not known from the pages to be third person, to be hosted on one player's machine, or to have an
extraction or arena mode. The study calls it "the controlled experiment": same studio, same world,
same players as Deep Rock Galactic, with only the game changed.

### The short version (plain words)
The same studio, the same world and the same fans gave the parent game 97.1% thumbs up and this game
70.3%. Half of everything said is about how the game is designed (1,321 of 2,643 points made), and only
3.2% is about crashes, lag or speed: players are not saying it is broken, they are saying they disagree
with it. The thing named most is the run timer (112 times), and most people who mention it still
recommend the game (69 of the 112). The score fell from 74.3% thumbs up in June to 50.0% in September
after a July hotfix, and what players wrote about patches turned sour a month before the votes did.
On the 2026-10-03 re-count, reviews carry 117.2 praises and 193.2 complaints per 100 reviews read, 0.61
praise for each complaint (the older cross-game table has 116.8, 194.2 and 0.60): below Back 4 Blood
(0.86) and Aliens: Fireteam Elite (0.88), far below the parent game (7.27 on the older table, 7.43 on
the re-count). The game gets argued about in more detail than its parent: 3.25 points per review
against 2.11.

### What players praised, most to least
1. **They defended the game against its own review page.** Said 83 times, the third most-said thing in
   the game. About one review in ten argues with the other reviews instead of describing the game.
   *"I really struggle to understand the majority of the reviews for this game."* (226862716);
   *"I think you should be required to take a written test before leaving a review for this game."*
   (227460186). Cross-game: 10.2 per 100 reviews read here, against 7.1 in Back 4 Blood, 1.3 in
   Helldivers 2 and 0.2 in Deep Rock Galactic.
2. **They used the parent game's catchphrase and salute.** Said 61 times (7.5 per 100 reviews read).
   The parent game's players do it 28.6 times per 100, so the ritual carried over at about a quarter of
   its strength (the page says 26%).
3. **Liked the timer** (see complaints, item 1): 69 of the 112 timer mentions are on thumbs-up reviews.
   *"I also like the timer, because it actually put pressure on me"* (226877922).
4. **Worth buying even unfinished.** Said 38 times: sold openly as Early Access and already worth the
   money. It fell from 5.8 per 100 at launch to 3.2 in June and 2.6 after July.
5. **Hard in a good way.** Said 34 times.
6. **Shooting hits with weight and feedback.** Said 34 times.
7. **Much better with friends.** Said 34 times.
8. **Builds are deep and varied.** Said 32 times (against 79 saying the upgrades barely change
   anything - see complaints).
9. **The game needs the team to work together, and that is good.** 32 times. (The old page listed this
   as a complaint; the rebuilt page files it as praise. See Limits.)
10. **Patches made things better.** Said 31 times that a change improved something (3.8 per 100 reviews,
   the highest of the first four games; Back 4 Blood 0.0, Deep Rock Galactic 1.6, Helldivers 2 1.2),
   and 30 times that a patch fixed a real complaint (3.7 per 100, also the highest; Back 4 Blood 3.0,
   Deep Rock Galactic 0.3, Helldivers 2 1.6). Both peaked in June: made it better 2.7, 6.4 then 3.5 per
   100; fixed what mattered 2.3, 6.9 then 3.5.
11. **The studio has earned their trust.** 26 times.
12. **Each class plays its own way.** 26 times.
13. **The studio listens and acts.** 25 times, 3.1 per 100 reviews read (Helldivers 2 is 4.7, Deep Rock
    Galactic 1.8, Back 4 Blood 0.4). By period: 2.1 at launch, 6.0 in June, 1.8 after July.
14. **Fighting teammates for the upgrades is the fun.** 23 times: 4.6 per 100 at launch, 0.5 in June
    (after Update 1 loosened the shared pool), 0.0 after. See Co-op.
15. **Plenty to do.** 18 times.
16. **Does not feel unfinished.** 15 times.
17. **The studio says what it is doing.** 13 times.
18. **Fair price.** 12 times.
19. **Unlike anything else.** 11 times. **Better than its rivals.** 11 times.
20. **The randomness keeps runs fresh.** 10 times. **Teamwork pays off.** 10 times.
21. **Keeps pulling you back.** 9 times. **Upgrades change how you play.** 9 times.
22. **People bought it to support the studio.** 3 of the 4 points about how the game makes money.
23. **Lines players quote back.** 0.5 reviews per 100 (cross-game section 23; Earth Defense Force 5
    leads at 7.6, every other large game is at 0.6 or less).
24. **Colour-blind support works.** 1 review (a point no other game has).
25. "Good game" with nothing else: 99 times (12.2 per 100). It counts as a thumbs up but carries no
    detail.

### What players complained about, most to least
The pages give the thumbs split only for the timer. Overall, each thumbs-up review carries 1.17
complaints (and 1.53 praises) - people who recommend it still list faults (Immortal: Unchained page).
Counts are the page's of 2026-09-02; where the 2026-10-03 re-count differs, both are given.
1. **A timer inside the run decides when you leave.** 112 times, the most-said thing in the game (109 on
   the re-count, after 3 were moved to a praise heading, "the clock is the thrill"). This
   is a split, not a pure complaint: 69 on thumbs-up reviews, 43 on thumbs-down reviews. 61.6% of the
   people who raise it recommend the game, and that share stayed between 60.7% and 62.7% across ten
   batches, even while the thumbs-up rate fell 24 points. *"The timer makes this game impossible
   especially if solo"* (227471291). The other games in the first four have zero on this. By period it
   fell: 16.8 per 100 at launch, 11.0 in June, 3.5 after July.
2. **Upgrades barely change anything.** 79 times. It rose after July: 10.2, 7.3, then 12.3 per 100.
3. **Luck decides whether you win, not how you played.** 53 times. Cross-game, complaints about
   randomness reach 6.6 reviews per 100 here, second only to The First Descendant (7.2) in the corpus.
4. **The store page and trailer promised a different kind of game.** 51 times (the old page marked it
   as mixed in tone; the rebuilt page files it as a complaint).
5. **Teammates can take the upgrades you wanted.** The shared upgrade pool is the most-complained-about
   system: 47 times that teammates take your things, and 15 times that the rules set teammates against
   each other - 62 in all. 5.8 per 100 reviews here; 0.2 in Back 4 Blood, zero in Deep Rock Galactic
   and Helldivers 2. By period it fell with Update 1: 8.1, 2.8, then 1.8 per 100.
6. **Too little content for the price.** 44 times (42 on the re-count).
7. **Ammo, health or supplies run out too fast.** 43 times.
8. **Another game does it better** (mostly the parent game). 38 times.
9. **Whole kinds of choices are never worth taking.** 38 times.
10. **Repetitive.** 35 times. It rose after July: 4.4, 3.2, then 6.1 per 100.
11. **Not worth buying yet - come back later.** 30 times.
12. **Not finished enough to recommend, no specific fault named.** 28 times (27 on the re-count). It
    rose: 1.5, 5.5, then 7.0 per 100.
13. **It could have been more and settled for less.** 27 times.
14. **Too hard or punishing to play alone.** 24 times.
15. **Too hard.** 21 times.
16. **Some enemies cannot be fought back against.** 21 times, rising from 1.7 per 100 at launch to 2.8
    in June and 6.1 after July.
17. **One player can hold the whole team up.** 20 times.
18. **Worse with strangers.** 19 times.
19. **Costs too much for what it is.** 18 times.
20. **Buggy.** 17 times. **Jobs inside the run feel like chores.** 17 times.
21. **Reuses parts from the parent game.** 16 times. **No breathing room.** 16 times.
22. **Moving feels slow and heavy.** 11 reviews (from cross-game section 24: it is the commonest
    movement complaint after Gunfire Reborn's 15).
23. **The studio ignores feedback.** 15 times, 1.8 per 100 reviews read (the highest of the first four
    games).
24. **Updates to the parent game slowed while this one was made.** Named from this side too, 8 times:
    *"updates for DRG came to an almost complete stop for this, it doesn't feel like it was worth the
    wait."* (227898874, 100 people marked it helpful); *"they literally pulled drg out back and had it
    shot in order to make this game"* (228545314).
25. **Politics in the cast or world put them off.** 9 times in all (4 about the characters' writing, 3
    about the world, 2 about how the characters look) - 0.4% of everything said. Loud inside a single
    review, tiny in the counts.
26. **The class decides your character's gender.** Seen in this game (ZCREW's page counts 4 sightings
    across ZCREW and this game, no split).
27. **Small, one-review complaints only this game has:** the discount for owning the parent game is
    too small; the game gives no credit for time spent in the parent game; it is sold as finished and
    unfinished at the same time; the owner says the game is failing. 1 review each. "No shared
    identity" among players: 2.
28. "Bad game" with nothing else: 26 times.

### How it changed over time
| Month written | Thumbs up | Thumbs down | % up | Patch points: good / bad |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-05 launch | 343 | 139 | 71.2% | 24 / 5 |
| 2026-06 Update 1 | 162 | 56 | 74.3% | 29 / 5 |
| 2026-07 hotfix 01.02 | 40 | 23 | 63.5% | 4 / 4 |
| 2026-08 | 21 | 18 | 53.8% | 2 / 2 |
| 2026-09 (part month) | 6 | 6 | 50.0% | 2 / 2 |

Praise and complaints per 100 reviews, by period (re-count of 2026-10-03):

| Period | Reviews read | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Launch (2026-05) | 482 | 71.2% | 110.8 | 197.3 |
| June, Update 1 | 218 | 74.3% | 109.6 | 159.6 |
| July to September | 114 | 58.8% | 71.9 | 214.9 |

- Best month June (74.3%), worst September (50.0%): a 24-point fall in three months.
- The 70.3% overall is mostly the launch month, which is 59% of the sample (and 75% of all English
  reviews).
- Praise fell from 110.8 and 109.6 per 100 to 71.9 after July. Complaints dipped in June (159.6) and then rose
  to 214.9, the highest of the three periods.
- **What fell:** the run timer, teammates taking your upgrades, "fighting for upgrades is the fun" and
  "worth it while unfinished" (numbers in the lists above).
- **What rose:** shallow upgrades, "needs more work", enemies with no counterplay, and repetitive.
- What players wrote about patches went from 4.8 good for every bad at launch, to 5.8 to 1 in June, to
  1 to 1 in July. The thumbs-up rate turned in July and kept falling. The words moved a month before the
  votes. Players tie the turn to the July hotfix: *"Patch RC: EA: 01.02 broke the game. No one bothered
  to actually playtest their patch... Their first big patch was a great one, but they decided to
  absolutely decimate the game with a 'hotfix'."* (229967213). The cross-game page calls this the only
  patch signal in the corpus (at that point) that reversed inside one group.
- Update 1 (June) loosened the shared upgrade pool. Complaints about co-op design fell from 68% to 54%
  of what was said about co-op across the read, because of when reviews were written. Two reviewers then
  gave thumbs down because of the fix (see Co-op).

### Co-op and online play
- **The shared upgrade pool is the biggest co-op fight.** 62 complaints: teammates grab what you wanted
  (47) and the rules set teammates against each other (15).
- **But the friction had fans.** 23 reviews say fighting teammates for the upgrades *is* the fun (4.6
  per 100 at launch, 0.5 in June, 0.0 after). After Update 1 loosened it, two reviewers gave thumbs
  down over the fix: *"the devs bowed to people who play with randoms and took out all the teamwork and
  strategizing so now you just play by yourself with other people"* (228588560); *"they turned a fun
  team game into a solo game you can play with friends"* (228588082). One request asks to keep the
  shared upgrades: *"negotiating them is charming"*.
- **Players proposed a fix:** replace a taken upgrade so the next player keeps the same number of
  options, and other changes to the shared pool - 6 requests.
- **Better with friends:** 34 times. **Worse with strangers:** 19.
- **The game needs the team to work together:** 32 times (now filed as praise). **Teamwork pays off:**
  10.
- **One player can hold the whole team up:** 20 times.
- **Alone:** too hard or punishing alone, 24 times. *"The timer makes this game impossible especially if
  solo"* (227471291).
- **Hosting and dropping** (a tag count over the summaries): frequent disconnects 5; peer to peer, not
  dedicated servers 2; the host leaving ends everyone's run 2; cannot connect 2; high lag 2; a
  disconnect loses the run 1; the host can remove you at will 1.
- **Joining a game already running:** a late joiner is outmatched 1; you cannot choose what you get
  when joining late 1.
- **Talking:** cannot communicate with teammates 5.
- **Bots:** make solo possible 3, play the game for you 3, get stuck 2, cannot be given orders 1, harm
  you 1.
- **Friendly fire:** just a cost 5, makes stories 2, lets people grief 2.
- **Servers, lag and netcode:** inside the engineering share, which is only 85 points (3.2%) in all.
- Matchmaking and crossplay: nothing said.

### Combat, movement and feel
- Shooting hits with weight: 34 times.
- Moving feels slow: 11 reviews (cross-game section 24). Missing modern moves: 5; one reviewer asks for
  the parent game's grappling hook back. Movement unreliable: 2.
- Ammo, health or supplies run out too fast: 43 times.
- Abilities awkward to trigger: 4. Aim response inconsistent: 1. Stuns take control away: 1.
- Controls: unresponsive 3, expected key bindings missing 3, cannot rebind keys 1. Controller support:
  nothing said.
- Melee, camera and reading enemy attacks: nothing on the pages.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- Hard in a good way: 34 times. Too hard: 21. No breathing room: 16.
- The player is too fragile: 9. Difficulty badly scaled: 9. Too easy: 5. A harder setting is not worth
  it: 3. Well graded: 1.
- **Enemies with no counterplay:** 21, and rising (1.7 per 100 at launch, 6.1 after July).
- Bosses are a chore: 7.
- The timer makes it too hard alone (quote above).
- Supplies too scarce: 43.
- Enemy variety: nothing said.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- **Upgrades barely change anything:** 79 times - the second most-said complaint.
- **Builds are deep and varied:** 32 times, and 9 say upgrades change how you play. The same system
  gets both, and the complaint is more than twice as common.
- **Luck decides the outcome:** 53 times. **Randomness keeps it fresh:** 10.
- **Some whole kinds of choices are useless:** 38 times.
- **Each class plays its own way:** 26.
- **Shared upgrades can be taken by teammates:** 47 times.
- **What carries over between runs** (tag count): unlocks are grindy 9, unlocks are satisfying 7,
  nothing builds up between runs 5, nothing left to chase 4, a slow start 4, locked behind farming 3,
  progress does not carry over 1. One reviewer says the game gives no credit for time in the parent
  game.
- The class decides the character's gender: a small complaint shared with ZCREW (4 sightings across both
  games).

### Runs, content and replay value
- **The run timer** is the most-said thing (112). See complaints, item 1, for the split.
- A later study of Risk of Rain 2 moved 3 Rogue Core reviews to a new praise, "the clock is the
  thrill", after this page was written - more people praise the timer than the 69 above show.
- **Too little content for the price:** 44 (42 on the re-count). **Repetitive:** 35. **Settled for less
  than it could be:** 27. **Plenty of content:** 18.
- **Keeps pulling you back:** 9. **Jobs inside the run feel like chores:** 17. **Reuses parts from the
  parent game:** 16. **Cannot save a run and come back to it:** 1.
- **Hours on the review:** median 12; 20 show 100 or more, none show 1,000 or more; 11 show zero. The
  median is 9.5 for thumbs down and 14 for thumbs up.
- Nothing on run length or level generation on the pages.

### Money and price
- Very little talk about how the game makes money: 4 points in all. 3 are people who bought it to
  support the studio; 1 complains about small paid extras in a paid game.
- **Early Access value is split:** 38 say it is worth it unfinished, 30 say wait, 15 say it does not
  feel unfinished.
- **Too little content for the price:** 44. **Price too high:** 18. **Fair:** 12.
- One reviewer says the discount for owning the parent game is too small.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Only 85 of 2,643 points (3.2%) are about crashes, speed or netcode. The page says plainly: "This game
  is not being rejected for being broken."
- Stability: 9 points in 814 reviews, 4 of them repeated crashes. Buggy: 17.
- Frame rate: unstable 10, stutter 3, needs strong hardware 3; runs well 5, runs on a modest computer 4.
- Loading and saves: long loads 1, progress not saved 1.
- The one tech story is a patch: the July hotfix 01.02 is blamed for breaking the game (229967213).
- Language and translation: 1 point. Accessibility: 2 points (one says colour-blind support works).

### The studio, updates and community
- **Update 1 (June) was loved;** patch talk ran 5.8 good to 1 bad.
- **Hotfix 01.02 (July) turned it.** Patch talk went to 1 to 1, and the thumbs-up rate fell from there.
- **The studio listens and acts:** 3.1 per 100 reviews (25). **Ignores feedback:** 1.8 per 100 (15).
  **Says what it is doing:** 13. **Has earned their trust:** 26.
- **Players praise the studio's response and still reject the game.** The page treats these as two
  separate things: this game leads the first four games in patch praise while getting the worst scores
  of them.
- **The parent game was starved for this one:** see complaints, item 24 (8 times).
- **Defending the game against other reviews:** 83 times.
- **The parent's culture came along weaker:** the shared catchphrase and salute, 7.5 per 100 here
  against 28.6 in the parent (Helldivers 2 24.5, Back 4 Blood 0.0). The cross-game page calls this
  "the cleanest measurement in the corpus of something a studio cannot copy across".
- Community talk is 9.2% of everything said (243 points), close to Back 4 Blood (9.4%) and far below
  Deep Rock Galactic and Helldivers 2 (about 28.5% each). Cross-game section 10 notes the games with
  little community talk are the ones that did worse.
- Two reviews say players have no shared identity; one says the owner has said the game is failing.

### What players asked for
A word search of the review summaries finds 74 points worded as a request. By subject: more content 10;
upgrades that matter 7; replace a taken upgrade so the next player keeps the same number of options, and
other changes to the shared pool, 6; a neutral review option on Steam 4; a change to the run timer so
side content is worth doing 4; less luck 4; a way to change how your character looks 4; less repetition
3; shorter sessions 3; missing comforts in the menus 2; information the game hides 2; no forced team
make-up 2; better explanations for new players 2; more to mess about with 2; and 19 single requests,
among them keeping the shared upgrades (*"negotiating them is charming"*) and bringing back the parent's
grappling hook. Two reviewers asked for the shared-pool friction back after it was loosened. Also: 30
reviews say "come back later" (not ready yet) and 44 say there is too little content.

### Only in this game
- **The in-run timer** (13.8 per 100 reviews read; zero in Back 4 Blood, Deep Rock Galactic and
  Helldivers 2).
- **Teammates taking your upgrades** (5.8 per 100; at most 0.2 in the other three).
- **One in ten reviews defending the game against its reviews** (10.2 per 100; only Back 4 Blood is
  near, at 7.1, and later Redfall argues it 124 to 9).
- **The cleanest test in the study of carrying a culture across:** the catchphrase survived the spin-off
  at about a quarter of its strength.
- **The only patch signal (at that point) that reversed inside one group:** 4.8 to 1, 5.8 to 1, then 1
  to 1.
- **Six points no other game has** (1 or 2 reviews each): players have no shared identity; the discount
  for owning the parent is too small; sold as finished and unfinished at once; no credit for time in the
  parent game; the owner says the game is failing; colour-blind support works.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **A run timer will be the most-argued feature, and that is fine if it is a real choice.**
   Evidence: the timer is 112 mentions, the top item; 69 of them are on thumbs-up reviews (61.6%), and
   the split never moved as the score fell. Dominion could: keep a clock as pressure, but test it alone
   and with four, since the solo complaint is the sharpest one (227471291); think about a softer clock
   for solo or smaller teams; and do not treat "people talk about the timer" as "people hate the
   timer". Strength: strong.
2. **Shared loot that teammates can grab is the biggest co-op fight - and some players love the
   fight.** Evidence: 62 complaints (47 + 15), the most of any system; loosening it in Update 1 drew two
   thumbs-down reviews from people who wanted the friction back. The rebuilt page adds 23 reviews saying
   the contest for upgrades is the fun (almost all at launch). Dominion could: give each player their
   own pick of upgrades by default (protects games with strangers), and offer shared or contested picks
   as a friends-only or optional rule. Strength: strong on the complaint; the friction-lovers were weak
   (2 reviews) and are now medium (23).
3. **Upgrades have to change how you play, not just add a few percent.** Evidence: 79 say upgrades
   barely change anything, 38 say whole kinds of choices are useless, against 32 praising deep builds.
   Dominion could: make each run upgrade change a behaviour (how a gun or ability works) and check that
   every option gets picked. Strength: strong.
4. **Luck must not decide the run.** Evidence: 53 say the draw decides the outcome; randomness
   complaints reach 6.6 reviews per 100, second highest in the corpus. Dominion could: let players
   re-roll, bank, or pick from several upgrade offers, and give a bad-luck floor. Strength: strong.
5. **Do not starve players of ammo, health or supplies.** Evidence: 43 say supplies run out too fast.
   Dominion could: tune resupply to the team size and to the timer, so scarcity is a choice, not a wall.
   Strength: medium.
6. **One rushed patch can undo the goodwill a good patch earned.** Evidence: patch talk 5.8 good to 1
   bad after Update 1, then 1 to 1 after hotfix 01.02; thumbs-up fell from 74.3% to 50.0%; "No one
   bothered to actually playtest their patch" (229967213). Dominion could: playtest every hotfix with a
   full team before release, and be ready to roll back. Strength: strong (clear time line), though the
   later months are small.
7. **Watch what players write, not only the score - the words turn first.** Evidence: patch talk went
   sour in July, the score turned a month later. Dominion could: read new reviews and community posts
   after every patch and act on the tone before the score moves. Strength: medium.
8. **A run-based follow-up to a loved game is judged against that game.** Evidence: 38 say another game
   does it better; 51 say the store framing promised something else; the parent's players are angry it
   was slowed for this one (227898874). Dominion could: describe runs, the timer and the shared rules
   plainly on the store page and in trailers, so no one buys the wrong game. Strength: medium.
9. **Early Access is fine if the price matches what is there.** Evidence: 38 say worth it unfinished,
   30 say wait, 44 say too little content for the price, 28 say not finished enough. Dominion could: if
   it goes into Early Access, say what is in and what is coming, and price for what is in. Strength:
   medium.
10. **A culture cannot be copied in; it has to grow.** Evidence: the parent's catchphrase shows up 28.6
    times per 100 there and 7.5 here. Dominion could: leave room for players to make their own rituals
    (emotes, a squad call-out, a shared line) instead of borrowing one. Strength: medium (one clean
    case).
11. **Players can like the studio and still dislike the game.** Evidence: highest patch praise of the
    first four games, worst scores among them. Dominion could: not read praise for fast fixes as proof
    the design is right. Strength: medium.
12. **Movement must feel quick.** Evidence: 11 reviews say moving feels slow (cross-game section 24).
    Dominion could: make sprint, dodge and climbing feel snappy, since a third-person shooter is judged
    on it. Strength: weak here (stronger across the corpus).
13. **Small, loud complaints are small.** Evidence: the cast and politics complaint is 9 of 2,643 points.
    Dominion could: triage by counts, not by how angry one forum post is. Strength: medium.
14. **Solo has to be tuned on purpose, not just allowed.** Evidence: 24 say it is too hard or punishing
    alone; the sharpest timer complaint is about solo (227471291); 3 say bots make solo possible.
    Dominion could: scale enemy count, supplies and the clock to the number of players, and playtest
    every run type alone. Strength: medium.
15. **One slow or lost player must not stall the other three.** Evidence: 20 say one player can hold
    the whole team up; 19 say it is worse with strangers; 5 say they cannot talk to teammates. Dominion
    could: avoid objectives that need every player at one spot to move on, add a ping-and-marker system
    and quick call-outs, and let the team carry on while one player catches up. Strength: medium.
16. **A host leaving or a dropped player must not end the run.** Evidence (small, but it is Dominion's
    exact setup): the host leaving ends everyone's run 2; peer to peer, not dedicated servers 2;
    frequent disconnects 5; a disconnect loses the run 1; the host can remove you at will 1. Dominion
    could: as a listen-server game, plan host migration or a save-and-resume point, let a dropped player
    rejoin the same run, and think about what a host may do to guests. Strength: weak here (stronger in
    Aliens: Fireteam Elite).
17. **Give a fixed number of choices even when loot is shared.** Evidence: 6 requests to replace a taken
    upgrade so the next player keeps the same number of options. Dominion could: if it ever offers a
    shared pick, refill the slot when a teammate takes one. Strength: weak (a player idea, few voices).
18. **Every enemy needs an answer, and late patches can take it away.** Evidence: 21 say some enemies
    cannot be fought back against, rising from 1.7 to 6.1 per 100 as the game was patched; bosses are a
    chore 7. Dominion could: give each enemy a clear tell and a counter, and re-check counters after
    every balance patch. Strength: medium.
19. **Hard is welcome; no breathing room is not.** Evidence: hard in a good way 34, against too hard
    21, no let-up 16, the player too fragile 9. Dominion could: build quiet moments into each run (a
    safe room, a lull before extraction) and keep pressure in waves. Strength: medium.
20. **Jobs inside a run must feel like play.** Evidence: 17 say tasks inside the run feel like chores.
    Dominion could: make each objective a fight or a choice, not a walk-and-wait. Strength: weak to
    medium.
21. **Reused parts from an older game are noticed.** Evidence: 16 say it reuses the parent's parts.
    Dominion could: give each new mode or biome its own look and enemies. Strength: weak to medium.
22. **Something should build up between runs.** Evidence: nothing builds up between runs 5, unlocks
    grindy 9 against satisfying 7, nothing left to chase 4. Dominion could: give each run a lasting
    reward (an unlock, a cosmetic, a step on a long goal) without making the grind slow. Strength: weak.

### Limits
- 814 reviews, ±3.26% (the plan was 1,298; Steam stopped returning reviews early). The launch month is
  read at 8.0% and every later month at about 16%; launch is 75% of the English group, so launch-month
  figures are the weakest.
- The sample says 70.3% thumbs up; Steam says 60.1%. The page puts the gap down to the launch month being
  under-read.
- English only: 7,220 reviews in other languages are unread.
- Early Access, still changing. Every figure is a snapshot taken 2026-09-02.
- 158 reviews (19.4%) were edited after posting; each is counted in the month it was first written. The
  2026-10-03 re-count finds 79 (9.7%) carrying an edit date; the two counts are not reconciled.
- The later months are small (63, 39 and 12 reviews), so the fall in score rests on few reviews per
  month. Per-period margins are not counted.
- **Changed since the last entry:** "the game only works if the team works together" (32) was listed
  as a complaint; the rebuilt page files it as praise, so it moved to the praise list. "The store
  framing promised a different game" (51) was marked mixed and is now filed as a complaint. "Updates to
  the parent slowed" had no count and now has 8 (a tag count of 2026-10-03).
- Counts moved slightly on the 2026-10-03 re-count: points 2,643 to 2,654; the timer 112 to 109 (3
  moved to "the clock is the thrill"); too little content 44 to 42; needs more work 28 to 27. The old
  figures are kept where the old page used them.
- Many smaller counts (hosting, bots, movement, unlocks, frame rate) come from a tag count over the
  summaries on 2026-10-03, not from the old page. The request list is a word search, so a few hits are
  wishes a reviewer describes rather than asks for.
- Reviewers' claims about what Update 1 and hotfix 01.02 did, the parent game's update slowdown, and
  prices are their own and not checked against patch notes or the store.

---

## Deep Rock Galactic

**Read:** 2,130 of 215,564 English reviews (1.0%; 2,133 were sampled and 3 turned out not to be
reviews of the game; the findings pages give no margin of error), written 2018-02 to 2026-08, 103
months. **97.1% thumbs up** in the sample (Steam's own figure in the cross-game table: 97.1%,
*Overwhelmingly Positive* per `findings/drg-rogue-core.md`; the pages give no all-languages percentage).
**Pages:** `findings/deep-rock-galactic-english.md`, `findings/deep-rock-galactic.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` sections 1 to 9 (section 9 is the Rogue Core spin-off measured against this
game; sections 20 to 25 recount it beside the later games). Also `Rico notes.md` (two notes from this
game).

**What it is:** A co-op shooter about fighting waves of insects in caves, with classes that each have
their own job, and maps built by the computer so each mission is different. It came out in early
access in February 2018 (the studio's anniversary events, *"Seven Years in Orbit"* in Feb 2025 and
*"Eight Years in Orbit"* in Mar 2026, count from then) and left it later; the pages do not give the 1.0
date. It is a paid game. New content after launch has been free (seasons, events), with cosmetic DLC
on top (the Skullcrusher DLC in 2025-02 is named in the patch notes). Season 05 "Drilling Deeper"
shipped 2024-06-13 and Season 06 "Relics of Hoxxes" in 2026-01. A roguelite spin-off, Rogue Core, came
later. The Steam store page calls it *"a 1-4 player co-op FPS"* (a first-person shooter; checked
2026-10-03). The pages do not say who made or published it, who hosts, which engine it uses, or how a
mission ends.

**How close to Dominion:** Close in spirit, not in camera. It shares a sci-fi setting, a small squad
of friends against alien hordes, PvE only, classes that need each other, generated maps, and four
players. It differs in being first-person, in using missions rather than runs that build power, and
in having no player-versus-player risk. It is the corpus's model of a loved co-op game, so it is the
best guide to what players reward when the co-op works. The cross-game page warns that this game and
Helldivers 2 are *"phenomena whose lessons may not transfer to a solo project"* - read its lessons as
direction, not as a promise of the same result.

### The short version (plain words)
Almost nobody dislikes this game: 97.1% thumbs up, only 61 thumbs-down in 2,130 kept reviews, and
good things are said 7 times as often as bad ones (3,744 against 515). The single most common thing
players write is the community's catchphrase, 610 times, 14% of everything said - more than any
remark about the game itself. The people (friends, kind strangers, a shared culture) are 28.8% of
everything said, almost as much as the game's design at 30.3%, and close to half of what players
praise. The few complaints are the cost of the same design: it gets repetitive (29 times), it is hard
alone (27), and you need a group (22). The game's best feature and its main complaint are one thing
seen from two sides. Counted the same way as the later games, it has the most praise per 100 reviews
(175.9) of the ten games in the newest cross-game table, and only two games have fewer complaints.

### What players praised, most to least
Counts are times it was said, out of 2,130 kept reviews; "per 100" means times per 100 reviews.
1. **The catchphrase and salute, used as a greeting** - 610 times, 28.6 per 100. Players write it
   unprompted. It outweighs combat (42), the whole art side (133), price (65), progression (267) and
   difficulty (92) added together (610 against 599). It is 13.5% of everything written about the game.
   The top praise is 21 times the top complaint (610 against 29).
2. **Much better with friends** - 215 times, 10.1 per 100. Across the corpus only Aliens: Fireteam
   Elite says it more often (10.4); Remnant II ties at 10.1.
3. **Players describe themselves in the game's own words** - 141 times, 6.6 per 100.
4. **The community is welcoming; strangers are kind** - 123 times, 5.8 per 100.
5. **It keeps pulling you back** - 121 times, 5.7 per 100. This is the first praise about the game
   itself, and it comes fifth.
6. **Silly actions with no game effect** - dancing, drinking in the bar, petting the loot bugs,
   kicking the barrel, prop hunt in the lobby, breaking gravity - 111 times, 5.2 per 100. This is the
   seventh most common thing anyone says. The whole subject of these toys (114) beats the
   difficulty (92), the enemies (75), all sound (57), the levels (20) and the story (14).
7. **Every class has a job the others cannot do** - 98 times, 4.6 per 100.
8. **It works fine alone** - 79 times, 3.7 per 100.
9. **Updates arrive regularly** - 69 times, 3.2 per 100 (Gunfire Reborn leads the corpus at 3.9;
   Warframe 3.6).
10. **The game's satire lands; players get the joke** - 68 times, 3.2 per 100.
11. **The studio earned my trust; I will buy what they make next** - 63 times, 3.0 per 100.
12. **The computer-built maps keep it varied** - 59 times, 2.8 per 100.
13. **The price is fair** - 57 times, 2.7 per 100 (65 remarks about price in all).
14. **Builds and gear choices are deep** - 57 times, 2.7 per 100.
15. **The missions vary** - 56 times, 2.6 per 100.
16. **The best game of its kind** - 54 times, 2.5 per 100.
17. **The art direction looks well done** - 44 times, 2.1 per 100 (the art as a whole, 133 remarks).
18. **The difficulty levels are clear steps and you pick your own** - 42 times, 2.0 per 100.
19. **Playing with strangers is good** - 41 times, 1.9 per 100, against 4 who say it is bad, 10 to 1.
    Helldivers 2 runs 14 to 9 and Back 4 Blood 8 to 30 the other way.
20. **There is plenty of content** - 40 times, 1.9 per 100.
21. **Unlocking things feels satisfying** - 40 times, 1.9 per 100.
22. **The studio listens and the feedback changes the game** - 39 times, 1.8 per 100.
23. **The shooting has punch** - 36 times, 1.7 per 100.
24. **The game only works if the team works together** - 36 times, 1.7 per 100.
25. **A patch made it better** - 35 times, 1.6 per 100.
26. **New content after launch is free, and that is why I trust them** - 1.5 per 100 (the pages give
    the rate, not the count).
27. **Hard in a good way** - 1.2 per 100.
28. **Old season rewards can still be earned later** - 9 times (see "How it changed").
29. **Friendly fire makes funny stories** - 0.6 per 100 (a floor; the page says this row undercounts).
30. **Worth playing even with nothing to earn** - one reviewer (85232716) Rico agreed with: *"the
    missions themselves are intrinsically rewarding... I'm never playing just to reach a goal."* The
    pages give no count for this.
31. In early access: **good value while unfinished** (said from 2018-03 to 2020-12, then never again)
    and **does not feel unfinished** (2018-11 to 2023-11). No counts given.

Also: 440 thumbs-up reviews (20.7 per 100 on the English page, 20.6 in the cross-game table) say
nothing specific - about one review in five, the same in every game. 62 times a reviewer placed the
game against others without saying how (61 on today's script run). Destruction that changes how you
play is praised at 1.4 per 100 (EDF 5 leads at 2.5), and memorable lines at 0.4 reviews per 100 (EDF 5
leads at 7.6).

Where the praise sits: the people are 32.9% of all praise, game design 27.7%, and the studio's and
game's reputation only 5.0% (Back 4 Blood, which failed: 8.3%, 32.9% and 18.5%).

### What players complained about, most to least
The pages do not split each complaint by thumbs up or down. The English page names the top four as
the complaints the 61 thumbs-down reviews agree on. Counts are out of 2,130 kept reviews.
1. **It gets repetitive** - 29 times, 1.4 per 100.
2. **It is noticeably harder or worse alone** - 27 times, 1.3 per 100.
3. **I have nobody to play with, and the game suffers** - 22 times, 1.0 per 100.
4. **There is too little content** - 20 times, 0.9 per 100. (In Space Marine 2 the same complaint is
   the top one at 5.9 per 100.)
5. **A thumbs down with nothing said** - 17 times, 0.8 per 100. (The English page lists this fifth;
   the ranked master page leaves it out.)
6. **The unlocks are a grind** - 15 times, 0.7 per 100.
7. **The cast leaves people out; players ask for a roster that includes them** - 9 times, 0.4 per 100.
8. **It runs out fast** - 9 times, 0.4 per 100.
9. **A patch made it worse** - 8 times, 0.4 per 100.
10. **Build choices are shallow** - 8 times, 0.4 per 100.
11. **Some weapons or choices are useless** - 8 times, 0.4 per 100.
12. **Only buy it on sale** - 7 times, 0.3 per 100.
13. **Its fame is bigger than the game** - 7 times, 0.3 per 100.
14. **Hard to tell where to go in the caves** - 7 times, 0.3 per 100.
15. **Buggy** - 7 times, 0.3 per 100.
16. **Price too high for what it is** - 6 times, 0.3 per 100.
17. **One class is weaker than the rest** - 6 times, 0.3 per 100.
18. **A class I expected is missing** - 6 times, 0.3 per 100.
19. **Nothing left to unlock, so no reason to keep going** - 6 times, 0.3 per 100.
20. **Trolls and griefers spoil runs** - 6 times, 0.3 per 100.
21. **Updates come too slowly** - 5 times, 0.2 per 100.
22. **The systems are too shallow** - 5 times, 0.2 per 100.
23. **Ammo, health or supplies run out too fast** - 5 times, 0.2 per 100.
24. **Weapons feel weak, hits feel like nothing** - 5 times, 0.2 per 100 (on the master page's list;
    today's script run does not rank it in its top 25).
25. **Not enough kinds of enemy** - 5 times, 0.2 per 100.
26. **Teammates who play badly or carelessly** - 5 times, 0.2 per 100 (today's script run ranks it;
    the master page's list does not hold it).

Also: 7 reviews have a thumb that says the opposite of the words. The master page had counted this as
a complaint; a correction (round 470) struck it out as neutral. In early access, 25 remarks waited for
promised content (13 of them in 2018-19), and players worried the game would die (2018-04 to 2019-06,
then never again).

**What is missing from the complaints:** no crashes, no servers, no money tricks and no fights with
the studio in the top 25. All the big complaints are trade-offs of the design, not defects. Of all
515 complaints, 46.4% are about game design, 10.5% about how much content there is, 12.4% about other
players, 7.4% about tech, 4.3% about updates, 3.9% about reputation and 2.7% about price and sales.
Design and content together are 57% of the complaints. The whole complaint list is 515 remarks -
under a quarter of Back 4 Blood's 2,284, over nearly twice as many months. Counted the newer way
(504 complaint remarks, 23.7 per 100), only Crab Champions (18.7) and Risk of Rain 2 (18.0) have fewer
complaints per 100; Gunfire Reborn has 33.1, EDF 5 38.9, Escape from Duckov 40.4, Warframe 50.9,
Helldivers 2 55.5, ARC Raiders 56.7 and The First Descendant 122.2.

**Who leaves, and when:** the 61 thumbs-down reviewers show a median of 8 hours played; the thumbs-up
reviewers show 40. Over all kept reviews the median is 40 hours; 562 show 100 hours or more and 29
show 1,000 or more.

### How it changed over time
- **Complaints fell, then rose.** Complaints per 100 reviews: 55.5 in 2018, 43.7 in 2019, 26.4 in
  2020, 21.4 in 2021, 19.7 in 2022, **10.3 in 2023**, 11.9 in 2024, then **22.9 in 2025 and 27.1 in
  2026** - an 81% fall from 2018 to 2023, then more than double from that floor. Today's script run,
  with the wordless reviews left out, gives the same shape: 54.8, 42.6, 24.8, 20.2, 18.7, 9.7, 10.8,
  20.7, 24.9. Thumbs-down went from 4.1% (2018) to 1.7% (2023) and back to 4.0% (2026). The pages do
  not know why it rose again, and the last two years are the thinnest part of the sample (443 reviews
  across the two years).
- **Praise per 100** peaked at 234.0 in 2019 and settled at 148 to 165 from 2023 on (script run, with
  the wordless reviews left out: 213.2 in 2019, 128.8 to 145.1 from 2023 on).
- **What fell** from 2018 to 2026 (per 100 reviews, script run): too little content 5.5 to 0.0;
  repetitive 4.1 to 1.7; much better with friends 15.1 to 5.1; steady updates 5.5 to 1.7; the game
  needs teamwork 4.8 to 0.6.
- **What rose:** the catchphrase 8.9 to 36.7 (peak 41.4 in 2023); players calling themselves by the
  game's name 1.4 to 6.2 (peak 12.7 in 2022); the satire lands 0.0 to 3.4 (peak 6.4 in 2025); the
  cast leaves people out 0.0 to 1.7.
- **Review volume on Steam** by year: 4,221 (2018), 10,669 (2019), 28,489 (2020), 29,508 (2021),
  37,241 (2022), **51,740 (2023, 24% of all)**, 30,299 (2024), 15,811 (2025) and 7,586 (2026 so far).
  It fell every year after 2023 while the studio kept shipping seasons and events on schedule.
- **November is the biggest month in 7 of 8 years** - the Steam autumn sale and Steam Awards. The
  biggest Novembers: 989 (2018), 2,411 (2019), 6,802 (2020), 7,305 (2021), **10,316 (2022)**, 8,529
  (2023) and 2,008 (2025). In 2022 November had 2.4 times the next month, with nothing shipped. The one
  year November lost was 2024, to June 2024 (4,365 against November's 3,329), the month Season 05
  shipped with four patches in eight days. 2026's biggest month so far is January (1,518), when Season
  06 shipped. Smaller things (events, hotfixes, cosmetic DLC, the anniversary) leave no mark on the
  monthly count. November 2024 was a dev livestream and November 2025 an announcement - neither
  shipped content.
- **Reviews got shorter as the game aged.** One-remark reviews went from 51.4% (2018) to 76.9%
  (2023); reviews with five or more remarks fell from 14.4% to 6.9%; the catchphrase went from 3.6% of
  all remarks (2018) to 24.0% (2023). The biggest review year said the least. From 2025 the reviews
  grew a little again (69.7% one-remark in 2026).
- **The one shipped feature that moved a count:** letting players go back to old seasons' reward
  tracks. Announced 2024-04-14, live with Season 05 on 2024-06-13 (studio patch notes). Praise that
  missed content comes back was said 9 times in all - in 2022-11, 2023-11, 2024-01, 2024-08, 2024-10,
  2024-11 twice, 2026-06 and 2026-08. Two of the nine came before the feature; 4 came in the eight
  months after it shipped; 2024 holds 5 of the 9 in a remark that averages one a year. Quote:
  *"MASSIVE respect to the devs for allowing players to switch their battlepass progress to any of the
  previous seasons"* (233551125).
- Studio patch notes also show the official metal song (2024-07), seasonal events (Space Beach Party,
  Rocktoberfest, Halloween, Yuletide, Lunar Festival), Season 06 announced and the Rogue Core spin-off
  delayed in 2025-11, a board game Kickstarter (2025-07) and an $85 mug (2025-02). The patch record
  only reaches back to 2024-03 and covers 29 of the 103 months.

### Co-op and online play
- **Friends are the core.** Much better with friends: 215. Needs a group: 22. Hard alone: 27.
  Fine alone: 79. The same choice makes both lists.
- **Strangers are a plus here, unlike in most games.** Good with strangers 41 against 4 bad (10 to
  1). Welcoming community 123. Trolls and griefers only 6; careless teammates 5.
- **Teamwork is built in.** Every class needed: 98. The game only works if the team works together:
  36. A class missing: 6. One class weaker: 6.
- **Players come for different things in the same run** - one reviewer with 1,790 hours (178648003)
  hand-picks teammates because some play for fun, some for rewards, some only for the mission. Rico's
  view (Rico notes): this is a signalling problem, best fixed outside the game first (Discord channels
  or a role picker: for fun, for rewards, for clears), with an in-game lobby flag as the costlier
  version.
- **Finding a game:** one reviewer praises a server browser that tells you what the team needs, and
  one says the harder settings filter out weaker players. One sighting each; no other game has either.
- **Friendly fire:** praised as story-making at 0.6 per 100 (Helldivers 2: 2.3). Across the whole
  corpus only 3 people filed it as a cost.
- **Loot taken by teammates:** 0.0 per 100 here, against 5.8 in Rogue Core, where the spin-off made
  pickups shared.
- **Bots:** complaints that bots are useless are 0.1 per 100 here (Back 4 Blood 2.6).
- **Hosting, lag, joining a running game, scaling to the number of players, servers:** the pages
  report nothing. Tech is only 3.2% of all remarks.

### Combat, movement and feel
- The shooting has punch: 36. Weapons feel weak: 5. Combat as a whole: 42 remarks.
- Silly actions with no effect (dance, beer, barrel, pet the loot bug): 111 - more than any remark
  about enemies or levels.
- One reviewer praises a class whose job does not need aim (filed under help for players with limited
  hand movement).
- Movement, controls, camera, stuns, loss of control and readability: nothing said on the pages.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- The enemies as a subject: 75 remarks. Not enough kinds of enemy: 5.
- Difficulty as a subject: 92 remarks. Clear difficulty steps you pick yourself: 42. Hard in a good
  way: 1.2 per 100. Supplies run out too fast: 5.
- Hard to tell where to go in the caves: 7 (the levels as a subject, 20).
- **Fear of the insects:** 2 of the 10 accessibility remarks are about phobia - one player could not
  play it, one wants a way to remove the creepy look.
- Bosses and the end game: nothing said on the pages.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- Deep builds: 57. Shallow builds: 8. Some options useless: 8. Systems too shallow: 5.
- Satisfying unlocks: 40. Grindy: 15. Nothing left to chase: 6. Progression as a whole: 267 remarks.
- Old season rewards can be earned later: 9 (and the feature behind it moved the count).
- One sighting each: cosmetic rewards that leave you nothing to show for the work, and no way to
  change how your character sounds. Neither appears in any other game.
- Rico's note: the reviewer he agreed with says games that make you *"play to get stronger to play to
  get stronger"* go stale, and this one does not because each mission is fun on its own. Rico: *"I
  feel this way playing Helldivers 2."*
- Random drops and luck: nothing said on the pages.

### Runs, content and replay value
- **The trade-off, side by side:** repetitive 29 against varied maps and missions 115; hard alone 27
  against fine alone 79; needs a group 22 against better with friends 215; grindy 15 against
  satisfying unlocks 40. People who like the design say so 449 times; people who do not, 93.
- Keeps pulling you back: 121. Runs out fast: 9. Too little content: 20. Plenty: 40.
- Repetition is the tightest complaint in the corpus: 1.8, 1.4 and 1.4 per 100 in Back 4 Blood, this
  game and Helldivers 2 - a loved, a hated and a contested game complain about it at the same rate.
- Too little content fell from 5.5 per 100 in 2018 to 0.0 in 2026 as the seasons piled up.

### Money and price
- Price fair: 57 (2.7 per 100). Too high: 6. Buy only on sale: 7 (0.3 per 100, against Back 4
  Blood's 8.6).
- New content after launch is free, named as a reason to trust the studio: 1.5 per 100 (Back 4
  Blood 0.0, Helldivers 2 0.1).
- Price and sales are only 2.7% of all complaints. The pages report no shop or battle pass complaint;
  the seasons' reward tracks are free (one reviewer calls it a "battlepass", 233551125).
- Not sold in my country, and the owner puts players off: 0.0 per 100 here (Helldivers 2: 2.4 and 2.8).

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
Tech is 3.2% of everything said - for an eight-year-old online game, almost nothing. Buggy: 7. No
crash, lag or server complaint reaches the top 25. Tech is 7.4% of complaints (Helldivers 2: 21.7%).
One sighting: mods made it worse.

### The studio, updates and community
- Steady updates: 69. Updates too slow: 5. Studio earned my trust: 63 (Back 4 Blood: 0). Listens and
  acts: 39. A patch made it better: 35 (1.6 per 100); made it worse: 8; fixed what mattered: 0.3 per
  100; ignores feedback: 0.0 per 100. Updates stopped: 0.0 (Back 4 Blood 4.7).
- One sighting each: a studio that pays players for their work, and the good players having left.
- The culture: the catchphrase 610 (28.6 per 100), the identity 141 (6.6 per 100). All culture
  remarks are 35.7 per 100 here, 29.7 in Helldivers 2, and **0.0 in Back 4 Blood**, which failed.
- **Comparing it to other games is rare here:** placing the game against others is 3.1 per 100 (Back 4
  Blood 25.1, Helldivers 2 0.9). Players argue the game was judged unfairly at 0.2 per 100 (Rogue Core
  10.2). Back 4 Blood kept borrowing an older game's name; this game grew its own words.
- The satire lands: 68. The cast leaves people out: 9. The story as a subject: 14. Sound: 57.
- **The ritual cannot be copied.** Rogue Core, by the same studio with the same catchphrase, gets it
  at 7.5 per 100 - 26% of this game's strength.
- **Accessibility:** 10 remarks in all; two of them are about phobia, one about a job that needs no aim.

### What players asked for
The pages do not keep a separate request list. The asks inside the complaints: more content (too
little, 20; runs out fast, 9; nothing left to chase, 6), more kinds of enemy (5), a wider cast (9), a
missing class (6), more promised content (25 waiting, 13 of them in 2018-19), faster updates (5),
a way to remove the look of the insects for players with a phobia (1), a way to change how your
character sounds (1), and a way to earn missed season rewards (granted - 9 thanks).

### Only in this game
- **Silly actions with no effect, praised 111 times** (5.2 per 100; Helldivers 2 0.4, Back 4 Blood
  0.0).
- **Every class needed** at 4.6 per 100 (Back 4 Blood 1.7, Helldivers 2 0.0).
- **Players like the strangers the game gives them** (10 to 1, the only game where it runs this way).
- **The only shipped feature in the corpus that visibly moved a count** (old seasons coming back).
- **Studio earned my trust** at 3.0 per 100 and free post-launch content at 1.5 per 100, both near
  zero in the other early games.
- **Reviews turned into a greeting**: by 2023 a quarter of all remarks were the catchphrase.
- **The most praise per 100 reviews** of the ten games in the newest cross-game table (175.9), and a
  praise-to-complaint ratio of 7.4 to 1.
- **14 kinds of remark found in no other game**, most at one sighting: the cast leaves people out (9),
  a server browser that tells you what you need, harder settings that filter the players, cosmetic
  rewards with nothing to show, no way to change how you sound, mods made it worse, the good players
  left, a studio that pays players for their work, phobia (2), and a job that needs no aim.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Make the game great with friends, and still fine alone and with strangers.**
   Evidence: better with friends 215, fine alone 79, good with strangers 41 to 4; and on the other
   side hard alone 27, needs a group 22 - three of the top four complaints are about having no group.
   Dominion could: tune solo and duo runs so they work (scale enemies by player count), make
   matchmade strangers useful to each other, and give a solo player a reason to queue with others.
   Strength: **strong**.
2. **Give players a shared ritual - a salute, a phrase, a gesture - and let them own it.**
   Evidence: the catchphrase 610 times (28.6 per 100), identity 141; Back 4 Blood, which failed, has
   0.0. But Rogue Core, with the same phrase, gets only 7.5 per 100: a ritual cannot be pasted in.
   Dominion could: put a salute or call-out button and a voice line in from day one, tie it to a
   moment that happens every run (the drop-in, the extraction), and see if players pick it up rather
   than forcing it.
   Strength: **medium** (strong count, but the cause is unclear; one failure against two survivors).
3. **Build cheap toys with no game effect into the hub and the run.**
   Evidence: dancing, the bar, petting the loot bug, the barrel - 111 times, more than enemies (75)
   and levels (20).
   Dominion could: give the pre-run hub a drink, a dance, a kickable object, a pettable creature, a
   lobby mini-game - things friends do while waiting for the host.
   Strength: **strong**.
4. **Give each class a job the others cannot do.**
   Evidence: every class needed 98; teamwork required 36; one class weaker 6; a class missing 6.
   Dominion could: design classes around a team need (lighting, mobility, defence, resupply) rather
   than damage flavours, and check none falls behind.
   Strength: **strong**.
5. **Fight repetition with generated maps, but expect it to stay the top complaint.**
   Evidence: repetitive 29 against varied 115; the same rate (about 1.4 per 100) in every game.
   Dominion could: vary objectives, modifiers and map pieces between runs, and plan a content
   pipeline for players who run out (too little 20, runs out fast 9, nothing left to chase 6).
   Strength: **strong**.
6. **Make every run fun with nothing to earn; do not lean only on unlocks.**
   Evidence: Rico's note on reviewer 85232716 (no count) and the pull-you-back praise 121; grind
   complaint 15, nothing left to chase 6.
   Dominion could: judge each run by whether it is fun at max level with all unlocks done.
   Strength: **weak** on numbers, but Rico agrees with it.
7. **Let missed time-limited rewards come back.**
   Evidence: the old-season feature is the only shipped feature in the corpus that moved a count (4 of
   9 thanks in the eight months after it shipped).
   Dominion could: never lock seasonal or event rewards away for good; let players switch to old
   reward tracks.
   Strength: **medium** (small count, clear link).
8. **Steady, free updates buy trust that lasts years.**
   Evidence: steady updates 69, studio earned trust 63, listens and acts 39, free post-launch
   content 1.5 per 100; price and sales are only 2.7% of complaints.
   Dominion could: plan a regular free update rhythm and sell only cosmetics on top, and say so.
   Strength: **strong**.
9. **A clear difficulty ladder the player picks for themselves.**
   Evidence: well-graded difficulty 42; hard in a good way 1.2 per 100.
   Dominion could: offer named difficulty steps per run, each clearly harder, chosen at the start.
   Strength: **medium**.
10. **Let players say what they want from a run before it starts.**
    Evidence: the 1,790-hour reviewer (178648003) and Rico's note: some want fun, some rewards, some
    clears. Six complain of trolls, five of careless teammates.
    Dominion could: start with Discord channels or a role picker (Rico's free version); later a lobby
    flag such as "casual / full sweep / fast clear".
    Strength: **weak** (one review, Rico's own view).
11. **A shared joke in the tone helps the culture form.**
    Evidence: the satire lands 68; it is 3.2 per 100 here and 3.3 in Helldivers 2, 0.0 in Back 4 Blood;
    it rose from 0.0 per 100 in 2018 to a peak of 6.4 in 2025.
    Dominion could: give the corporation or the war a voice players can laugh at and quote.
    Strength: **medium**.
12. **Make strangers good for each other.**
    Evidence: strangers good 41 to 4; welcoming community 123; no loot stealing (0.0 per 100 here vs
    5.8 in Rogue Core with shared pickups).
    Dominion could: give each player their own loot or a fair split, reward reviving and helping, and
    avoid systems where one player's gain is another's loss.
    Strength: **medium**.
13. **Read the review text, not only the score, and know Steam's calendar.**
    Evidence: November sales beat every update but one season in eight years; the catchphrase turned
    reviews into a greeting (one-remark reviews 76.9% in 2023); complaints per 100 doubled in 2025-26
    while thumbs-down stayed under 5%. Rogue Core's text moved a month before its score.
    Dominion could: track complaint rates per month, not just the percentage; time big updates away
    from or into the autumn sale on purpose.
    Strength: **medium**.
14. **Widen the cast.**
    Evidence: 9 ask for a roster that includes people it leaves out (rising from 0.0 to 1.7 per 100
    between 2018 and 2026); 6 miss a class; one wants to change how their character sounds.
    Dominion could: let players pick body type, voice and look for their soldier.
    Strength: **weak**.
15. **Think about phobias when designing bugs or swarms.**
    Evidence: 2 of 10 accessibility remarks are about phobia - one player could not play, one wants a
    way to remove it.
    Dominion could: consider an option that changes the look of the creepiest enemies.
    Strength: **weak**.
16. **Win the first hours; that is where people decide to leave.**
    Evidence: the 61 thumbs-down reviewers show a median of 8 hours, the thumbs-up reviewers 40; the
    top complaints of those who left are about having no group and running out.
    Dominion could: make the first few hours of runs strong for a solo player and a new pair of
    strangers, not only for a set group of four, and check where new players stop.
    Strength: **medium** (the hours are clear; 61 reviews is small).
17. **Save the big push for full seasons; small drops do not move players.**
    Evidence: only Season 05 (June 2024, 4,365 reviews) beat a November in eight years, and Season 06
    made January the biggest month of 2026; events, hotfixes, cosmetic DLC and the anniversary left no
    mark on the monthly count.
    Dominion could: bundle new content into fewer, larger updates with a clear launch date, and keep
    small events as upkeep rather than as the plan to bring players back.
    Strength: **medium** (one game, and the patch record covers only 29 of 103 months).
18. **Aim for complaints that are trade-offs, not defects.**
    Evidence: no crash, server, money or studio complaint in the top 25; tech is 7.4% of complaints and
    price 2.7%; design and content are 57%. Helldivers 2's tech share is 21.7%.
    Dominion could: treat stability on a listen server, fair prices and honest talk as the floor, so
    the only complaints left are the design choices Dominion made on purpose.
    Strength: **medium**.
19. **Help players find the right game before they join.**
    Evidence: one reviewer praises a server browser that tells you what the team needs; one says the
    harder settings filter the players. One sighting each, found in no other game.
    Dominion could: show each open lobby's class mix, difficulty and the host's goal in the browser,
    and let the hardest settings act as a filter for experienced players.
    Strength: **weak** (two reviews, but cheap and a natural fit for player-hosted games).
20. **Let a class do its job without fine aiming.**
    Evidence: one reviewer praises a class whose job does not need aim, as help for players with
    limited hand movement; accessibility is 10 remarks in all.
    Dominion could: give at least one class a support job (resupply, lighting, shields) that a player
    with weaker aim can do fully.
    Strength: **weak** (one review).
21. **Keep teamwork demands alive as players get skilled.**
    Evidence: praise that the game needs teamwork fell from 4.8 per 100 in 2018 to 0.6 in 2026, and
    much better with friends from 15.1 to 5.1, while the catchphrase rose.
    Dominion could: add harder steps or modifiers that force coordination again for veteran squads.
    Strength: **weak** (the fall may come from shorter reviews, not from the game).

### Limits
- A 1.0% sample, with no margin of error on the pages; 20 reviews a month at the floor, so single
  months are rough. Per-year margins are not counted either.
- **What changed from the earlier entry:** the read is now 2,130 kept reviews (2,133 sampled; 3 are not
  reviews of the game - one describes another online game, one a game played on a phone, one is a
  personal story), so per 100 rates use 2,130. The complaint list gained "a thumbs down with nothing
  said" (17) at row 5 and "careless teammates" (5) from the newer script run; "the thumb says the
  opposite of the words" (7) was struck out as neutral by a correction. The "nothing said" share is
  14% on the round-111 count (650 remarks) and 14.4% on today's script run (649). Remarks in all are
  4,511 at round 111, 4,514 in the early cross-game table and 4,519 on today's script run.
- Two counts are given for praise and complaint: the master page's (with the wordless reviews in) and
  today's script run (without them). Praise to complaint is 7.27 to 1 in the early table, 7.43 to 1 in
  the section 9 recount and 7.4 to 1 in the later tables; the early figures are left as they were.
- Only 61 thumbs-down reviews; their pattern is clear but small.
- English only. Russian (65,935), Simplified Chinese (36,562), Spanish (8,308) and LatAm Spanish
  (2,138) reviews were pulled and not read - 112,943 in all. 4 reviews in the English group are written
  in another language. 286 reviews (13.4%) were edited later and are counted on the day they were
  written.
- The patch record reaches back only to 2024-03; nothing about early access, 1.0 or seasons 1 to 4 can
  be linked to the counts.
- The pages cannot say why reviews collapsed after 2023, or why complaints rose in 2025-26 - the sample
  says nothing about players who stopped playing, because they stopped writing too.
- The friendly-fire row counts only one of two places it was filed before a merge, so it is a floor.
- The camera and player count in "What it is" come from the Steam store page; hosting and engine are
  not on any page.
- Small mismatches between pages: thumbs up is 2,072 of 2,133 on one count and 2,069 of 2,130 on the
  other (both 97.1%); complaints per 100 are 24.1 in the early cross-game table and 23.7 in the later
  recount; accessibility is 10 remarks now (9 before the phobia branch was built); the ranked complaint
  list on the master page leaves out the 17 wordless thumbs-down that the English page lists fifth.
- Nothing a reviewer says about prices, events or the studio was checked; the patch dates come from the
  studio's own patch notes on Steam.

---

## Back 4 Blood

**Read:** 1,663 of 33,766 English reviews (4.9%), the findings page prints no margin of error, written 2021-10 to 2026-08 (59 months). **69.2% thumbs up**
in the sample (Steam: 69.2%; the pages do not give Steam's label). Plus every Latin American Spanish
review: 712 of 712, a census with no margin, 81.3% thumbs up (Part B below).
**Pages:** `findings/back-4-blood-english.md`, `findings/back-4-blood.md`, `findings/back-4-blood-latam.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` sections 1 to 8 (also the table at the top of the page, and short mentions in
sections 9, 10, 12, 13, 20, 22 and 23).

**What it is:** A four-player co-op zombie horde shooter in first person (the Steam store page calls it *"a thrilling cooperative first-person shooter"*, checked 2026-10-03), made by the studio behind an
older, much-loved co-op zombie game (Left 4 Dead) and sold on that link; published with Warner Bros. You
build a deck of cards that changes how you play, run by run. Paid, with three paid expansions (2022-04-12,
2022-08-30, 2022-12-06). Launched 2021-10-12, and was on a subscription service from day one
(2021-10-06). Announced as always-online before launch (2021-06-20); an offline solo campaign shipped
2021-12-19. The studio was bought on 2021-12-17 and announced no more content on 2023-02-02. Copy
protection (Denuvo) was removed 2024-05-16. (These dates come from the game's own news feed, 116 items,
which the page used as its patch record.) Bots fill empty seats. No review read carries the Early Access
mark: it was released whole. The pages do not say who hosts or give the engine; 3 reviews complain there
is no player hosting.

**How close to Dominion:** Shares four-player co-op PvE, bots, a build system that changes play during a
run (the cards), random rule changes per run, and a paid model with expansions. Differs in camera (first
person), setting (modern zombies, not sci-fi), structure (horde campaign, not extraction or arena runs)
and in being sold as the successor to a famous game.

### The short version (plain words)
The reviews are an argument about a different game. The second most common thing said is "this is the
successor to that older game" (241 times), and the fourth is "the comparison was unfair" (140 times):
535 points, 12% of everything said, are about a game that is not this one. Two of the top three
complaints are about money: wait for a sale (143) and too expensive (106). The shooting and the card
system are well liked (96 and 97 times). The game has no player culture at all - zero catchphrases,
in-jokes or shared rituals in 2,375 reviews across both languages - and its best year was 2025, two
years after updates stopped. On the 2026-10-03 re-count, English reviews carry 118.0 praises and 137.2
complaints per 100, 0.86 praise for each complaint - beside Aliens: Fireteam Elite (0.88), far below
Deep Rock Galactic (7.27) and Helldivers 2 (1.95). The cross-game page calls it "a positioning failure":
10.5% of its complaints are about how it was sold, two and a half times the share in Deep Rock Galactic
(3.9%) or Helldivers 2 (4.2%).

### What players praised, most to least
English counts (LatAm counts in Part B).
1. **"It is the true successor to the older game."** 241 times, the most-said thing after "good game".
   Peaked at launch (73 in 2021), then 25 to 39 a year. 14.5 per 100 reviews read; 0.0 in Deep Rock
   Galactic and 0.6 in Helldivers 2.
2. **Much better with friends.** 125.
3. **The game is better than its reputation.** 118. It rose over time: 11 (2021), 21, 17, 28 (2024), 26,
   15 (2026). 7.1 per 100 reviews read; by period 2.6 per 100 in 2021, 11.0 in 2024, 8.9 in 2026.
4. **Card builds are deep and varied.** 97. The page calls the card deck the single most-praised design
   feature in the corpus.
5. **Shooting hits with weight.** 96 (5.8 per 100, the highest of the first three games).
6. **Looks great.** 69.
7. **The cards change how you play,** not just numbers. 51.
8. **Patches fixed what mattered.** 50; peaked at 18 in 2022, the year of the expansions.
9. **Keeps pulling you back.** 45.
10. **Levels are well built.** 33.
11. **Every class has a job the others can't do.** 28 (1.7 per 100).
12. **Hard in a good way.** 27.
13. **Fair price.** 27. Grew from 1 (2021) to 8 (2024).
14. **Randomness keeps each run fresh.** 27.
15. **Works solo.** 23. Grew from 2 in 2021 to 6 in 2024.
16. **Plenty of content.** 23.
17. **Deep once you learn it.** 22.
18. **The team has to work together.** 21.
19. **Punchy sound effects.** 18.
20. **Paid add-ons are fair.** 17.
21. **Story worth following.** 17.
22. **Satisfying progression.** 16.
23. **Difficulty levels well graded.** 16.
24. **Random rule changes each run are welcome.** 14.
25. **Runs well.** 13. Runs on a modest computer: 7.
26. **Fine with strangers.** 8 (against 30 saying poor with strangers, across both languages; Deep Rock
    Galactic page).
27. **Smaller praise from a tag count over the summaries:** teamwork pays off 13; good enemy variety 12;
    crossplay works well 11; bots help in a fight 10; bots make solo possible 9; movement feels
    responsive 9; memorable special enemies 7; a welcoming community 5. One reviewer says lasting damage
    makes you play carefully; one says the game works with an adapted controller setup.
"Good game" with nothing else: 431 (25.9 per 100 reviews; Deep Rock Galactic 20.6, Helldivers 2 22.5).

### What players complained about, most to least
English counts. The pages do not split complaints by thumbs up and down for this game, but the whole
group is 69.2% thumbs up while its points are more negative (2,284) than positive (1,961): many
complaints come from people who still recommend it (0.77 complaints per recommending review, Immortal:
Unchained page).
1. **Buy it on sale only.** 143 (8.6 per 100). Peaked in 2024 (35), three years after launch.
2. **The successor comparison was unfair** - the framing set a bar it could not clear. 140. Never below
   17 in any year: 17, 31, 24, 28, 21, 19. 8.4 per 100; zero in Deep Rock Galactic and Helldivers 2.
3. **Too expensive for what it is.** 106. 42 in 2021, falling to 8 to 16 a year (10.0 per 100 in 2021,
   3.2 in 2025, 5.9 in 2026).
4. **Updates stopped.** 78. Zero before 2023, then 28, 21, 12, 17. 4.7 per 100.
5. **No mod support.** 50 (49 on the re-count; 3.0 per 100; zero in Deep Rock Galactic and Helldivers
   2).
6. **Dead game.** 49 (0.9 per 100 in 2021, 7.1 in 2026).
7. **Nobody to play with, and the game suffers for it.** 45.
8. **Bots useless in combat.** 43 (2.6 per 100). The first complaint about how the game plays is this
   one, at eighth in the combined list. It fell from 5.9 per 100 in 2021 to 0.6 in 2026.

13. **Too little content.** 33.
14. **Could have been more, settled for less.** 31.
15. **Repetitive.** 30 (1.8 per 100 - almost the same rate in every game).
16. **Overcomplicated.** 30. Overwhelming at first: 17.
17. **Thin, forgettable story.** 28.
18. **Shipped broken.** 28.
